    each line a continuous faint hairline (was one dot in three). Labels `+50°` (column 1, rows 1..h − 4) and `11h`/`11h30m` (row 0), ≤ 8 per
    axis, found in the same pass that draws the line. **Only from 120 columns**: below that it would
    crowd the figure.
  - Background: `0.12·cells` candidate stars (≤ 4000) with a steep magnitude distribution (level
    `45 + 150·u³`, *changed* from `30 + 110·u³`). Each chart keeps a candidate with probability `0.25 + 0.75·exp(−(b/12°)²)`, b the
    galactic latitude of its sky position (NGP α 192.859°, δ 27.128°, J2000), so Cygnus, Crux and
    Scorpius sit in a denser field than Ursa Major.
  - Strokes stop 0.9 row short of both stars and trace in at constant pen speed.
  - Figure stars *(changed)*: in Unicode, half-block discs in `Blackbody(T_eff)`, radius by magnitude
    tier: mag ≤ 0.6 → 2.25 px (21 round pixels), ≤ 1.6 → 1.6 px (3 × 3), ≤ 3.0 → a 2 × 2 blot on a
    pixel corner, fainter → one pixel (only these radii come out round in square pixels; others make a
    plus). While the chart fades each shrinks to one pixel. ASCII keeps the magnitude glyphs (`@ * + .`
    for mag ≤ 1.5, ≤ 2.5, ≤ 3.3, fainter).
  - Blooms *(changed)*: stars of magnitude ≤ 1.5 get a glow of half-block pixels beyond the disc edge,
    `exp(−(d − r)²/2σ²)`, σ = 1.6 px, peak 0.7 (less by up to 30 % toward mag 1.5; red stars × 0.62),
    cut below 0.12, while fade ≥ 0.5. Scintillation *(new)*: the peak swings by ±18 % with two sines at a
    hashed 1–3 Hz rate, so no two stars pulse together. **False colour**: `Ramp(Ice)` for B−V < 0.4,
    `Ramp(Ember)` above, the ramp nearest the star's hue (a dimmed `Blackbody` would redden). Unicode only.
  - Milky Way glow *(changed; replaces the braille dust)*: a density
    `p(l, b) = 0.3·exp(−(b/12°)²)·clump·(1 − 0.85·lane)`, (l, b) the galactic coordinates of its cell's
    sky position (IAU 1958 pole and l of the NCP, 122.932°). `clump ∈ [0.3, 1]` is two octaves of value
    noise on 6° and 2° lattices in (l, b) (star clouds); `lane` is a soft dark lane for the Great Rift
    (b ≈ +2° ± 2.2°, l 25°–88°, ends tapered over 8°) and the Coalsack (centre l 302.8°, b +0.4°, from
    RA 12h50m, Dec −62.5°; radius 3°). Per chart `p` is kept as one byte a cell (built in `resize`). Each
    half-block pixel is `Ramp(Starlight)` at level `72·p/0.3·fade` *(M12 round 2; was `Plasma` 50)*, `p`
    interpolated between cell rows, so the band thins out with the density instead of ending at an edge
    and fading dims it evenly. Levels below the visible floor are plain background, no longer dissolved,
    so no speckle shows through the wash; Starlight's floor is near black, so the fringe meets the sky
    with almost no step. The graticule stays `Dim` 30 (0.12 of full). None on the title row. Unicode only (in ASCII the denser star
    field alone marks the band). **Stylised**, false colour: a picture of the band's unresolved
    starlight and its two best-known dark lanes, not a map.
  - Meteors *(new; decorative timing)*: from 5 s into each chart, one every 3.5 s, 0.7 s long: a 9-row
    (aspect-corrected; at most half the chart's rows) braille streak down-left or down-right from a hashed
    point in the upper third, flaring in and burning out, head bright (`Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 230), tail
    fading; never reaching the name block. Meteors are real sky events; their times and paths are not.
  - The α-star name goes 3 cells right of the star (past its bloom), else left; it is dropped if neither
    fits. It is always on a star row, above the name block.
- **Raster**: two `Dots` layers in `update` (the graticule, Unicode only; the strokes and the meteor),
  and two `Pixels` layers (the Milky Way glow; the figure-star discs and their blooms); the glow
  densities in `resize`. Background stars, the ASCII star glyphs, pen and text are drawn in `render`,
  the glow and graticule first so background stars sit on them; a star cell's empty half keeps the
  glow's pixel.
- **Inks**: stars `Blackbody(T_eff)` (disc by magnitude; ASCII glyph by magnitude, `.` while fading);
  strokes `Ramp(Ice)` 170 × fade *(changed; was `Structure` 210)*; graticule `Dim` 30 × fade; graticule
  labels `Dim` 90 × fade; pen `Accent`; names `Text`; background stars `Dim` 45–195; blooms
  `Ramp(Ice)`/`Ramp(Ember)`; Milky Way `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 72; meteors `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 230.
- **Acceptance**: `CON-A1`: every edge references valid stars and every star is on an edge; charts put east left and north up, and the gnomonic projection matches analytic cases; `CON-A3`: the figure index advances every 12 s and wraps; `CON-A2`: a render at t = 0 has no stroke cells, non-decreasing after that; `CON-A4` *(changed)*: T_eff strictly decreases in B–V (to the clamp); Betelgeuse and Antares < 4000 K; Rigel, Acrux and Shaula > 9000 K; at 120×40 every figure star's cell shows its `Blackbody(T_eff)` (fg or bg in Unicode, the glyph in ASCII); discs grow with brightness, and every size is round (one pixel, or a square box ≥ 70 % filled); `CON-A1` *(changed)*: figures fill 0.8 of the chart area (the frame less 5 rows), preserving shape; `CON-A5` *(changed)*: meridians deviate < 0.05 cell from their chord; every braille cell is `Dim` ≤ 30 (graticule), a stroke (`Ramp(Ice)`) or a meteor (`Ramp(Starlight)`); labels on rows < h − 2; every figure has a label at 200×50; `CON-A6`: a stroke stops `STROKE_GAP` short of both stars, is absent at progress 0, reaches half the edge at 0.5, and is not drawn for an edge too short to leave a gap; `CON-A7` *(new)*: at 40×12 … 200×50 (7 sizes), fully typed, every figure's letter-spaced name is intact letter for letter and its epithet stands intact with no letter run into either end; from 20 rows, every figure star is at least two rows above the name row; `CON-A8` *(changed)*: no graticule and no glow at 100×30 on Ursa Major's chart; `sky_of(screen_of(α, δ)) = (α, δ)`; the NGP has b = 90°, Deneb is (84.28°, +2.00°) ± 0.05° and Sgr A* (0°, 0°) ± 0.05°; at 200×50 Cygnus keeps more than twice as many background stars as Ursa Major. Glow at 200×50, over every chart *(M12 round 2)*: its pixels are `GLOW_ROLE` (`Ramp(Starlight)`); the mean level falls strictly across |b| bands 0–5°, 5–10°, 10–15°, 15–20° (each sampled by > 500 pixels), is non-zero in the 10–15° band and ≤ 72 in the first; none on Ursa Major's chart or on the title row, and no pixel above 72; on Cassiopeia, Cygnus and Crux some glow shows at fade 0.4 and no pixel brightens as the fade falls from 1; on Cassiopeia and Crux the summed level per 10 × 5-cell block varies (coefficient of variation > 0.2); `p` at the Coalsack's centre and in the rift (l = 60°) is at most 0.15 of the band's. Rendered: the glow (fg or bg `Ramp(Starlight)`) covers over a quarter of Cygnus's cells and none of Ursa Major's; `CON-A9` *(changed)*: at 200×50, for every star of magnitude ≤ 1.5, the pixels just outside its disc (1.2 px beyond the radius, left and right) are bloom in `Ice` (B−V < 0.4) or `Ember`, at most `255·0.7·1.18`; ASCII draws no `Starlight`/`Ember` glow and no backgrounds; the GEN-R13 test is kept. `CON-A10` *(M12 perf)*: `resize` builds only the showing chart's dust and background-star levels (one chart, not six: 72 ms at 500×150 before); `update` builds the others ahead in slices of ⌈(cells + stars)·dt/4 s⌉ per step (a chart per 4 simulated seconds), the showing chart and then the next ones first, so each is complete seconds before it shows, and a chart still incomplete when due (a resize just before a hand-over) is finished at once. Frames are identical to building every chart in `resize`: at 120×40 over a whole cycle of the six charts, with steps of 1/60 s and of 0.25 s, and across a resize. `CON-A11` *(new)*: scintillation lies in [−1, 1], swings by > 0.3 within a second and differs between stars; at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50 every chart shows ≥ 40 frames (at 60 per s) with a meteor, none before 5 s, every meteor inside the chart rows (above the name block); at 120×40 ≥ 6 cells change between 7.0 s and 7.3 s.

---
## Batch B — particle systems (M5)
Shared helpers: `sciart_core::particles` (CORE-R28..R32). Pools are sized in `resize` from the area and are
bounded (GEN-R8); particles are respawned rather than reallocated. Positions are in **rows** unless stated
(x on screen = `cx + x·ASPECT`). Physics uses `dt` from `update` only.

### black_hole  (Deep Cosmos, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a Luminet-style black hole (Luminet 1979; *Interstellar*): a black shadow, the thin
  lensing ring (secondary image) hugging it, and an inclined accretion disk whose far side is lensed up
  over the shadow and, as a lower arc, under it; the approaching (left) side burns white-gold, the
  receding side dull ember; the hot gas visibly streams in sheared spiral clumps; behind it a quiet field
  of steady point stars, crowding along a Milky Way band, lensed round the hole. The disk is the
  brightest thing on screen.
- **Model** (units `G = c = M = 1`): Schwarzschild null geodesics from the Binet equation
  `u'' = −u + 3u²` (`u = 1/r`), integrated once per scene with RK4 into a table `u(b, φ)` over 320
  impact parameters (asinh-spaced about `b_c = 3√3`) × 257 sweep angles `φ ∈ [0, 2π]`, plus each ray's
  end angle (capture at `u = ½` or escape at `u = 0`); beyond `b = 48` the first-order weak-field orbit
  and the second-order deflection are used. A camera ray at impact parameter `b` and screen angle `ψ`
  crosses the disk plane (inclination 80°) at `γ_n = γ₀(ψ) + nπ`, `γ₀ = atan2(cos i, −sin ψ sin i)`; the
  thin, opaque disk (`6 ≤ r ≤ 24`, ISCO to outer edge) is hit by the first crossing that lands on it —
  order 0 (direct image) or order 1 (the secondary image: the lensing ring above the shadow, the
  brightest photon subring, and the lower arc below it). The fainter n ≥ 2 subrings are omitted. Per hit
  the redshift is `g = √(1 − 3/r) / (1 + Ω b cos ψ sin i)`, `Ω = r^{-3/2}`: `√(1 − 3/r)` is the
  gravitational redshift times the transverse (time-dilation) Doppler shift, the denominator the
  line-of-sight Doppler shift. The emitted temperature follows the Newtonian (Shakura–Sunyaev)
  zero-torque thin-disk profile `T ∝ [r⁻³(1 − √(6/r))]^{1/4}` (peak at `r = 49/36 · 6`; the relativistic
  Page–Thorne correction is omitted), the bolometric weight `(g T)⁴`. A pixel sums `F = Σ (gT/T_peak)⁴ ·
  clump` over 2 × 2 supersamples (partial coverage cools and dims the edges) and shows the blackbody
  colour of the display temperature (see Stylisation). Nodes within 0.8 of the shadow's edge trace the
  order-1 image with 6 × 6 samples. Clumps: a log-normal multiplier (mean 1, σ_ln = 0.45, easing to 0
  between 0.8 and 1 × the outer radius so no clump shows past the feathered rim) over 48 `ln r`
  × 256 `φ` bins, made of two layers of seeded noise born 3 s apart and weighted `sin(π age / 6 s)` (so
  `w₀² + w₁² = 1`); each layer is carried by the disk flow — Kepler ω rescaled so the ISCO period is
  2.5 s, inward drift `dr/dt = −0.02 r` — in closed form (gas now at `(r, φ)` came from
  `ln r + 0.02 age`, turned by `ω(r)(1 − e^{−0.03 age})/0.03`), so differential rotation shears it into
  spiral streaks before it is replaced. Escaping rays look up the sky in the direction they came from
  (observer at `D = 60`: screen angle `b / D`, source angle `b / D − α(b)` with `α = φ_end − π`). Stars:
  2400 seeded points (a quarter crowd a tilted great circle, the Milky Way band), one per texel of a
  1024 × 512 equirectangular map, levels 40–150. Of the supersamples that see a texel on one side of
  the hole (`ψ ≥ 0` or `ψ < 0`; a star's direct and lensed images lie on opposite sides), only the one
  nearest the texel's centre carries its star, so each image is a single pixel and, as the sky turns by
  whole texels, never drops out between texels. Tracing runs in `resize` on a lattice capped at 24 000 nodes (ADR-0012;
  200 × 50 is traced 1:1); each step advects the clump texture, sums the hit list and looks up the sky.
  The camera fit uses the disk image's traced extent: the outer edge spans 90 % of the width, with one
  cell clear above and two below (title row).
- **Stylisation (stated)**: colour contrast is exaggerated: the display temperature is
  `T_peak · F^{3/4} = T_peak (gT/T_peak)³` with `T_peak = 6 000 K`; from 600 K to 4 500 K it is mapped
  onto 1 000–4 500 K (the coolest blackbody level up) and lifted in log T by the power 0.8, so the dim
  receding and outer disk glows as a continuous ember envelope round its clumps; above 4 500 K it is
  rolled off in log T as `x/(1 + x)` towards 10 000 K (never clipping), so the disk runs from white
  through gold to ember in visible colours (real disks peak in the UV or X-rays and their colour
  contrast is far smaller). The
  redshift `g` is computed exactly, but the display cubes its colour effect along with the radial
  temperature contrast, so the Doppler colour asymmetry is exaggerated by the same power; the ordering —
  approaching side brighter and bluer, the gravitational redshift towards the ISCO — is physical. Colour
  is false. The lensing ring is drawn at full coverage wherever it touches a pixel, so it reads as a
  one-pixel line (it is thinner than a pixel). Stars are points whose lensed images keep their brightness
  (magnification is not shown); sky rays deflected by more than π (the sky's own photon subrings, within
  ~0.15 M of the shadow's edge) are not traced and are drawn dark. The Milky Way is shown only as a
  crowding of stars, not as diffuse light. The outer 45 % of the disk is feathered to zero emission. The clumps are
  illustrative turbulence, regenerated every 6 s. The star field drifts slowly round the disk axis (once
  per 150 s). The disk is infinitely thin and opaque; plunging gas inside the ISCO is not drawn; the sky
  mixes an observer at infinity (for the disk) with one at `D = 60` (for the sky angles).
- **Motion**: clumps stream and shear with the differential rotation (inner orbit 2.5 s); the lensed
  star field drifts round the hole.
- **Resize**: lattice, hit list, per-node star texels and sample map; the geodesic table, image extent,
  star map and clump noise are size-independent and survive.
- **Inks**: disk `Blackbody` (the display temperature), stars `Star` (levels 40–150, below the disk's
  hottest level in every theme), shadow and empty sky blank. ASCII
  fallback: `raster::ascii_ramp_cell` (CORE-R91), the stronger (CORE-R87) half-pixel's level on `.:-=+*#%@`.
- **Acceptance**: `BH-A1` ω strictly decreases with r and the ISCO period is 2.5 s; `BH-A2` *(changed)* the shadow is black: no ray with `b < b_c` receives sky light, and the only disk light inside the shadow is the direct image of the near half (the far half is seen only outside it); `BH-A3` *(changed)* the approaching half is bluer and brighter: `g(ψ = π) > g(ψ = 0)` at every radius and the mean Blackbody level of the left half of the image exceeds the right's; `BH-A4` *(changed)* the clumps ride the disk flow: a parcel orbiting at Kepler ω while drifting in at `dr/dt = −0.02 r` maps back to its birth position through the layer's closed-form transport, the two layers' weights satisfy `w₀² + w₁² = 1`, and the clump multiplier averages 1 with at least 5 % of bins below 0.6 and above 1.6; `BH-A5` *(new)* the geodesic table is right: rays are captured iff `b < 3√3` (outside the table's resolution there), for `b ≥ 20` the deflection matches `4/b + 15π/(4b²) + 128/(3b³)` within 1 %, and early on every ray is the straight line `u = sin φ / b` to first order; `BH-A6` *(new)* the order-1 (secondary) image lies outside the shadow; above the hole (`sin ψ ≥ 0`) it is a thin lensing ring, `b < b_c + 0.8`, drawn without gaps (at 120 × 40 and 200 × 50 every pixel it passes through above the shadow, where its gas is hot enough to show, is lit), and below it the wider lower arc; `BH-A7` *(new)* through the poles of the image (`cos ψ = 0`) there is no line-of-sight Doppler shift: `g = √(1 − 3/r)`, gravitational redshift times the transverse (time-dilation) Doppler shift; the emitted temperature peaks at `r = 49/36 · r_isco` and is 0 at the ISCO; `BH-A8` *(new)* the sky is a quiet field of steady point stars: over a full turn of the star field, stars light at most 5 % of the frame's pixels; however strongly lensed, a star shows in at most two nodes (one per image); and at 80 × 24, 120 × 40 and 200 × 50 over 20 s, a star image that vanishes with no star within two pixels has lasted at least 0.55 s on average; `BH-A9` *(new)* the disk is the hero and never clips: the display temperature starts at the coolest blackbody level, rises strictly and continuously (also at 4 500 K), lifts the dim disk (2 000 K shows above 2 600 K) and stays below 10 000 K, fewer than 5 % of the disk's pixels come within 3 % of it, and the disk's hottest level exceeds every star's (at 80 × 24, 120 × 40 and 200 × 50).

### galaxy_collision  (Deep Cosmos, M5, M12)
- **Intent** *(M14, ADR-0017: M11's sparse look on the M12 dynamics)*: two disk galaxies swing past each other and pull out tidal tails and a bridge (Toomre & Toomre 1972), drawn as a sparse field of star dots on black: a warm and a cool dotted spiral disk whose arms turn, bright streams of dots in the tails and bridge, and a bright nucleus in each.
- **Model**: restricted three-body: two cores (masses `M1 = 1`, `M2 = 0.8`) on a bound, eccentric, softened (`ε = 0.3` of disk radius) orbit integrated with kick-drift-kick leapfrog at `dt` sub-stepped to ≤ 1/120 s; massless test stars (`clamp(0.25·area, 64, 3000)`; *M14*, was `0.3·area`; split ∝ mass and interleaved: star `i` belongs to galaxy 2 exactly when `⌊(i+1)·M2/M⌋ > ⌊i·M2/M⌋`, so every prefix of the buffer keeps the mass split; the force loop is O(N)) start on circular orbits `v = √(G M r² / (r² + ε²)^{3/2})` in disks of radius `Rd`, radii uniform in `r` from `0.1 Rd` (surface density ∝ 1/r, centrally concentrated) along two trailing log-spiral arms with scatter, one disk prograde and one inclined/retrograde, projected to the screen. Cores start at apocentre of an orbit with `a = 3.5 Rd`, `e = 0.63` (Kepler period mapped to 24 s). A cycle is 40 s (approach, pericentre ≈ 12 s, tails, second close passage ≈ 36 s) then restarts in place from a re-seeded configuration (galaxy 2 spin 40–140°), reusing the star buffer; the camera eases to frame both cores. A resize keeps the running encounter: a shrink keeps a prefix of the stars (still split by mass), a grow keeps the stars it has and the next cycle starts with the new count.
- **Rendering** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: every test star is one braille dot (`raster::Dots`, drawn in `prepare`) at its projected position in its host's colour; no exposures, glow, blur or tone curve. A star's level is `100 + 110·(0.6·crowd + 0.4·arm)`, `crowd = clamp((n − 1)/4)` of the `n` stars sharing its cell (so light concentrates towards the nuclei) and `arm = (1 + 0.75 cos ψ)/1.75` its host's spiral pattern (below); a tail or bridge star (beyond the pattern, weight `1 − inside`) is at least 150, so the tails, the subject, are never dimmer than the disks; all times the cycle fade (0.3 → 1 over the first 1.5 s, back over the last 2 s). Each nucleus is a 2 × 2-dot cluster at 240 in its host's colour. There are no star-forming knots.
  - **Spiral arms**: inside its disk a star's light follows `1 + 0.75·cos ψ`, `ψ = 2(φ + 2.7·ln(r/0.1 Rd) − 2πt/30 s)`: a two-armed trailing density-wave pattern (pitch ≈ 20°) turning once per 30 s, fading out between `Rd` and `1.4 Rd`, so the arms persist while the stars' own initial arms wind up.
- **Inks**: galaxy 1 `Ramp(Sienna)` (a warm old population, ~4500 K light), galaxy 2 `Ramp(Cobalt)` (a cooler, bluer one, ~9000 K), star dots 100–210 and nuclei 240; 70 faint background stars `·` in `Star` 50, below any galaxy star. ASCII: the dots' dot-count fallback (CORE-R64).
- **Stylisation**: each host has one muted population colour, warm or cool (real disks are blue-white with yellow bulges; the colour tells the galaxies apart); the arms are an imposed density-wave pattern (the test stars feel no self-gravity); the second close passage near 36 s is not a merger — a restricted three-body model cannot merge.
- **Acceptance**: `GAL-A1` the core pair conserves energy within 1 % over a full cycle (leapfrog), with pericentre at 1–2.2 `Rd`, and the centre of mass stays at rest; `GAL-A2` initial star speeds equal the softened circular speed, galaxy 2 holds `⌊n·M2/M⌋` of the stars, every prefix of the buffer is split by mass within one star, and radii lie in `[0.1, 1] Rd`; `GAL-A3` after pericentre some stars are farther than `2·Rd` from both cores (tails form; ≥ 20 of 600), none before; *(M14)* 20 s in at 200×50 (seeds 42 and 7) at least 15 tail or bridge stars (beyond their disk's pattern) show as their own lit dots; `GAL-A4` the cycle restarts at 40 s, reusing the star buffer, and the restarted encounter matches a fresh one; resizing from 200×50 to 120×40 and 80×24 keeps the running encounter with `star_count` stars, galaxy 2 holding its mass share within one star, and growing back keeps it too; `GAL-A5` *(M14)* at 200×50 (t = 2 s and 20 s) there are never more lit dots than stars on screen (plus the nuclei) and at least half as many (a few share a dot), every dot cell is in a host ramp, and a star's level is 210 where 5 stars crowd its cell on an arm's crest, lowest for a lone star between the arms, at least 150 in the tails, and scales with the fade; `GAL-A6` *(M14)* at 200×50 (seed 42, t = 2 s) every drawn cell is a `Sienna` or `Cobalt` star dot (each more than 60 cells) or a `Star` sky dot no brighter than 50, and fewer than 12 % of the frame is lit; `GAL-A7` *(M14)* each nucleus is a lit 2 × 2-dot cluster at its core, its cell in its host's ramp at the nucleus level times the fade; `GAL-A8` the arm pattern's crest lies at `φ = −2.7·ln(r/0.1 Rd)` (trailing), a ring's mean light is unchanged by it, it is absent beyond `1.4 Rd`, and it turns forwards at 1/30 turn per second.

### stellar_nursery  (Deep Cosmos, M5, M12)
- **Intent** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: an emission nebula in the Hubble "Pillars" look, drawn as a dim, muted haze so that dark dust pillars carry the frame: an off-frame cluster carves the pillars and lights their star-facing crowns in Hα, while gas streams onto protostars that glow white inside the dust, ignite, flare bipolar jets, then shine.
- **Model** (dynamics NUR-A1..A3 unchanged): 3–5 protostars at seeded positions; a gas-particle pool (`≈ 0.02·area`, max 600; *M12 round 2*, was 0.03) spawned in the nebula, accelerating toward the nearest protostar (`a = gm / (d² + ε²)`), absorbed within 0.8 rows, adding mass; a protostar ignites when mass ≥ `M_ign` (brightness ramps over 1 s) and then emits short-lived jet particles along a seeded axis (22 per second; *M12 round 2*, was 45, and 90 before M12); after 25 s of shining it fades (2.5 s) and is replaced by a new protostar elsewhere. Gas is stored in polar coordinates about its target: only the radial motion follows the softened gravity, the swirl is kinematic (pure rotation), which keeps NUR-A1 exact; new gas targets the nearest *accreting* protostar. Jets are pulsed (Herbig–Haro knots); jet pool `clamp(area/60, 4, 240)`.
  - **Source**: the ionising cluster sits off-frame at a seeded upper corner (side `s = ±1`, direction `d̂ = (s·sin 35°, −cos 35°)`, y down); its light bleeds in from that corner.
  - **Nebula**: a slowly drifting, separably domain-warped sum of 12 sines, log-uniform wavelengths 5–42 rows, weights `U(0.5, 1)·(λ/42)^0.8` (a 1/f-like spectrum: filaments with texture), plus a brightness gradient towards the source; shaded per half-block pixel in `update`. *(M14, ADR-0017)* Its strength `m = 0.16·(1 − e^{−2.6 t})^1.8·(1 − 0.85·veil)`, `t = (z − 0.55)/2.6`: a shoulder, not a clip, so filaments keep their texture, faded next to a pillar by `veil`, the share of dust among the pixel's 3 × 3 neighbours (precomputed per size), so the silhouette's pixel steps grade into the haze. Below `z = 0.55` it is black, and strengths under 5/128 are dissolved into black (CORE-R89), so the haze fades out smoothly with black between its wisps. *(Was: `(1 − e^{−1.6 t})^1.5`, `t = z/2.6`, peaks 0.85 [O III] and 0.95 Hα in saturated ramps.)*
  - **Palette** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: the haze is `Role::Div` at `m·(1 − 2p)`, `p = smoothstep((ion − 0.44)/0.12)` of an ionisation proxy (rising towards the source and in the young stars' bubbles, falling in dense gas, which recombines to Hα): [O III] on its cool side, a muted teal-blue, Hα on its warm side, a muted dusty rose, both low in chroma this close to zero; the boundary between them passes through zero, a dark lane, rather than switching hue.
  - **Pillars** (static per size, at pixel resolution): 2–3 per sky, each with its own height (0.35–0.75 h), lean of the tip towards the source (0.03–0.16 w), base half-width (0.04–0.07 of `max(w, 110)` columns, so narrow terminals keep columns, not sticks), neck (0.45–0.6 of the base) and head (1.9–2.3× the neck, decaying over s ≈ 0.24 below a round dome). The edges carry lobes of `±30 %` with periods 0.3–0.6 of the pillar's length, the two sides out of step (necks and bulges), growing in below the head, plus `±2 %` roughness, and 1–2 round side knobs (0.35–0.6 of the local half-width, half-emerged). *(Changed)* Erosion: each edge is further displaced by two octaves of 1-D value noise, `±0.34` and `±0.16` of the local half-width at 9 and 26 bumps per pillar length, the two sides seeded apart, so the flanks are ragged with bays and fingers; the dome is roughened by the same noise.
  - **Rims** are ionisation fronts lit by `smoothstep((n̂·d̂ + 0.1)/1.0)` *(changed; was + 0.2)*, `n̂` the outward normal of the 7 × 7-smoothed outline: faces edge-on to the source keep a dim rim (the diffuse ionising field and the photoevaporative flow), faces turned away and lobe or knob undersides stay dark. *(Changed)* A thin front, not an outline: one bright pixel with a faint second one inside it (at 0.15, was 0.45); the model's rim light (40–105, ≤ 0.42 of full) is brightest at the tip and falls to 0.4 at the base. *(M14, ADR-0017)* It is drawn at `190·t³` of its share `t` of that range: in `Ramp(HAlpha)` from level 120, as warm grey `Ramp(Starlight)` at 0.45 of its level below that, and as plain dust below 24; and the crown fades inward (away from the source, and straight down, so it sits on the pillar's top) over two pixels of warm grey at 0.45 and 0.2 of the front. So the Hα crown is bright on the star-facing top (rule 6) and tapers into the dust through neutral tones, never as an outline all round or dark crimson.
  - **Dust** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: the pillars are a faint grey dust fill, dark silhouettes against the haze: `Ramp(Starlight)` at `8 + 0.14·dust + 0.2·limb` of the model's dust light (M12 round 2: `lerp(0, 62, 0.45·texture + 0.55·(1 − along))`, `texture` two-octave value noise (6 and 2.5 px), `along` the pixel's place from head (0) to base (1)) and of its limb brightening (up to 56 levels within 5 px of a face lit ≥ 0.5 where a rim lit ≥ 0.5 lies within 5 px), plus a fine value-noise grain of ±3 levels (3 px cells), within 4–32: brighter at the head and on the lit side just inside a crown, darker at the base, and textured, not a flat cut-out.
  - **Stars**: an embedded protostar is drawn only while it sits in a pillar: a soft Gaussian `Ramp(Starlight)` glow *(M14: the scene's white accent; was `Ramp(Ember)`)* (σ = half its radius, 1.0–1.8 px with mass, was 2.0–3.4; peak 0.6–0.9) that only brightens what is under it. *(M12 round 2)* These are the pillars' focal points and may outshine the subdued rims. In the open nebula an accreting hub shows only through its infalling gas. A shining star is a `✦` with dot spikes in `Blackbody` 22 000 K over a small `Ramp(Ice)` glare `0.35·b·(1 − r/R)²` to `R = 1.6·b` px *(M14; was 0.85 and 3.5·b)* that only brightens.
  - **Flow**: gas streaks span the last 0.18 s of infall at `Particle` `70 + 110·closeness` *(M12 round 2; was 0.35 s at 130–255)*; jets are `Accent` streaks of 0.3 s of flight that become knots after 60 % of their life. Gas, jets and spikes are braille dots over the nebula (`blit_over`).
- **Inks** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: nebula haze `Role::Div` (cool side [O III], warm side Hα, within ±0.16); pillars a faint, textured `Ramp(Starlight)` 4–32 dust fill with `Ramp(HAlpha)` crowns fading through warm `Starlight`; embedded protostars `Ramp(Starlight)` (white); shining stars `Blackbody` and a small `Ramp(Ice)` glare; gas `Particle`; jets `Accent`. ASCII: fronts, stars and glare by level (`.:+#`, the `stronger` pixel, CORE-R87); the haze `.` or `:` by strength, its fainter half blank; the dust fill blank, so pillars read as silhouettes inside their lit crowns, with embedded protostars showing through.
- **Stylisation**: a narrowband composite in the Hubble "Pillars" look, muted (ADR-0017): Hα a dusty rose and [O III] a teal-blue on the diverging role's two sides, the dust a faint grey lit only at its star-facing crowns (false colour); each pixel shows its dominant line, channel blending is not modelled; embedded protostars are overlaid as infrared sources (a composite, not a narrowband channel); a young star's glare is drawn in a small blue of the high-ionisation bubble it would carve; pillar shapes are procedural. Time-lapse and recycling: infall, ignition and jets play out in seconds (real: ~10⁵ yr); a protostar 'ignites' at a fixed accreted mass, and after 25 s of shining a star fades and is replaced elsewhere, a recycling device, not stellar evolution; only the gas's infall follows gravity, its swirl is kinematic.
- **Acceptance**: `NUR-A1` a gas particle's distance to its target protostar is non-increasing until absorption (for dt ≤ 1/60); `NUR-A2` protostar mass is non-decreasing while it accretes and ignition happens exactly when mass first reaches `M_ign`; `NUR-A3` particle pools never exceed capacity; `NUR-A4` a face turned to the source is fully lit, one turned away or a flat mask is dark, and lighting turns on smoothly with the facing angle; at 160×48 (seeds 3, 8) ≥ 85 % of well-lit rim pixels have open sky on the source side within 3 px, and down a lit side the surface light hardly ever flips between rows (≤ 1/8 of row pairs); for seeds 1, 3, 5, 8, 13 at 160×48, down each pillar's body the source-side surface is lit in ≥ 2/3 of rows and its dark rows come in ≤ 3 runs (shadows under lobes and knobs, not a zipper); `NUR-A5` for seeds 1–6 every pillar tip is offset from its base towards the source by at least 3 % of the width; `NUR-A6` *(changed, M14)* nebula pixels are only `Role::Div` haze (below zero [O III], above it Hα) or a shining star's `Ramp(Ice)` glare, and the [O III] fraction in the source half exceeds the far half's by > 0.2; after 20 s at 120×40 (seeds 8, 21) there are more than 200 haze pixels, none stronger than 0.16 (in `Div` units), and fewer than 5 % at the strongest level; `NUR-A7` for seeds 1–12 the head is ≥ 1.3× the narrowest neck, the width down the body turns at least once, and each pillar has a knob; at 160×48 (seeds 3, 8) deep dust spans ≥ 12 distinct levels, all ≤ 62, and dust within 2 px of a lit rim averages ≥ 5 levels brighter; *(changed, M14)* over 8 s at 120×40 (5 seeds) an accreting protostar outside the dust has no glow pixels (`Starlight` above the dust fill), and one inside has ≥ 3 distinct levels peaking ≤ 0.9·255; `NUR-A8` *(changed, M14)*: the crown ramp is `HAlpha` and every plain pillar pixel is `Starlight` 4–32 (up to 0.45 of the crown's peak within two pixels of a front); the drawn front peaks at 190 and a rim lit at 0.3 of the model's range or less is plain dust; the model's rim cap is ≤ 0.42 of full; for seeds 3 and 8 at 160×48, the mean dust level of the pillars' top third exceeds the bottom third's by more than 5.

### brownian_motion  (Forces & Waves, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a dark-field view of a micron-sized grain (like the particles Brown saw released from pollen grains; the pollen grains themselves, tens of µm across, barely move). The grain is a lit pale-violet sphere jittering in a quiet field of dim, neutral solvent specks; its random-walk path is the hero, a 32 s ice trail whose newest 10 s glow white → pale cyan. Specks that bounce off the grain flash. A tracking stage keeps the grain near the centre of the view. A faint rms-displacement ring grows from a marked origin, and the grain wanders inside and outside it. The ring's radius is `√⟨r²(t)⟩` of free Langevin (Ornstein–Uhlenbeck) motion, which tends to `√(4Dt)` for `t ≫ 1/γ`. The module doc and `description` read "A micron-sized grain jittering among solvent molecules (Langevin dynamics)".
- **Model**: grain obeys the Langevin/Ornstein–Uhlenbeck equation `dv = −γ v dt + σ √dt ξ` (γ = 2 s⁻¹, D = 4 rows²/s, σ = γ√(2D), so `√(4D·1 s)` = 4 rows of rms displacement per √s), exact OU update for the velocity; the joint position–velocity OU transition is sampled exactly. The grain walks in unbounded free space (no walls). *Tracking stage*: the view shows world point `cam` at its centre. After each step, if the grain is more than `0.45 × half extent` from `cam` on either axis (the half extents of the grain band, the view inset by the grain radius), the stage starts gliding, `cam += (1 − e^{−dt/0.9 s})·(grain − cam)` each step, and stops once the grain is within 0.15 of the band; `cam` is then clamped so the grain's centre never leaves the grain band. At every resize the stage opens centred on the grain. Solvent is `0.16·area` molecules (at most 1600) moving ballistically with Maxwell–Boltzmann velocities in view coordinates, periodic across the view (`wrap`), sliding by −Δcam each step (split over the molecule substeps); they reflect off the grain, and a hit sets that molecule's glow to 1, which decays as `e^{−t/0.3 s}`. A frame step is clamped to 0.25 s (GEN-R4's range, bounding the frame's work), and the molecules move in up to 16 substeps, short enough that a 3σ-fast molecule moves at most half the grain's contact reach per substep, so none tunnels through the grain. Every resize opens on a pre-rolled past: 20 s of exact OU track (trail and MSD lags) are simulated before the first frame from a stationary velocity at the world origin, so the trail, the ring and the estimate show from t = 0 (an honest past, not a fake one). The trail and the ring are world coordinates drawn relative to `cam`. The trail is sampled at 32 Hz (4 per 0.125 s MSD tick) and keeps 1024 samples (32 s). Ring epochs: at each epoch start the ring's centre becomes the grain's current position; the first epoch is already 2 s along at the first frame (at most half an epoch), centred where the trail recorded the grain then; its radius is the exact free-space rms displacement `√msd_ou(age)` = `√(4D[t − (1 − e^{−γt})/γ])`. An epoch lasts `epoch_len(half)`, the age at which that radius reaches 0.8 of the grain band's half short side, capped at 16 s (≈ 4.2 s at 80×24, 16 s at 200×50); the ring fades in and out over 0.8 s at each end. The grain radius is `0.06·min(rows, w/ASPECT)`, clamped to [0.6, 3.5] rows; it is drawn only, and the grain's own dynamics (γ, σ, D) do not depend on it.
- **Inks**: solvent `Ramp(Starlight)` at `0.15 + 0.05·speed` (≤ 0.2, a quiet ground), or at its glow when that is higher, capped at 0.6, with a dimmer tail dot (0.6 × that value) where the molecule was 0.06 s ago, so the solvent reads as motion rather than salt; a glow above 0.35 draws the molecule as a 5-dot bead. The trail is `Ramp(Ice)`: segments under 10 s old at `1 − 0.15·age/10 s` (white → pale cyan), older ones fading from 0.7 to 0.3 at the 32 s tail (`0.3 + 0.4·fresh^1.2`); the newest 8 s are a two-dot stroke (the segment doubled one dot across its direction), older trail a one-dot line. The hot head is brighter than the ring and every flash, so the hero path is never painted over. The grain is a `Pixels` half-block sphere in `Ramp(Plasma)`: `0.45 + 0.4·Lambert + 0.15·Lambert^12`, lit from the upper left. The ring is `Ramp(Plasma)` at `0.2 + 0.25·fade`, one dot in two, with a 5-dot cross at its centre. Readout `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`. Palette ownership in the dynamics group: the named ramps cannot give four scenes eleven unshared colours, so each pair a scene *identifies* things by is kept apart from its neighbours'. `double_pendulum` pairs Aurora with Hα (its twins); `three_body` uses Hα + Ice + Plasma (no Aurora); `brownian_motion` uses Plasma (grain, ring) + Ice (path) + Starlight (solvent), sharing Ice + Plasma with `three_body`, where they identify different things (bodies there, grain and path here); `strange_attractors` uses all six, one per attractor, where a palette names a shape, not a body, so its reuse does not confuse identities. Every palette is false colour (ADR-0015).
- **Readout** (GEN-R13): `D 1-path 4.11 rows^2/s  true 4.00  t 42s` (ASCII, right-aligned), naming the estimator as this one grain's time-averaged MSD at a 2 s lag with the exact OU inertial correction (a single grain's `r²/4t` from the start never converges); `t` is the track's age, pre-roll included. The estimator needs 80 lag samples (10 s of lag samples, about 12 s of track, since the first sample lands once the 2 s lag window has filled): before that one path holds under one independent 2 s window and the estimate swings by a factor of four. The 20 s pre-roll supplies them, so an estimate shows from the first frame; without them it reads `D 1-path ... rows^2/s`. The estimate accumulates over the whole run, so it converges towards the true D. When it would reach the title trailing parts (t, then the true D) are dropped, never clipped, so even the shortest form carries the unit, and the readout is hidden when even `D 1-path … rows^2/s` does not fit.
- **Raster**: solvent, trail and ring are `Dots`; the grain is `Pixels`. Both are rasterised in `update` and `resize`, and `render` blits the dots, then the pixels, then the readout. In ASCII a grain cell is `@` (both halves) or `o`. Nothing is drawn on the readout row.
- **Stylised**: the specks are decorative: they exchange no momentum with the grain, whose kicks are the Langevin noise term, and a flash marks a speck bouncing off the grain, not the kick that moves it. Speeds are scaled for visibility: a micron-sized grain is some 10¹⁰× heavier than a water molecule, so by equipartition the molecules move ~10⁵× faster. The tracking stage is a display choice, like a particle-tracking microscope stage: the grain's dynamics are free OU in unbounded space, so the ring (the free-space ensemble rms) is exact; the solvent is a statistically homogeneous bath seen through the moving window, drawn periodic across the view. The grain's velocity memory 1/γ = 0.5 s is stretched ~10⁶× (a real micron grain in water forgets its velocity in ~0.1 µs), so the ballistic start of the ring and of the MSD correction is visible; D itself is a free choice of units. The readout is one path's estimate: over a 60 s window it scatters by about ±30 % around the true D (it is labelled `1-path` and shows `true` beside it). The colours are false colour.
- **Acceptance**: `BRO-A1` `D = σ²/(2γ²)` with `√(4D) = 4`; the stationary velocity variance per axis converges to `σ²/(2γ)` (seeded statistical test, 5 %); `BRO-A2` the ensemble MSD at time t is `4Dt` within 10 % for t ≫ 1/γ (seeded, many walkers); `BRO-A3` `wrap` is periodic on `[lo, hi)` (NaN or an empty interval → lo); the grain's view position stays inside the grain band and every molecule inside the solvent box for any dt sequence and resize (1×1 included); the trail length is ≤ 1024; `BRO-A4` a molecule sent into the grain reflects with glow 1, and one second later its glow is `e^{−1/0.3}` (1e-9); a 3σ-fast molecule just outside the contact reach, under an update of `dt = 10⁹` (clamped to 0.25 s), bounces off the grain (glow > 0, velocity reversed) rather than tunnelling through; `BRO-A5` 4000 free stationary-start OU walkers lie inside `√msd_ou(t)` with probability `1 − e^{−1}` ± 0.031 (4 s.e.) at t = 1, 4 and 12 s; `epoch_len(half) ∈ [0, 16]`, with `√msd_ou(epoch_len) = 0.8·half` when below the cap; in the scene the ring age never exceeds the epoch, and each restart recentres on the grain (≥ 2 restarts in 3 epochs); the fade is 0 at both ends and 1 mid-epoch; `BRO-A6` the trail is 1024 samples = 32 s; the grain's lit pixels are within 20 % of `π(2r)²`; the readout row has no dots; the trail value is 1 at age 0, 0.3 at the tail and non-increasing with age, and for its newest 10 s it is ≥ 0.85 and above both the ring's brightest (0.45) and the flash cap (0.6); the solvent ground is ≤ 0.2; the newest 8 s of trail are a two-dot stroke (two dots per column when shallow, per row when steep) and older trail is a one-dot line; `BRO-A7` (readout) the estimate is this one grain's time-averaged MSD at a 2 s lag, inertia-corrected, within tolerance of the true D over a long run; the first frame opens on the 20 s pre-rolled past: a 641-sample trail, at least 80 lags, an estimate labelled `D 1-path d.dd rows^2/s` and ending `  t 20s`, and a ring 2 s into its epoch, centred on the trail sample 64 back, with radius > 3 and full fade; with fewer than 80 lags the readout reads `D 1-path ... rows^2/s`; `BRO-A8` over 300 s at 120×40 (seeded) the grain wanders more than a half view height, yet its offset from the stage centre never exceeds 0.75 of the half view (per axis, max-norm) and is within the 0.45 recentre band in > 90 % of frames.

### molecular_dynamics  (States of Matter, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a Lennard-Jones fluid, drawn as round glowing beads on the terminal's black, is slowly heated and cooled. A crystal melts into a hot gas of streaking atoms, which condenses back into droplets and crystallites. Each atom's colour shows its own kinetic energy relative to the current temperature on a cool → hot ramp in one hue family: the calm bulk a violet, the atoms hotter than their surroundings (evaporating off a cluster's edge) near white, in every phase. Hot atoms leave faint, dim violet streaks, a long exposure of their paths. On large terminals a small instrument at the top right shows the live speed histogram against the 2-D Maxwell–Boltzmann law; atoms pass behind it.
- **Model**: reduced LJ units, `U(r) = 4(r⁻¹² − r⁻⁶)` shifted at cutoff 2.5, in **2-D**; constant reduced density ρ = 0.25, inside the 2-D LJ vapour–liquid dome (r_c = 2.5 truncated-shifted: T_c ≈ 0.46, ρ_c ≈ 0.33–0.35, triple point ≈ 0.40); per-terminal layout: rows per σ `s = clamp(√(ρ·A₁/240), S_MIN, 2.5)` and `N = clamp(round(ρ·A₁/s²), 8, 240)` with `A₁` the interior area in σ² at one row per σ; above ≈ 250×50 the scale clamps and ρ falls. There is no box border (M12): the box spans the whole canvas above the title row (rows `0..h − 1` when `h ≥ 8`, else all rows), so the walls are the screen edges. `S_MIN = 1/(R_MIN·√3/2) ≈ 1.029` (R_MIN = 2^{1/6}): the start crystal's rows are then exactly one terminal row (two pixels) apart, and the first row sits on a pixel centre, so the single-pixel atoms of 80×24 fall on evenly spaced pixel rows (a scale of 1.1 left a blank pixel row every ≈ 7 crystal rows). 80×24 gets N = 205. Scales between about 1.07 and 1.15 (≈ 90×28) can still show one such gap; snapping the scale would break the fixed density. One simulated second = 6 τ; velocity-Verlet with `dt_md ≤ 0.004` sub-steps per frame; cell list for O(N) forces; reflecting walls; atoms start on a triangular crystal; below `r = 0.8` the potential continues linearly so forces stay finite; a 30 s temperature cycle `T = 0.25 + 0.75·u²`, `u = (1 − cos 2πt/30)/2` (below T_c for about half the cycle, ≈ 2 T_c at the peak), by gentle velocity rescaling (Berendsen, τ = 0.5 s).
- **Stylisation (stated)**: *false colour*. The colour is relative to the current temperature *(M12 round 4; was `0.35 + 0.65·clamp(ē/(2·T), 0, 1)`)*: an atom is `Ramp::Plasma` at `0.3 + 0.7·clamp((ē/T − 0.7)/0.65, 0, 1)²`, where T is the live kinetic temperature, the readout's T (k_B = 1; in 2-D the mean kinetic energy per atom is T), and ē is the atom's kinetic energy `v²/2` (LJ units) smoothed by an EMA with τ = 0.4 s: one atom's instantaneous energy is exponentially distributed, so without smoothing a crystal at one temperature reads as speckle; the smoothed value is a local kinetic temperature. An atom at or below 0.7 of the mean keeps the 0.3 floor, which keeps a crystal at rest visible; one at the mean sits at ≈ 0.45; one at 1.35× the mean or more is white; a non-positive or NaN T gives the floor. The squared ramp keeps the bulk calm and spends the top of the ramp on the few atoms clearly hotter than their surroundings. So in every phase the atoms hotter than their surroundings (evaporating off a cluster's edge) glow and the bulk stays calm, instead of the whole condensed phase sinking to one dim violet on an absolute scale. The absolute temperature is the readout's job, and the inset's bars and key keep the absolute scale (below). Why this curve: with the 0.4 s smoothing the spread of ē is narrow. A linear ramp to 2·T (round 3) left the condensed median at ≈ 0.68 and the hottest 3 % at ≈ 0.8, too close to tell apart; the squared ramp from 0.7·T to 1.35·T puts the median at ≤ 0.5 and the hottest 3 % at ≥ 0.95. On every (re)seed each atom's ē starts at the mean kinetic energy per atom (2-D equipartition, ⟨v²/2⟩ = T), so the opening crystal is one colour. Reduced LJ temperature is not a colour; the cool → hot ramp is used only because "hotter = brighter" reads at a glance. One hue family carries hot and cold by brightness in every theme, and violet keeps the scene off the gallery's many ambers and cold blues. Atoms are drawn as braille discs of radius 0.42 σ (a display choice; LJ atoms have no hard edge), below half the contact distance, so touching atoms keep a thin dark seam. Dots within 0.62 r are the atom's colour, the rim dots 0.78 of its level; a braille cell keeps its strongest ink, so only a bead's core follows the colour rule exactly. Under each bead a solid half-block body (and, for the hottest atoms, a small halo) makes the atom read as a filled sphere (Draw, MD-A10). Streaks are a long exposure, not a physical field.
- **Draw** (everything is rasterised in `update`/`resize`; `render` only blits): *Streaks*: after every Verlet sub-step each atom deposits `(ē − 0.5)₊² · h` into a `raster::Density` at its display pixel (the density spans `⌈w/2⌉ × ⌈h/2⌉` cells so that its dots are the box's `w × 2h` pixels), so atoms in droplets and crystals leave nothing. The density fades with `exp(−dt/0.12 s)`, is tone-mapped with `Tone::asinh(0.02, 0.25)` and shaded into `raster::Pixels` in `Ramp::Plasma`, capped at 0.12 of the ramp (level 31): below the dimmest rim dot of any atom (0.78 × the 0.3 floor, level ≈ 60), so atoms stay brighter than streaks in every theme, Mono included. A streak pixel whose tone level is under 64 is left empty: the faint fringe had shown as near-black navy half-block squares beside the beads. *Beads*: every atom lights the braille dots of its disc (dot centres within r = 0.42·4·s dots of its centre, plus its centre dot) in a `Dots` layer over the box, blitted over the streak pixels (`blit_over`, so a streak glow carries on behind a bead). Dots in the inset panel's cells and its margin are skipped (atoms pass behind it). *Bodies* *(M12 round 4, MD-A10)*: after the streaks are shaded, the half-block pixel holding each atom's centre is set to 0.85 (`BODY_V`) of the bead's level. Under the braille bead it fills the bead's core cell solid (a braille cell over the layer takes its stronger pixel as background), so the atom reads as a bold filled sphere, while neighbours ≈ 2 pixels apart keep dark cells between them and the lattice stays legible. Atoms at ramp value ≥ 0.9 (`HALO_FROM`) also light their four neighbouring pixels at 0.4 (`HALO_V`) of their level, a small glow halo. The body and halo never dim a brighter pixel. The inset panel and its margin are cleared after the bodies, so atoms still pass behind it.
- **Inset** (`MD-A7`, `MD-A8`): shown when `w ≥ 100` and `h ≥ 30`: a 26 × 6-cell panel at the top right (column `w − 27`, row 0) over a blank one-cell margin; below that size it would hide over a tenth of the box and its bars and curve merge into one braille blob. Atoms and streaks pass behind it, and the start crystal is centred in the part of the box below the panel, so the opening frame is clear. Row 0 reads `speeds` in the bars' colour (the absolute scale at e = 1) at the left and `Maxwell-Boltzmann` (`Text` 150, the curve's ink) at the right. Rows 1–5 are a braille plot of 26 speed bins over `|v| ∈ [0, 4]`; the last bin also holds every faster atom. Bars are the fraction of atoms per bin, smoothed by an EMA with τ = 0.3 s, each in `Ramp(Plasma)` on the *absolute* scale `clamp(0.1 + 1.3·u(e), 0.35, 1)` at its bin's energy e = v²/2, with `u(e) = min(1, ln(1 + e/0.4) / ln(1 + 3/0.4))` (e = 0.25 at ≈ 0.40 of the ramp, e = 1 at ≈ 0.86, e ≥ 3 near white), so the inset keys absolute speed while the atoms are coloured relative to T. The curve is one dot per bin at the exact 2-D MB bin probability `exp(−v₀²/2T) − exp(−v₁²/2T)` (the tail for the last bin) at the *measured* kinetic T, in `Text` 150; a braille cell has one ink, so the curve is drawn first and a bar reaching its cell recolours it (grey marks show where the live bars fall short). The MB peak is scaled to 92 % of the plot height.
- **Captions** (GEN-R12): `meta.captions = ["cool atoms cling", "hot atoms scatter"]`; `caption()` returns the first while the thermostat *target* is below the 2-D critical temperature T_c ≈ 0.46 of the LJ potential truncated and shifted at 2.5 (Smit & Frenkel 1991, J. Chem. Phys. 94, 5663; ≈ 0.53 untruncated), else the second. It follows the thermostat, not a measured phase. `meta.title` stays `Condensation · cool atoms cling`.
- **Label** (GEN-R13): right-aligned on row `h − 1`, the longest form that fits `title::readout_room`: `T 0.40 -> 0.25  U/N -2.63  N 240  LJ units`, `T 0.40 -> 0.25  U/N -2.63  N 240`, `T 0.40 -> 0.25`, `T 0.40`, else none. The caption says whether the thermostat target is below or above T_c (MD-A9); the arrow in the readout shows which way T is being driven.
- **Inks**: atoms `Ramp(Plasma)` by kinetic energy relative to the current temperature (1.35·kT tops the ramp), each with a solid body pixel at 0.85 of its level and, from ramp value 0.9, a four-pixel halo at 0.4; streaks `Ramp(Plasma)`, capped far below the atoms; inset bars and the word `speeds` `Ramp(Plasma)` on the absolute scale; MB curve `Text` 150; readout `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`. No box, no bond dots.
- **ASCII**: a bead cell is the braille dot-count fallback (`+` / `#`, `:` / `.` for a grazing rim); a streak-only cell is `.`: only pixels at streak level (≤ 31) become `.`, so bodies and halos add no ASCII glyphs. The inset's braille falls back to dot counts, as everywhere else.
- **Acceptance**: `MD-A1` the force equals `−dU/dr` (finite-difference check) and vanishes beyond the cutoff; `MD-A2` with the thermostat off, total energy drifts < 1 % over 5 simulated seconds; `MD-A3` atoms always stay inside the box; `MD-A4` the thermostat moves temperature toward its target; `MD-A5` the layout (border-free interior) keeps ρ within 5 % of 0.25 wherever `s` is not clamped, 80×24 gets N = 205 and its start crystal's atoms occupy evenly spaced pixel rows exactly two pixels apart, and at 80×24 (seeded) the mean coordination within 1.5 σ in the cold phase (28–33 s) is > 2× that around the peak (14–19 s) and > 3.5; `MD-A6` `u(e)` is non-decreasing in e, 0 at e = 0 (and for NaN or negative e), 1 from e = 3 on (and at +∞); the absolute ink (inset bars and key) is `Ramp(Plasma)` at `clamp(0.1 + 1.3u, 0.35, 1)`: 0.35 at e = 0, 0.40 ± 0.02 at e = 0.25, 0.86 ± 0.02 at e = 1 (and level(1) − level(0.25) ≥ 0.4), ≥ 0.99 at e = 3, non-decreasing; at 200×50 near the hot peak every lit pixel is `Ramp(Plasma)` (streak) with at least one streak pixel, every lit streak pixel is at or above its cut (tone level 64), and more than 4N bead dots are lit; a bead's lit dots are within 25 % of πr² and `2·ATOM_R < r_min` (seams at contact); the streak cap `0.12·255` is below the rim level (0.78 ×) of an atom at the relative colour's floor (MD-A9), so atoms outshine streaks; at t = 0 (80×24, 120×40, 200×50) every atom's level `255·u(ē)` is within 10 levels of every other's; `MD-A7` for T ∈ {0.25, 0.5, 1} the MB bin probabilities sum to 1 within 1e-12, and at 200×50 (seed 7), after 6 s from t = 12 s, the smoothed histogram sums to 1 within 1e-9 and its L1 distance to the MB bins at the measured T is < 0.25; `MD-A8` at 200×50, 120×40, 100×30, 99×40, 160×29, 80×24, 40×12 and 30×7, with a title row (h ≥ 8) every cell of row `h − 1` is `Text` or `Background`; the inset exists exactly when `w ≥ 100` and `h ≥ 30`, starts right of the title span, ends above row `h − 1` and before the last column; at 120×40, 100×30 and 200×50 no atom starts in the panel or its margin, and at t = 0, 5 and 60 s every box pixel there is empty and no bead dot is lit in the panel's cells or its margin; `MD-A9` the atoms are coloured relative to the live temperature *(M12 round 4)*: the atom ink is `Ramp(Plasma)`, its level 0.3 at e = 0 and at e = 0.7·T, 0.45 ± 0.02 at e = T and full (> 0.99) from e = 1.35·T; it is scale-free (`(e, T)` and `(2e, 2T)` give the same ink); a non-positive or NaN T gives the floor; at 120×40 (seed 42), 5 s in (condensed, T < 0.5), the median atom is ≤ 0.5, the hottest 3 % are ≥ 0.95, and no atom is below the floor. `caption()` is `cool atoms cling` exactly when `target_temperature(t) < 0.46` (sampled every 0.05 s over a 30 s cycle), and `meta.captions` is the two-caption list; `MD-A10` *(M12 round 4)* an atom's body is the one pixel holding its centre, at 0.85 of its level; a cool atom has no halo, while an atom at ramp value ≥ 0.9 lights its four neighbouring pixels, each lit and dimmer than the body; a body never dims a brighter pixel; at 120×40 (seed 42), 5 s in, more than half the atoms' centre pixels hold a `Ramp(Plasma)` body, and the top 2 % of those body pixels are ≥ 2× the median and ≥ level 200.
- **Perf** (200×50, step + render, 120 s run, the four M12 matter scenes in one release run, load ≈ 30 on 14 cores): mean 0.47 ms, heaviest 1-s window median 0.59 ms (at 8 s). Worst single frame (`examples/worst_frame`, fastest of 3 replays per frame, machine under load, M12 round 4): 0.68 ms at 200×50, 0.95 ms at 500×150. The bodies and halos go into the existing streak `Pixels`: no new buffers. Live bytes at 500×150 are 2.25 MiB (TEST-R7 ceiling 4 MiB): the streak buffer is at pixel resolution, a quarter of a dot-resolution one.

### particle_accelerator  (Forces & Waves, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: charged particles made visible by the tracks they leave in a magnetic field, in two
  acts. Act 1 (26 s), *collider event*: the end view of a general-purpose LHC-style detector with
  bunch crossings every 2.2 s from 0.05 s (the first event is on screen within three frames), stiff jets, curling loopers, calorimeter towers, the odd muon pair
  bending back in the return yoke, and a K⁰_S vee; one hero event at a time, the previous one
  fading out. Act 2 (18 s), *bubble chamber*: a 1960s heavy-liquid (propane/freon) chamber
  photograph every 3 s with a dim negative beam from the left, a flashing interaction star, e⁺e⁻
  pairs spiralling inward over several turns, δ rays curling off the beam, fiducial crosses and
  film grain in a round window.
- **Model**: one exact path for both acts, `Spiral`: m = 1, `v̇ = (−k + iqB)v`, so
  `z(t) = z₀ + v₀(1 − e^{−λt})/λ`, `λ = k − iqB`: curvature radius `p(t)/B`, `p(t) = p₀e^{−kt}`,
  turning clockwise on screen for qB > 0 (y down); k = 0 is a circle, B = k = 0 a line.
  Act 1 is to scale in the transverse plane, in metres: `B → 0.3·B` with B = 3.8 T in the solenoid
  and −1.8 T in the return yoke, so `ρ = pT/(0.3B)` (1 GeV ⇒ 0.877 m). Radii (m): tracker layers
  0.16 (Phase-1 pixel layer 4), 0.43, 0.80, 1.08; ECAL 1.29–1.75; HCAL 1.81–2.95; coil 2.98–3.18;
  muon stations (12-sided) 4.0/4.35, 4.9/5.25, 5.9/6.25, 6.95/7.3. A charged track stops at the
  ECAL face at its first crossing of R = 1.29 m, from any start point: about the circle's centre
  Z = z₀ + c (c the pole, ρ = |c|), `cos(θ − arg Z) = (R² − |Z|² − ρ²)/(2|Z|ρ)`, and the swept
  angle to the nearer root in the turning sense over |qB| is the time; if `|Z| + ρ < R` it is a
  looper and draws its whole circle (the transverse projection of a helix). Muons cross to the
  coil's outer radius, then continue with `qB·(−1.8/3.8)` to 7.3 m. Each crossing: 6–12 soft
  tracks, pT = 0.45·u^{−1/2} GeV (≤ 20); with p = 0.7 a back-to-back dijet (4–7 tracks each,
  2–25 GeV, σφ = 0.1, plus 2–4 photon ECAL towers); p = 0.3 a dimuon (20–45 GeV); p = 0.3 an
  isolated electron (8–40 GeV); p = 0.55 a K⁰_S of p_K = 1.5–6 GeV flying `βγ·cτ·(−ln u)`
  (cτ = 2.684 cm, clamped to 0.12–0.45 m) unseen (dashed), then a two-body decay K⁰_S → π⁺π⁻ in
  the transverse plane: p* = 0.206 GeV back to back at a uniform rest-frame angle, boosted along
  the flight, so the pions' momenta sum to p_K along it and their invariant mass is m_K. Towers
  at the exit azimuth, E = pT (η ≈ 0): electrons all in the ECAL, hadrons 25 % ECAL / 75 % HCAL;
  length `depth·ln(1 + E/0.5)/ln(1 + 80/0.5)`, capped at the depth, 0.087 rad wide (one 5° HCAL
  tower; the ECAL tower is the 5 × 5 crystals in front of it). Events stay bright 1.6 s, then fade
  with τ = 0.45 s (about ¼ when the next crossing arrives, < 0.1 half a second later); each
  crossing drops the events ≥ 3.8 s old, so at most two share the screen. Pools: 192 tracks,
  144 towers. The view spans 2.6 m at a fit radius ≥ 20 rows and zooms in to 1.8 m at ≤ 10 rows
  (linear between), so at 80×24 the tracker is ≥ 30 columns across and the outer calorimeters
  are cropped instead.
  Act 2 in chamber units (stage height 1), B = 1: beam of 5–8 π⁻-like tracks from the left edge,
  p = 6·width (nearly straight), bubble density 0.3; one ends at an interaction star on a nucleus
  of the liquid (nuclear break-up allows any prong count and net charge) with 3–6 secondaries
  (p = 0.06 + 0.5u², k = 0.12, bubble density `min(1, 0.45 + 0.05/p)`); 1–2 photons convert
  0.08–0.3 away into e⁺e⁻ (energy 0.15–0.45 shared 25–75 %, opening ±0.04 rad, density 0.55)
  that keep 0.6 of their radius per turn (`k = −ln 0.6·B/2π`) and are drawn for 4 turns; 0–2 δ
  rays per beam track (e⁻, p = 0.04–0.1, k = 1.0, ~70° off the beam). Bubbles are kept per sample
  with the track's density (fixed hashes, no shimmer). Pool: 64. The round window's rim fits
  between the stage's top and bottom rows.
- **Motion**: tracks grow outward from their origin over 0.35 s (δ rays and V⁰ daughters after
  their parent, towers after their track), events fade; the primary vertex flashes for 0.5 s as its
  star appears; each photograph appears, holds and goes dark for 0.25 s as the film advances; each
  act fades in and out over 0.8 s.
- **Resize**: act-1 paths are in metres and act-2 paths in stage heights, so they survive a resize;
  `Dots`, `Pixels` and the glow grid are rebuilt over the stage (the glow restarts dark). The stage
  is every row above the title row while the title shows.
- **Inks** (false colour): act 1 colours charged tracks by the sign of their charge (a display choice; positive tracks curl clockwise, i.e. the solenoid field points out of the screen).
  Positive tracks are `Ramp::Ice` (cyan), negative `Ramp::HAlpha` (rose), from 0.45 for the
  softest tracks to 0.82 at pT ≥ 10 GeV (0.82 for electrons), so every hue stays saturated and
  the tracker's centre stays calm. Charged hadrons under `SOFT_PT` = 1 GeV are soft tracks, drawn as a thin dotted trace (every
  third sample, about two dots apart) at the soft end of their ramp (0.45, kept at ACC-A12's floor
  over the outline), so the stiff tracks carry the event's axis through the centre (ACC-A14); those
  under ≈ 0.74 GeV (0.3·B·R_ECAL/2) curl up inside the tracker as loopers. Muons are near-white `Ramp::Starlight` 1.0, the brightest on
  screen; the K⁰_S flight is a dim `Starlight` dash (0.45). A track's charge is fixed at birth, so
  a muon keeps its hue through the return yoke's field flip. Calorimeter energy is thin radial
  bars down each tower's axis, outside the tracker: ECAL `Ramp::Aurora`, HCAL `Ramp::Plasma`, level
  `0.5 + 0.45·min(E/30 GeV, 1)`; a bar is 0.25 of the 5° cell wide (one stroke, two at large
  sizes; the physical tower width of ACC-A6 is unchanged). Detector outlines are `Dim` at level 30,
  about 0.15 of full brightness (below every flavour's fresh track in every theme, mono included;
  the outer two muon wheels at 0.7 of that, so a wide terminal's edges stay quiet). The only glow
  is each crossing's IP flash (a bright pixel and a softer cross) in `Ramp::Ice` (a `raster::Glow`,
  CORE-R85, `Tone::asinh(1.5, 40)`, τ 0.3 s, ≤ 0.6 of the ramp), carried under dotted cells
  (`Dots::blit_over`, CORE-R84); there is no deposit glow and no Ember anywhere in act 1. Act 2 is sepia film on black: bubbles, rim, crosses and grain in
  `Ramp::Ember`, beam bubbles at 0.5 of the secondaries' brightness, δ rays 0.75, pairs 0.85. Readout
  `Text` at `READOUT_LEVEL`: `event N  B 3.8 T` or `B 3.8 T` (from the solenoid constant);
  `film frame N` or `frame N`. An old event recedes under the outlines as it fades (it is at about
  a quarter when the next crossing arrives), so the new one is the only bright thing.
- **Stylisation**: growth over 0.35 s (real tracks form in ns); crossings every 2.2 s (really
  25 ns) with the previous event left fading on screen, where an event display shows one
  triggered crossing; energies and multiplicities are illustrative, not a generator tune; η ≈ 0
  (pT = p) and the V⁰ decays in the transverse plane; the K⁰_S flight is clamped to 0.12–0.45 m so
  the vee is always visible in the tracker (at p_K = 1.5 GeV about 77 % of real decays fall inside
  12 cm); the IP flash and the chamber's vertex flash are decorative; the
  chamber's units, field and energy losses are illustrative, and the bubble densities of the beam
  and the pairs are set for legibility (both are near minimum ionising in reality, within ~10 %);
  only the slow secondaries' 1/p rise follows dE/dx; colours are false.
- **Perf** (update + render, fastest of 5 replays per frame over 110 s at 1/60 s, seed 42, machine
  load ≈ 14): 200×50 mean 0.14 ms, worst 0.24 ms; 400×100 mean 0.35 ms, worst 0.55 ms. M12 round 2
  (`worst_frame`, dotted soft tracks): 200×50 worst 0.20 ms; 500×150 worst 0.71 ms. Live bytes at 500×150
  are 2.24 MiB (TEST-R7).
- **Acceptance** (ACC-A1 and ACC-A3, the bunch ring, are retired): `ACC-A2` a `Spiral`'s curvature radius equals p(t)/B, its turning sign equals the charge, its speed is its momentum, and with k > 0 p decreases; B = k = 0 is a straight line; `ACC-A4` two acts, 26 s + 18 s: `caption()` is `collider event` / `bubble chamber` by act, and each act keeps only its own objects alive; `ACC-A5` exit times put a track exactly on r = 1.29 m from the IP (agreeing with `2 asin(R|qB|/2p)/|qB|`) and from 500 random displaced starts (never beyond r before it), loopers are exactly the circles with |Z| + ρ < 1.29 m, 1 GeV in 3.8 T gives ρ = 0.877 m; in generated crossings every hadron or electron, IP or V⁰ daughter, stops at the ECAL face or is a full looper circle inside it, and every muon leaves through r = 3.18 m and continues from that point with the field ratio −1.8/3.8; `ACC-A6` tower length is monotone in E, 0 at E = 0, capped at its calorimeter's depth, and a tower is 5° wide; every tower sits in its calorimeter; every electron's full pT lands in an ECAL tower at its exit azimuth; `ACC-A7` event brightness is 1 while fresh, then decreasing and positive, 0.15–0.35 when the next crossing arrives, < 0.1 half a second later and < 0.01 when dropped; over act 1 (mixed dt) pools stay bounded, ≤ 2 events share the screen and 2 do at times, and the crossing count is `floor((26 − 0.05)/2.2) + 1`; `ACC-A8` each photograph has 5–8 negative beam tracks from x < 0 that are the stiffest tracks; pairs start at one point with opposite charges, keep 0.5–0.8 of their radius per turn and run ≥ 3 turns; δ rays are negative, p < 0.12, k > 0 and start on a beam track; slower secondaries have denser bubbles; secondaries are brighter than pairs, pairs than the beam; the window's rim fits between the stage's top and bottom rows (80×24, 120×40, 200×50, 60×40); `ACC-A9` at 80×24 the stage is 80×23, and at act-1 and act-2 times row 23 holds only the right-aligned readout within `title::readout_room`; tiny sizes never panic and ASCII is pure; `ACC-A10` the K⁰_S vee conserves momentum (the pions sum to p_K along the flight, 0 across, 1e-12) and has invariant mass m_K (1e-9) for p_K 1.5–6 GeV at any decay angle, p* = 0.206 GeV; generated vees start both pions (π⁺ then π⁻) where the dashed flight ends, 0.12–0.45 m out; `ACC-A11` the view spans 1.8 m at fit radius ≤ 10 rows and 2.6 m from 20 rows, the full 2.6 m at 200×50, and the tracker is ≥ 30 columns across at 80×24; `ACC-A12` in astro and mono the detector outline is ≥ 10 luma below every flavour's fresh track at its softest, for both charges (hadron, electron, muon and the dashed K⁰_S flight); `ACC-A13` over act 1 (120×40, seed 7, sampled every 0.37 s) every hadron and electron track's ramp is `Ice` if its charge is positive (qB > 0 at birth) and `HAlpha` if negative, muons and the K⁰_S flight are `Starlight`, more than 20 tracks of each charge are seen, and every rendered cell's fg and bg role is one of {Ice, HAlpha, Starlight, Aurora, Plasma, Dim, Text, Background} (no Ember); `ACC-A14` each hadron of a collider event (120×40, 3 s), drawn alone, whole and fresh: a soft track (a charged hadron under `SOFT_PT`) lights < 70 % of the dots of the same path drawn solid, a stiff track is drawn solid, and the event has at least one of each.

### rocket_launch  (Voyages, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a physically integrated launch profile, from the pad to the edge of space. A night launch on black: the pale vehicle clears the tower on a white-hot flame that runs out across the pad and pitches over, and the camera pulls back until the whole gravity-turn arc hangs in frame over a sparse, dim smoke trail. Max-Q, main-engine cutoff, stage separation and the upper stage's vacuum plume follow. The Δv ledger stays in the model.
- **Model**: a Falcon 9-class two-stage vehicle, using approximate public figures:
  - stage 1: 411 t propellant, 25.6 t dry, 7607/8227 kN sea-level/vacuum thrust, vacuum Isp 311 s, constant mass flow;
  - stage 2: 107.5 t propellant, 4 t dry, 981 kN, Isp 348 s;
  - payload: 15 t.

  It is a 2-D point mass over a flat Earth with:
  - inverse-square gravity, less the centrifugal relief `vx²/(Re + h)`;
  - an isothermal atmosphere (ρ₀ = 1.225 kg/m³, H = 8.5 km);
  - Mach-dependent drag, with a transonic hump on Cd = 0.26 and A = 10.75 m²;
  - back-pressure thrust loss.

  The flight rises vertically, pitches 3.2° from T+9 s over 4 s, then flies a zero-angle-of-attack gravity turn. Cutoff comes at stage-1 depletion (T+152 s), separation 3 s later, and second-stage ignition 8 s after cutoff. The ascent is integrated once in `build` with RK4 (four substeps per 0.1 s sample), including a Δv ledger:
  - ideal `∫T/m`;
  - gravity loss `∫g sinγ`;
  - drag loss `∫D/m`;
  - steering loss `∫T/m (1 − cos α)`.

  The spent stage coasts ballistically. The 60 s loop replays the ascent on a time-lapse clock: countdown and liftoff at 1×, the climb on a Hermite ease peaking near 8×, staging at 2×, and the upper stage at 4×.
- **Look** *(M14, ADR-0017: the M11 night launch with today's physics)*:
  - The camera spans `√(150 m² + (h/0.62)²)` of altitude; the rocket drifts right as the pad recedes. The sky and the ground are black: the horizon is a 1-px `Starlight` 42 line on the ground line (which curves away as `x²/2Re`), the pad a brighter `Starlight` 90 segment (±15 m) on it; sparse stars (one per 90 cells, `Star` 40–130) fill the sky, never in a cell the flame, body or smoke uses and never below the horizon.
  - The vehicle is solid half-block pixels one or two across, `Starlight` 235 with a dark interstage band (60), to scale near the pad and at a readable minimum size once the camera pulls back; after staging the upper stage stays at least 4 pixels long. The spent stage is a solid `Starlight` 150 sliver, drawn only while it is 3 px clear of any flame pixel. The launch tower is a 1-px `Starlight` 70 line 24 m from the pad, 85 m tall.
  - The plume is its `Blackbody` core only, one pixel wide: hottest on the axis at the nozzle (5200 K), cooling toward its tip (2600 K), with single white-hot 6000 K pixels on the axis for the shock diamonds low down (spaced as `1/√p`, only while `p/p₀ > 0.27`) — the scene's one accent. Its length follows the air: 1.5 vehicle lengths at the pad and 2.5 in thin air; past 45 % of its length (30 % in vacuum) it is thin gas, braille `Blackbody` dots whose density follows its heat, so the stars show through. On the pad the flame stops at the ground and runs out along the row above it both ways, as far as it would have gone down: a bright line across the pad at ignition and liftoff.
  - The smoke trail and pad steam are re-deposited each frame into a pixel-resolution density buffer and tone-mapped (asinh, knee 0.15, white 1.5); smoke laid down `t` s ago at pressure ratio `p` has radius `(8 + 30 t^0.75)/√p` m and is sheared downrange by a wind rising to 25 m/s at 12 km. It is drawn as sparse dim braille (`Starlight` 32–80, at most 35 % of a dense pixel's four dots lit, by a fixed hash so the texture holds still), only in cells whose two pixels are dark and above the horizon. Above ~45 km the trail fades out.
- **Stylisation**: the vehicle is Falcon 9-class, not a specific flight. It is expendable (it burns to depletion, with no landing reserve). There is no throttle bucket, so max-Q comes out a little high and late (39.7 kPa at 15.2 km, T+76 s; a Falcon 9 sees ~30–35 kPa near 12 km), and cutoff low and flat (61.5 km, 2.9 km/s, 16° above horizontal; a Falcon 9 is at ~65–70 km and ~25°, and ~2.2–2.4 km/s on reusable flights; this expendable profile stages faster): the numbers are indicative. The speed of sound is held at 340 m/s. Art-directed rather than computed: the diamond cutoff (`p/p₀ > 0.27`, ~11 km); the flame's temperatures, length and widening law; its run along the pad; the vacuum fan and its gas; the trail's fade above 45 km; the smoke's growth law and the wind profile; a night launch on a black sky (ADR-0017). The current time-lapse factor is shown on screen (`x2`…`x8`, hidden below 2×).
- **Text**: the countdown (`T-3`…), event callouts (`LIFTOFF`, `MAX-Q`, `MAIN ENGINE CUTOFF`, `STAGE SEPARATION`, `SECOND STAGE IGNITION`) and telemetry on row 0 (`T+01:12  ALT 12.3 km  VEL 452 m/s  q 31.0 kPa  x8`), all in dim grey (`Text` 100) (`draw::label`). There is no Δv bar (ADR-0017: decoration removed).
- **Captions** (`meta.captions`): `liftoff`, `gravity turn`, `max-Q` (±8 s around it), `stage separation`, `second stage`. The countdown shows none (the title's `stage drops away`; only stage 1 drops).
- **Resize**: the buffers and stars are rebuilt; the ascent does not depend on size.
- **Inks** *(M14)*: sky and ground black (`Background`); the horizon, pad, tower, vehicle, spent stage and smoke in `Ramp(Starlight)` (42, 90, 70, 235 with a 60 interstage, 150, 32–80); the plume's core, its gas (braille) and the shock diamonds in `Blackbody` (2600–5200 K, diamonds 6000 K); stars in `Star`; text in `Text` 100. ASCII: the flame `#`, the vehicle and tower `|`, the horizon `_`.
- **Acceptance**: `ROC-A1` with drag off, a vertical first-stage burn's ideal Δv lies between the vacuum rocket equation `ve ln(m0/m)` and its sea-level-thrust fraction, speed equals ideal Δv minus gravity loss (±0.1 %), flight stays vertical before the kick, mass is `m0 − ṁt`; `ROC-A2` pad steam appears only from ignition, only while the plume reaches the pad (below 150 m), and the pool never exceeds its capacity; `ROC-A3` altitude never falls before cutoff, after the kick the flight path only turns toward horizontal, cutoff comes at 55–90 km, 10–40° above horizontal, at 1.9–3.2 km/s; `ROC-A4` max-Q comes at T+55–90 s, at 9–16 km, at 20–45 kPa; `ROC-A5` *(M14)* on the pad, low in the climb and high after staging, the sky and ground are black: besides the flame, the vehicle, the tower and the spent stage, the only lit half-block pixels are the horizon line (`Starlight` 42, the pad 90), one per column on the ground line, which spans more than 100 columns while the climb is low; `ROC-A6` the plume's width never shrinks as altitude rises and is 1 at sea level, diamonds appear at 1 km and not at 12 km and their spacing grows as pressure falls, the smoke radius strictly grows with age, is wider in thinner air and more than 4× its initial size after 60 s, and is drawn as more than 20 dim (`Starlight` ≤ 80) cells in the climb; the plume in air is at least 1.5 vehicle lengths below 10 km and the vacuum fan opens from 3–3.2 nozzle widths at 40 km to 4.8–5 at 100 km; after liftoff the plume holds a `Blackbody` pixel of at least 5000 K and every flame pixel lies between 2600 K and white-hot 6000 K; the vehicle is at least 4 solid `Starlight` 235 pixels on the pad, in the climb and on the upper stage; at 80×24 after second-stage ignition the spent stage shows and no flame pixel lies within 2 px of it; at 80×24 and 200×50 late in the upper-stage burn the vacuum plume's opaque flame pixels are fewer than twice its braille gas cells, there are at least 4 gas cells, and every gas cell is at least the 2600 K level; at T−1 on the pad there is flame and none of it below the ground line; at T+14 s at 200×50 shock diamonds are single white-hot pixels (no two side by side), each next to flame; `ROC-A7` the Δv ledger closes (ideal − losses = speed, ±1 %), losses are positive, gravity loss at cutoff is 0.9–1.7 km/s and drag loss 20–300 m/s, and ideal Δv stays below the stack's budget; `ROC-A8` mission time is strictly increasing in scene time and hits liftoff and cutoff exactly at act boundaries, and the loop restarts, and during the countdown the row-0 clock and the centred `T-n` show the same whole seconds still to go; `ROC-A9` *(M14)* at 160×48 the row-0 telemetry is dim grey `Text` 100 on the terminal's background; `ROC-A10` *(M14, replaces the Sun)* a sparse night frame: through a whole loop at 160×48 (title hidden) every ink is `Background`, `Starlight`, `Blackbody`, `Star` or `Text`, the flame shows, and fewer than 14 % of the cells are lit.

---
## Batch C — grids, automata & stochastic processes (M6)
Shared helpers: `sciart_core::grid` (CORE-R33..R36) plus `particles` and `draw`. Grids are rebuilt (or `resize`d) in `resize`.

### fractal_growth  (States of Matter, M6, M12; renamed M11 from `crystal_growth`)
- **Intent**: diffusion-limited aggregation (Witten–Sander) in two alternating acts. The first is a frost dendrite growing from a seed in the middle of the screen; the second a metal forest electrodeposited on a cathode strip along the bottom, filling the width of wide terminals. Each aggregate is drawn as fine braille filaments on the terminal's black, in the colours of its arrival times (a deep core and pale fresh tips). Then the other act starts at once while the old aggregate fades out behind it as a dim ghost, tips first, so the screen is never empty between acts.
- **Model**: DLA on the square **braille sub-cell lattice** (2×4 dots per cell = 1/4 row per side with `ASPECT = 2`), so walks, contact distances and particle cost are all isotropic (weighting steps on the 2:1 cell grid only fixes diffusion and grows vertical needles, D_f ≈ 1.44); 8-neighbour sticking; far-field and near-field jump accelerations (Meakin / Ball–Brady; no measurable effect on D_f); sticking probability `p_s ∈ [0.3, 1]` (seeded per run: lower = denser). Each crystal site stores its attach time. *Hand-over*: at the end of the hold the chemical distances (8-neighbour BFS from the roots) are measured and every grown site enters the ghost at strength `1 − 0.65·chem/max_chem`, so tips are weakest; the next act is seeded in the same step, and its readout restarts from its own N. A ghost site's strength decays as `exp(−t/τ)`, τ set so that a root reaches the 0.08 cut exactly at `FADE` = 3 s; a site below the cut is gone, and the ghost is empty after 3 s. (The old melt phase and its melting front are retired.) *Art area*: the lattice covers every row except the title row when `h ≥ 12` (80×24 is a 160×92 lattice). *Lattice cap (ADR-0012)*: the lattice is `grid::lattice_for(art dots, 2^17, exact aspect)`, one site per dot; up to about 250×65 cells (200×50 is 80 000 dots) it is the whole art area. When capped, the frost's window is centred in the art area and the forest's is centred across but sits on the art area's bottom row, so the cathode is the picture's floor on any terminal. A resize reseeds, so nothing is resampled. The sites in attach order form a fixed-capacity pool of `area/4 + 1` (growth ends at a quarter fill at most, and a walker never sticks to a full pool); the chemical-distance BFS queue and the ghost have the same capacity, bounding memory at about 14 bytes per site; a resize clears the ghost. *Frost act*: walkers launch on a circle just outside the cluster radius and are relaunched beyond `2×` it; it grows to `R(0.85)` of the art area (within the window). *Forest act*: the cathode is the whole bottom row of the lattice (as much of it as the site pool holds: all of it unless the lattice is under 4 dots tall): attach time 0, the chemical-distance BFS roots, and marked in the free-distance map. Walkers launch uniformly in x on the launch row, the highest row at or below `extent + 2` above the cathode (extent = the highest deposit's height). A walker that steps above that row is put straight back on it at its exact first-passage point: from height a, a Cauchy offset `x + a·tan(π(u − ½))` (the half-plane Poisson kernel), folded into the strip by the reflecting walls (method of images), so there is no kill height and no absorbing lid over the valleys. Near-field jumps use the free-distance map; walls reflect. It grows until its tallest tree reaches 0.8 of the lattice height (≈ 10 s at 200×50). *Why the forest act is honest*: DLA from a line (Meakin 1983, Phys. Rev. A 27, 2616) is DLA's line geometry, and diffusion-limited electrodeposits are DLA fractals (Brady & Ball 1984; Matsushita et al. 1984; Grier et al. 1986; Sawada et al. 1986, mostly in radial cells); the cathode strip is the line-geometry version. Real cells add ion migration and convection; the scene models the diffusion-limited limit only. *Budgets*: the frost act gets `8000·(R/21.25 rows)^2.5` walker steps per second, boosted 4× at the start of the act and easing to 1× over its first 2 s (a small aggregate is cheap), so it opens on a visible crystal: N ≈ 700 after 1 s at 200×50, growth takes 6–7 s there (5.6–8 s at 80×24). The forest gets 40 steps per lattice column per second, boosted 3× at the start of the act and easing to 1× over its first 3 s, so its first trees rise while the frost's ghost fades. *Timing*: grow (at most 40 s) → hold 5 s → hand-over to the other act, whose growth runs over the old act's 3 s ghost.
- **Stylisation (stated)**: *false colour*: `Ramp(Ice)` for frost and `Ramp(Starlight)` for the metal (pale silver-white, not Ember; the metal sits `METAL_LIFT` = 0.15 higher on its ramp than the frost, since the lower half of Starlight reads as a dull grey-brown and silver should gleam).
