Frost here is DLA, not a Reiter snow-crystal model. The cross-fade is a staged hand-over between acts (sites fading tips-first by chemical distance), not modelled dissolution or sublimation.
- **Draw**: `Dots` braille. The ghost is drawn first, each site in the old act's ramp from 0.22 (at the cut) to 0.45 (at full strength), below the live core's 0.5, so it reads as an after-image and the live crystal draws over it. Each cell's ink is `ramp(act, 0.5 + 0.42·arrival/t_grown)` of its youngest site, where `t_grown` is the growth clock, frozen at the end of growth; the metal is lifted by `METAL_LIFT` = 0.15, `min(1, 0.5 + 0.42·arrival/t_grown + 0.15)` on `Starlight`; a site younger than 0.3 s is at the top of the ramp (a flash). Nothing is drawn behind the crystal (CRY-A8): a cell-resolution glow outlines dot-resolution filaments in stair-stepped blocks, so every cell keeps the terminal background, the filaments are the brightest thing on screen and the fjords stay dark. Walkers are `Dim` 110.
- **Captions** (GEN-R12): `meta.captions = ["walkers stick", "ions deposit"]`; `caption()` returns the live act's. `meta.title` stays `Fractal growth · walkers stick`.
- **Label** (GEN-R13): right-aligned on row `h − 1`, `p_s 0.62   N 812   D_f ~1.6` in `Text` at `READOUT_LEVEL`, parts dropped whole from the left to fit the act's `readout_room`. N and D_f are measured during growth and frozen from its end through the hold; at the hand-over the readout restarts with the new act's N. D_f is a live mass–radius fit, shown for the frost act only and only from N ≥ 600 (a smaller cluster's fit is noise); at `p_s = 1` a grown cluster (N ≈ 1 500 at 200×50) fits 1.45–1.65, the finite-size underestimate of the asymptotic 1.71, and low `p_s` fits higher, since its small scales are denser; hence one decimal and `~`. The forest shows no D_f (its mass–height law is a different fit), and its N counts deposit only, not the cathode.
- **Acceptance**: `CRY-A1` the cluster is always 8-connected to a root (the seed or the cathode) in both acts, and the ghost is seeded from every root's BFS; the cycle runs grow → hold → hand-over (the act changes only from Hold, straight into Grow, with a non-empty ghost), then the ghost empties; `CRY-A2` during growth cells are only added (count non-decreasing, attach times never change), frost and forest; `CRY-A3` walkers stay within the kill radius (frost) and the lattice, frost and forest; `CRY-A4` the mean mass–radius fractal dimension of a small seeded ensemble is in `[1.45, 1.95]` (single clusters scatter ±0.08; finite-size fits read ≈ 1.63 vs the asymptotic 1.71); `CRY-A5` at every size the lattice holds at most 2^17 sites, is the whole art area when that fits, and with a capped lattice the frost's window is centred (margins differ by at most one dot) and the forest's is centred across with no margin below it; the crystal and its walkers stay inside the window, and 70 s at 500×150 (both acts grow, hold and hand over, the ghost non-empty in each act's ramp) never reallocate the site pool or the ghost; `CRY-A6` a seeded 200×120 forest (p_s = 1, grown to height 60) is a sparse, screened deposit: the occupied fraction of rows 5–20 above the cathode is < 0.45, rows 35–50 are sparser than rows 5–20, and every site is 8-connected to the cathode; `CRY-A7` at hold, levels strictly increase with arrival time in 1 s steps; the core is `ramp(Ice, 0.5)`, a tip younger than 0.3 s is `ramp(Ice, 1)`, the forest is `Ramp::Starlight`, and a forest core is `ramp(Starlight, 0.65)`, capped at 1; the acts alternate `walkers stick → ions deposit → walkers stick` within 90 s at 80×24; `D_f` stays `None` while N < 600 and is `Some` once the frost has grown (200×50), within [1.4, 1.8] at the hold; `CRY-A8` at 200×50 and 80×24, sampled every 0.5 s over 60 s (both acts), in both glyph modes, the frame draws something and no cell has a background ink; `CRY-A9` a root's ghost lasts exactly `FADE` = 3 s, tips go before roots, and the ghost ink is in the old act's ramp below `RAMP_LO` and ≥ 2× `MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL` at the cut; at 120×40 (seed 42) over 62 s there are ≥ 2 hand-overs, each moves every old site into the ghost and restarts the readout's N below half the old N, and after the first second every frame has ≥ 120 cells of crystal or ghost.
- **Perf** (200×50, same run and load as molecular_dynamics): mean 0.05 ms, heaviest 1-s window median 0.09 ms. Worst single frame (`worst_frame`, 3 replays, M12 round 2, after the ghost hand-over): 200×50 worst 0.45 ms, mean 0.06 ms; 500×150 worst 0.91 ms, mean 0.10 ms. Live bytes at 500×150 are 3.10 MiB (`leak_free`, TEST-R7).

### radioactive_decay  (Forces & Waves, M6, M12)
- **Intent**: a lattice of unstable nuclei flickering through a decay chain while live population curves trace the exponential law.
- **Model**: chain `A → B → C` (C stable) with half-lives `T_A = 8 s`, `T_B = 3 s`. Each step samples every live
  nucleus once from the chain's exact 3×3 transition matrix over `dt` (the single-species decay probability is
  `1 − 2^{−dt/T}`, and `A → B → C` within one step is included), so the populations are exactly binomial with the
  Bateman fractions for any sequence of steps. The per-nucleus draws come from a `SplitMix64` seeded from `ctx.rng`
  once per step (CORE-R57): ChaCha8 per draw was a third of a 500×150 frame (RAD-A9). Each nucleus is a square of 2 × 2 half-block pixels on a pitch of 3 pixels, so the nuclei
  are separate dots with a one-pixel black gap. The pixel lattice is `lat_w × 2·rows` (rows = h − 1 while the title
  shows, RAD-A4), `lat_w` = panel start − 2 (the full width without a panel); the block of `nx × ny` nuclei
  (`n = (len − 2)/3 + 1` along each axis) is centred in it. That is ≈ 700 nuclei at 120×40 and ≈ 1 500 at 200×50,
  so the measured curves show real binomial scatter, honest for those N, and the late log tail is visibly stepped.
  The plot panel is the right 30 % of the width, shown only when it gets ≥ 24×10 cells (≈ terminals ≥ 80 columns),
  with measured `N_A, N_B, N_C` and the analytic Bateman curves `N_A = N₀ 2^{−t/T_A}`,
  `N_B = N₀ λ_A/(λ_B − λ_A)(e^{−λ_A t} − e^{−λ_B t})`. Reset to all-A when `N_A + N_B < 2 %` of N₀ (≈ 50.6 s); a
  resize starts a fresh run. Every decay flashes; only a budget of them is tracked: the budget is counted in lattice pixels, not nuclei,
  `lat_w·lat_h/800` slots in [4, 60], rounded down to even. The
  tracked decays are a uniform sample of each step's decays (reservoir sampling, then a uniform pick for the free
  slots), so tracks start anywhere on the lattice. Slots are counted per track: an `A → C` event needs two, and
  when only one is left it keeps its α and loses its β. A tracked decay bursts (a 12-dot braille ring growing from 2.5 to
  4.5 dots over 0.12 s, in the daughter's ramp) and leaves a braille track over the lattice in square pixel units,
  starting at the nucleus centre but drawn from 1.5 px out (clear of its square) and clipped to the lattice, built of straight pieces of 0.02 s of flight: α
  25 px/s for 0.2 s (5 px, ω = 0.6 rad/s, no scattering, drawn two dots thick), gone at 0.35 s; β⁻ 40–90 px/s for
  0.25 s (10–22.5 px, always longer than an α; ω = −5 rad/s, and a fixed random heading kick of up to ±0.35 rad per
  piece for multiple scattering), gone at 0.4 s; an `A → C` within one step emits both. The path depends only on
  the track, so it is the same for any dt. The plot's y axis eases from linear to log (3 decades) over 18–20 s of
  each run by `plot_y(f, m) = lerp(f, max(0, 1 + log10 f / 3), m)`; on the log axis every exponential is a straight
  line of slope `−λ/(3 ln 10)` per second, and N_B's line turns parallel to N_A's (transient equilibrium,
  N_B/N_A → λ_A/(λ_B − λ_A) = 0.6 since T_B < T_A). Measured counts are bright curves, two dots thick, over dim
  analytic curves, braille `Dots`, two samples per cell.
- **Inks** (false colour): the nuclei sit on a quiet black ground. A is `Ramp::HAlpha` 0.5 (warm crimson) and B is
  `Ramp::Ice` 0.8 (pale teal), far apart in grey so mono parts them at a glance; no amber, the gallery's most used hue.
  Settled stable C is not drawn: the lattice fades out to the ground as it decays, and only the flashes carry real
  brightness; there is no per-site grain. A decay flashes its nucleus at the top of the daughter's ramp (255) and
  eases to the daughter's settled level over 0.3 s (quadratic ease-out); it is "hot" (level ≥ 215, ASCII `*`) for
  its first 0.18 s. A B → C flash eases toward C's near-ground level 0.07 on `Ramp::Plasma`, then the site is
  empty. A restart does not flash A. Tracks in hues no species uses: α `Ramp::Aurora`, β `Ramp::Starlight` (near
  white), at 0.95 of the ramp while moving, fading to 70 % of that, one ink per track. Marks, bursts and tracks carry the lattice under their cells as background (`Dots::blit_over`,
  CORE-R84). Plot: measured curves in the species ramps at 0.9, drawn solid (A), dashed 4-on-2-off (B) and dashed
  2-on-2-off (C, pairs of dots rather than single ones); C's curve is `Ramp::Plasma` (lavender), so no measured
  curve is grey. The analytic (theory) curves are `Structure` 55, the only grey lines; axes `Structure` 110,
  header and counts `Text` 200, time labels at `READOUT_LEVEL`. The y-axis ticks are labelled as fractions of N₀ in
  the time labels' ink: `50%` on the N₀/2 tick of the linear plot (read the half-life off the A curve), `10%` and
  `1%` on the decades of the log plot, each just inside the axis on its tick's row, over the curves (a crossing
  curve loses a cell or two; the label stays legible). Legend per species: a `█` chip in its lattice ink (C's, which
  the lattice does not draw, is a dim `Plasma` 0.3 tile), then its stroke (`─` `╌` `┈`) in the curve's ink, then the
  count; ASCII chips `-` `o` `,`. ASCII lattice, per cell from the nuclei under its two pixels: a hot flash `*`,
  then B `o`, then A `-`; settled C, gaps and off-lattice cells are blank.
- **Stylisation**: a fictional A → B → C chain with half-lives of seconds; tracks drawn as if in a cloud chamber in
  a magnetic field (α short, straight and dense, β⁻ longer, thin, curling the other way and knocked about), with
  display-scale ranges, speeds, curvatures and scattering: the sign and ordering of the curvature are right, but the
  α : β radius ratio is compressed from ~70 : 1 (a 5 MeV α against a 1 MeV β in the same field) to 2–5 : 1 so both
  read on screen. Only a sample of decays is tracked and the burst is decorative; colours are false. Stable C is not
  drawn, so the lattice fades out as it decays. In mono A and B part by grey (0.5 vs 0.8) and the curves by stroke.
- **Perf** (update + render, `worst_frame`, 120 s, 3 replays, machine under load): 200×50 worst 0.05 ms, mean
  0.04 ms; 500×150 worst 0.38 ms (M12 round 3), mean 0.23 ms. Live bytes at 500×150 are 1.74 MiB (TEST-R7). The cost is
  one uniform draw per live nucleus and one 2 × 2 pixel write per nucleus per step, so it grows with the terminal's
  area (one nucleus per 3 × 3 pixels). The lattice is exempt from ADR-0012's cap: the nuclei at a
  fixed pitch are the picture, and the scene restarts on resize, so it has no evolved state to resample.
- **Acceptance**: `RAD-A1` per-step decay probability is `1 − 2^{−dt/T}` and composes exactly (`p(dt₁) ∘ p(dt₂) = p(dt₁ + dt₂)`); `RAD-A2` a seeded 20 000-nucleus run keeps `N_A(t)` within 4σ of `N₀ 2^{−t/T_A}` and `N_B(t)` within 4σ of Bateman; `RAD-A3` `N_A + N_B + N_C = N₀` at all times; `RAD-A4` (M12, GEN-R13) whenever the science title shows (`title::span` is `Some` for the title with its longer caption), the lattice and its tracks fill rows `0..h − 1` only, so the title does not punch through the last row of nuclei; with no title they fill all `h` rows. `RAD-A5` each nucleus fills its 2 × 2 pixels with `nucleus_ink` and every gap pixel is empty; the lattice is `((lat_w − 2)/3 + 1) × ((2·rows − 2)/3 + 1)` nuclei, centred (margins differ by ≤ 1 px); at t = 0 every nucleus is A's settled ink and the nuclei cover 35–50 % of the lattice pixels; a B or C flash starts at 255 in its ramp, and fresh A does not flash; Unicode draws half blocks and braille, and ASCII is pure ASCII, each lattice cell `*` > `o` > `-`; settled C and gaps are blank. `RAD-A6` `log_blend` is 0 until 18 s, ½ at 19 s, 1 from 20 s; `plot_y` is the identity at m = 0, one decade is ⅓ at m = 1 and values ≤ 10⁻³, 0 or NaN sit at the bottom; on the log axis N_A's slope is `−λ_A/(3 ln 10)` (1e-9) at 2–40 s and N_B's is within 2 % of it at 36–44 s; `caption()` is `on a log scale` from 19 s and back to `random yet lawful` after a reset; the y ticks read `50%` on the linear plot and `10%`, `1%` on the log plot (about a third of the plot apart), each label just inside the axis on its tick's `+` row. `RAD-A7` every measured sample is lit at `dot_y(plot_y(N/N₀, m))` of its dot column where its stroke is drawn (zeros skipped on a log axis), curves stay inside the plot's data rows and columns, and the measured cells wear species ramps. `RAD-A8` the ground is dim and only events are bright: a flash is 255 at decay, ≥ 215 while hot, < 215 from 0.18 s, monotone, and exactly settled at 0.3 s; every live track's fresh ink is ≥ 1.15× B's settled level and its faded ink stays above A's; settled nuclei are never ≥ 215; every β range (at the slowest β) is > 1.9× the α range (exactly 5 px), and an α runs straight; over a 50 s run at 120×40 (mixed dt) fewer than 5 % of lattice cells (glyph or background) are at level ≥ 215 on average and never more than 10 %, tracks, bursts and flashes included; track ramps are distinct from each other and from every species ramp. Over 20 s at 200×50 (seed 42) the tracks' start rows average within 0.05 of the lattice's mid-height (as a fraction of it) and each third of the lattice holds ≥ 20 % of them. The lattice fades out: every settled C site is empty, every other nucleus is drawn, and after 30 s more than 60 % are C and the mean nucleus-pixel level is < 40 % of the fresh one. The legend row reads `█─ ` … `█╌ 0` … `█┈ 0` with the A chip in the lattice's ink and its stroke in the curve's; A's stroke is solid and the three strokes differ; the panel is ≤ 30 % of the width. Track slots are counted per track: the capacity is even, and with one slot taken and a step of capacity-many `A → C` events the pool fills exactly to capacity, the last event keeping only its α. `RAD-A9` *(M12 perf)*: through `update` itself (SplitMix64 draws, seed 42, 200×50, > 1 000 nuclei), N_A and N_B stay within 4σ of the Bateman binomial at every whole second to 20 s. `RAD-A10` A's settled level is in [0.45, 0.55] and B's in [0.75, 0.82], with B ≥ A + 0.25 (mono parts them at a glance); C's settled level is ≤ 0.08 (in the code it is `Ink::BLANK`); the species ramps are exactly HAlpha, Ice and Plasma, with no `Starlight` among them; no scene ramp (species or track) is Ember or Sulfur, and the measured curves are at ≥ 0.8 of their ramps.

### gray_scott  (Reactions, M6; upgraded M10; renamed M11 from `chemical_reaction`)
- **Intent**: upgrade of the M6 scene; this section replaces the M6 one, and the scene moves to `Category::Chemistry` (CORE-R77). It shows living reaction–diffusion patterns (Pearson's self-replicating spots, not a Turing instability) from the Gray–Scott system `U + 2V → 3V, V → P` (Gray & Scott 1983; Pearson 1993). One culture walks through five regimes of Pearson's (F, k) plane: spots that bud and divide, a short beat of branching coral, then three regimes that never settle (loops, a swarm of spots and short worms, and moving spots). Each regime melts into the next, so the pattern reorganises instead of being replaced. The field is drawn in half-block pixels in `Ramp::Eosin`: dim wine rims, rose-magenta bodies and pale-pink spines and cores inside faint wine depletion halos, floating on the terminal's own black, which stays the majority of the screen in every regime a visit opens in. The colour is false colour (u and v are dimensionless concentrations with no hue), chosen to read as an H&E-stained tissue section and to keep the dish apart from the Aurora greens of `aurora`. The live regime names the title (`caption()`), and the parameters are a title-row readout; nothing else is drawn. At 80×24 there are about forty mitosis spots, each ≈ 5 columns × 2½ rows. At 200×50 there are about sixty, each ≈ 10 columns × 5 rows, round with graded edges. The scene stays distinct from `belousov_zhabotinsky` (travelling spiral fronts) and `liesegang_rings` (fixed concentric bands) because its forms are near-stationary blobs and stripes that divide, branch and reorganise.
- **Stylisation (stated)**: the colour is false colour (u and v have no hue); each regime runs at its own reaction rate in reaction time units per second (a display time-lapse, not a physical clock, see Model); passaging is an added sink on v that is not part of Gray–Scott (see Model); and the exposure normalisation (`v_top`, see Inks) rescales the display only, never the fields.
- **Captions**: `meta.captions` = `spots divide`, `coral branches`, `loops form`, `spots swarm`, `spots glide`, one per preset in walk order. `caption()` is the hold's preset, and during a morph the source preset until m = ½, then the target, so the title reads e.g. `Spots and worms · coral branches` and its fade restarts on each regime (CORE-R74).
- **Model**: `∂u/∂t = D_u∇²u − uv² + F(1 − u)` and `∂v/∂t = D_v∇²v + uv² − (F + k)v`, with `D_u = 2 D_v` and `D_v = 0.03` nodes² per reaction time unit. Kept from M6: the isotropic 9-point Laplacian (Patra–Karttunen), a periodic lattice, and an explicit Euler sub-step τ = 0.5 inside the positivity bounds `τ(10/3·D_u + 1 + F) ≤ 1` and `τ(10/3·D_v + F + k) ≤ 1`. The bounds are const-asserted for every preset and checked along the whole walk (CHEM-A5); the step is a convex combination plus non-negative sources, so u, v ≥ 0 and u ≤ 1, and decaying diffusive modes stay decaying (τ·D·16/3 ≪ 2). Every preset has its own **reaction rate** (reaction time units per simulated second, see the walk): 400 for mitosis and coral (800 sub-steps/s, ≈ 13.3 per frame), 70 for loops, 110 for swarm and 150 for spots. A morph lerps the rate by the same weight m as F and k, and τ stays 0.5, so the rate only sets how many sub-steps a frame runs (at most 200 per `update`, the 400 rate at dt = 0.25) and the positivity bounds are unaffected. At a single rate of 400 the moving regimes, which evolve far faster in reaction time than mitosis divides, read as a boiling field: loops renewed ≈ 60 % of the ASCII cells in 0.25 s (≈ 13 % per frame, swarm ≈ 11 %). **Lattice (ADR-0012)**: square nodes decoupled from the display, one node per half-block pixel below the cap: `lattice_size(display) = grid::lattice_for(Size(max(w, 32), max(2h, 16)), MAX_NODES = 5 120, BUCKET = 1.12)`, with the 32×16 floor restored inside the cap when the aspect snap undercuts it on extreme displays. Below the cap `lattice_for` returns its input (80×24 → 80×48, 40×12 → 40×24). Above the cap the lattice stops growing (200×50 → 100×50, two pixels per node), its aspect snaps to a power of 1.12 (stretch ≤ ±6 %), and the pattern zooms instead of getting denser. A pixel is square, so the lattice stays isotropic; the pixel scale holds 7–8 pattern wavelengths across an 80×24 screen, which keeps small displays from locking into a torus mode (the M10 draft's 40×24 lattice held 3–4 and settled into diagonal plane-wave stripes; a 20×12 lattice visibly tiled). The 32×16 floor keeps a culture alive through tiny transient sizes. **Preset walk**: a looping 10-beat `Timeline` of five holds, each followed by a smoothstep morph to the next, 119 s in all. Name, (F, k), rate, hold, morph that follows: mitosis (.0367, .0649; Sims), 400, 8 s, 6 s; coral (.0545, .062; Sims), 400, 3 s, 6 s; loops (.018, .051; Márquez-Neila's "spots and loops"), 70, 24 s, 8 s; swarm (.016, .052), 110, 24 s, 8 s; spots (.014, .054; Márquez-Neila's "moving spots"), 150, 24 s, 8 s; then back to mitosis. The morphs into and out of coral take 6 s rather than 8 s so the blown-out labyrinth is brief: at 80×24 the share of time with ≥ 50 % `#`/`@` cells fell from 7.25–8 s to 6.25–6.5 s, and with ≥ 30 % of the half-block pixels at Seq ≥ 192 from ≈ 8 s to 6.5 s (seeds 1, 3, 7, 42). The coral hold is short because coral branches only during the morph into it and the first seconds of the hold; a 12 s hold spent ≈ 10 s on a frozen, blown-out labyrinth (2–8 % of cells changing per 4 s, 57 % `#`/`@`), which the rejection criteria below exclude. From seed bumps, mitosis spots divide for ≈ 12 s, through the 8 s hold and into the morph, then jam once they fill the torus (a 16 s hold was still for its last 4 s, 3 % change). **Passaging**: without it, the return to mitosis inherits a full field of moving spots, which divide until they tile the torus and then jam: the returning hold was still for ≈ 8–10 s (4 s change 0.00–0.02). So the return is passaged, like a culture split into fresh medium. The first `resize` draws a seed from `ctx.rng` and builds a mask on the lattice of blocks of ≈ 6×6 nodes (`round(W/6) × round(H/6)` blocks tiling the torus exactly), each active when a SplitMix64 draw keyed by the seed and the block index is below 0.8; it is rebuilt from the same seed on every lattice change. During the spots → mitosis morph, each sub-step is followed by the sink `v ← v(1 − τκ m² mask)` (Lie splitting, κ = 0.02 per reaction unit, m the morph's smoothstep), a shrinking factor, so positivity holds. About four in five moving spots fade out over the morph's second half, and the survivors divide into the freed room through the mitosis hold. Measured at 80×24: masking half the blocks left ≈ 20 survivors that refilled the torus within ≈ 6 s, and the hold still froze for its last 2–4 s (4 s change 0.07–0.11); with κ·m instead of κ·m² the spots died by mid-morph and its last 4 s were still (0.06–0.12). With 0.8 and m², every 4 s window over 160 s from the mitosis and the spots slot (12 seeds each) changes ≥ 15 %; the returning hold changes 0.20–0.24 per 2 s, as the first one does. Each preset sits within 0.005 of the saddle-node curve `F = 4(F + k)²` (F − 4(F + k)² ∈ [−0.0046, +0.0002]). **Morphs follow the saddle-node curve**: F is interpolated, and k keeps the interpolated signed offset from the curve, `k = k_sn(F) + lerp(k_a − k_sn(F_a), k_b − k_sn(F_b), m)` with `k_sn(F) = √F/2 − F`. A straight chord can bulge across the curve into the region where the uniform v₊ sheet is stable (the M10 draft's worms → holes chord reached F − 4(F + k)² = +0.0028 and reseeded every loop), so following the curve is what lets the culture carry over. Rejected regimes, measured on this lattice: worms (.078, .061) never grows from seed patches (it reseeds for its whole hold); holes, maze and chaos cover 98–100 % of the screen and freeze, and maze and chaos lock into diagonal stripes at 80×24; chaos at Márquez-Neila's .026 is a near-homogeneous Hopf oscillation; solitons (.030, .062) and pulsating solitons (.025, .060) freeze within ≈ 12 s; waves (.014, .045) becomes one pulsing blob. The seeded start is a whole start slot, the start of the mitosis, loops, swarm or spots hold (never coral, whose labyrinth fills the torus). A `sim` visit of 15 s shows one regime develop and keep moving (from mitosis: spots divide, then elongate toward coral), and a long `run` shows the walk. Seeds are Gaussian bumps `v = ¼·exp(−(r/r₀)²)`, `u = 1 − 2v` (Pearson's u = ½, v = ¼ at the centre), r₀ ∈ [1.5, 3] nodes, ±5 % noise per node, 3 + nodes/300 of them; a bump only raises v and lowers u. The first `resize` pre-rolls 200 sub-steps (100 reaction units, ¼ s at the mitosis rate), so the first frame already shows graded bodies. A once-per-second featureless check (max v < 0.02, or max v − min v < 0.05) reseeds and counts it; the spread, unlike σ(v), does not mistake a young sparse culture for a dead one. It never fires in the walk (CHEM-A4, CHEM-A5).
- **Motion**: sub-steps advance the fronts every frame. Mitosis spots swell and divide for ≈ 12 s; loops, swarm and spots jostle, merge and split without ever settling, as organisms rather than a boiling field: at 80×24 and 200×50 about 2–4 % of the ASCII cells change per frame and ≈ 20 % per 0.25 s (loops 2.1–2.8 % and 0.18–0.22, swarm 3.0–3.6 % and 0.19–0.23, measured over seeds 1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 11), while 50–80 % still change per 4 s at the end of each hold. The 6–8 s morphs reorganise the whole field: mitosis spots elongate into coral branches, the coral breaks into loops, loops shrink into a swarm and then into moving spots, and most moving spots fade (passaging) while the rest slow into dividing mitosis spots. The walk repeats every 119 s, but frames never repeat because the culture carries over.
- **Resize**: if `lattice_size` is unchanged (every display at or above the cap in the same bucket), the simulation is untouched. Otherwise u and v are resampled onto the new lattice with `grid::regrid_bilinear` (the next-step buffers serve as its scratch, ADR-0012), so the pattern is stretched to the new domain rather than cropped. The bilinear edge clamp leaves a seam that diffusion heals within ≈ 1 s. If the node scale changed, spots divide or merge back toward the intrinsic wavelength, which is physically faithful. Nothing is reseeded unless the field is featureless. `Pixels` and the `grid::SampleMap` (lattice → pixels) are rebuilt here. The view (u, v → Pixels) is refreshed at the end of `resize` and of `update`, so `render` only blits and writes the readout.
- **Inks**: `Role::Ramp(Ramp::Eosin)` only (ADR-0011, ADR-0015; M12 replaced `Aurora`, which `aurora` uses); the M6 `Hot`, `Cold` and `Accent` inks are retired. u and v are sampled bilinearly into the `Pixels` through a `grid::SampleMap`; v first, and u only where the pixel is not pattern (the halo needs it). A pixel with `v ≥ 0.12` is pattern (the M10 edge, q = 0.3 of 0.40, which keeps dense regimes' thin tails dark, CHEM-A8: at a lower edge the v ≈ 0.05–0.12 tails between loops read as a wall of `:` and `+`). It is shaded against the **exposure** `v_top`: `p = clamp((v − 0.12)/(v_top − 0.12), 0, 1)`, level `255·(0.26 + 0.74·p²)` from a 256-entry table built once, so the pattern edge is a dim purple rim (level 66, above the halo's peak) and the top of the ramp is kept for the spine. `v_top` is the v below which 97 % of the pattern nodes lie (a 64-bin stack histogram over [0.12, 0.45], one pass per `update`), clamped to [0.24, 0.45] (so a young culture of seed bumps is not blown out) and eased towards that target with time constant 1.5 s; the first resize sets it directly. Why: at a fixed full scale (M10: 0.40, exponent 0.8; M12 round 0: exponent 1.3) worms and coral, whose plateaus sit at v ≈ 0.3–0.37, were a flat mid-level band with no spine; with the exposure every regime shows a bright ridge inside its bodies (5–22 % of body pixels at level ≥ 200 at 80×24) while most of each body stays magenta. Otherwise the pixel gets the halo, which fades in with substrate depletion from zero at `1 − u = 0.2` to its peak at 0.6: `ink::ramp(Eosin, 0.1·clamp((1 − u − 0.2)/0.4, 0, 1))`, and `Ink::BLANK` (the terminal background) where that level rounds to 0, so there are no hard black notches in the valleys. The readout is `Role::Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`.
- **M14 look** (ADR-0017; supersedes the Eosin palette and the halo): v alone, on black, in a neutral `Ramp(Starlight)` (u and v have no hue; Eosin's saturated rose was loud over large areas). The body ramp starts at 0.16 (a dark rim) and keeps `p^2` for the spine; the depleted-substrate halo is off (`HALO_PEAK` 0), so the floor is black. Pixels sample v bilinearly up to 2× magnification and Catmull-Rom above (GEN-R17, CORE-R102). The readout is `F=.037 k=.065` (no "Pearson"), right-aligned, when it fits.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure uses the brighter half, so thin features survive. Ramp levels 13–65 give `.` (the stronger half of the halo); fainter halo, which Unicode shows as a fade to black, is left blank. From the body's lowest level (66) up, `:` `+` `#` `@` by quartile of `(level − 66)/189`, keeping that pixel's ink. A cell with both halves empty is left untouched. The look matches M6: `.:+#@` blobs on blank.
- **Readout** (replaces the M10 row-0 label): `draw::label_right` on row `h − 1`, `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`, the longest form that fits `title::readout_room`: `Pearson F=.037 k=.065` (room ≥ 21), `F=.037 k=.065` (room ≥ 13), otherwise none. F and k are rounded to thousandths with no leading zero, written from integers into a stack buffer, and track the morph continuously (coral's F = .0545 shows as .054 or .055, by f32 rounding). `Pearson` names the (F, k) parameter plane of Pearson (1993); the loops, swarm and moving-spots presets are later explorations of that plane (Márquez-Neila), not Pearson's own pattern classes. The M12 round-0 Pearson-map inset was dropped: an unlabelled braille squiggle in a knocked-out corner box read as a rendering hole over worms and swarms, and the readout and regime caption already carry the science.
- **Perf**: at the 400 rate (mitosis, coral), 80×24 runs 3 840 nodes × 13.3 sub-steps ≈ 51 k cell updates per frame and 200×50 (and above) 5 000 nodes ≈ 67 k (~3 ns each); loops, swarm and spots run 2.3, 3.7 and 5 sub-steps per frame. The passaging sink adds one multiply per node and sub-step during one morph. The view samples v for every pixel and u only off the pattern, plus the ink mapping and the blit. Measured (`cargo bench`, frame = update + render, seed 42, M12 on a machine at load average ≈ 36): 0.30 ms mean at 200×50; worst frame over a whole walk ≈ 0.37 ms (minimum of 5 replays per frame, seeds 1 and 42). The exposure adds one pass over ≤ 5 120 nodes per `update`. The first resize's 200-sub-step pre-roll is a one-off scene-entry cost of ≈ 2 ms. (M10 round 3: 0.26 ms at 200×50, 0.90 ms at 400×100.) The simulation is capped at any size; the view grows with the display (every pixel samples v bilinearly), so 400×100 costs ≈ 3× 200×50 (informational, GEN-R8 is measured at 200×50). `render` and steady `update` stay allocation-free.
- **Acceptance**: `CHEM-A1` u, v stay finite and in `[0, 1]` for any dt ≤ 0.25, and the kernel's clamp is only a guard: at every 4 s frame of the whole-walk runs of CHEM-A8 (seeds 1 and 7), the unclamped reference Euler update of the live fields (core `laplacian9`) is already in `[0, 1]` and its v stays below 0.7 (measured ≤ 0.63, loops colliding); `CHEM-A2` the uniform state `(u, v) = (1, 0)` is a fixed point; `CHEM-A3` with the reaction terms off, diffusion conserves `Σu` and `Σv` (periodic); `CHEM-A4` from each start slot (mitosis, loops, swarm, spots) and seeds 1, 2, 3 and 7, an 80×24 run develops structure within 10 s: var(v) > 3e-3 and at most 90 % of the ASCII cells between row 0 and the readout row are blank or `.`; from the mitosis slot the number of nodes with v > 0.1 at least doubles (the moving regimes are already developed by the pre-roll); and no reseed happens in the first 20 s; `CHEM-A5` the preset table is exactly the five presets above (names, F, k, rates, holds, morphs); `preset_at(t)` visits them in order, equals each preset exactly during its hold (its F, k and rate), is between preset i and i + 1 at mid-morph with the rate at the midpoint of the two rates, changes F and k by ≤ 1e-4 and the rate by ≤ 1 per 10 ms across the whole walk, keeps the rate within [70, 400], and wraps at 119 s; every seeded visit opens at the start of a start slot, all four occur over 40 seeds, and coral never does; at every 10 ms of the walk both Euler positivity bounds hold, F, k > 0, and F − 4(F + k)² ∈ [−0.005, +0.0015]; at mid-morph F is the midpoint and k − k_sn(F) is the midpoint of the endpoints' offsets; `k_sn` solves the curve to 1e-12; `uniform_states(F, k)` returns (1, 0) plus, iff F > 4(F + k)², the two non-trivial homogeneous states `v± = [F ± √(F² − 4F(F + k)²)]/(2(F + k))`, `u = (F + k)/v`, each with ODE residual < 1e-9 in f64; one full walk plus 2 s from the mitosis slot at 80×24 for seeds 1, 5, 7 and 11 never reseeds; `CHEM-A6` for displays 1×1 … 1000×300 `lattice_size` gives ≤ 5 120 nodes and at least 32×16, equals `(max(w, 32), max(2h, 16))` whenever that has ≤ 4 500 nodes, gives 80×48 at 80×24, 40×24 at 40×12, 100×50 at 200×50 and 80×63 at 120×50, and gives 200×50, 300×75 and 400×100 one shared lattice (100×50); a resize that keeps the lattice leaves u and v bit-identical; in the mitosis hold, 200×50 → 120×50 → 200×50 resamples with the reseed counter unchanged, keeping mean v within 5 % of before, and 2 s later std(v) is within 30 % of before with still no reseed; `CHEM-A7` for `v_top` ∈ {0.24, 0.33, 0.45}, `v_ink(u, v, v_top)` is `None` iff v < 0.12 (M14: no halo), and is otherwise `Role::Ramp(Starlight)` with level non-decreasing in v for fixed u; the level at v = 0.12 is in [35, 50], at `v_top` it is 255, and at the midpoint of [0.12, v_top] it is ≤ 140; no depletion draws a halo; a pattern pixel never reads u; `exposure_target` of a uniform ramp of pattern values is its 97th percentile to within one bin, ignores substrate nodes (v < 0.12), is 0.45 with no pattern and clamps to [0.24, 0.45]; in live 80×24 frames (seed 7: mitosis at 4 s and 16 s, loops, swarm and spots at 10 s) 3–35 % of the body pixels (level ≥ 41) are at level ≥ 200; `ascii_cell` leaves levels < 13 blank, gives `.` for 13–40 and `:` from 41, and uses the brighter half; an 80×24 Unicode frame after 3 s uses only `Ramp(Eosin)`, `Text` and `Background` inks; with the title hidden every row of the 80×24 ASCII frame above the readout row is from `.:+#@` and blank; `CHEM-A8` from each start slot at 80×24 and 200×50 (seeds 1 and 7), 10 s into the hold (3.9 s into the 8 s mitosis hold) at most 50 % of the ASCII cells between row 0 and the readout row are `#` or `@` and at least 20 % are blank or `.`; at least 5 % of cells change in the next 4 s; and in the loops, swarm and spots holds at least 25 % change between 20 s and 24 s. In the loops and swarm holds at 80×24 and 200×50 (seeds 1 and 7), sampled at 2, 5, 8, 15 and 18 s, each sample with the frame 1/30 s later and the frame 0.25 s later: on average over the samples at most 4 % of the ASCII cells change per frame and at most 35 % per 0.25 s, at least 50 % of the half-block pixels below row 0 are near-black (`Ink::BLANK` or the halo, level below 32) and at least 45 % of the ASCII cells are blank or `.`, and no single sample has under 45 % near-black or under 40 % blank or `.`. Over the whole walk and on through the passaged return to mitosis and its hold (to 136 s), from the mitosis slot at 80×24, and from the spots slot through the first return and its hold (to 48 s), seeds 1 and 7: each frame at 4i s has at most 50 % `#`/`@` cells (60 % in the coral beat, from mid-morph into coral to the end of the morph out of it: a labyrinth covers ≈ 55 % of the cells at any exposure, measured 0.51–0.56), and in every 4 s window [4i, 4i + 4] at least 10 % of the cells change, with no exemption. `passage_mask` is a pure function of lattice and seed (different seeds give different masks), holds only 0 and 1, is constant on blocks (at most one change of value per 5 nodes along a row), and marks 55–85 % of the nodes on average over 8 seeds at 80×48, 100×50, 32×16 and 40×24; `CHEM-A9` for each preset i, `caption()` is caption i in mid-hold and at 45 % of morph i → i + 1, and caption i + 1 at 55 %; the readout ends at the last column with a blank cell each side; at 80×24, 60×12, 40×12, 30×12 and 20×8 (loops slot) it is exactly `F=.018 k=.051` when `readout_room` ≥ 13 and absent below (M14: no "Pearson"); its ink is `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`.

### spacetime_grid  (Deep Cosmos, M6, M12)
- **Intent**: the rubber-sheet picture of a binary black-hole merger (GW150914): a dim purple braille
  mesh funnelling down into two orbiting black holes — dark cores with hot, glowing rims — with cold,
  fading orbit trails; quadrupole ripples spiral outward as magenta-to-orange crests and tighten as the orbit
  shrinks; the holes plunge and merge in a flash; the merger's burst sweeps across the sheet as an
  expanding band of light while the single remnant rings down; a strain inset (top-left) draws the chirp
  a distant detector would record, brightening to the merger.
- **Model**: sheet height `z = −K Σ m / √(d² + ε²)` (softened Newtonian wells, `K = 0.16`, `ε = 0.16`)
  plus the quadrupole wave `A f(r) S(t_r) cos 2(φ − Ψ(t_r))` with retarded time `t_r = t − r/c`,
  `c = 0.35` sheet units/s, `A = 0.08` and `f(r) = (1 − e^{−(r/0.5)²}) √(0.5/r)`, the 2-D sheet's
  energy-conserving fall-off (real strain falls as 1/r). The source runs a 17.1 s cycle: *inspiral* on
  the Newtonian Peters chirp `a = a₀(1 − τ/τc)^{1/4}` (τc = 15 s, Ω₀ = 2π/4 s), Kepler Ω and closed-form
  phase, from `a₀` to `0.5 a₀`, strain `S = a₀/a` (`h ∝ a²Ω² ∝ 1/a`), with no switch-on: each cycle's binary has always been
  inspiralling, so waves with retarded times before the cycle began are the same Peters chirp
  extrapolated backwards (`τ < 0`, `u = 1 − τ/τc > 1`, capped at `u = 4`, far beyond any retarded time
  on the sheet), and the sheet and the inset (`wave_state(t_r, now)`) never go quiet between cycles (SPT-A11);
  *merger* (0.5 s): `a = a_m(1 − s²) → 0` while the orbital speed doubles linearly, `S` rises by 2.2×,
  and 5 % of the mass is radiated; then one remnant of `0.95 (m₁ + m₂)` at the centre of mass with an
  `m = 2` *ringdown*, `S ∝ e^{−τ/0.35 s}`, pattern speed 1.2 × the end-of-plunge orbital speed (which is
  `2 Ω_m`), i.e. `2.4 Ω_m`; *quiet* for 2.5 s after coalescence (about the sheet's half-depth over `c`, so
  the burst band is still crossing the outer sheet at the cut); then a cut to a new binary at a new angle,
  with a brief dip 0.3 s each side down to half brightness (smoothstepped), never to black. Merger flash: `s²` through the plunge, 1 at
  coalescence, then `e^{−τ/0.3 s}`. Mesh: perspective camera (pitch 55°, slow yaw sway), lines every ~7
  columns / ~2.8 rows, 3 samples per edge, rasterised as braille `Dots` segments with the parts inside
  each black hole's moat clipped out, fitted into rows 1 … h − 3 (a clear top row; the title row and a
  gap below). Display low-pass: waves shorter than 2.5 line spacings are faded by `(Ω_max/Ω)²` instead of
  aliasing into moiré. Black holes *(changed)*: half-block discs on their own `Pixels` layer (drawn in `update`, blitted
  over the mesh and trails) of radius `0.12 m^{1/3}` dots (× (1 + flash); at least 2.5 dots, drawn at
  least 1.5 px): a filled dark core (`Ramp(Plasma)` 14) inside a one-pixel hot `Blackbody` rim, ringed by
  a one-pixel cooler halo, round at any size (replacing the braille rings); round each, a dark moat to
  1.4 × the radius plus a dot that the mesh and trails keep out of; and a 1 s orbit trail (braille
  `Dots`) in one cold hue that fades by level. The camera fit
  includes the remnant's lifted well bottom, derived from `K`, `ε`, the radiated fraction and the lift. Inset:
  the source strain, signed-square-root scaled to the merger peak, over the last 4 s, one sample per dot
  column, in a 3-row strip at the top-left, `w/6` columns (12–22) wide (cleared under, labelled `strain`
  above its left end); the mesh slides right into any free margin, and where that is not enough the
  sheet is narrowed (its scale kept) until the inset has its full width; it narrows to stay two columns
  clear of the mesh, and is dropped when narrower than 12 columns or on canvases under 48 × 16.
- **Stylisation (stated)**: the rubber sheet is a metaphor (Newtonian potential depth, not a metric); the
  wells follow the masses instantly (Newtonian), only the ripples travel at c; wave speed, amplitude and
  time scales are artistic (real ripples are vastly weaker and longer than the orbit); merger and
  ringdown are phenomenological, not numerical relativity; the black holes are drawn as dark cores with
  hot Blackbody rims so they read in their funnels — the rims mark them (black holes emit no light), rim
  size is not horizon size, and the merger flash is a visual cue for the energy radiated; the display
  low-pass hides the shortest ringdown ripples on the sheet (the inset shows them), but the mesh's light
  still carries the unfiltered wave energy density (`∝ ḣ²`), so the burst reads as a band of light where
  its ripples are too fine to draw; the loop restarts with a cut, the new binary's earlier inspiral
  already on the sheet.
- **Motion**: black holes orbit and spiral in (4 s → 1.4 s orbits), brighter crests stream outward; a
  merger flash and ringdown every 17 s.
- **Resize**: mesh sample lists, camera fit and sheet width, low-pass limit, the three `Dots` layers,
  the black holes' `Pixels` and the inset.
- **Inks**: mesh `Seq` (a field of sheet energy; inferno-like in astro): resting level 60, + 45 in
  wells, + 110 on full crests (troughs dimmed by up to 30 %), + 220 × the wave's energy density
  `ḣ² ∝ (f(r) S(t_r) Ω(t_r))²` in units of its value at the envelope's peak with the end-of-inspiral
  `S = 1/Q` and `Ω_m` (so the late chirp and the burst are the bright bands); nearness shades it by at most 30 % and the foreground edge fades; all
  scaled by the cut and brightened up to 1.6 × by the merger flash; capped at level 210, and at 170
  within 2.5 moat radii of a black hole (easing back to 210 by 3.5), so the rims win in their funnels.
  Black-hole cores `Ramp(Plasma)` 14 (the ramp's darkest violet); rims `Blackbody` (30 000 K and
  25 000 K, levels ≥ 240; remnant 30 000 K), halo pixels at 0.35 × the rim temperature (≥ 8 750 K, still white-blue, so each marker reads as one cold beacon), warming with the
  flash; trails in one cold hue, `Ramp(Ice)`, fading by level from 150 at the head to 40 at the tail
  (a `Blackbody` level is a temperature, so it cannot carry the fade), scaled by the cut.
  Inset trace `Ramp(Aurora)` from level 110 (quiet) to 255 (merger peak) with a dim axis, label `Text` at
  `title::READOUT_LEVEL`. ASCII: a black-hole cell whose stronger pixel is rim or halo (`Blackbody`) is `O`; the core is blank.
- **Captions**: `meta.captions = ["merger", "ringdown"]`; see SPT-A7.
- **Acceptance**: `SPT-A1` single-mass well radially symmetric, deepening toward the mass; `SPT-A2` circular binary: fixed centre of mass and constant separation; `SPT-A3` the wave has period π in azimuth and its phase at radius r equals the source phase at `t − r/c` (inspiral); `SPT-A4` *(new)* the merger is continuous: separation shrinks monotonically to 0, phase and well depth are continuous at both joins, the centre of mass stays fixed, and afterwards one mass `0.95 (m₁ + m₂)` sits at the centre; `SPT-A5` *(new)* the strain envelope peaks at the merger, then decays as `e^{−τ/τ_rd}` while the pattern turns faster than at the end of the inspiral; `SPT-A6` *(new)* the inset draws the (scaled) source strain at every dot column of its window, its rectangle stays inside the canvas and above the title row (none below 48 × 16), no mesh dot ever enters its cleared box, and from 120 columns up it has its full width (at least 20 columns); `SPT-A7` *(new)* `caption()` is `merger` from 0.5 s before the plunge until 0.5 s after coalescence, `ringdown` from then until 2.5 s after coalescence, and `None` otherwise; `SPT-A8` *(new)* the merger is the brightest moment: the flash is 1 at coalescence, 0 through the inspiral and from 3 s after it, and it raises the mesh level; `SPT-A9` *(new)* the black holes sit in deep funnels and outshine the mesh: at 80 × 24, outside the cut, the sheet under the heavier mass lies at least 2 rows below the sheet 0.5 units to either side of it, no mesh dot lies inside a black hole's moat, the brightest rim level is at least 1.5 × every mesh cell's level within two moat radii (about three disc radii) of a black hole, and the camera fit's lowest point equals the remnant's lifted well bottom; `SPT-A10` *(new)* the chirp and the burst light up the sheet: at 120 × 40, the mean on-screen mesh level over the last 3 s of the inspiral exceeds that over its early half, the mean over the 3 s after coalescence is at least 1.4 × the early-inspiral mean, crests reach level 120 during the inspiral and the burst reaches level 180; `SPT-A11` *(new)* the waves before a cycle are its own chirp extrapolated back and the sheet never goes dead between cycles: `wave_state(t, t)` is the source the bodies show; across a cycle's start `wave_state` is continuous in strain and phase (within 10⁻⁶); 0.5–9 s before it the source is the Peters inspiral at `u = 1 − τ/τc > 1` (`a = a₀u^{1/4}`, `Ω = Ω₀u^{−3/8}`, `S = u^{−1/4} > 0.5`, not merged), finite however far back; at 120 × 40 over cycles 3–6, every 1 s window clear of the cut dip has a mean on-screen mesh level ≥ 0.8 × that of mid-inspiral and a crest of level ≥ 100 (at least 45 such windows); `SPT-A12` *(new)* the black holes are filled dark discs: for body radii 2, 5, 9 and 14 dots, every pixel centred inside `r − 1` px (`r` the drawn radius in pixels, ≥ 1.5) is the core ink, every pixel of the rim band is the rim ink (≥ 4 of them), nothing is drawn past the halo, discs of ≥ 2 px radius have ≥ 4 core pixels, and the core's level is under 20.

---
## Batch D — physics sims & narrative scenes (M7)
Shared helpers: `ode` (RK4), `timeline` + `ease` (CORE-R37..R39) plus `particles`, `grid`, `draw`. Narrative scenes loop forever: every phase has a fixed duration in simulated seconds, so a run is deterministic per seed and resizes never desynchronise the story.

### double_pendulum  (Forces & Waves, M7, M12)
- **Intent**: chaos made visible. Two double pendulums, an ice-cyan twin and an eosin-rose twin, start 10⁻³ rad apart and swing superposed as one pendulum drawn as two parallel strokes, one per hue, twin B displaced one braille cell right and up (dashed rods and trail alternating the hues where they share cells). Once they are about a braille cell apart they are visibly two, and each lower bob traces a 3 s glowing path. A fan of ghost pendulums, all started inside the 10⁻³ rad gap between the twins, is drawn as the line joining their lower bobs: chaos stretches and folds that material line across the swing, and it dissolves once it outgrows its samples.
- **Model**: exact Lagrangian equations for point masses `m₁, m₂` on massless rods `L₁, L₂` (rows, aspect-correct on screen), `g` scaled so the upper arm alone has a small-angle period of 2.2 s (the coupled slow mode is ≈ 2.78 s); physics runs in model units (`L₁ = 1`) with a separate screen scale, so motion is identical at any terminal size; both twins share one pivot; state `(θ₁, θ₂, ω₁, ω₂)` advanced with `ode::integrate` at `h ≤ 1/240 s`; the twin starts with `Δθ₂ = 10⁻³` rad; high-energy seeded initial angles from rest (`θ₁ = ±π·U[0.55, 0.92)`, `θ₂ = ±π·U[0.52, 0.97)`, independent signs: both arms above horizontal); `GHOSTS = 96` ghost pendulums with the same Lagrangian and integrator, ghost `k` starting at twin A's state with `θ₂ += 10⁻³·(k + 1)/97`, so the fan spans exactly the gap between the twins; trails sample both lower bobs at 120 Hz and keep 360 samples (3 s) in model space; a divergence readout (`|Δθ| e-folding`) and energy drift; restart every 45 s with new seeded angles.
- **Inks**: twin A is `Ramp(Ice)` (cyan) and twin B is `Ramp(Eosin)` (rose), a near-complementary false-colour pair that stays apart for red–green colour-blind viewers (M12 round 3 replaced Aurora/HAlpha) and that no other dynamics scene uses (`three_body` shares Ice, but in a triad with HAlpha and Plasma, never with Eosin). Each twin is always drawn in its own ramp at its part's value, from the first frame. **Superposition**: closer than a braille cell (a 2×4 cell shows one ink) the twins share cells, so neither is simply drawn over the other: rods are cut into matching dashes of about 6 dots and trails into dashes of 4 samples, and each dash has the other twin on top (`dash_order`: B over A in even dashes, A over B in odd), so coinciding twins read as a two-tone stripe. A trail sample's dash comes from its run-wide index, so dashes stay on their stretch of path as the trail scrolls (the index restarts with the run). **Merge offset** (DP-A11): superposed twins inside one braille cell can only show one ink, so they had read as a chopped two-tone dash. While the twins are merged, twin B (its rods from a shifted pivot, bobs, trail and glow) is drawn `merge_shift(split) = (1 − split)·(2, −4)` dots from its true position, one braille cell right and up (up, away from the readout row), easing to nothing as they part, so the pair reads as two parallel strokes, one per twin. Each trail sample keeps the offset of the moment it was taken (from the `apart` stored with it), so a merged stretch of trail stays offset after the twins part. Twin A's bob discs are drawn whole, then twin B's on top clipped to the dots right of `x − cut`, `cut = max(0, 2r·split − 0.5)`: its right half while the twins are merged, growing back to the whole disc as they part. **Split**: `apart` is the farthest the twins' matching bobs (upper or lower) have been apart this run, in model units, never shrinking within a run; `split = smoothstep((apart·dots_per_unit − 4)/(8 − 4))`. The pendulum outshines its trail, and the bodies sit in the saturated middle of the ramps (above ~0.8 both wash towards white, and the two-tone with them): rods 0.66; bob discs 0.72 with a one-dot core at 0.9; the upper bob is r 1.8 dots, the lower bob r 2.8 dots, or 3.5 dots from 40 rows up; trail values run `0.12 + 0.48·fresh²` (top 0.6). (M12 lift from 0.62 / 0.68 / 0.8 / 0.56, so the arms read as lit over their glow; the order trail top < rods < bobs < core is kept, also as a compile-time assertion.) The pivot is a `Text` 230 disc of r 1, so the hinge is always visible. The ghost line runs from twin A's lower bob through every ghost's, in start order, to twin B's (98 samples, 97 links), at ramp `0.45·fade`: twin A's ramp for the first half, twin B's for the second, with a one-dot bead per ghost. A link longer than 0.35 model units (L₁ = 1) is left out. `fade = split · smoothstep((kept − 0.3)/0.4)`, where `kept` is the fraction of links joined: it fades in with the split (before that it lies under the superposed twins) and out as the line breaks up. Readout `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`.
- **Glow**: under the line art is a half-block glow, one `raster::Glow` exposure per twin rebuilt every update from the model-space trail, rods and bobs (so a resize keeps it): each trail segment is stroked with the shared 3×3 `raster::STROKE_KERNEL` (0.36 / 0.12 edge / 0.04 corner) once per pixel of length at weight `0.45·fresh²` (`Glow::stroke`, CORE-R96), each rod at 0.4, and each bob adds a radius-3 halo (`raster::halo_kernel(3)`, `w = (1 − r²/3.5²)²`) at 2.5 (the upper bob 0.6× that). A pixel is toned by `Tone::asinh(0.4, 5)` from `max + ½·min` of the two twins' exposures and drawn in the ramp of the twin that exposed it more, at ramp value `0.4·level/255` (blank below `MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL`); where the two exposures are within 35 % of each other (superposed twins) the twins alternate on a pixel checkerboard, the glow's analogue of the dashed two-tone lines. The glow's cap 0.4 is under the trail's top (compile-time assertion). `Dots::blit_over` carries the glow as the dotted cells' background (CORE-R84). ASCII draws no glow.
- **Raster**: the line art is a braille `Dots` lattice over a half-block glow (`Pixels`), both rasterised in `update` and `resize`; `render` only blits and writes the readout. The view leaves half a row for the lower bob's disc, so no dot reaches the readout row. The former top caption lines ("two double pendulums, released 0.001 rad apart" and the normal-mode sentence) are removed: the title already says it, and the lines crossed the pendulum.
- **Label** (M12 polish, GEN-R13): right-aligned on the last row in one of three forms, long `|dtheta| 3.1e-2 rad = 31.0x start (3.4 e-folds)   energy drift 2e-9   t 12/45s`, mid `|dtheta| 3.1e-2 (3.4 e-folds)` and short `e-folds 3.4`. The numbers carry no inner padding. Each form has a fixed width (84, 34 and 14 chars), the widest it can reach for any separation in `[0, π√2]` and any finite drift. The form is the first whose width fits `title::readout_room`, and its text is padded at the front to that width, so neither the choice nor the readout's left edge moves as the numbers change.
- **Stylised**: the colours are false colour; the glow is a visual device (light spilling from the lines), not a physical quantity; the dashed two-tone superposition and the half-disc bobs are a visual device for two bodies in the same cells. While the twins are merged, twin B is displaced by one cell on screen so both are visible. Display only; the physics is untouched. The ghost line is the true image of the start segment only where its 98 samples (97 links) resolve it: links stretched beyond 0.35 L₁ are dropped rather than drawn as chords, and the whole line fades out as most of its links are dropped, so a line stretched past its sampling dissolves rather than leaving scattered beads. The physics is unchanged. The superposed-to-parted change is set by on-screen separation (4 to 8 braille dots), so it comes earlier on larger terminals; the readout's |dtheta| and e-folds are the size-independent measure of divergence.
- **Acceptance**: `DP-A1` total energy drifts < 0.5 % over 20 s; `DP-A2` for small angles the two normal-mode frequencies match the linearised analytic values within 1 %; `DP-A3` from a seeded chaotic start the twins' separation (max so far — it saturates near π) grows ≥ 100× within 30 s; `DP-A4` rods keep their lengths exactly (bob positions derived from angles); `DP-A5` for seeds 0–3 at 80×24, at 0 s and 1 s (`split` still 0) at most 2 cells above the readout row are `Text` (the pivot) and the `Ramp(Ice)` (cyan) and `Ramp(Eosin)` (rose) cell counts are within 3:1, and at 30 s `split` is 1 and both ramps are drawn; `split` is 0 at 4 dots and 1 at 8; at 200×50 with the lower bobs 0 and 2 dots apart and a swept trail, both ramps light cell counts within 3:1, and the 5×5 cells around the lower bob include at least one cell of each twin's bob ink; 10 dots apart both ramps light comparable cell counts (within 3:2); `dash_order` alternates (`[B, A]`, then `[A, B]`), two coinciding dashed rods light ≥ 8 cells of each ramp, pushing a trail sample advances the dash base by one, and after a restart the pushed-sample count and the trail length are both 1; trail top < rods < bobs < core (also a compile-time assertion); at 120×40 (seed 0, 30 s) at least two cells around twin B's lower bob carry its ramp at a value in [ROD_V, BOB_V] = [0.66, 0.72]; `DP-A6` ghost starts differ from twin A only in `θ₂`, strictly increasing inside `(θ₂A, θ₂B)`; `ghost_fade(split, kept)` is 0 when `split` is 0 or `kept ≤ 0.3`, equals `split` from `kept = 0.7`, and is monotone in `kept`; neighbours join only within 0.35 model units, and when `split` first reaches 1 (seed 1, bounded by a 45 s run) every link along A → ghosts → B is joined; at 30 s (seeds 0–3) the ghosts' lower bobs span > 0.5 model units; `DP-A7` the trail holds 360 samples = 3 s, and after a resize the first frame already shows the trail (> 100 cells lit at 120×40); `DP-A8` (GEN-R13) at 80×24, 40×12 and 200×50 over a 45 s run, no dot is lit on the readout row, and at any size from 1×1 and any angles every bob stays on screen; `DP-A9` seeded starts are high energy from rest (`π/2 < |θ₁|, |θ₂| < π`, `ω = 0`) and the twins differ only in `θ₂`, by exactly 10⁻³ rad; every 45 s the run restarts with new seeded angles, the twins again 10⁻³ rad apart and the trail reset to 1 sample; a resize keeps the run (twin states and run time unchanged); `DP-A10` at 120×40 (seed 0, 30 s), and on the first frame after a resize from 80×24, each twin's lower bob has ≥ 12 glow pixels in its own ramp within 3 pixels; no glow pixel exceeds the ramp level of 0.4; > 300 pixels glow; every braille cell over a glow has a brighter ink than its background; in ASCII no cell has a background and the rows above the readout equal the plain `Dots` blit; `DP-A11` `merge_shift(0) = (2, −4)`, `merge_shift(1) = (0, 0)` and `merge_shift(0.5) = (1, −2)`; with the twins posed at a 0-dot gap at 200×50, each hue lights ≥ 10 cells, and twin B's cell centroid is > 0.5 cell right of and > 0.5 cell above twin A's (DP-A5's balance and bob-hue checks still pass unchanged).
- **Perf** (`worst_frame`, 3 replays, M12 round 3): 200×50 worst 0.25 ms; 500×150 worst 0.72 ms (round 2: 0.29 ms and 0.97 ms, means 0.20 ms and 0.65 ms). Live bytes at 500×150 are 2.81 MiB (TEST-R7).

### mars_rover  (Voyages, M7, M12)
- **Intent**: a Perseverance-style rover crossing rust-red dunes under a dark, dusty sky, with parallax ridges and dust devils, working one plan per sol: it drives and drills a sample in the morning, images through the afternoon, relays data to an orbiter over its UHF antenna at the evening pass and sleeps through the night, as the sol runs through a blue Martian sunset aureole and a dusty twilight to a starry night.
- **Model**: terrain and suspension unchanged from M7.
  - Terrain: seeded fractal value noise, a pure function of world x, in 3 parallax layers.
  - Rocker–bogie suspension solved by Newton's method.
  - One work plan per sol, a `Timeline` exactly as long as the sol (a compile-time assertion), starting at 09:00 with the sol clock: drive 18 s → deploy 3 s → drill 4 s → stow 2 s → image 13 s → UHF relay 6 s → sleep 44 s. Driving and coring happen in full daylight, the relay at the evening orbiter pass with the Sun a few degrees up, and the rover sleeps, still, after dark (a real rover does not drive in the dark). The orbiter crosses the sky during the relay only.
  - Samples are numbered one per sol within Perseverance's 38 sample tubes (`CORING SAMPLE #1` … `#38`, then wrapping).
  - Readout (row 0): `SOL n  LTST hh:mm` on the left (local true solar time, 12 h at the Sun's transit; no equation of time, so not LMST) and the phase on the right: `DRIVE x.x m`, `ARM DEPLOY`, `CORING SAMPLE #n`, `ARM STOW`, `MASTCAM-Z IMAGING`, `UHF RELAY > ORBITER`, `SLEEP`.
  - Relay goes over the small, unsteered UHF antenna on the rear deck: a faint dotted link from its tip to the orbiter, drawn before the rover so it passes behind it and, over the half-block sky, with the sky restored behind each dot (CORE-R86), so it shows by day as well as by night; data packets climb along it. The high-gain antenna is the X-band direct-to-Earth dish and stays parked.
  - A 90 s sol at Jezero's latitude on a warped clock (hour angle piecewise linear in time), with Phobos on its real synodic period (scaled).
  - Dust particles and 0–2 dust devils.
  - Shadow *(new)*: by day the rover casts a shadow on the near ground: its footprint (±2.6 units about the rover) stretched down-Sun by `BODY_V.1 / tan e` (the body's top, 1.8 units, over the Sun's elevation `e`; at most 14 units), darkening the top 3 cell rows (6 pixels) of the near ground by `50 % × daylight`; none once the Sun is down, so it swings from one side in the morning to the other through the afternoon and stretches toward sunset.
  - Camera *(M12 round 2; replaces round 1's Mastcam-Z field-of-view dots, which are removed)*: the rover drives across the frame, its column going from 0.30 to 0.62 of the width in proportion to the distance driven while the terrain scrolls behind it (the camera follows at a lag). It holds at 0.62 through the rest of the work plan, and the camera eases back (smoothstep) to 0.30 over the sleep, so the next sol starts where this one did.
- **Look** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: the sky, Sun, Phobos, the three ground layers and the rover are one half-block Pixels image, rasterised in `prepare`; dust, the UHF link and packets and the stars are braille over it. Each column reads sky, then far ridge, mid plain and near ground, going down.
  - The sky is black overhead. Low down a dim band of dusty air in `Ramp(Sienna)`, `60·(y/horizon)³` by day (scaled by the daylight or the low Sun's glow), fades smoothly into black below level 8 (Sienna's floor is near black, so no dither).
  - Around a low Sun, forward scattering makes a compact blue aureole, the scene's accent: glow `g = smooth(25°, 4°) · smooth(−12°, −2°)` times a Gaussian in angle from the Sun (radius 0.23 of the Sun's height range); a pixel turns `Ramp(Ice)` `60 + 40 g` where `(g − 0.45)/0.25` passes a 4×4 Bayer threshold.
  - The Sun is a small disc at the top of `Ramp(Ember)`, white-hot, drawn until it sets behind the ridge.
  - Stars come out only once the aureole has faded (`smooth(−11°, −18°)`), as braille dots with varied, twinkling levels, only in cells whose two pixels are both black sky, and off the readout row.
  - The far and mid ridges are black silhouettes drawn by their crests alone: 1-px `Ramp(Sienna)` lines at 0.2 (far) and 0.28 (mid) of the ramp times the light. The near ground is `Ramp(Sienna)` at 0.32 of its M13 shade (near slopes that face the Sun a little brighter), darkened under the rover's shadow.
  - The rover is a half-block pixel sprite in `Starlight`: a solid pale body box (225) and camera head (240), belly 150, 1-px struts, RTG, mast, high-gain and UHF antennas (140, RTG 100), disc tyres (60) with a bright hub pixel (200) that turns with the odometer, the arm (170) and its turret (230), and a 1-px `Accent` eye; its inks dim with the daylight (to 35 % at night). Dust devils lift the pixels behind them toward warm dust, `Ramp(Sienna)` ≤ 150, and fade out with the day's heating. Wheel and drill dust are `Ramp(Sienna)` braille dots (≤ 170, dimmed with the light). The orbiter is 1–3 `Starlight` pixels (190–240, blinking); the UHF link (110) and packets (230) are `Wave` braille dots.
- **Stylisation**: the sky is dark (ADR-0017), so the butterscotch Martian day sky is only a dim dusty band at the horizon; the aureole's blue is a separate ramp dithered in. A 90 s sol on a warped clock: the day runs at an easy pace, the sunset lingers and the night is brief. Phobos's period is scaled with the sol.
- **Inks**: the sky band, ridge crests, near ground and dust in `Ramp(Sienna)`; the aureole in `Ramp(Ice)`; the Sun at the top of `Ramp(Ember)`; stars in `Star` (braille); the rover and orbiter in `Starlight`, the rover's eye in `Accent`; the UHF link and packets in `Wave`; the readout `Text` 170.
- **Acceptance**: `MR-A1` every wheel contact lies on the surface within 0.5 row; `MR-A2` terrain height is a pure, continuous function of world x; `MR-A3` the sol timeline runs in order and loops, and is exactly one sol long: over two sols the Sun is more than 20° up whenever the rover drives, deploys, drills or stows, between 0° and 20° during the relay, and whenever it is below the horizon the rover is asleep (phase after the relay) and still; sample numbers stay in 1–38 and wrap after 38; the rover moves only while driving, continuously; dust rises only while driving and drilling; `MR-A4` at 120×40 the sky's upper part (above the horizon row) is black but for the Sun; sky pixels are black, `Ramp(Sienna)` ≤ 150 (the band, or a dust devil over it) or the aureole's `Ramp(Ice)`, every `Ramp(Ice)` pixel within 3 glow radii of the Sun; at noon more than 200 band pixels and no aureole, at sunset (Sun < 5° up) more than 10 aureole pixels, and at night (Sun < −30°) a black sky with stars; stars appear only where the aureole's strength is below 0.05, the daylight is above 0.2 wherever the aureole's strength is above 0.5, in rings around the Sun the mean sky luminance (astro theme) never rises going out (± 0.01) and falls by more than a third, and at 200×50 at 45, 47 and 50 s, straight up from the setting Sun, the mean luminance over 4-pixel windows never rises going out (± 0.02); at sunset every aureole pixel is blue in astro (b > r) and their mean is brighter than the band's in mono; the far and mid ridges are black or `Ramp(Sienna)` with a crest line (the far crest lit in more than half the columns), the near ground `Ramp(Sienna)`, and at night the far crest is under a third of its noon luminance; `MR-A5` down every column the layers never go back up the stack (sky → far → mid → near), and each column ends in near ground; `MR-A6` the rover is a half-block pixel sprite: at least 20 pixels of its body are `Starlight` ≥ 200, and no glyph sprite (`◘▣◐◓◑◒•╱╲│─`) is drawn; `MR-A7` at noon and in the afternoon the Sun's pixels are at their ramp's top level (255) and every other ramp pixel is below 245; `MR-A8` at night every star cell is a braille glyph, none on row 0, and more than 20 show at 120×40, and at 200×50 through the night every star cell's two pixels are black sky; `MR-A9` the rover's hull at night is below 0.6 of its level at noon; `MR-A10` dust devils show by day and lift pixels no higher than `Ramp(Sienna)` 150; `MR-A11` during the UHF relay (2 s in, the Sun still up) the orbiter is above the horizon, at least 4 `Wave` braille link cells show at 120×40, and row 0 reads `UHF RELAY`; `MR-A12` *(new)*: no shadow for `e ≤ 0` (or NaN); by day it covers the footprint and extends only down-Sun (on the side away from the Sun), longer as the Sun sinks, and never longer than 14 + 2·2.6 units; *(changed, M12 round 2)* the rover's column is 0.30·w at the start of the sol, 0.62·w at the end of the drive and through stow, and 0.30·w again at the end of the sol; it never decreases during the drive, and is at least 0.1·w further right at 30 s than at 5 s.

### voyager_journey  (Voyages, M7, M12)
- **Intent**: Voyager 2's Grand Tour, 1977–1989, then its run out of the heliosphere. Each flyby visibly flings it faster: the inset's heliocentric speed jumps at Jupiter, Saturn and Uranus and ends above the Sun's escape speed. At Neptune the craft dives over Neptune's north pole toward Triton; Neptune's pull bends it 48° south of the ecliptic and slows it. The map then pulls back until the craft crosses the heliopause into interstellar space (2018-11-05, 119 AU).
- **Model**:
  - A log-compressed heliocentric top-down map (VOY-A4): a distance `r` is drawn at `ρ = ln(1 + r/0.6 AU) / ln(1 + r_edge/0.6 AU)` of the map's radius.
  - Planets are on circular orbits with J2000 elements (Standish) and Kepler periods (VOY-A2).
  - The spacecraft flies five Kepler conics. Four are launch → Jupiter → Saturn → Uranus → Neptune, each the prograde Lambert solution (universal variables, bisection on z) from one planet's model position on its real encounter date to the next's. The fifth leaves Neptune on its encounter date at Voyager 2's excess speed today (v∞ = 14.9 km/s, ~15.4 km/s at 119 AU); its flight-path angle is fitted by bisection so the craft reaches 119 AU on 2018-11-05, which puts it at 83.6 AU at the termination-shock crossing (2007-08-30; measured 83.7 AU). All are propagated with the universal-variable Kepler solver.
  - A 1500-sample table of the path, uniform in mission day, is built once; the trail and the speed inset are drawn from it, and only the craft's own position is solved each frame.
  - The clock maps 0–54 s to launch → heliopause, with slow-downs at the encounters, over three acts: tour 43 s, outward 11 s, beyond 5 s.
  - During the outward act the map's edge eases (in log) from 34 AU to 150 AU. The termination shock (83.7 AU) and the heliopause (119 AU) are drawn as rings, each once the map holds it, brightening once the craft has crossed it, the heliopause the denser.
- **Stylisation**:
  - Planets are circular and coplanar, so the flybys are the model's geometry; Lambert speeds are within a few km/s of Voyager 2's real ones. The flyby log prints the model's gains (+10.1, +5.6, +3.7, −4.2 km/s; Voyager 2's were roughly +10, +5, +2 and −2 km/s): the circular, coplanar planets and Lambert legs overstate Uranus and Neptune, so the log's header reads `flybys (model)` *(M12 round 2)*.
  - The post-Neptune dive 48° south of the ecliptic is drawn in the map plane. The model's Neptune slow-down (~4.2 km/s, 21.0 → 16.8 km/s) is about twice the real one (~2 km/s), since its Uranus–Neptune leg arrives fast.
  - The heliosphere is drawn as two Sun-centred circles at Voyager 2's own crossing distances; the real boundary is not spherical (Voyager 1 met the termination shock at 94 AU and the heliopause at 122 AU).
  - The map is turned so the outbound leg points at the roomier screen axis (left on a landscape canvas, down on a portrait one); north is still up out of the page, prograde counter-clockwise. Where the inset would overlap it, the map moves left (its rim a cell inside the left edge), shrinking only if that is not enough.
  - Distances are log-compressed (`ρ` above), so Earth through Neptune, and later the heliopause, fit on one screen. Directions and dates are true, but the orbits' spacing and the path's shape are not to scale: the rings for 1, 5.2, 9.5, 19.2 and 30 AU come out nearly evenly spaced, the 1977–89 path looks like a tight spiral, and the craft at 44 AU is drawn just outside Neptune's 30 AU ring.
  - Planet discs are sized for legibility, not to scale, and coloured in false colour.
  - The gravity assists are the jump between two conics, not an integrated flyby.
  - The Pale Blue Dot epilogue is cut (Voyager 1 took it).
- **Layers**: the Sun (a tone-mapped glow with a white-hot core), the planet discs and the craft are half-block Pixels. Each planet is centred on a pixel centre, so every disc is a symmetric stamp, never a tetromino, with its dark side kept visible; *(changed)* its radius snaps to one that is round in square pixels: under 0.8 px one pixel, under 1.9 px a 3 × 3 square, else at least 2.25 px (a 21-pixel disc and up), never a plus or a diamond. Jupiter is banded, Saturn ringed, each disc is lit on its sunward half, and each giant flares for 2 s as the craft swings past it. The craft is a white pixel in a small Ember glow. The flyby flash rings are drawn in the planet's own ramp *(changed)*. Stars, orbits, trail (its newest tenth at full strength and two dots wide), flyby rings, heliosphere rings and the speed inset are braille Dots. All are rasterised in `update`.
- **Text**: the row-0 readout `VOYAGER 2  1981-08-26  9.6 AU  20.5 km/s` (shortened to fit); encounter captions and outward lines on row 1, centred in the columns left of the inset; each planet's initial (`E J S U N`) beside its disc; the heliosphere rings' names (`heliopause 119 AU`, `termination shock 84 AU`, shortened or skipped to fit) beside the heliopause ring's right edge; the inset's labels (`heliocentric speed`, `40 km/s`, `escape`, `AU`, `J S U N` distance ticks). Labels keep off rows 0–1, the title row and the inset.
- **Flyby log** *(new)*: under the inset's initials (at least 4 rows below them, centred in the rows left above the title gap; dropped if it would reach the last two rows, and absent without an inset), a header `flybys (model)` *(M12 round 2; was `flybys`)* and one row per giant: its name, its encounter date and, once passed, the model's heliocentric speed gain there (`+10.1 km/s`), in the planet's ramp; the next target is marked `▸` (ASCII `>`) in bright `Text`, later ones dim `Text`. The map, its stars and rings keep out of it as they do the inset.
- **Inset**: top-right, 40×10 cells, only on canvases ≥ 110×30. It plots speed (0–42 km/s) against log distance (0.8–130 AU), with the Sun's escape speed `√(2μ/r)` dotted in. The map keeps out of the inset's cells and its labels (one column left of the plot, two rows below it): its rim keeps a cell clear of them, no star is placed there, and no ring (a flyby's flash can reach past the rim) is drawn there.
- **Captions** (`meta.captions`): `Jupiter assist`, `Saturn assist`, `Uranus assist` and `Neptune flyby` from 0.8 s before each encounter until 4.5 s after it or 1.5 mission years after it, whichever comes first (the clock races after Neptune); then, by mission date, `to the heliopause` until 2018-11-05 and `interstellar space` from then on; none on the tour's cruise legs (the title's `planets fling it on`). The row-1 lines keep the same windows: each encounter's line with its caption, the termination shock's from a year before the crossing to 1.5 years after it, the heliopause's from the crossing, on the same frame as `interstellar space`.
- **Inks** *(changed)*: the Sun in `Ramp(Ember)`, its core at the ramp's top; planets in `Ramp(Ice)`, Jupiter in `Ramp(Ember)` and Saturn and its ring in `Ramp(Sulfur)` (kept below the Sun's core), so the Sun and Jupiter keep the only amber; orbits and the inset axes and escape curve in `Ramp(Ice)`; the trail and the inset's speed trace in `Ramp(Starlight)` (was `Ember`); flyby rings in the planet's own ramp; the craft's glow in `Ramp(Ember)`; the flyby log in the planets' ramps and `Text`; heliosphere rings and their labels in `Ramp(Plasma)`; the craft and stars in `Star`; text in `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `VOY-A1` at each encounter date the spacecraft is at the planet's model position (< 1e-6 a) and within 0.5 cell on screen, and the path is continuous, outbound (within 1 % while leaving perihelion) and prograde; `VOY-A2` planet periods follow Kepler and match the real ones within 1 %; `VOY-A3` calendar conversion round-trips, and the clock is strictly monotone, prints the exact dates at the key times (including 2018-11-05) and slows at encounters; `VOY-A4` the radial map is strictly monotone at every edge and fits Neptune during the tour, and every planet is drawn in its own ramp throughout the tour (except where the craft covers it), at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50 each planet's silhouette is symmetric across both axes through its centre pixel with every pixel at or above the last visible level, and the Sun's core is the top of its ramp, no other ramp pixel within 250; `VOY-A5` each Lambert leg conserves energy and angular momentum (1e-9) and lands on its target planet on time (< 1e-6 AU), propagation round-trips, and Lambert recovers a Hohmann transfer's speed; `VOY-A6` heliocentric speed jumps by more than 5 % at Jupiter, Saturn and Uranus, Neptune slows it by 1–5 km/s, the craft is bound before Jupiter and above escape speed after it all the way out, and it is at 14.8–16 km/s at the heliopause; `VOY-A7` the craft is at 119 ± 0.01 AU on 2018-11-05 and within 1 AU of 83.7 AU on 2007-08-30, the map edge runs monotonically from 34 to 150 AU, and at the heliopause the craft is at least 2 cells inside the frame (and above the title row) at 200×50, 120×40, 100×30, 80×24 and 40×40; `VOY-A8` the speed trace (`Ramp(Starlight)` braille) lies inside the top-right inset, well clear of the title row, there is no inset at 80×24, and at 110×30, 120×40 and 200×50 every encounter caption and the intact inset header ` heliocentric speed` share row 1; at 120×40, 110×30, 160×45 and 200×50 the map's rim, every trail point and every Sun, planet and craft pixel stay out of the inset's cells all through the loop; at 200×50 no event line is on row 1 two mission years after its event, and the heliopause line shows exactly when the mission date has reached 2018-11-05 and the caption is `interstellar space`; `VOY-A9` at 200×50 and 80×24 each planet's initial is within 5 cells right of its disc; `VOY-A10` *(new)*: at 120×40, 25 s in, the trail is `Ramp(Starlight)` braille on > 20 cells and no braille cell is `Ramp(Ember)`; Saturn is not `Ember`; `round_radius` maps 0.3 and 0.79 → 0.5, 0.8, 1.2 and 1.89 → 1.5, 2.0 → 2.25, and keeps 3.1; `VOY-A11` *(new)*: at 120×40 the log lies right of the map (x > 60), more than a row below the inset and ending above row 38, and there is none without an inset; at 2 s it reads `flybys (model)`, `> Jupiter 1979-07-09` … `Neptune 1989-08-25` with no speeds; at 46 s every giant's row shows its km/s gain.

### moon_landing  (Voyages, M7, M12)
- **Intent**: Apollo 11's powered descent. The LM, its descent stage in gold foil, pitches over and throttles above a cratered Mare Tranquillitatis raked by an 11° Sun, with long dark shadows off every rim, black crescents under the crater walls and its own shadow running ahead. It kicks up flat sheets of dust, lights "CONTACT LIGHT", reports "The Eagle has landed" and plants the flag, while the Earth, a gibbous, hangs motionless high in the black sky.
- **Model**: unchanged from M7:
  - a 2-D point-mass LM with real Apollo numbers (`T ≤ 45.04 kN`, Isp 311 s, lunar g), integrated with RK4;
  - P64 E-guidance to a gate 8 m above the site, then P66 sink-rate descent with position hold;
  - a seeded crater field that is a pure function of x;
  - ballistic dust;
  - the `Timeline` descent 23 s → touchdown 4 s → flag 5 s → earth 4 s.

  The last beat was `earthrise` and is now `earth`. The Earth never rises at Tranquility Base (0.7° N, 23.5° E): it hangs ~66° up, fixed. It is drawn in every act at the same place, high on the side the LM flies toward. It shows the phase of 20 July 1969: a six-day waxing Moon, so a gibbous Earth, `(1 + cos α)/2 = 0.65` lit, from the Sun's side of the sky. The mid-Atlantic (~45° W, 5° S) faces the Moon (approximate), and the day side is the correct, western hemisphere: at the landing, 20:17 UTC, the subsolar point was near 123° W, 20.6° N, so the lit limb shows the Americas and the Pacific, with Africa and Europe on the night side. Drawn north up, the globe's west limb is on the left; when the scene's Sun is on the right, the globe is drawn south up (turned half a turn), so the lit limb still shows the Americas. The globe's Sun lies in the sky direction of the subsolar point seen from the sub-lunar one, tilted ~22° toward north, and the viewer sits 5° south of the equator: in July the whole Arctic is lit and the Antarctic dark.
- **Look (M12)**:
  - The regolith is a half-block Pixels image rasterised in `update`, and fills the bottom quarter (up to 12 rows).
  - The skyline is lit by the Lambert term of each slope under the 11° Sun. A single down-Sun sweep casts shadows `h / tan 11°` long; where terrain up-Sun or the LM shades it, the skyline's two-pixel surface is the darkest regolith grey (level 16), never black, so the ground's silhouette stays unbroken against the black sky. The LM's shadow is a thin sliver on that surface, running ahead of it.
  - The plain in front is evenly lit, with world-anchored rocks and pits, and darkens toward the viewer. Sixteen seeded craters (3–25 m) lie on it as ellipses, foreshortened more toward the skyline, and never over the skyline's surface. The sun-side rim's shadow is the bowl minus the bowl shifted 0.8 radii away from the Sun (the reach `depth / tan 11°` of a mature, shallow mare bowl, depth ≈ D/13): a crescent against the sun-side wall, a little over a third of the bowl. Its core (the bowl minus a 0.4-radius shift) is the terminal's black; the rest is dark grey, filled by light scattered off the sunlit far wall. The lit floor is a mid grey, the far wall facing the Sun bright, and the outer rim bright on the sun side, dim on the far slope.
  - Black is the terminal's black (an empty pixel), since even `Structure`'s lowest level renders as a grey in every theme; regolith below the last visible level (12, CORE-R66) is black.
  - The Earth is painted with `satellite_orbit`'s globe, `h/12` rows in radius (0.8–3.4), so the LM stays the focus.
  - Dust flies as braille sheets. The descent engine's hypergolic plume is nearly invisible in vacuum, so it is a faint flickering cone of Plasma dots.
  - The LM sprite, flag, astronaut and text are glyphs; they take the regolith pixel under them as background (CORE-R86). The sprite's footpad row is whole cells, so the site under the sprite, when it lies up to a cell below the pads, is raised to them: the LM stands on regolith, never over a sky-black notch.
- **Stylisation**:
  - The Earth is drawn larger than its true 1.9°.
  - The plain's craters are laid on the plain as a picture, not in perspective from the terrain profile.
