When it grows, new birds are cloned from bird `j mod N` with the same velocity and a `gaussian2` jitter of `r_s·σ'` truncated at `2.5·r_s·σ'` (clamped into the box), and they start calm. σ, `κ_i`, the sky height, the small-sky factor ρ and the margin `m`, the hash and `Dots` are rebuilt, and any bird scaled below the new sky's bottom edge is reflected back by the wall. Bird arrays are allocated at capacity 1000 in `build`, so only the hash buckets and `Dots` reallocate. Timeline time is untouched.
- **Look** (M14, ADR-0017; replaces the M12 sky, reed bank, bird shading and escape-wave inks): the sky is the terminal's black and has no pixels. Each bird is two braille dots, its head and its tail `max(1.5σ, 1.5)` dots behind along its heading (the M11 stroke), clipped to the sky. A bird cell is its braille glyph with no background, in `Ramp(Starlight)` from 170 (one lit dot) to 235 (eight), so folds read denser than sparse edges. The escape wave shows only in the flock's motion: no pale front, no dark band, no wings.
- **Falcon** (M14): a 3 × 2-dot braille body in `Ramp(Ice)` 235 (false colour, the one cold accent) over its cell's birds, with its 0.3 s streak as a 1-dot trail tapering from `Ice` 60 at the tail to 140 at the head; a cell takes its brightest streak dot's ink, and birds win a cell the streak crosses. ASCII keeps the 8-way heading glyph.
- **ASCII**: each bird is a heading-aware stroke on `Dots`. A level bird lights 2 dots: the head at x and the tail at `x − 1.5σ v̂`. Dots that round into the label row are not drawn, so a tail or wing never reaches it. The `Dots` fallback (1 `.`, 2 `:`, 3–4 `+`, ≥ 5 `#`) turns the flock into a `.`/`:` haze with `+`/`#` ridges (about a quarter of the lit cells at 200×50). In ASCII the falcon is an 8-way heading glyph `→ ↘ ↓ ↙ ← ↖ ↑ ↗` chosen by `round(atan2(vy, vx)/(π/4))`, with ASCII `> \ v / < \ ^ /`. It is drawn with `Canvas::put` over the blit, in the sky's last row at most. In ASCII (M12), each sky cell with bird dots shows the braille dot-count fallback of its mask in `Structure` 150, a streak-only cell shows `.` in the streak ink.
- **Captions** (M12): `escape wave runs` while any bird is alarmed and for `WAVE_HOLD` = 1 s after the last one calms, whatever the beat; otherwise patrol shows `None` (the title's own mechanism), stoop `a falcon stoops`, and pursue and climb `the flock closes`. The wave itself is short (≈ 1–1.3 s at seed 42: t ≈ 6.1–7.4 s, 19.1–20.1 s and 29.1–30.1 s, mostly in the stoop, not the pursue), so the caption follows the wave, not the beat, and never promises a wave that is off screen; the hold keeps it up long enough to read (≥ 1.5 s).
- **Label**: `N 192  k=7` (ASCII, preformatted in `resize`) right-aligned on the last row (GEN-R13), drawn only when `w ≥ 32` and `h ≥ 10` and it fits `title::stable_readout_room` (CORE-R83), so it never comes and goes with the caption, with a blank cell either side.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 (N = 1000, hash of 134×67 buckets of 3 dots), `update(1/60)` is one substep, dominated by the hinted kNN; with the padded hint no bird falls back to the full horizon. Rasterising ≤ 4000 dots into `Dots` happens inside `update`, and `render` only blits. Criterion on a loaded machine (load average ≈ 30–45, where molecular_dynamics measured 0.73 ms against its 0.55 ms quiet figure): 0.40–0.64 ms at 200×50 and 0.40–0.61 ms at 400×100 (0.40 ms at load ≈ 18; 0.68 ms at load ≈ 80–99 after the margin and ρ were cached in `resize`, with 0.37 ms measured at M10 integration under load ≈ 6–25), against the < 1 ms aim. M12 (fastest of 5 runs per frame over 3600 frames, load ≈ 11–25): mean 0.42 ms, worst frame 0.87 ms (M10: 0.39 / 0.83 ms); the sky is shaded only in `resize`. At dt = 0.25 it takes 15 substeps, but GEN-R8 is measured at dt = 1/60. Steady state is allocation-free.
- **Acceptance**: `BOID-A1` for seeded clouds (N ∈ {1, 7, 8, 500}, including coincident points and points outside the box), the spatial hash's `k_nearest(i, 7, R)` returns exactly the brute-force set of ≤ 7 nearest within R, excluding i, sorted by (d², index), for any hint radius; and for a seeded 1000-bird cloud, the hint disc `reach` built from each bird's exact 7-neighbour list holds all 7 (no hinted query falls back to R); `BOID-A2` `N`/`σ` follow the formulas (80×24 ⇒ 192/1.0, 200×50 ⇒ 1000/1.0, 400×100 ⇒ 1000/2.0, 1×1 ⇒ 24/0.5), N is constant across updates, a resize from 200×50 to 80×24 keeps the first 192 birds with positions scaled by (W'/W, H'/H), and a resize from 80×24 to 200×50 yields 1000 birds with every clone within `3·r_s·σ'` of its scaled source; `BOID-A3` order emerges: in the seeded 100×30 scene (N = 300; seeds 0, 1 and 2) with the falcon disabled, global polarisation `Φ = |Σ v̂|/N` is < 0.2 at t = 0, and over 1 s samples in t ∈ [8, 20] s the median local alignment `⟨v̂_i · (1/k)Σ_{j∈N_i} v̂_j⟩` (the mean of the unit headings, not renormalised) is ≥ 0.9 and the median order parameter `max(Φ, M)` is ≥ 0.75, where `M = |Σ r̂_i × v̂_i|/N` about the centroid is the milling parameter, since a flock circling its roost is ordered although its Φ is low (wild flocks reach Φ ≈ 0.96, Cavagna et al. 2010); the global bound depends on the seed (a flock bent part-way into a mill, Φ ≈ 0.5 and M ≈ 0.6, misses it on about 2 of seeds 0–11), while median local alignment is ≥ 0.95 on all of them (~3 s in debug); `BOID-A4` over 20 s at 80×24 with attacks (seed 42), every bird is finite and inside the sky `[0, W] × [0, H_s]` after every substep, speed stays in `[0.5, 2]·v₀σ`, the specular wall acts on < 2 % of bird-substeps after t = 5 s, at every 1 s sample after 5 s the median distance to each bird's true nearest neighbour (brute force, at the current positions) is in `[0.35, 1.0]·r_s` (1.4–4 dots ≈ 0.4–1.2 m; Ballerini et al. 2008 report ≈ 0.7–1.5 m), at ≥ 90 % of the 0.5 s samples after 5 s the flock's 2–98 % extent stays at least one cell (2 dots across, 4 down) inside the sky, and the falcon is within 1 dot of an edge of the sky in < 3 % of substeps (~1.2 s in debug); and in a small sky (40×12 and 32×10, seeds 0–2, 60 s each at dt = 1/30, 0.5 s samples) σ is 0.7, the bottom row shows exactly the label with a blank cell either side, and the mean share of birds within 4 dots of a wall of the sky is ≤ 7 % at 40×12 and ≤ 16 % at 32×10 (with ρ forced to 1: ≈ 12 % and ≈ 21 %) (~2 s in debug); `BOID-A5` the alarm is an excitable wave: in the pure `alarm_step` on a chain graph (bird j's only neighbour is j − 1, T_a = 21 substeps), firing bird 0 at step 0 fires bird n at exactly step `n·L` for n ≤ 10, the sign propagates unchanged, and re-exposure within the same attack never refires a bird; in the seeded 100×30 scene, ≥ 80 % of birds have fired within 1.0 s of the first stoop's first alarm and ≤ 20 % of those fired directly, and the front outruns any bird: the least-squares slope of each firing's distance from where the first alarm fired against its time since then exceeds `V_MAX·v₀σ = 2 v₀σ` (≈ 132 dots/s ≈ 4.4 v₀ for seed 21); `BOID-A6` in the seeded 100×30 scene the falcon's beats are patrol [0, 5) / stoop [5, 7.5) / pursue [7.5, 9.5) / climb [9.5, 12) s and repeat at 12 s, every recorded fire step over 30 s lies inside a stoop or pursue window, and in each of the first two attacks the falcon's minimum distance to the flock centroid during stoop plus pursue is < 0.25 of that distance at stoop start; `BOID-A7` a large flock is not a round ball: `κ_i(N)` equals κ for N ≤ 300, never increases with N and equals 0.03 s⁻² at N = 1000; and in the seeded 200×50 scene (seed 42, with attacks), over 0.5 s samples in t ∈ [8, 40] s, the median principal-axis ratio `√(λ_max/λ_min)` of the positions' covariance is ≥ 1.5 and `+`/`#` make up ≤ 40 % of the lit cells of the ASCII frames (a per-bird trap of κ on every bird gives ≈ 55 %); `BOID-A8` black sky, bright birds, at 80×24 and 200×50, seed 42, at t = 5 and 20 s: no cell has a background, every bird cell's ink is `Ramp(Starlight)` 170–235, birds are present and under a quarter of the cells are lit; `bird_ink` is 170 for one lit dot, 235 for eight and rises with the count; and at 200×50, t = 3 s, the birds light between 1.4 N and 2 N dots (two per bird, shared where they coincide); `BOID-A9` captions and folds: over 36 s at 100×30, seed 42, `caption()` is `escape wave runs` exactly while a bird is alarmed or was within the last 1 s, and otherwise `None` in patrol, `a falcon stoops` in stoop and `the flock closes` in pursue and climb; each caption is in `META.captions`; every wave caption run lasts ≥ 1.5 s and there are ≥ 3; and at t = 6.5 s (stoop) the seeded 200×50 scene has cells with ≥ 4 lit dots inked brighter than a lone bird; `BOID-A10`/`BOID-A11` the wave is motion and the falcon one pale dot: through a whole attack at 120×40, seed 42, the falcon layer never holds more than 6 dots and shows at least once with ≥ 4, and every lit cell is a bird (`Starlight`), the falcon (`Ice` 235), its streak (`Ice` ≤ 140) or text.

### dna_helix  (Molecules of Life, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: the double helix of Watson and Crick's 1953 figure: two backbones winding right-handed around a ladder of coloured base pairs and turning in space. Then a replication fork runs through it. A helicase ring unzips the ladder, and each separated strand is copied into a daughter helix that is half old (ice blue) and half new (rose): the leading strand continuously, the lagging strand in Okazaki fragments.
- **Model**: B-DNA in Å, with world +y up (CORE-R45 maps it to screen up) and the axis along +x.
  - **Helix geometry**:
    - Twist is `Ω = 2π/10.5` per bp, i.e. 34.29° (Wang 1979), with a rise of 3.4 Å, so the pitch is 35.7 Å.
    - The phosphorus backbone radius is `R = 8.9 Å` (Arnott & Hukins 1972).
    - The Watson angle is `θ_W(x, t) = φ(t) + 2πx/35.7 Å`. Crick is `θ_C = θ_W + Δ`, with `Δ = 2.2 rad` from C1′–C1′ = 10.5 Å at r = 5.9 Å (Saenger 1984). That gives axial groove widths of 12.5 Å (minor) and 23.2 Å (major).
    - A backbone point is `(x, y₀ + R cos θ, R sin θ)` about its duplex axis at height `y₀`. θ increases with x, so the helix is right-handed. Watson runs 5′→3′ along +x.
  - **Layout**: base pair `i` sits at `xᵢ = x_L + (i − 4.5)·3.4 Å`, where `x_L` is the world x at column 0, so column 0 falls midway between bp 4 and bp 5. Four pairs lie beyond each screen edge, so the camera sway never exposes a blunt end.
  - **Rungs**: a chord at `xᵢ` between the two backbones (the phosphorus positions), split at its midpoint so each half shows that strand's base. Rungs are drawn only every `stride`-th pair, so at least 2 columns separate them, and a rung seen end-on at a backbone crossing (a slanted chord under 2 rows, over 4 rows, or flatter than 1 column per 2 rows) is culled rather than drawn as a diagonal along a row. *Simplifications*: joining phosphorus positions puts each chord ≈ 4 Å off the axis on the minor-groove side (real B-DNA base pairs sit on it), and the helicase is a symbolic collar, smaller than to scale (a hexameric DnaB ring is ≈ 12 nm across, ≈ 3× the duplex).
  - **Sequence**: `N_MAX = 512` Watson bases in a stack array (enough to span the width at any aspect ratio up to w/h ≈ 50; beyond that the height fit wins and the duplex ends short of the right edge). The GC count is a seeded uniform integer in [⌈0.35·N_MAX⌉, ⌊0.65·N_MAX⌋] (each G or C at random), placed by a Fisher–Yates shuffle from `ctx.rng`. Crick is the complement.
  - **Spin**: `φ(t) = φ₀ − ω·t` on loop-local time, which wraps at the `reset` midpoint (where the fade is 0), so the apex angle is identical in every loop, with `ω = 2π·v_f/10.5 = 1.7952 rad/s`. That is the unwinding a fork at `v_f = 3 bp/s` needs, so the apex angle stays fixed. The unreplicated duplex spins as if its far end were free; in vivo it cannot rotate, and topoisomerases instead relax the positive supercoils that build ahead of the fork.
  - **Fork**: the apex is at `f(τ) = v_f·τ` bp, where τ is the time since `unwind` began. It keeps advancing through `hold` and is frozen during `reset`. There is no fork during `helix`. `v_f` is ≈ 6–17× slower than human forks (1–3 kb/min) and ≈ 300× slower than *E. coli* (≈ 1 kb/s).
  - **Arms**: behind the fork (`s = f − i > 0`) the parental strands separate into two arms, displaced by `±Y(s) = ±1.7R·smoothstep(s/6)` in world y.
    - The upper arm (+y) is the Watson template, which is the lagging-strand template. A DnaB-style helicase ring encircles it at the apex, translocating 5′→3′.
    - The lower arm is the Crick template (the leading strand).
    - Each new strand takes its partner's angular slot (Watson + Δ, or Crick − Δ), so each daughter is a B duplex like the parent. Keeping B geometry in the arms is artistic licence; real single-stranded DNA at the fork is unwound.
  - **Synthesis** is 5′→3′ only:
    - Leading: new base `j` is made at `f(τ) − 2 = j`, i.e. `τ = (j + 2)/3` (the polymerase trails the helicase by 2 bp).
    - Lagging: Okazaki fragments of `L_ok = 8` bp (scaled down from 100–200 nt in eukaryotes and 1–2 kb in bacteria) cover `[8k, 8k + 8)`. Fragment `k` is primed at its high-x end when `f ≥ 8k + 9` (`τ_k = (8k + 9)/3`) and extended toward decreasing x at `v_pol = 8 bp/s`, so base `j` is made at `τ_k + (8k + 7 − j)/8`. The fragment is complete 7/8 s after priming, and ligase seals the nick 0.5 s after completion. *Simplification*: primase lays a short RNA primer at the start of each fragment, which is drawn here as DNA; its removal and the gap fill (RNase H / FEN1 and Pol δ in eukaryotes, Pol I in bacteria) are omitted, so ligase seals each fragment directly.
    - Template bases that are not yet paired show as half-rungs.
  - Every quantity is a closed-form function of scene time; nothing is integrated.
- **Motion**: a `Timeline` of `helix` 4 s → `unwind` 14 s → `hold` 4 s → `reset` 2 s, looping every 24 s.
  - **Spin**: from the first frame the spin reads as a barber-pole drift along +x at 10.2 Å/s. That equals the fork speed, so the fork travels with the pattern. It is ≈ 7 columns/s at 80×24 (≈ 11 while zoomed) and ≈ 16 at 200×50.
  - **Camera**:
    - Yaw is `0.2·sin(2πt/19 s)`, capped at `asin(13 Å / visible half-width)` so the edges swing ≤ 13 Å in depth; pitch is 0.18 rad, and distance is `max(40R, 6·visible half-width)` (gentle perspective).
    - The story's zoom `z(t)` is 2.5 during `helix`. It eases to 1 over the first 3 s of `unwind` (`in_out_cubic`), stays 1 until the `reset` midpoint, and steps back to 2.5 there, while every level is 0. Views of at least 20 rows fit their content instead of following `z(t)`: each frame takes the largest zoom in `1..=2.8` (found by 7 bisection steps; zoom 1 always fits, by `FIT_R`) at which everything on screen fits 0.88 of rows `[1, h − 1)`. That is the four backbones, sampled 8 times per pair whether synthesised or not, and the helicase collar, whose rows shrink smoothly as the screen edges clip it. The content is then centred on those rows. The fork's opening arms therefore pull the camera back as they reach the screen (the dolly becomes content-driven), and an intact stretch fills the frame. It is a pure function of the view and `t`. Views under 20 rows keep the story's `z(t)`, at most 2.5. (M12 round 1 used 2.5 everywhere, filling about 75 %; round 2's fixed 2.8 opening overflowed at 257×61.)
    - The zoom is anchored at the left edge, so column 0 stays between bp 4 and bp 5, and the dolly-out opens room for the arms.
  - The fork enters at τ ≈ 1.3 s and leaves the screen at τ ≈ 12.4 s at 80×24 (≈ 14.1 s at 200×50). Every visible fragment is sealed before `reset` at both sizes.
  - Fresh nucleotides are drawn at level 255 and fade linearly to their normal level over 0.4 s.
  - `reset` fades the daughters out (1 s). At its midpoint, while the level is zero, it draws a new sequence, and it then fades the parental helix back in (1 s).
- **Resize**: view only; there is no lattice (ADR-0012).
  - View (M12): the helix fits rows `[1, h − 1)`. Row 0 holds the sequence label and the bottom row is left to the title (GEN-R13); the projection centre is `(w/2, (h − 1)/2)`.
  - The zoom-1 scale is `min(max(h − 3, 1)/(FIT_R·R), w/(2·35.7))` rows/Å with `FIT_R = 6.0` (M12; 6.6 before), floored so N_MAX pairs still span the width at extreme aspect ratios. The 6.0 covers the open fork's ±2.7R plus pitch, sway and perspective, as DNA-A4 checks (it holds at 6.0, not at 5.7), so the daughters fill the rows instead of floating in a band of empty ones. The width term keeps at least one turn visible on narrow, tall terminals.
  - The pair count is `n = min(⌈w/cols_per_bp + 0.5⌉ + 8, 512)`: ≈ 42 at 80×24 (2.4 columns/bp, R ≈ 3.1 rows) and ≈ 47 at 200×50 (5.2 columns/bp, R ≈ 6.9 rows).
  - The two `Dots` layers are resized.
  - Scene time and the sequence are kept, and column 0 stays between bp 4 and bp 5.
  - Small views: the fork runs only when the zoom-1 backbone radius `R·scale` is ≥ `FORK_MIN_ROWS` = 1.95 rows (scaled with `FIT_R`, so the same heights keep or drop the fork; h ≳ 15; 50×15 has it, 50×14 does not). Below that the arms, daughters and helicase share too few rows and knot into braille, so the view shows the intact helix at zoom 2.5 all cycle: no fork, arms or helicase, while the `reset` fade and the new sequence at its midpoint still run. The `Story` itself is unchanged; only what the view shows is.
  - Large views: from `R·scale` ≥ 4.5 rows (h ≳ 32, e.g. 120×36 and 200×50) near-layer backbone lines get twins along the screen normal, so the near strands are 3 dots wide, and 4 once the backbone radius at the current zoom reaches 9 rows. A 1-dot thread would otherwise be outweighed by the full-cell `█` rungs, and the near/far depth cue needs the area. Far strands stay 1 dot and dim, so they recede.
- **Inks and weight** (M12; all false colour, ADR-0015): the backbones are the hero.
  - Parental backbones are `Ramp(Ice)` and new strands `Ramp(HAlpha)`, so semiconservative replication (Meselson & Stahl 1958) shows as two colours. The depth cue is `level = 85 + 150·(z_local + R)/(2R)`, where `z_local` is the camera-space depth of the point minus that of its duplex axis at the same x. New nucleotides flash to 255 when made.
  - Rungs are drawn at `max(0.8 × backbone level, 100)`, under the near backbones. An upright rung in front of its duplex axis (`z_a + z_b ≥ 0`) is the full-cell slab `█` (two braille dots wide), one behind it the half-cell `▐` (one dot), so the near ladder reads as stacked base-pair plates and the depth shows; a slanted rung is a short `╲`/`╱` staircase with those uprights. Each is coloured by pair type: A·T (two hydrogen bonds) `Ramp(Aurora)`, G·C (three) `Ramp(Plasma)`. Plasma is shared only with action_potential's voltage map.
  - The pyrimidine half of a rung (T, C, one ring) is drawn at 0.6 of the purine's level (A, G, two rings), so the two halves of every rung differ. *Stylisation*: the halves split at the chord's midpoint, and ring count is shown by brightness only (a real purine spans more of the C1′–C1′ distance).
  - The helicase is a filled doughnut: 9 circles of 48 samples at 0.40–1.0 of its 0.6R collar radius (M12; 0.35R before) about Watson at the fork apex, in `Ramp(Sulfur)` level 235 (M12; replaces `Hot`): the frame's one chrome-yellow object, apart from the Ice/HAlpha strands and the Aurora/Plasma pairs. False colour. It follows the bacterial model: like DnaB it encircles the lagging-strand template (Watson, 5′→3′ along +x, the fork's direction) and translocates 5′→3′; the eukaryotic helicase (CMG) rides the leading-strand template instead. It is rasterised into its own braille layer blitted last, so it occludes the backbone under it and reads whole, with a dark core where the strand passes through.
  - Every level is multiplied by the `reset` fade.
  - Label `Text`, with the bases in their pair inks, so it keys the rungs.
- **ASCII**: backbones are two `Dots` layers (far `z_local < 0`, near `≥ 0`), which fall back to `. : + #` by dot count. Rungs are `█`/`▐` uprights and `╲`/`╱` slants, `|`, `\` and `/` in ASCII. The helicase is its own braille layer, marked in ASCII by two `o` cells at its top and bottom.
  - Draw order: far dots → rungs (painter-sorted by depth) → near dots → helicase → label.
  - At 80×24 the frame shows dotted strands braiding across the screen over a comb of `|` rungs; in the fork phase they split into a sideways Y.
- **Captions** (M12), from what the view shows: `reset` shows `None`; while the helicase is on screen, `helicase unzips`; once the fork's apex has passed the right edge, `half old, half new`; otherwise (an intact helix, a view without a fork, or an apex not yet on screen) `right-handed helix`.
- **Label**: top-left (row 0, col 1), e.g. `5'-GATTACAGCCTAGGTC-3'`, in ASCII. It shows 16 Watson bases when the width is ≥ 60, or 8 when it is ≥ 30.
  - The window starts at the first on-screen pair (bp 5), 5′ first, and is fixed for the cycle: it changes only at the `reset` midpoint, with the new sequence.
  - It is dropped below 30 columns or 6 rows and is never clipped.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 the backbones are sampled about one dot of arc apart: ≈ 800 samples per strand × 4 strands in the fork ≈ 3.2k projections and plots ≈ 70 µs, plus a twin line for each near segment on bold views (≈ 1.6k more lines, no extra projections). The content fit costs about 8 extent passes per frame: ≈ 0.31 ms mean per frame at 200×50 (was 0.15 ms).
  - Rungs: up to ≈ 94 (two daughters) × ≈ 12 cells, plus a sort of the visible rungs in a `Vec` reserved in `resize` (allocation-free after it).
  - Two layer blits ≈ 20 µs.
  - The `Dots` are rasterised at the end of `update` and `resize`, because `render` takes `&self`.
  - Total ≈ 0.2 ms per frame at 30 FPS, and ≈ 1.5 ms per frame in the 15-step low-power worst case. There is no heap use after resize.
- **Acceptance**: `DNA-A1` B-DNA geometry: the twist is `2π/10.5` per bp (34.2857°), the rise 3.4 Å and the pitch 35.7 Å; with `R = 8.9 Å` and `Δ = 2.2 rad` the axial groove widths are 12.50 ± 0.05 Å (minor) and 23.20 ± 0.05 Å (major), summing to the pitch, and the C1′ chord `2·5.9·sin(Δ/2)` is 10.5 ± 0.1 Å; both strands are right-handed, i.e. the triple product `(a × b)·c` of any three successive world chord vectors at 0.5 bp steps is > 0; `DNA-A2` rendering is not mirrored: through the scene's own projection at `yaw = 0`, `pitch = 0.18`, zoom 1, every parental backbone sample with `z_local ≥ 0.25R` lies on a segment with screen slope `dy/dx > 0` (a `\` slant with y down, as a right-handed helix along +x requires), world +y projects to a smaller row, and at equal x a near sample's level exceeds a far sample's; at 200×50 and 120×36 the near braille layer lights ≥ 1.5× as many dots as the far layer (bold near strands), and at 80×24 and 40×12 ≤ 1.3× (both 1 dot wide); the rung glyph set is `█ ▐ ╲ ╱` and no upright of either kind lands in column 0 of the landing frames; the helicase collar (`RING = 0.6`) is clamped on the upper strand: its centre and plane match the apex, it lights ≥ 8 collar dots with an unlit dot at the projected centre, and every collar cell of a rendered frame is in the collar's ink; `DNA-A3` Watson–Crick pairing: sequences use only A, C, G, T; `comp` is an involution with A↔T and G↔C; `Crick[i] = comp(Watson[i])`, so the duplex has #A = #T and #G = #C (Chargaff); for 64 seeds, every drawn sequence's realised GC fraction over all N_MAX = 512 bp lies in [0.35, 0.65]; once synthesis at bp `i` is complete, both daughter pairs at `i` equal the parental pair `(Watson[i], Crick[i])`, and each daughter holds exactly one parental strand (semiconservative); `DNA-A4` fork kinematics: `f(τ) = 3τ`, and the spin `ω = 2π·3/10.5 = 1.79520 rad/s` keeps the apex backbone angle constant through `unwind` and `hold` (`|Δθ| < 1e-9` rad); no pair with `i ≥ f(τ)` is ever separated; `Y(s)` is 0 for `s ≤ 0`, non-decreasing, and equal to `1.7R` for `s ≥ 6`; at 200 sampled times over a cycle, at 80×24 and 200×50, every projected backbone sample of every visible strand lies within rows `[1, h − 1)`, and bp 0 projects left of column 0; in the opening helix the strands' extent over the visible columns is ≥ 0.85 of `h − 2`, lies within rows [0.5, h − 1.5], and its midpoint is within 1 row of `(h − 1)/2`, at 80×24, 200×50 and 120×36 for t ∈ [0, 4) s; on tall views (80×24, 120×40, 200×50; t every 0.2 s over the 24 s loop, fade = 1) the content spans ≥ 80 % of rows `[1, h − 1)`, lies within rows [0.5, h − 1.5], and its midpoint is within 1 row of `(h − 1)/2`; views with `R·scale` < 1.95 rows (40×12, 40×13, 50×14, 200×10) show no fork at any time (no τ, zoom 2.5, the story's fade and draws kept, no half-rungs or helicase in mid-`unwind`), while 50×15 and 80×24 show the story unchanged; `DNA-A5` synthesis is 5′→3′ only: leading base `j` is made at `τ = (j + 2)/3`, so leading synthesis times increase strictly with `j` and no leading base is within 2 bp of the apex; lagging bases come in 8 bp fragments, each primed only once `f ≥ 8k + 9`, with synthesis times strictly decreasing in `j` within a fragment; every fragment is complete before its successor is primed, and each nick is sealed exactly 0.5 s after completion; at 80×24 and 200×50 every visible base of both daughters is synthesised and every visible nick sealed before `reset` begins; `DNA-A6` the story is a pure function of time: driving the pure `Story` state (fork index, synthesised bases with their ages, sealed nicks, phase, zoom, fade) with 1/60 s steps and with a seeded irregular sequence of `dt ∈ [0, 0.25]` to the same time gives identical state and identical RNG position; the timeline is `helix` 4 → `unwind` 14 → `hold` 4 → `reset` 2 s and loops at 24 s; exactly one new sequence is drawn per cycle, at the `reset` midpoint, and for seed 42 consecutive cycles' sequences differ; the zoom changes discontinuously only at an instant where the fade level is 0; `DNA-A7` palette, hierarchy and margin, at 80×24 and 200×50 (t = 2, 10 and 20 s): `pair_role` gives Aurora for A and T and Plasma for G and C, and a purine's base ink is brighter than its partner's; every braille cell is Ice, HAlpha or the helicase's `Ramp(Sulfur)`, and every rung cell is Aurora or Plasma; Ice cells occur, HAlpha cells occur exactly once the fork has run, and Sulfur occurs exactly mid-unwind; the mean backbone level exceeds the mean rung level; the bottom row is blank; and at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50 (t = 0.5, 1.5, 3 s) an upright `█` rung is always a near one and `▐` a far one, and both occur; `DNA-A8` captions, at 80×24 (with a fork) and 40×12 (without), stepping 0.1 s through one 24 s loop: `caption()` matches the rules above and every caption is in `META.captions`; `helicase unzips` shows only while the helicase is drawn; all three captions occur with a fork, and only `right-handed helix` without one; `DNA-A9` rungs and helicase ink: near rungs are `█`, far rungs `▐`, both `|` in ASCII; once pairs are ≥ 6 columns apart (`2·3.4·k`, the zoomed-in frames) each upright rung cell is doubled into the next column, `██` (4 dots) near and `▐▌` (2 dots straddling the cell edge) far, `||` in ASCII, while zoomed out rungs stay one column; the helicase role is `Ramp(Sulfur)` and differs from every strand and pair ramp.

### electron_orbitals  (States of Matter, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: the hydrogen atom as its quantum states actually look. A Monte-Carlo cloud of electron positions is drawn from |ψ_nlm|² and turns slowly in 3-D. Lobes are coloured by the phase of the wavefunction (its sign in a pure state; in a superposition the hue winds at the slowed beat), and the nucleus glints through the nodes. Between states the atom passes through a real superposition, and the charge cloud sloshes from lobe to lobe in a calm breath: the true Bohr beat, slowed by a factor the label states. That oscillating dipole is how the atom emits or absorbs Lyman-α, Hα or Paschen-α light. The cloud is centred. The r₉₀ surface of 4f spans 0.8 of the height, and smaller n is drawn only a little smaller (every hold is the hero, filling 0.64–0.8 of the stage, while the size still grows with n). Point density is matched to each orbital's on-screen size, so 1s is a soft ball, not a solid disc, and 4f's lobes are not sparse. Each lobe is a smooth glowing body in its sign's colour family, with a sparse sparkle of phase-coloured dots over it. The eye reads lobe shape first, then the colour of the phase, then the corner label. Nothing else is on screen.
- **Model**: atomic units, a₀ = 1. `ψ = R_nl(r)·Y(r̂)` with `R_nl = √((2/n)³(n−l−1)!/(2n(n+l)!)) e^{−ρ/2} ρ^l L^{(2l+1)}_{n−l−1}(ρ)`, ρ = 2r/n (generalised Laguerre by its three-term recurrence). Real harmonics on the unit direction (x, y, z): `s = 1/√(4π)`, `p_z = √(3/4π) z`, `p_x = √(3/4π) x`, `d_z² = √(5/16π)(3z² − 1)`, `d_xz = √(15/4π) xz`, `d_xy = √(15/4π) xy`, `f_z³ = √(7/16π)(5z³ − 3z)`, `f_xyz = √(105/4π) xyz`. The gallery is a closed dipole-allowed loop (Δl = ±1): 1s → 2p_z → 3d_z² → 4f_z³ → 3d_xz → 4f_xyz → 3d_xy → 2p_x → 1s. Energies are `E_n = −R_H/n²` with `R_H = R∞hc·μ/mₑ = 13.5983 eV` (reduced mass). Lines are `λ = hc/ΔE` with hc = 1239.842 eV·nm: Lyα 121.57 nm, Hα 656.47 nm, Pα 1875.6 nm (vacuum). Sampling is exact and separable, because every real harmonic here is a product Θ(θ)·Φ(φ): `|ψ|² d³r = [r²R² dr]·[Θ² d cosθ]·[Φ² dφ]`. Each factor is tabulated once as a 1024-knot inverse CDF (r ∈ [0, 1.5·r₉₉.₉], cos θ ∈ [−1, 1], φ ∈ [0, 2π)). One sample is three linear-interpolated lookups driven by `ctx.rng` uniforms. Each point also stores fixed uniforms `u_i` (visibility) and `v_i` (mixture), plus ψ of its own orbital and of its partner (f32 storage). Superposition a → b: `Ψ = c_a ψ_a e^{−iE_a t} + c_b ψ_b e^{−iE_b t}` with `c_a = cos χ`, `c_b = sin χ`, so `|Ψ|² = c_a²ψ_a² + c_b²ψ_b² + 2c_a c_b ψ_a ψ_b cos ωt`. Display sampler (exact intensity, per-orbital density): orbital k uses a prefix of `M_k = clamp(round(0.8·π(4ρ_k)²), 256, 16384)` iid points, where ρ_k is its on-screen r₉₀ radius in rows (View), which gives ≈ 0.4 visible points per dot inside r₉₀. For a → b: `M(χ) = (c_a²/M_a + c_b²/M_b)⁻¹`, `p_a = M(χ)c_a²/M_a` and `p_b = M(χ)c_b²/M_b`, so p_a + p_b = 1. Point i < M_a of slot S_a is proposed iff `v_i < p_a`, and point i < M_b of S_b iff `v_i < p_b`, so proposals have intensity exactly `M(χ)·q` with `q = c_a²ψ_a² + c_b²ψ_b²`. A proposed point is visible iff `u_i < w/2`, where `w = |Ψ|²/q = 1 + 2c_a c_b ψ_a ψ_b cos ωt / q ∈ [0, 2]` (AM–GM), evaluated in f64 (q ≤ 0 or non-finite ⇒ hidden). The visible points therefore have expected density exactly `(M(χ)/2)·|Ψ(t)|²` at every instant, and their count glides from M_a/2 to M_b/2. Display fade: on screen a point is proposed iff `v_i < p_k·τ(r)`, where τ is 1 inside `r_in` and falls linearly to 0 at `r_out`. The window is (r₈₈, r₉₅) of the states on screen (the wider (r₉₀, r₉₇) left lone single-dot braille cells a few columns out from the lobes, which read as grain rather than cloud), blended geometrically (`exp(lerp(ln r_a, ln r_b, c_b²))` componentwise, exactly a's at c_b² = 0 and b's at 1) and shared by both slots, so the displayed density is exactly `τ(r)·(M(χ)/2)|Ψ|²`, and the far dust ends inside the frame. Given a proposal, `v_i/p_k` is a fixed uniform independent of `u_i`, so the fade is shimmer-free. Because u and v are fixed per point, the cloud changes only where thresholds are crossed: no shimmer, no pops. At the endpoints c² (and therefore p) is set to exactly 0 or 1. The centroid follows `⟨r⟩(t) = sin 2χ·d_ab cos ωt` with `d_ab = ⟨a|r|b⟩`: 0.7449, 2.452, 5.188, 2.995, 3.867, 3.867, 2.123 and 0.7449 a₀ around the loop. Time is slowed, and the label says by how much: the beat is the true Bohr frequency ΔE/h (h = 4.135667696×10⁻¹⁵ eV·s; Lyα 2.466 PHz, Hα 456.7 THz, Pα 159.8 THz) divided by a round per-line factor S = 4×10¹⁵ / 10¹⁵ / 5×10¹⁴, so `f_disp = ΔE/(h·S)` = 0.62 / 0.46 / 0.32 Hz for Lyα / Hα / Pα: a calm breath with 1.8 / 1.4 / 1.0 cycles per 3 s beat, still ordered by ΔE as the physics is. (The previous `2.0 Hz·√(ΔE/ΔE_Lyα)` compression made 1s–2p_z flip every 0.25 s, a vibrating blob 2–4× faster than anything else in the scene.) ω = 2π f_disp with `t` counted from the rise start; a pair that is not a gallery α line would have no line, no factor and no beat. Timeline per orbital: `hold` 6 s (pure; this is χ = π/2 of the (prev, a) pair and χ = 0 of the (a, next) pair, which give the same visible set), `rise` 2 s (χ 0 → π/4, smoothstep), `beat` 3 s (χ = π/4, full sloshing), `fall` 2 s (χ π/4 → π/2). That is 13 s per orbital and a 104 s loop, starting at a seeded orbital's hold. Two-slot ring: during a's hold the free slot is filled with S_next, and S_a's partner ψ is rewritten to ψ_next (≤ 256 points per 1/60 s of dt; any remainder completes at the rise boundary). View: orbital (x, y, z) maps to world (x, z, −y), so orbital z is screen-up. One world unit is the orbital's r₉₀ times `g_k = (r₉₀,k/r₉₀,4f)^{0.1}` (r₉₀ = 2.66 / 7.99 / 15.80 / 25.99 a₀ for 1s / 2p / 3d / 4f), so `ρ_k = R·g_k` with `R = fit_radius(stage, 0.8)`, which is 0.79 R, 0.89 R, 0.95 R and R. The stage is every row above the label (all rows when the label is hidden), and the nucleus sits in its middle cell with the projected origin at that cell's centre, so a nodal plane through the nucleus empties one whole row. During transitions ln r₉₀ and ln g are lerped with the weight `glide(c_b²)`, so the larger state of the pair sets the scale while it holds at least half the weight: `1 − (1 − 2w)₊²` into a larger state, `(2w − 1)₊²` out of one. A plain c_b² would draw the larger state bigger than in its own hold (× `(r₉₀,b/r₉₀,a)^{0.9(1−w)}`, 1.64 for 1s → 2p_z at w = ½) and push its dust off the frame. Camera: `geom::Camera` with distance 6 and a per-state pose (α_k yaw offset, p_k pitch, rock amplitude, phase φ_k), lerped component-wise with weight c_b²: 1s, 2p_z, 3d_z² and 4f_z³ (0, 0.15, 0.6, 0); 2p_x and 3d_xz (0, 0.15, 0.25, π/2); 4f_xyz (π/4, 0, 0.8, 0); 3d_xy (0, 1.05, 0.6, 0). On the Timeline clock t, with θ = 2π(t − 3 s)/13 s + φ_k, `yaw = α_k + rock·cos θ` and `pitch = p_k − 0.10·sin θ`. One rock period is one orbital's 13 s visit, so the 104 s loop is exactly periodic and every hold sees the same part of the rock: the rock peaks at mid-hold (3 s in), and states with φ_k = π/2 pass through broadside there. The pitch wobble is in quadrature, so the camera always turns at ≥ 0.10·2π/13 s ≈ 2.8°/s and never stalls. Pose rationale: m = 0 states sit low, so their nodal planes stay open (at pitch 0.32 the 2p_z gap fills in). 2p_x and 3d_xz have a vertical nodal plane that is edge-on at yaw 0 and fills in when turned further (at ±0.6 the 2p_x node column holds up to 0.86 of the lobe column's dots, ≤ 0.26 at ±0.25). 4f_xyz is turned 45° and kept level, so its z = 0 nodal plane stays an open gap through the nucleus between two tiers of lobes; at mid-hold the rock turns it to yaw π/4 + 0.8 ≈ π/2, where its vertical nodal planes are edge-on and the lobes stand as four blocks (±0.6 never separates them). 3d_xy lies in the horizontal plane and is seen from above. The camera only rotates and scales the whole atom, so none of this touches the physics. References: Bethe & Salpeter (1957) for ⟨r⟩, ⟨r²⟩ and d(1s–2p).
- **Motion**: the camera rocks about each state's pose, never slower than 2.8°/s and at up to 0.6·2π/13 s ≈ 17°/s of yaw (0.8 rad for 4f_xyz: ≈ 22°/s), with the pitch nodding in quadrature. Even a 1s or m = 0 state, whose shape is invariant under yaw, visibly turns because the finite sample rotates, so the frame changes within the first second (GEN-R9). Otherwise a stationary state stays still, as it does in nature; there is no resampling shimmer. Every 13 s there is a transition. The second lobe grows in, the cloud breathes between lobes at f_disp (1s–2p_z swells up and down once every 1.6 s, the 3d–4f pairs roll over once in about 3 s), and then the new orbital settles and the framing eases to its size. Every 15 s visit sees at least one transition.
- **Resize**: samples live in atomic units and don't depend on the terminal size. They are drawn in the first resize (current and next slot) and then during holds. A resize rescales the view, resizes `Dots` and recomputes every M_k. When an M_k grows past the sampled count, the resize tops up that live slot (allocation is allowed there). Timeline, samples and uniforms are kept. Zero-area sizes make plotting a no-op. ADR-0012 needs no lattice.
- **Inks** (M12): the cloud uses `Role::Phase`, `ink::phase(π/2 + arg(c_aψ_a e^{iθ_a} + c_bψ_b e^{iθ_b}))`,
  the phase of Ψ in a chosen gauge. Only the relative phase `θ_a − θ_b = ωt` is physical (it is the phase
  in |Ψ|²); a common phase is unobservable, so where one term alone is non-zero the hue is a choice of gauge.
  The gauge is `θ_a = ωT·h(t/T)`, `θ_b = θ_a − ωt` over the transition's T = 7 s from the rise start, with
  `h(x) = x²(2 − x)`, and θ = 0 in holds: the outgoing state keeps its hold colours as the rise begins
  (h′(0) = 0), the incoming one arrives in its hold colours without a jump (h(1) = h′(1) = 1), and since
  0 ≤ h′ ≤ 4/3 neither lone term's hue ever turns faster than 4|ω|/3, so the beat stays the fastest colour
  motion on screen. (The mean-energy gauge `θ_a = c_b²ωt`, `θ_b = −c_a²ωt` wound the lone terms' hues
  2.5|ω| rad away by the fall's start and back over its 2 s, at up to ~3.4|ω|.) The constant π/2 puts
  the two signs of a real state at +π/2 (level 64) and −π/2 (level 192), equally far from phase 0, so every
  theme draws the two lobes equally bright (mono greys phase by its distance from 0, RND-R10, and would
  otherwise draw the ψ > 0 lobe near black), as their equal |ψ|² demands. During a transition the hue winds
  with the beat. Each cell wears the phase of its nearest visible sparkle point (camera depth; a per-cell depth and
  ink `Grid`), so the colour follows the cloud's front surface in coherent bands instead of mixing front and
  back. The winding takes the sign of the energy step: ω = (E_b − E_a)/ħ on the slowed clock, positive going
  up the ladder and negative going down (2p_x → 1s, …), so the final state's term leads by |ω|t on the way
  down; |Ψ|² sees only cos ωt. Behind the dots is a glow, the cloud's body (the dots are its
  sparkle; M12 round 3, ORB-A12: every visible point exposes the glow, but only one in `SPARKLE = 6` is drawn as a
  phase-coloured dot, chosen by the point's fixed index so a dot never flickers, a sparse sparkle over the body
  rather than the speckle of the full sample): a screen-space exposure of the visible points at pixel resolution (one `raster::Glow`, CORE-R85),
  each point spread over a 3×3 splat (0.36 centre, 0.12 edges, 0.04 corners; the 5-tap left visible mottling at
  the brighter glow; the node through the nucleus still glows < ½ of the lobe rows) and weighted by the radial
  window (1 inside r₇₀, falling linearly to 0 at r₉₅, blended by c_b² through transitions). τ = 1.0 s in holds
  (the time-mean of a stationary state is its |ψ|²) and 0.25 s through rise, beat and fall, so the glow follows
  the slosh with a lag well under the beat period. Before toning, the exposure is blurred by a 3×3 binomial
  tent (`[1 2 1]ᵀ[1 2 1]/16`, renormalised over the in-bounds taps at the edges; interior pixels read the
  buffer directly, ORB-A11): it smooths the Monte-Carlo grain, and it spreads a point by one pixel and no
  further, so a nodal plane still reads as a dark seam. Tone-mapped by `Tone::asinh(0.5, 8)` to g; blank for
  g ≤ 0.2, else level `MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL + (HAZE_MAX − MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL)·smoothstep((g − 0.2)/0.8)` with
  `HAZE_MAX = 170`, about two-thirds of the ramp (92 before round 3), so the lobes read as smooth half-block bodies. Colour by sign:
  a per-cell balance (Σ ±w of the points in the cell, + where sin(phase) ≥ 0, decayed with the glow; one f32 a
  cell) summed over the cell (×2) and its four neighbours picks `Ramp::Aurora` (under the olive-green +π/2 dots)
  or `Ramp::Plasma` (under the lavender −π/2 dots), so each lobe glows in its own family and overlapping lobes
  split into coherent patches; in a beat the colour flips with the winding phase. The glow shows in every phase
  (no hold envelope). Dotted cells carry it as their background (`Dots::blit_over`, CORE-R84); ASCII mode draws
  it as a character ramp (see ASCII), never as a background. (A second per-sign pixel exposure was tried and took the scene past the TEST-R7 4 MiB ceiling,
  4.14 MiB at 500×150.) The nucleus stays `Accent`
  180; the label `Text` at `READOUT_LEVEL`.
- **Stylisation**: phase hues and the glow are false colour. Hues in single-term regions are gauge-fixed
  (see Inks); only the relative phase ωt is physical. The glow is the same |Ψ|² samples, exposed for ~1 s in
  screen space and smoothed by its splat and by the camera's turn (the points are fixed, so time alone does
  not smooth them); it trails the turn slightly. In a transition the exposure is short (0.25 s), so the glow trails the slosh slightly; its sign colour is the dominant sign of the cell's recent points. In a transition the glow's sign is that of sin(arg Ψ) in the chosen gauge, not a physical sign. The smooth χ sweep between states is a display choice (a coherent,
  driven-like transfer); a spontaneous decay (the loop's downward half) is not a slow coherent sweep, though
  the superposition drawn at each instant is a real state. In the mono theme a winding phase shows as a grey
  that pulses between near black (phase 0) and light grey (π), so in a transition dots near phase 0 dim below the glow; holds are two equal greys on a grey glow (the dots are sparkle over the body, not required to outshine it).
- **ASCII**: the glow's body is a light character ramp: of a cell's two glow pixels, the stronger one's level over `HAZE_MAX` picks `` ` `` `'` `~` `=` in quarters, in that pixel's ink; an empty cell stays blank. None of these is a braille dot-count fallback, so the sparse sparkle dots (`.`, `:`, `+`, `#` by dot count) still read apart from the body, and the nucleus is `o`. 2p_z is two stacked lobes, `=~'` bodies fading to a `` ` `` fringe, sprinkled with `.:+`, with the `o` nucleus in the thinner band between them. Labels use their ASCII variants.
- **Label**: right-aligned on row h−1 (GEN-R13), static strings only (GEN-R11). Hold: `3d_z²` (ASCII `3d_z2`). Transition: `2p_z → 3d_z²  Hα 656.5 nm  slowed 10¹⁵` (ASCII `2p_z -> 3d_z2  H-a 656.5 nm  slowed 1e15`), and likewise `Lyα 121.6 nm  slowed 4×10¹⁵` / `Ly-a … slowed 4e15` and `Pα 1876 nm  slowed 5×10¹⁴` / `P-a … slowed 5e14`: the one liberty the scene takes with time is stated on screen. Parts are dropped, never clipped, to fit `title::readout_room`: first the slow-down part, then the line part, then the arrow part (leaving the current state's name), and the whole label when `w < 14` or `h < 8`.
- **Perf** (update + render, `worst_frame`, 3 replays, loaded machine): M12 round 3 worst 0.96 ms at 200×50 and 2.14 ms at 500×150 (round 2: 1.02 ms, mean 0.59 ms; 2.19 ms, mean 1.78 ms (1.84 ms without the blur; a naive bounds-checked blur had cost 9.7 ms). Per frame: the v-test over ≤ 2 × 16384 f32 SoA points and the projection of ≤ 8 k visible points (the camera rotation hoisted out of the loop, `Camera::projector`); the glow's decay, splat (9 taps per visible point), per-cell sign balance, 3×3 blur and tone map over `w × 2h` pixels; the `front` grid fill (sparkle points only); `Dots` clear and blit; the ASCII glow ramp (no buffer); and slot preparation of ≤ 256 samples plus ψ pairs per 1/60 s during holds. A resize late in a hold forces synchronous completion of a 16384-point slot (≈ 2 ms in release, still < 5 ms). Memory: two SoA slots of 7 × 16384 f32 (≈ 0.9 MB), 192 KB of tables (8 samplers × 3 `InverseCdf<1024>`), the glow (one f32 and one `Ink` per pixel), the sign balance (one f32 per cell) and the `front` grid (one u32 per cell); the blur needs no scratch buffer. Live bytes at 500×150 are 3.86 MiB (TEST-R7), unchanged by round 3.
- **Acceptance**: `ORB-A1` every gallery orbital satisfies ∫R²r² dr = 1 (trapezoid, h = 0.01, r ≤ 90; 1e-6) and ∫Y² dΩ = 1 (48-point Gauss–Legendre × 96 φ, 1e-9), and distinct gallery orbitals are orthogonal (|⟨i|j⟩| < 1e-9); `ORB-A2` quadrature gives ⟨r⟩ = (3n² − l(l+1))/2 (1.5 / 5 / 10.5 / 18 a₀) and ⟨r²⟩ = n²(5n² + 1 − 3l(l+1))/2 (1e-6 relative), and 20 000 sampler draws per orbital give a mean r within 4σ of ⟨r⟩ and ⟨cos²θ⟩ within 4σ of 3/5 (2p_z) and 11/21 (3d_z²); `ORB-A3` ⟨1s|z|2p_z⟩ = 2^{15/2}/3⁵ = 0.74494 a₀ (1e-4), every consecutive gallery pair including the wrap has |⟨a|r|b⟩| equal to the Model's d_ab table (1e-3) and > 0.5 a₀, and the line wavelengths are 121.57, 656.47 and 1875.6 nm within 0.1 % and agree with the label strings as rounded, and every consecutive gallery pair (wrap included) is labelled with its own line (a pair that is not an α line shows no line part and has no beat rather than a wrong one); ΔE_Lyα/h = 2.466 PHz (0.1 %), and each line's beat is exactly ΔE/(h·S) with S the factor its label prints (ASCII `4e15` parses to S, Unicode `4×10¹⁵` / `10¹⁵` matches it): 0.6165 / 0.4567 / 0.3197 Hz (1e-3), each ≤ 0.7 Hz, decreasing with ΔE, and ≥ 0.9 cycles per 3 s beat; `ORB-A4` exact superposition: for 1s–2p_z at χ = π/4 with M_a = M_b = 40 000, the visible count is 20 000 within 4σ and the visible centroid z̄ equals 0.7449·cos ωt within 4 SE at cos ωt ∈ {1, 0, −1}, with x̄ and ȳ zero within 4 SE; with M_a = 10 000 and M_b = 40 000 the visible count is M(χ)/2 = 8 000 within 4σ; the display fade is exact: for 1s–2p_z at χ = π/4, cos ωt = 1 and M_a = M_b = 40 000 with the c_b² = ½ window, the displayed count matches `(M/2)∫τ|Ψ|²` = 10 000·(∫τR_1s²r² dr + ∫τR_2p²r² dr) within 4σ and z̄ matches `(∫τR_1sR_2p r³ dr)/√3` over half that bracket within 4 SE (both by quadrature; per-slot windows missed by 23σ and 4.5 SE); after a slot switch the live slot carries its new partner's ψ (at the 2p_z → 3d_z² beat z̄ = ±⟨2p_z|z|3d_z²⟩ = ±2.452 a₀ within 4 SE); `ORB-A5` no pops: the displayed set is identical at the end of each phase and the start of the next (hold→rise and fall→hold, including the slot switch), and over a full 1s → 2p_z transition at 200×50 (dt = 1/60) no step toggles more than 6 % of the sampler's visible count (the expected bound is sin 2χ·(ω dt/2)·∫|ψ_aψ_b| d³r ≤ 1.35 % of M(χ), i.e. 2.7 % of the visible count at Lyα's 0.62 Hz with ∫|ψ_1sψ_2p| = 0.419, plus ≤ 2 % from χ drift; measured 4.7 %), nor more than 7 % of the displayed count, which adds the drift of the fade window as it widens 3× (measured 5.1 %); `ORB-A6` the Timeline visits 1s, 2p_z, 3d_z², 4f_z³, 3d_xz, 4f_xyz, 3d_xy, 2p_x in that order with 6 / 2 / 3 / 2 s phases and a 104 s loop, and the label is pure ASCII in ASCII mode, complete at 80×24 (slow-down included), right-aligned on row h−1 within `title::readout_room` chars: at h = 12 the slow-down part needs w ≥ 80, the line part w ≥ 54 beside the phenomenon and w ≥ 64 once the full title shows (from w = 55), and 3d_xz → 4f_xyz w ≥ 34 (33 in Unicode), and it is absent when `w < 14` or `h < 8`; over one loop at dt = 0.25 no dot lands on the label row (80×24, 40×12, 14×8); `ORB-A7` composition at 80×24 (seed 42; the first three at yaw 0.3, pitch 0.15): in the 1s hold ≤ 5 % of lit cells are full 8-dot cells; in the 4f_z³ hold ≥ 16 rows hold ≥ 3 lit cells; in the 2p_z hold the nucleus row (the nodal plane) has ≤ 60 % of the lit cells of the fullest row. With each hold's own camera swept over a whole rock cycle: 3d_xy spans ≥ 0.6 of 4f_z³'s rows, and a ring at 0.5ρ_k about the nucleus meets ≥ 4 sign runs for 3d_xy and 3d_xz (clovers); the 2p_x node is judged on the glow, the cloud's body (the sparse dots are too noisy to measure a gap by): the nucleus column's summed glow level is ≤ 40 % of the fullest lobe column's within ±3, and 2p_x is broadside (yaw 0) at mid-hold; the nucleus glyph shows through the 2p_z node in ≥ 80 % of frames; the 4f_xyz nucleus row holds ≤ 50 % of the fullest row's lit cells; its centre band (nucleus column ± 1) empties to ≤ 15 % of the fullest lobe column for ≥ 16 of 52 clocks; and at its mid-hold yaw is π/2 within 0.02 and the nucleus column holds ≤ 60 % of the fullest lobe column (four blocks, not two slabs), with the 104 s loop a whole number of rock cycles. Every pose's camera turns at ≥ 2°/s throughout. No hold leaves more than 1 lit cell per frame beyond 1.25ρ_k (the far dust fades out). Over one loop at dt = 0.1 (80×24 and 200×50) no dot reaches the outermost stage rows in a hold or beat, and the χ ramps leave ≤ 0.1 cells per frame on them. `ORB-A8` the phase is `π/2 + arg(c_aψ_a e^{iθ_a} + c_bψ_b e^{iθ_b})` with `gauge(ω, t) = (θ_a, θ_b)`: in a hold (θ = 0), at the rise start and, for the incoming state, at the fall's end it is +π/2 or −π/2 (1e-9) by sign; at every time of a transition `θ_a − θ_b = ωt` (1e-12), the ψ_a-only and ψ_b-only phases differ by ωt, and where ψ_a = ψ_b at χ = π/4 the phase is their mean (a half turn on while cos(ωt/2) < 0); stepping every transition of the loop at 80×24 (dt = 1/60), the ψ_a-only and ψ_b-only hues (where that term's amplitude is non-zero) never turn faster than 1.5|ω|, and the next hold's hue is reached without a jump. ω = (E_b − E_a)/ħ is signed (`beat_omega(n_a, n_b)` = ±2π·`beat_hz`, + for n_b > n_a), and stepping the atom 30 frames into the 2p_x → 1s beat the ψ_b term leads the ψ_a term by |ωt| (lags in 1s → 2p_z). In the 2p_z hold at 80×24 every cloud cell is a Phase cell at level 64 or 192 and each sign lights > 20 cells; 20 frames into the 1s → 2p_z beat ≥ 25 % of cloud cells are off those two levels. Levels 64 and 192 are the same grey in mono, and both show (non-zero luma) in astro and mono; the dots are sparkle over the glow, not required to outshine it (ORB-A12). Early in the 3d_z² → 4f_z³ beat every dotted cell wears the phase of the nearest visible sparkle point projected into it (> 50 cells checked). `ORB-A9` glow: in the 4f_z³ hold at 80×24, once exposed (1 s + 30 frames), ≥ 2/3 of dotted cells have a glow background (`Ramp::Aurora` or `Ramp::Plasma`); no glow ink or background exceeds `HAZE_MAX` (170); the four stage corners stay blank and the label row takes no glow; in ASCII mode the frame is pure ASCII, no cell has a glow background, and the glow is drawn as its character ramp (every glow-ink cell is a backtick, `'`, `~` or `=`, > 50 such cells at 80×24). In the 2p_z hold at 80×24 the nucleus row's summed glow is < ½ of the brighter lobe row 3 rows above or below it, and the glow holds > 20 distinct levels. In the 2p_z hold at 200×50 (3 s in), down each of the 9 pixel columns through the nucleus ± 4, from the first glowing pixel of the upper lobe to its brightest, no pixel is more than `HAZE_MAX/2` (85) levels above the one before it, and ≥ 7 of the columns climb over ≥ 3 pixels. `ORB-A10` in the 2p_z hold at 120×40 (2 s in) the upper lobe's glow pixels are > 200 and > 20× more `Aurora` than `Plasma`, the lower lobe's > 200 and > 20× more `Plasma`; over one loop at 80×24 (dt = 0.05) after the first second every frame has > 20 glowing pixels, holds and transitions alike, and each sign's ramp leads (> 10 pixels) in more than a quarter of the beat frames. `ORB-A11` the glow blur: a single exposed pixel of 16 blurs to 4 in itself, 2 in an edge neighbour, 1 in a corner and 0 two pixels away; a uniform exposure is unchanged, corners and edges included; the kernel sums to 16. `ORB-A12` *(M12 round 3)* the glow is the body and the dots a sparse sparkle: `HAZE_MAX ≥ 160` and `SPARKLE ≥ 3` (compile-time), and over a whole loop at 80×24 (dt = 0.1; > 10 000 glowing cells, a cell glowing when its background or its drawn glyph is a glow ink) fewer than 60 % of glowing cells carry a dot.

### globular_cluster  (Deep Cosmos, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: an ancient globular cluster seen through a slowly turning telescope. A blazing, unresolved core thins into a sparse halo of single stars, with a handful of orange giants glinting among them. Every visible halo star is a live gravitating body or one of the three faint companions riding with it: the swarm breathes, the unresolved glow shifts with the bodies as they cross the core, and the camera's slow turn gives the halo depth through parallax. It is calm, dense and three-dimensional, the stellar counterpart to the flat `starfield` and the disk-shaped `galaxy_collision`.
- **Model**:
  - **Units and softening**: direct N-body in Hénon units (`G = M = 1`, virial radius 1, `E ≈ −1/4`), equal masses `m = 1/N`, Plummer softening `Φ_ij = −G m²/√(r² + ε²)` with `ε = 0.05` (0.085 of the Plummer scale radius `a = 3π/16`).
  - **Forces and integration**: an O(N²/2) Newton-3 symmetric pair loop, so `Σ m a = 0` to rounding. `ode::verlet_integrate` (KDK leapfrog, CORE-R44) runs with `h_max = 1/128` N-body units at a rate of `0.4` N-body units per simulated second: one force evaluation per frame at `dt = 1/60`, 13 at `dt = 0.25`. The crossing time `t_cr = 2√2` is 7.1 s.
  - **ICs**: Plummer sphere `ρ ∝ (1 + r²/a²)^{−5/2}`, sampled per Aarseth, Hénon & Wielen (1974) in `G = M = a = 1`:
    - Radius `r = (X₁^{−2/3} − 1)^{−1/2}` with mass cut `X₁ < 0.999` (so `r ≤ 38.7 a`).
    - Isotropic directions (`z = 1 − 2u`, `φ = 2πu`).
    - Speed `v = q·√2(1 + r²)^{−1/4}`, with `q` from `g(q) = q²(1 − q²)^{7/2}` by rejection under the bound 0.1 (the maximum is 0.0923).
    - COM position and velocity subtracted.
    - Positions scaled so the unsoftened `W = −1/2` (then `a ≈ 3π/16` and `r_h ≈ 0.769`).
    - The density centre moved to the origin: 4 passes that each subtract the mean position of the bodies inside the half-mass radius. The heavy tail (`r_rms ≈ 3.0 a` with the 0.999 cut) puts the COM ≈ `r_rms/√N` off the body core (0.08 units at N = 500, 0.22 at N = 64). The view and the glow are centred on the origin, so without this the core would sit off the glow and swing around it as the camera turns. It is a translation, so `W` and the dynamics are unchanged, and the momentum stays zero, so the cluster does not drift.
    - Velocities scaled so `2K = |W_ε|` (virial equilibrium in the softened potential), giving `E ≈ −0.248`.
  - **N**: the largest bucket in `{64, 96, 128, 192, 256, 384, 500}` that is `≤ max(area/5, 64)`. 80×24 gives 384, and 200×50 and anything larger gives 500 (the ADR-0012 cap).
  - **Unresolved light**: each body stands for ~10³ real stars, so 500 bodies cannot fill a braille core; their light is smoothed into a glow (see Inks) whose Plummer part uses `a_g = r_h/1.3048`, where `r_h` is the body system's 3-D half-mass radius about the origin, smoothed with `τ = 4 s` and reset at every reseed, so the glow follows the real core contraction.
  - **Luminosity tags** (visual only): the first `g_n = clamp(N/50, 3, 10)` bodies (sample order is random) are red giants, and every other body gets a colour class from a salt hash (see Inks). The dynamics stay equal-mass, so there is no mass segregation.
- **Motion**:
  - Bodies orbit in the self-consistent potential: the core crosses in about 2 s and halo stars drift slowly.
  - `geom::Camera` yaws at `2π/48 s` *(changed; was 90 s)*, with pitch `0.30 + 0.15·sin(2πt/67 s)` and perspective `distance = 10` units, so near-side stars slide against far-side ones and the halo visibly swings past the core within a minute. The camera alone moves an `r = 1.5` star ≈ 7.5 dots/s at 200×50.
  - Motion streaks *(new)*: every coloured (`Blackbody`) star outside the glow (`g < 0.12`) trails a faint `Star` 26 braille line back to where it was 0.4 N-body units (≈ 1 s) ago along its velocity. **Stylised**: a long exposure; the eye sees no trails.
  - A looping `Timeline`: `evolve` 120 s (48 N-body units ≈ 17 t_cr). Spitzer's `t_rh ≈ 0.138 N r_h^{3/2}/ln(0.4N)` gives 8.8 units (22 s) at N = 500; softening at ε = 0.05 cuts ln Λ to ≈ ln(r_h/ε) ≈ 2.7, so a cycle spans ≈ 3–5.5 t_rh. Then a 1.5 s `fade`, a fresh Plummer sample from `ctx.rng`, and a 1.5 s `rise`: 123 s per loop.
  - Within a cycle the core contracts slightly and the odd star escapes. Core collapse needs ≈ 15 t_rh and is never reached. The reseed keeps the halo populated and bounds the accumulated energy error.
- **Resize** (ADR-0012 analogue, with N as the cap):
  - Within the same N bucket a resize only changes the view: `scale = draw::fit_radius(size, 0.92)/2.2` rows per unit, so `R = 2.2` units (≈ 93 % of the projected mass) fills the frame, and `Dots` is resized. Pos/vel are untouched.
  - On a bucket change, shrinking keeps the first N′ bodies (an unbiased subsample). Growing appends fresh AHW samples, scaled by the same length and velocity factors as the build sample. Then `m = 1/N′`, the momentum is re-zeroed (the COM position is not re-centred: that would break GC-A6's bit-identity and jump the view; the subsample's small COM offset, ≈ `r_rms/√N′`, persists until the next reseed), and velocities are re-virialised to `2K = |W_ε|` (one O(N²) pass). The cluster never visibly resets while you drag the window.
- **Inks**:
  - **Unresolved light**: each body (weighted by its near-eye fade `f_eye`) is deposited bilinearly on a fixed 64 × 64 lattice over the model plane `[−2, 2]²` at the origin's depth (capped and size-independent, ADR-0012) and blurred by three 5-wide box passes (σ ≈ 0.15 units, a quarter of the Plummer scale radius), in units of the Plummer central surface density `1/(π a_g²)`. The glow is `g = w·g_bodies + (1 − w)·(1 + R²/a_g²)⁻²` with `w = 0.5/(1 + R²/a_g²)`, `R` measured from the light's centre (its light²-weighted centroid): the bodies supply half the core's light, so it shifts and breathes at each crossing, and the smooth Plummer profile supplies the round outskirts, where 500 bodies are too few to trace a smooth edge. A soft knee `g²/(g + 0.04)` (no pedestal cut) keeps lone halo bodies from smudging the sky; level `Tone::asinh(0.3, 0.9)` of `(knee·b)^1.25`, toned once per lattice cell per frame and interpolated, in `Ramp(Starlight)` pixels; levels below 16 dissolve (CORE-R89).
  - **Companions**: each body carries 3 faint dots (`Star` 40) at fixed hashed 3-D offsets within 0.2 model units, drawn where the glow is below level 150: the halo resolves into stars that orbit, turn (parallax) and fade with their body.
  - **Streaks**: `Star` 26 braille lines behind the coloured halo stars (Motion).
  - **Stars** (`Dots::blit_over` the glow; colour class from a salt hash, a visual tag: the dynamics stay equal-mass): 76 % turn-off and subgiant stars as one `Star` 140 dot; 17 % red-giant branch (4300–4900 K) and 7 % blue horizontal branch (9000–12 000 K, CORE-R88) as 2 × 2-dot `Blackbody` stars. The first `g_n` bodies are red giants: a `✦` in `Blackbody` 3900 K (over the glow's `cell_bg`) where the glow is below 0.25 of the centre's, an ordinary 3900 K star inside. A body is lost in the core's glare where `g ≥ 0.25·(1 + 1.5u)` (`u` its own hash), so the core has no hard rim of stars; a fade thins the stars in a fixed hashed order (a body is drawn iff its hash is below `b·f_eye(z)`).
  - `f_eye = 1 − smoothstep((z − 2.5)/(10/3 − 2.5))` fades near-eye stars out between depth `z = 2.5` and `z = 10/3`: the eye at distance 10 sits inside the far halo (`r ≤ 38.7 a ≈ 23` units), so a near-side halo star would be magnified without bound and streak across the screen like a meteor.
- **ASCII**: glow pixel pairs as `#`/`+`/`:`/`.` by the `stronger` pixel's level (none below 40), stars via the braille fallback, giants as `*`.
- **Readout** (GEN-R13): right-aligned on the title row at `READOUT_LEVEL`, `N=500 3 crossings` — the body count and the whole crossing times (`t_cr = 7.1 s`, `1 crossing` in the singular) since the last reseed, so it changes only every 7.1 s; before the first crossing the clause is left out. The longest form that fits `title::readout_room` is used (`N=500`, else nothing), written into a stack `StackText` without allocating.
- **Stylisation**: the halo's coloured stars trail 1 s long-exposure streaks; each body stands for ~10³ stars: its light is smoothed on a 0.15-unit scale and it carries three faint companion dots; the unresolved glow is half the bodies' light and half a smooth Plummer profile, so its outline stays round with only 500 bodies; the glow has no colour gradient (a globular's integrated B − V ≈ 0.7 is nearly uniform), and the Starlight ramp is neutral grey while faint.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 with N = 500 a frame costs ≈ 0.5 ms.
  - Force: 124,750 pairs × (1 sqrt + 1 div + ~20 flops) ≈ 0.25 ms scalar.
  - Projection: 500 × 4 `Camera::project` (the body and its companions).
  - Glow: 500 bilinear deposits, 3 × 2 box passes over 64², 4096 tone evaluations and a bilinear sample per pixel inside the lattice's reach.
  - `dt = 0.25` gives 13 substeps (≈ 13× the force cost, a few ms). Only the GEN-R4 contract reaches it: the runtime feeds fixed 1/60 steps (ADR-0003), and GEN-R8 covers `update(1/60)`.
  - Pos/vel/acc `Vec<f64>`s have capacity 1500 from `build`, the cluster's half-mass scratch has capacity 500, and the lattice and its blur scratch are fixed at 64². The reseed at 121.5 s refills these in place (GC-A6), so update and render do not allocate.
  - Debug note: the contract suite's 600-step 200×50 run is ≈ 75 M pair evaluations, ≈ 1.5–2.5 s unoptimised (flagged, like `molecular_dynamics`).
- **Acceptance**: `GC-A1` the AHW sampler (`G = M = a = 1`, cut 0.999): a seeded 4000-star sample has KS distance < 0.03 from the truncated Plummer mass profile `M(r) = r³/(1 + r²)^{3/2}/0.999`, median radius within 4 % of `1.3048 a`, the speed fractions `q = v/v_esc(r)` (`v_esc = √2(1 + r²)^{−1/4}`) all in `[0, 1)` with KS distance < 0.03 from the CDF of `g(q) ∝ q²(1 − q²)^{7/2}` (the regularised incomplete beta `I_{q²}(3/2, 9/2)`, integrated numerically), while exponents 5/2 and 9/2 give > 0.03, and the raw N = 1500 virial ratio `2K/|W|` in `[0.9, 1.1]`; `GC-A2` after `plummer` for seeds 1–6 and N = 64, 256, 500, the density centre (mean position inside the half-mass radius) lies within 0.03 units of the origin and `|Σ m v| < 1e-14`; for N = 256 the unsoftened `W = −1/2` within 1e-12, `2K = |W_ε|` within 1e-12, and `E = −1/4` within 0.01; `GC-A3` for N = 64 seeded, the Newton-3 pair loop equals a naive per-star O(N²) sum within 1e-12 relative, `|Σ m a| < 1e-14`, each acceleration equals `−∇Φ_ε/m` by central differences within 1e-6 relative, and two coincident stars give a finite, zero mutual force; `GC-A4` for N = 128 seeded, `ε = 0.05`, `h = 1/128`, 10 N-body units (1280 steps, ≈ 10 M pair evaluations): `|Σ m v| < 1e-14` throughout and max `|ΔE/E₀| < 5e-4` (measured ≤ 4.6e-5); `GC-A5` in the same run, `r_h(t)/r_h(0) ∈ [0.7, 1.3]` at every 0.5-unit sample with time mean in `[0.9, 1.1]`, and the time-mean `2K/|W_ε|` is in `[0.9, 1.1]`; `GC-A6` N follows the bucket rule (80×24 ⇒ 384, 200×50 ⇒ 500, 1×1 and 0×0 ⇒ 64, never > 500), a resize within a bucket leaves pos/vel bit-identical, and a bucket change keeps the first `min(N, N′)` positions bit-identical while restoring `|Σ m v| < 1e-14` and `2K = |W_ε|` within 1e-12, and the reseed at 200×50 draws a fresh N = 500 sample into pos/vel/acc/scratch without changing their capacities; `GC-A7` at 200×50 (seed 42, t = 0, Unicode): every glow pixel is `Ramp(Starlight)` (no hue gradient anywhere), > 500 are lit, and every lit pixel next to the empty sky is below level 90 (no rim); the centre pixel is ≥ 180 and the pixel at `R = a_g` is dimmer; every star cell is `Star` or `Blackbody` 3800–12 500 K (> 150 star cells, > 20 coloured); at most `g_n` giants (`g_n` = 10, 7, 3 for N = 500, 384, 64), none where the glow is ≥ 0.5 of the centre's; `f_eye` is 1 up to `z = 2.5`, strictly decreasing, and 0 from `z = 10/3`; moving every body by +0.4 model units moves the glow's level-weighted centroid by > 3 pixels (the glow is the bodies' light). At 120×40, t = 1, 10 and 30 s, the floor contour's radius from that centroid varies ≤ 1.3× over 32 rays, and along every ray the light falls from 4× the floor to nothing over ≥ 3 px. At 120×40, t = 10 s, > 40 cells hold grain alone, and every one lies within a companion's reach of a projected body; `GC-A8` over 4000 bodies the class fractions are within 3 % of 76/17/7 %, every class temperature is in its range, and the hash is a pure function of `(salt, k, stream)`; `GC-A9` the readout reads `N=384 4 crossings` at age 4.5 `t_cr` (80×24), `N=384 1 crossing` at 1.99 and `N=384` before the first crossing; at 40×12 the full form, at 30×10 `N=384`, at 8×24 nothing, never longer than `readout_room`; at 80×24 after 8 s row 23 ends with ` N=384 1 crossing ` at `READOUT_LEVEL`; `GC-A10` *(new)*: the telescope turns once in ≤ 60 s; at 120×40 (seed 42) more than half the cells lit in either frame differ between 5 s and 30 s; with the bodies' velocities the dot layer holds > 20 more lit dots than with them zeroed (the streaks).

### ising_model  (States of Matter, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: the ferromagnetic phase transition as a living mosaic. A quench turns static into light and dark domains, ivory and steel blue, that swell, round off and straighten. Reheating melts them into a dim boil, and a slow cool through the critical point leaves clusters of every size breathing (critical fluctuations, the magnet's analogue of critical opalescence). Order lights up: aligned regions glow and disorder sinks toward the background, so the eye goes to the shrinking bubbles and the straightening walls: domains read as lit pillows with dark seams. A second act (M12) scrambles the magnet into an exact 50/50 mixture and runs spin-exchange (Kawasaki) dynamics at 0.6 T_c; swaps conserve M, so the mixture un-mixes into a labyrinth of bevelled ridges that slowly ripens, and the readout says `M fixed` instead of measuring |m|. A thermometer on the label chip marks T_c.
- **Model**: 2-D Ising `H = −J Σ⟨ij⟩ sᵢsⱼ`, `sᵢ = ±1`, `J = k_B = 1`, zero field, periodic (torus) boundaries. The lattice is capped: `L = grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h), 16 384, 1.25)` in square pixel units, so 200×50 → 178×91 (≈ 1.1 px per spin) and 80×24 → 80×48 (below the cap the lattice is the pixel grid itself). Kinetics are single-spin Glauber heat-bath (Glauber 1963), random-sequential, with one `u64` per attempt from a scene-local SplitMix64 seeded once from `ctx.rng` at the first resize (GEN-R7). Bits 32–47 and 48–63 pick `x = (r₁₆·Lw)>>16` and `y = (r₁₆·Lh)>>16` (≤ 0.3 % non-uniform, harmless because every heat-bath move leaves the Boltzmann distribution invariant). The local field is `h = Σ_nn sⱼ ∈ {−4,−2,0,2,4}`; set `sᵢ = +1` iff the low 32 bits are `< thr[h]`, where `thr = round(p₊·2³²)` as u64 (so `p = 0, 1` are exact) and `p₊(h) = 1/(1 + e^{−2h/T})`. The 5-entry table is rebuilt once per update with T clamped to `[0.05, 100]` (NaN ⇒ T_c). This is the same model-A equilibrium, universality and coarsening class as Metropolis; it replaces checkerboard Metropolis because deterministic sublattice sweeps flip every ΔE = 0 spin at high T (a period-2 strobe) and because random-sequential updates give smooth fractional sweeps per frame at any lattice size. The rate is `max(60·N/16 384, 1)` Monte Carlo sweeps (MCS) per simulated second (N = lattice sites; one MCS per 1/60 s step at the cap), realised as `⌊acc⌋` attempts from an accumulator gaining `rate·N·dt`. Domain size relative to the lattice scales as `√(MCS/N)`, so coarsening looks the same at every terminal size. Temperature is in units of J/k_B with `T_c = 2/ln(1+√2) = 2.269 185 314` (Onsager 1944). References: `m₀(T) = (1 − sinh(2/T)⁻⁴)^{1/8}` below T_c and 0 above (Yang 1952); exact energy per spin `e(T) = −coth 2β·[1 + (2/π)·k′·K(k)]` with `k′ = 2 tanh² 2β − 1` (signed complementary modulus, computed directly to avoid cancellation), `K = π/(2·AGM(1, |k′|))`, and the product `k′·K` taken as its limit 0 when `k′ = 0`, so `e(T_c) = −√2`. T/T_c follows a looping `Timeline` of 90 s: `coarsen` 20 s at 0.60 → `reheat` 8 s, smoothstep 0.60→1.25 → `hot` 5 s at 1.25 (ξ ≈ 2–3, a boil rather than a per-frame fizz) → `cool` 15 s, `1 + 0.25(1−p)²` (eases into T_c so most of the phase sits near criticality) → `critical` 8 s at 1.00 → `quench` 2 s, smoothstep 1.00→0.60 → `spinodal` 32 s at 0.60 (the Kawasaki act, below); it is continuous at every boundary. The first resize seeds a T = ∞ state (i.i.d. ±1), so t = 0 is the textbook quench. Magnetisation `M = Σs` is tracked incrementally and recomputed on resize.
- **Motion**: within 1 s (≈ 60 MCS at the cap) static condenses into domains 10–20 spins across; coarsening follows `ℓ ∝ t^{1/2}` (Allen & Cahn 1979; Bray 1994). Over `coarsen`, about 1200 MCS grow domains to lattice scale: bubbles shrink and vanish, walls straighten, and the phase often ends in a few domains or one stripe (≈ 1/3 of torus quenches end in stripes). In `reheat`, walls roughen and thermal clusters eat the domains as T crosses T_c. `hot` is a dim boil of small clusters. In `cool`, clusters grow fractal and slow down (critical slowing, z ≈ 2.17; Nightingale & Blöte 1996). `critical` breathes at all scales up to `t^{1/z}`. `quench` leads into the Kawasaki act, and the re-quench after it into `coarsen`, so every pass of `coarsen` starts from noise.
- **Resize**: if `lattice_for` returns the same lattice, only the display maps are rebuilt (cell-centre nearest-index tables px→lattice, `Vec<u16>`, allocated in `resize`) and the spins are untouched. A different lattice (bucket change, or any change below the cap) triggers `grid::resample_nearest` into a scratch grid and a swap (ADR-0012); spins stay exactly ±1 and M is recomputed. Above the cap the lattice-to-pixel ratio is not an integer (200×50: 178×91 → 200×100 px), so about every 8th pixel column and 11th pixel row repeats a lattice line; cell-centre sampling spreads the repeats evenly, and the resulting ±1 px wall jitter is accepted (an integer ratio would halve the lattice). `resize` never draws from `ctx.rng` after the first seeding. T comes from scene time, so a resize never desyncs the schedule.
- **Spinodal act (M12)**: after the Glauber act, `PHASES` gains `("spinodal", 32 s)` at index 6, so the loop is 90 s; T/T_c is 0.60 throughout, continuous with the quench before it and the `coarsen` after it. *Scramble*: on entering `spinodal` the lattice is set to exactly ⌊N/2⌋ down spins, Fisher–Yates shuffled with the scene SplitMix64 (no extra draws from `ctx.rng`). *Kawasaki attempt* (Kawasaki 1966): each attempt draws one u64; bits 32–47 and 48–63 pick the site as for Glauber, bits 30–31 one of its four neighbours. An antiparallel pair swaps when the low 30 bits are below `thr[(ΔE + 12)/4]`, with `ΔE = 2 s_i (h_i′ − h_j′)` and fields that exclude the partner's own spin. `thr` is the heat-bath rule `p = 1/(1 + e^{ΔE/T})` stored as `round(p·2³⁰)`, the same function as the Glauber `p₊` with `h = −ΔE/2`, so the same clamps apply. Swaps conserve M, so the mixture cannot order into one colour: it un-mixes into a labyrinth that slowly ripens (`ℓ ∝ t^{1/3}`, spinodal decomposition). *Rate*: swaps run `swap_speedup(local)` times the Glauber sweep rate, ramping linearly from 1× at the start of the act to 2× at 4 s: conserved coarsening is slower than non-conserved, while a fresh mixture, where nearly every attempt is a costly antiparallel pair, de-mixes within a few sweeps anyway (3× cost frame budget for little visible gain). *Re-quench*: on leaving `spinodal` the spins are refilled in place with independent ±1 values from the scene SplitMix64, so every pass of the Glauber act starts from T = ∞, not from the labyrinth. Lattice cap, torus, Glauber kinetics and resize rules are unchanged.
- **Stylisation (stated)**: *false colour* on black (M14, ADR-0017): down spins are the terminal's black and up spins a neutral grey, a light/dark pair readable without hue (colour-blind safe and Mono-proof). The model is symmetric under s → −s; only the drawing is not.
- **Inks** (M14, ADR-0017; replaces the M12 chamfer shading and the Ice fill): the lattice is the art, one half-block pixel per spin (nearest above the cap). Down spins are black. Up spins are neutral grey (`Role::Text`; Starlight read khaki): interiors a flat dim 20, and wall spins (an antiparallel non-speck neighbour, `Lattice::classify`) by their aligned count 0–3 at 8, 14, 110, 200, so straight domain walls are the bright 1-px lines and ragged, hot ones stay dim. Thermal specks (clusters under 6 spins) draw as interior flecks and seed no wall. The chamfer depth pass is gone.
- **ASCII** (M14): the brighter pixel of a cell decides: walls ≥ 120 `#`, interiors `+`, dimmer spins `:`, down spins blank.
- **Label**: top-left (col 1, row 0) chip `T/Tc 0.93  |m| 0.71` in `Text` 110; during `spinodal` it is `T/Tc 0.60  M fixed` (swaps pin M). No thermometer (M14, ADR-0017).
- **Captions** (GEN-R12): `meta.captions = ["spins align", "spins swap"]`; `caption()` is `spins swap` during `spinodal`. `meta.title` stays `Magnetic domains · spins align`.
- **Perf** (200×50, step + render, 120 s, the four M12 matter scenes in one release run, load ≈ 30 on 14 cores): mean 0.77 ms, heaviest 1-s window median 1.27 ms (criterion frame mean 0.91 ms at load 22–40). Worst single frame (`worst_frame`, M12 round 2): 0.97 ms at 200×50, 1.19 ms at 500×150. The worst phase is the Kawasaki act just after the speed-up reaches 2× (62–67 s), while the mixture is still fine-grained: a 0.73 ms margin to the 2 ms limit under load, above the ~1 ms aim; to be re-measured on a quiet machine. Worst single frame (`examples/worst_frame`, 180 s, fastest of 3 replays per frame, load ≈ 27–33 on 14 cores, M12 round 2): 1.23 ms at 148 s, under the 2 ms limit. The costs are the swaps (∝ speed-up), classify and the chamfer. Live bytes at 500×150 are 1.54 MiB.
- **Acceptance**: `ISING-A1` for h ∈ {−4,−2,0,2,4} and T ∈ {0.5, 1, T_c, 3, 10}, `p₊(h)/p₊(−h) = e^{2h/T}` within 1e-12 relative (detailed balance against Boltzmann), `p₊(h) + p₊(−h) = 1` within 1e-15, `p₊(0) = ½` and `thr(0) = 2³¹` exactly, thresholds are non-decreasing in h, and T ∈ {0, −1, NaN, ±∞} gives probabilities in [0, 1] with no NaN; `ISING-A2` `T_c = 2.269185314` (1e-9), `m₀(2.0) = 0.911319`, `m₀(1.5) = 0.986500`, `e(2.0) = −1.745565`, `e(3.0) = −0.817310` (all 1e-6), `e(T_c) = −√2` (1e-9) and `|e(T_c ± 1e-6) + √2| < 1e-3`, m₀ = 0 for T ≥ T_c, m₀ is non-increasing on (0, T_c) and strictly decreasing on [1, T_c); `ISING-A3` on a seeded 32² torus, from a cold start at T = 2.0 after 1000 MCS the means over the next 3000 MCS (one sample per MCS) are ⟨|m|⟩ = 0.9113 ± 0.015 and ⟨e⟩ = −1.7456 ± 0.015, and from a random start at T = 3.0 they are ⟨|m|⟩ < 0.2 and ⟨e⟩ = −0.8173 ± 0.015; `ISING-A4` on a seeded 192² random start at T = 0.6·T_c the excess wall density `ρ − ρ_eq` (ρ = fraction of antiparallel bonds, `ρ_eq = (1 + e/2)/2 = 0.006678`) strictly decreases across 24, 48, 96 and 192 MCS and its least-squares log–log slope over those four points lies in [−0.62, −0.32] (Allen–Cahn −½); `ISING-A5` spins stay in {−1, +1} and the tracked M equals Σs after any sequence of updates (dt ∈ [0, 0.25]) and resizes including 1×1, a resize between two displays in the same `lattice_for` bucket at or above the cap leaves the lattice bit-identical, a bucket change makes every new site equal to its `resample_nearest` source, and 60 updates of 1/60 s perform `rate·N` attempts within ±1 with `rate = max(60·N/16 384, 1)`; `ISING-A6` T/T_c(t) is 0.60 at t = 0, 10 and 70 s, 1.25 at 30 s and 1.00 at 50 s, is periodic with period 90 s and changes by ≤ 0.01 per 1/60 s step over a whole loop; at 80×24 the wall density goes from < 0.3 (the labyrinth at 89.9 s) to > 0.4 on the first frame after 90 s (the re-quench); the label for (0.93, 0.71) is exactly `T/Tc 0.93  |m| 0.71`, is `T/Tc 0.93` for widths 12–23, and is absent below 12 columns or 4 rows; `ISING-A7` for T ∈ {0.5, 1, 0.6 T_c, 3} the swap table satisfies `p(ΔE) + p(−ΔE) = 1` within 2·2⁻³⁰ and each entry is within 2⁻³⁰ of `1/(1 + e^{ΔE/T})`, `thr[3] = 2²⁹`, and T ∈ {0, NaN, ∞} stays in `[0, 2³⁰]`; `swap_speedup` is 1 at 0 s, 1.5 at 2 s and 2 from 4 s on; a seeded 96×64 scrambled lattice has exactly N/2 down spins; under 4 × 200 MCS of swaps at 0.6 T_c, M never changes, the tracked M equals Σs, the wall density strictly decreases and ends below 0.2; a 1-wide lattice never swaps and never panics; in the scene at 80×24 the caption turns to `spins swap` at 58 s, M is then ≤ 1 in magnitude and constant through the act, and the caption returns to `spins align` on the first frame at or after 90 s; `ISING-A8` (M14) down spins are blank, up interiors and specks are `Text` 20, wall levels rise strictly with the aligned count to ≥ 180 at three (more than twice the interior); and the classifier fixture: a lone flipped spin and a 2×2 speck are specks and leave their neighbours interior, a 2×3 cluster's corner is a wall of class 2, a straight wall is class 3 on both sides and the torus wraps; and the hot boil at s = 30 is at most 0.65 of the mean level of the coarsened domains at s = 20 (200×50); `ISING-A9` at 80×24 the ASCII top row starts ` T/Tc 0.60  |m| 0.` with no thermometer (`-|-` or `=`), and at 62 s (the Kawasaki act) it starts ` T/Tc 0.60  M fixed `; `ISING-A10` down is black and the up interior at least level 15, and the role is not an HAlpha, Aurora or Ember ramp (no red/green pair, no amber).

### liesegang_rings  (Reactions, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: the rhythm of periodic precipitation. A rack of gel columns stands side by side, each with its outer electrolyte poured on top. In each gel a faint supersaturation haze sinks and, every few seconds, snaps into a bright precipitate band. Bands grow wider, brighter and farther apart with depth (Jablczynski's spacing law). Each tube keeps its own tempo: the concentrated one packs bands tight (forming its first band later and deeper), the dilute one wide and slow, so the Matalon–Packter law reads as a visual polyrhythm. Then a redissolution front wipes the bands top-down, and the second act is Liesegang's own 1896 set-up: a drop of silver nitrate in the centre of a dichromate-loaded gel dish grows concentric rings that snap into place outward from the drop, with its live spacing ratio in the header. ("Liesegang rings" names the phenomenon; in tubes it forms bands.) Both acts use `Ramp::Cobalt` (ultramarine → cobalt → silver-blue), a cool mineral precipitate that brightens towards silver and keeps the scene apart from the gallery's amber (`Ember`) and crimson (`HAlpha`) scenes and from its neighbours' magenta (`gray_scott`) and red-to-blue (`belousov_zhabotinsky`); the yellow dichromate gel is drawn black so the bands read. **Stylisation (stated)**: false colour. Real silver dichromate/chromate precipitate is ruby to red-brown and the densest bands are the darkest; here lightness encodes the amount of precipitate in a cobalt-to-silver ramp, which keeps the scene off the gallery's crimson and amber. The scene opens with bands already forming. The title is `Liesegang rings · form in bursts`; the captions are `bands in tubes` (rack) and `a silver drop` (dish).
- **Model**: per tube, a 1-D reaction–diffusion system with nucleation-and-growth precipitation. The ingredients are Ostwald's (1897) supersaturation theory, the nucleation threshold of Keller & Rubinow (1981) and the aggregation threshold of Chopard, Luthi & Droz (1994). The reactions are A + B → C (soluble product, idealised 1:1) and C → D (immobile precipitate), as in Liesegang's 1896 experiment of AgNO₃ diffusing into K₂Cr₂O₇-loaded gelatin to precipitate silver dichromate. The equations are `∂a/∂t = a'' − k·a·b`, `∂b/∂t = b'' − k·a·b`, `∂c/∂t = D_c·c'' + k·a·b − P`, `∂d/∂t = P`, `P = λ·max(c − c_s, 0)·[nᵢ]`. After each Euler step the per-node nucleation flag nᵢ is set irreversibly when `cᵢ ≥ c*` (homogeneous nucleation at supersaturation) or when `cᵢ ≥ c_g` and a neighbour was nucleated before the step (growth on adjacent precipitate; a Jacobi update, so the result is sweep-order-free). The step time is stored in `t_nuc[i]`.
