Parameters (dimensionless) are `D_a = D_b = 1`, `D_c = 0.3`, `k = 1`, `λ = 0.05`, `c_s = 0.01`, `c_g = 0.037`, `c* = 0.04`, `b₀ = 0.05`, and five presets `a₀ ∈ {0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0}` (a₀/b₀ = 14–60). One unit ≈ 0.2 mm and one time unit ≈ ℓ²/D_A ≈ 27 s (D_A ≈ 1.5·10⁻⁹ m²/s), so the displayed 100 units are 2 cm grown in about 9 h, which are real Liesegang scales. The discretisation is part of the model: band positions shift about 10 % with τ while p changes by ≤ 0.02 between τ = 0.15 and 0.075. It uses a 3-point Laplacian and explicit Euler with `τ = 0.15`; positivity holds exactly since `τ(2 + k·a₀,max) = 0.75 ≤ 1` and `τ(2D_c + λ) ≤ 1`. Fields are f64 on 160-node columns, and node i sits at depth `x = i + 1` (the reservoir ghost is x = 0). The top ghost is `a = a₀` (reservoir) with no flux for b and c; the bottom ghost is `a = 0, b = b₀,c, c = 0` (semi-infinite gel; a closed bottom starves the front of b). All five tubes are always simulated, whatever is shown. Sim time is a warp of scene time s: `t_sim = T_END·(s/36 s)^{1.5}` with `T_END = 1200` through `grow`, then continuing at its final rate of 50 units/s, so `t_sim = 1500` when `hold` ends. Euler steps are taken whole from an accumulator, so the scheme always uses exactly τ. `scan_bands(d)` runs every 0.25 s: nodes with `d ≥ 5 %` of the column max form contiguous runs, each run is a band with d-weighted centroid x, width = run length and birth time `t_n = min t_nuc` over its nodes, and the first run is the junction zone, excluded from every law. Reference behaviour (a₀ = 2, t = 1500): bands at x = 8.9 (junction zone, 17 wide), 21.3, 28.3, 36.9, 48.6, 63.9, 83.6 and 106.8, ratios 1.33, 1.31, 1.32, 1.32, 1.31, 1.28 (last five mean 1.304), widths 5 → 12 (width law), and x_n/√t_n rising to 2.9 (last four mean 2.84; time law, Morse & Pierce 1903). Across the presets at t = 1400, p = 0.507, 0.406, 0.341, 0.299, 0.264, which fits `p = F + G·b₀/a₀` with F = 0.190, G = 4.41, R² = 0.999 (Matalon & Packter 1955; Antal et al. 1998). **Dish**: one axisymmetric 160-node column, the same chemistry in polar coordinates. Node i sits at radius `r_i = R_W + i + 1` with `R_W = 10`; the central well is held at `a = a₀ = 3` (the most concentrated tube preset) as the ghost at `r = R_W`. The drop is idealised as an inexhaustible reservoir (a held at a₀), as the tubes' top reservoir is; a real drop depletes, which shapes real ring spacing and count. The 3-point Laplacian gains the radial term, `f'' + f'/r ≈ (f₋ − 2f + f₊) + g·(f₊ − f₋)` with `g = 1/(2r)`: the neighbour weights `1 ∓ g` are positive and sum to 2, so the positivity bound `τ(2 + k·a₀) ≤ 1` is unchanged and diffusion conserves `Σ r·f`, the polar volume element. Tubes take the planar sweep (no radial term, no per-node division), bit-identical to M10. In the dish a band's position is its depth from the drop's edge, `x = r − R_W`, the analogue of the tube's depth below the junction; Jablczynski's law and the header ratio use that coordinate (measured from the drop's centre the same rings give ratios ≈ 1.20–1.24, still rising). Reference (t = 1500): junction zone to x ≈ 10.7, rings at x ≈ 23.7, 30.3, 39.0, 49.9, 63.3 and 80.8, ratios 1.28, 1.29, 1.28, 1.27, 1.28. **Story**: a looping `Timeline` of 6 beats, 100 s: `rack grow` 36 s, `rack hold` 6 s (bands thicken and the fronts leave the view), `rack dissolve` 8 s, then `dish grow`, `dish hold`, `dish dissolve` with the same lengths. A beat's phase is its index mod 3 and `t_sim` is the warp above of that phase. Each act starts from fresh gel with its own `b₀` jitter (every column reset); only the live act's columns step. Dissolve is a scripted redissolution with the reaction frozen, like an ammonia front complexing the silver salt to [Ag(NH₃)₂]⁺ (or Co(OH)₂ in excess NH₃). The front descends as `y_e = 120·√progress` nodes (it passes the view bottom at 69 % of the phase; in the dish it moves outward from the drop by the same `y_e`), and above it `c, d ← ·e^{−3·dt}`; a, b and the flags do not change. At each act boundary, every column resets to `(a, b, c, d, n, t_nuc) = (0, b₀,c, 0, 0, false, ∞)` with `b₀,c = b₀·(1 + 0.03ξ)`, where ξ ∈ [−1, 1] is drawn from `ctx.rng` then (ξ = 0 in the opening act). **Opening**: the first `resize` draws the opening act from `ctx.rng` and starts it part-grown: the rack 8 s into `grow` (t_sim ≈ 126, junction caps and a first band in every tube), the dish 16 s in (t_sim ≈ 356, the junction ring and a first ring). The rack steps five columns to the dish's one, so it opens earlier and both catch-ups stay within 4 500 column steps (rack 5 × 838, dish 2 370): a one-off scene-entry cost of ≈ 2–3 ms at 200×50 (release, loaded machine), accepted and bounded by a step-count test, not a clock.
- **Motion**: at the start of each act (s ≈ 0.7 s into `grow`; the opening act starts part-grown, see Opening) each junction zone precipitates as a dim, cloudy cap under the meniscus, and a dim blue haze starts to sink. Bands are then born 2–5 s apart, staggered across tubes (a₀ = 2: s ≈ 6, 8, 11, 15, 20, 28 s). At each birth a thin faint line appears just ahead of the haze, then brightens and thickens over about 1 s as it drains the haze, leaving a dark depletion gap between the band and the sinking haze. During `dissolve` a clear front sweeps down and erases the bands top-down; the gel rests clear for about 2.5 s before the next act.
- **Resize**: the chemistry never depends on the terminal. The five 160-node columns are the capped lattice, decoupled from the display (ADR-0012). 1-D columns have no aspect, so there is one bucket and nothing to resample; no resize after the first touches state or draws from `ctx.rng`. Only the layout is rebuilt: `n = clamp(⌊(w + 4)/24⌋, 1, 5)` visible tubes, showing presets {2}, {1,3}, {0,2,4}, {0,1,3,4} or {0..4} (always dilute → concentrated). The interior width is `clamp(round(w/(2.2n)), 1, 20)` columns (M12; was `w/(2.6n)`, at most 16) with even gaps: tubes about as wide as the gaps between them, the row centred (`left = (w − row)/2`), so the rack fills the width instead of leaving wide margins. Row 0 is the header, row 1 the liquid, and the gel ends at row `h − 2`, leaving the last row to the title (tubes run off-screen, no base). The gel's `D_px = 2·max(h − 3, 0)` pixel rows show nodes [0, V) with `V = clamp(2.4·D_px, 50, 100)` through linear box-filter weights built in `resize`: V = 100 from h = 24 (≈ 2.4 nodes/px at 80×24, ≈ 1.1 at 200×50), and short terminals zoom in on the top of the gel (V = 50 at 40×12) rather than merge bands and gaps into one striped column; the chemistry and the ratios always use the full column. A concave meniscus gives each pixel the depth `x = y − y₀ − sag·(1 − u²)`, with u ∈ [−1, 1] across the tube and sag = 0 below h = 22 (fewer than 40 gel rows, where a 1 px sag would only notch single pixels), 1 px for 22 ≤ h < 40 and 2 px from h = 40; pixels with x < 0 are liquid, so the interface and the bands curve gently. Cylinder shading is `× (0.8 + 0.2·√(1 − u²))`. Walls run over rows `1..h − 1`. The dish is centred at pixel `(w/2, h)`; its rim's inner radius is `R = max(min(h − 3, ⌊w/2⌋ − 2), 0)` pixels, the rim being the pixels with `R ≤ ρ < R + 1`. The gel shows depths `[0, V_d)` with `V_d = clamp(1.8·R − R_W, 30, 100)` at `scale = (R_W + V_d)/R` nodes per pixel of radius: finer than the tubes' 2.4, because at 2.4 the inner ring gaps are under a pixel wide and rasterise as dotted circles. A pixel at distance ρ is the drop when `ρ·scale < R_W`; otherwise its class comes from the box-filtered fields over the depths of radii `[ρ − ½, ρ + ½]`, and its ink is the mean of the inks of the two halves `[ρ − ½, ρ]` and `[ρ, ρ + ½]`: the ink curve is steep at low d, so a pixel just grazing a ring would otherwise light fully. Small dishes (80×24, R = 21) show the junction zone and the first rings only.
- **Inks**: gel pixels are `Role::Ramp(Ramp::Cobalt)` (M12; replaces `HAlpha`) at the highest of three levels, times the cylinder shading. Precipitate is `0.85·(0.30 + 0.70·min(d/0.2, 1)^{0.75})·clamp(d/0.02, 0, 1)` from the box-filtered d, a continuous fade-in, so a newborn band is a thin faint line that brightens and a dissolving band fades fully out; it is not drawn below d = 0.002. Early bands are ≈ 0.5 (cobalt blue) and deep mature bands → 0.85 (silver-blue): the ramp's near-white top is never used, so no band reads as white light. (M12 round 0 used `Ember`, the gallery's most used ramp, whose deep bands climbed to pale gold and read amber; a later round used `HAlpha`, crimson to rose.) The junction zone is scaled by 0.30 (keeping its cloudy gradient; in Ember the 0.45 cap was a rust block that outweighed the bands) so the eye goes to the bands. Haze is `0.25·clamp((c/c* − 0.5)/0.5, 0, 1)` (dim ultramarine, only above half the nucleation threshold, c ≥ c*/2 = 2·c_s). The haze visualises the dissolved product's supersaturation, which is invisible in a real tube; it is kept dim so the precipitate bands stay the only visible matter. Clear gel is not drawn (terminal background), so depletion gaps and virgin gel are both dark. The liquid row and meniscus pixels are `Role::Wave` level 60, the glass walls and the dish rim are `Role::Structure` level 100 and the drop is `Wave` 60 (the tubes' liquid ink; the real solution is colourless), and the header is `Role::Text` (ratios 170, legend 110).
- **ASCII**: walls are `|` (Unicode `▕` left and `▏` right, hugging the gel) and liquid cells are `~`. The `Pixels::blit` closure classes each gel pixel from the fields, not the shaded ink: band iff the box-filtered `d ≥ 0.04` outside the junction zone; faint iff otherwise `d > 0.002` (faint precipitate and the whole junction zone); haze iff otherwise haze > 0 (clear gel carrying only the supersaturation haze); otherwise empty. band/band ⇒ `=`, band on top only ⇒ `-`, band on bottom only ⇒ `_`, faint/faint ⇒ `:`, one faint or any haze ⇒ `.`, empty ⇒ space, and the ink carries the level. The haze is thus a light stipple: its large sinking cloud never outweighs the `-`/`=` bands or the `:` junction cap. Wavy bands read at half-cell resolution. The 80×24 snapshots open part-grown in the rack (8 s into `grow`): three `|` tubes under a `~` row, each with a `:` junction cloud and a first `-` band over a sinking `.` haze at t = 0, and by t = 5 three to four bands per tube under the header ratios. The dish uses the same classes; the drop is `~` and the rim is the class `Glass`, `o` in `Structure` 100.
- **Label**: the header is on row 0. Centred over each tube is its live ratio of its two deepest complete bands (a band is complete once a deeper band has nucleated), for example `1.31`, blank until that exists. The legend sits at column 1 while any ratio is shown, keeping a 2-column gap before the first tube's ratio column r₀ (the walls start below the header, so nothing else can collide): `x(n+1)/x(n)` when r₀ ≥ 14 (80×24, 200×50 and 40×12), `xn+1/xn` when 10 ≤ r₀ < 14, otherwise none. During `dissolve` a tube's ratio leaves once the front `y_e` passes the end of the deeper band it measures (or the view bottom), so the numbers follow the wipe and the rest shows clear tubes under an empty header; the legend leaves with the last ratio. Numbers are dropped when the tube interior is < 4 columns, and the header when h < 6. In the dish act the header has one ratio, at column 14, right of the full legend and its 2-column gap, so it never sits on the rim's top arc; it is hidden when R < 4 or w < 18 (the 4-character ratio needs columns 14–17), so it is never clipped to a number that misreads, such as `1.`. Written from a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: update is ≤ 5 tubes × 160 nodes × ≤ 6 Euler sub-steps per 1/60 s (the peak comes in `hold`; 1–2 early) ≈ 4.8 k node updates ≈ 25 µs, plus a band scan of 5 × 160 nodes every 0.25 s. `dt = 0.25` costs ≤ 84 sub-steps ≈ 0.35 ms. Render at 200×50 is ≈ 7 k gel pixels ≈ 25 µs plus blit ≈ 30 µs. Total ≈ 0.1 ms, independent of terminal size; all state is fixed-size arrays, so nothing allocates after `resize`. M12 closing review: worst frame 0.44 ms, mean 0.19 ms and first `resize` (the opening catch-up) 3.1 ms at 200×50, 2.6 ms at 80×24 (release, 125 s, seed 42, fastest of 3 replays, load ≈ 27). M12: worst frame ≈ 0.32 ms and mean 0.13 ms at 200×50 (two loops, seeds 1, 2 and 42, minimum of 5 replays per frame; criterion mean 0.13 ms at load ≈ 36). The dish costs three box-filter lookups per pixel inside its bounding box.
- **Acceptance**: `LIES-A1` for any sequence of `update(dt ∈ [0, 0.25])` over two full 100 s loops, for the dish column as well as the tubes, all fields stay finite with `0 ≤ a ≤ a₀`, `0 ≤ b ≤ b₀,c`, `c ≥ 0`, `d ≥ 0`, and during grow and hold every node's d is non-decreasing and its nucleation flag and `t_nuc` never change once set; `LIES-A2` on a closed 96-node column (no flux at both ends, a = 1 on the top 40 nodes, b = b₀ everywhere), after 10⁴ Euler steps `Σ(b + c + d) = 96·b₀` and `Σ(a − b) = 40 − 96·b₀` within 1e-9 relative (A + B → C → D stoichiometry); `LIES-A3` preset a₀ = 2 on a 160-node column run to t = 1500 gives ≥ 7 bands (junction zone included) with x ≤ 112, and excluding the junction zone the last five ratios `x_{n+1}/x_n` lie within ±4 % of their mean, the mean is in [1.27, 1.35] (reference 1.304), and the deepest band is wider than the first band after the junction zone (reference 12 vs 5 nodes; spacing and width laws); `LIES-A4` in the same run `x_n/√t_n` for the four deepest bands lies within ±8 % of their mean and the mean is in [2.65, 3.0] (reference 2.84; time law); `LIES-A5` across the five presets (160 nodes, t = 1400), p = mean of the last three ratios − 1 is strictly decreasing in a₀, and the least-squares fit `p = F + G·b₀/a₀` has R² ≥ 0.98, G ∈ [3.5, 5.5] and F ∈ [0.12, 0.26] (reference 0.190, 4.41, 0.999; Matalon–Packter); `LIES-A6` the loop is 100 s; in each act the beats at 0, 36 and 42 s are grow, hold and dissolve, `t_sim` is 1200 at 36 s and 1500 at 42 s (1e-9) and strictly increases through grow and hold; from either opening act, through its dissolve, the live act's a, b and flags do not change, its view mass Σd[0, 100) never grows and ends below 2 %; the next update is in the other act with every column in its reset state (`|b₀,c/b₀ − 1| ≤ 0.03`); in both acts at s = 49.9 of the act, at 80×24 and 200×50, every pixel that is not liquid or glass is `Ink::BLANK`; `LIES-A7` two seeded scenes driven by the same update sequence across a loop boundary, one of them resized through 1×1, 7×3, 80×24 and 200×50 in between, have bit-identical states in every column, across the act boundary (no resize after the first touches the chemistry or draws from `ctx.rng`), and the visible presets follow the n(w) table (w = 20 ⇒ {2}, 80 ⇒ {0,2,4}, 200 ⇒ all five); `LIES-A8` the legend is `x(n+1)/x(n)` for r₀ ≥ 14, `xn+1/xn` for 10 ≤ r₀ < 14 and absent below, and the long form shows at 80×24, 200×50 and 40×12; at 80×24 (seed 42) the header shows the legend and three ratios at s = 41.9 (hold) and is blank at s = 49.9 (end of dissolve); a pixel with `d ≤ 0.002` and haze > 0 is class haze and any cell with haze but no faint, band or liquid pixel is `.`, while faint/faint stays `:`; and V = 100 at 80×24 and 200×50, 50 at 40×12 and 2.4·26 at 80×16, with the deepest window of 40×12 ending at node 50; the meniscus sag is 0 at h = 12 and 21, 1 at 22 and 39, 2 at 40 and 50; an ASCII cell takes the ink of its higher-class half (a blank pixel never wins a tie), liquid over anything is `~` in the liquid ink, and faint over band is `_`, band over faint `-`, each in the band's ink; a tube's live ratio waits until a band deeper than the two it measures has nucleated, then reports their ratio and the deeper band's end; the running integral is an exact box filter (fractional windows included) and reads exactly 0 over a zero field; `LIES-A9` / `LIES-A10` in both acts at 80×24, 40×12 and 200×50, 2 s in, every pixel's role follows its class (empty → Background, liquid → `Wave` 60, glass → `Structure` 100, others → `Ramp(Cobalt)` at a level ≤ `ink::ramp(Cobalt, 0.85)`, below 230), `D_px = 2·max(h − 3, 0)`, and the rendered row `h − 1` is all blank; `LIES-A11` at 80×24 (seed 42) the rack opens with ≥ 2 bands in every visible tube and band pixels on screen, and the dish with ≥ 2 bands; over 64 seeds an unforced opening starts at `act·50 + (8 or 16)` s, both acts occur, and the catch-up takes at most 4 500 column steps; `LIES-A12` a closed 96-node radial column (a = 1 on the top 40 nodes) conserves `Σ r(b + c + d)` and `Σ r(a − b)` within 1e-9 relative over 10⁴ steps and precipitates; the tube column equals a verbatim M10 step bit for bit over 2 000 steps; the dish column at t = 1500 has ≥ 6 bands, and its last four ratios (in `x = r − R_W`) lie within ±4 % of their mean, which is in [1.25, 1.35]; `LIES-A13` at 80×24 and 200×50, R follows the formula above, `V_d` and `scale` follow it with `scale ≤ 1.8` unless `V_d` is at its floor; every glass pixel lies at `R ≤ ρ < R + 1` and every pixel with `ρ·scale < R_W` is liquid; the glass bounding box is symmetric within one pixel and lies in pixel rows `2..2(h − 1)`; the header row starts ` x(n+1)/x(n)  1.`; at 24 rows and widths 12–17 the dish header is blank, and from 18 to 20 it shows the legend and a whole ratio (> 1, 4 characters) at columns 14–17; `caption()` is `a silver drop` in the dish and `bands in tubes` in the rack.

### molecule_viewer  (Reactions, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: a slowly tumbling ball-and-stick model of a real molecule, lit like a physical model on a dark bench. Near atoms are bright and far atoms recede. The gallery runs water, methane, ethanol, benzene, caffeine, then buckminsterfullerene. Each molecule grows out of a point, turns, and collapses back into the point before the next one grows. M12: each molecule is lit per pixel, with shaded spheres and cylinders, specular highlights, depth cueing and true occlusion; the frame fills the art area, and bond orders show (double bonds are two strands, aromatic bonds a stick plus a dashed inner strand). The formula and molar mass are a right-aligned readout on the title row.
- **Model**: static tables in the module. Each holds element, position (Å) and bonds with an order of `Single`, `Double` or `Aromatic` (1.5). Benzene uses `Aromatic`; caffeine and C60 use Kekulé single and double bonds. Planar molecules are stored with their plane in world xy (normal along z), so yaw takes them through face-on and edge-on views. Geometries:
  - water: r(OH) 0.9572 Å, ∠HOH 104.52° (Benedict, Gailar & Plyler 1956).
  - methane: T_d, r(CH) 1.087 Å.
  - ethanol: trans (anti H–O–C–C) conformer, r(CC) 1.512, r(CO) 1.431, r(OH) 0.971 Å (NIST CCCBDB experimental); the remaining C–H bonds and angles come from CCCBDB, or are tetrahedral with r(CH) 1.09 Å.
  - benzene: D₆h, r(CC) 1.397 Å, r(CH) 1.084 Å (Herzberg 1966).
  - caffeine C8H10N4O2: the 3-D conformer of PubChem CID 2519 (24 atoms, 25 bonds, planar purine), with double bonds C2=O, C6=O, C4=C5 and C8=N9.
  - C60: generated at build, not typed in. Each of the 30 edges of an icosahedron carries two atoms at fractions `t` and `1 − t`. The icosahedron's vertices are the cyclic permutations of (0, ±1, ±φ); its edge is 2, so it is scaled by `a/2`. With `a = 2·r₅₆ + r₆₆` and `t = r₅₆/a`, the pentagon (5:6) bonds are r₅₆ = 1.458 Å and the hexagon-fusing (6:6) bonds, which lie along the old edges, are r₆₆ = 1.401 Å (Hedberg et al. 1991, gas-phase electron diffraction). Every atom then sits 3.562 Å from the centre.

  Every table is recentred on its centre of mass (H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999 u). Ball radius is 0.20 × the Bondi (1964) van der Waals radius (H 1.20, C 1.70, N 1.55, O 1.52 Å), which keeps ≥ 0.5 Å of visible stick on C60's 1.40–1.46 Å bonds, so the cage reads as rings of balls rather than a heap of blobs.

  View (M12): the camera is `geom::Camera { yaw, pitch, distance: 5·r_eff }`, with `r_eff = max(maxᵢ(|rᵢ| + r_ball,ᵢ), 1.25 Å)` so water (raw ≈ 1.16 Å) is framed but not ballooned. The art area is rows `[0, h − 1)` when `h ≥ 6` (else all rows), centred at `((w − 1)/2, (art − 1)/2)`. The scale is `s(t)·draw::fit_radius(w × art, 0.95)/(1.05·r_eff)` rows per Å, where `s(t)` is the gallery scale (Motion): a sphere of radius `r_eff` seen from `5·r_eff` projects to at most `5/√24` ≈ 1.021 of its radius, and the ≈ 3 % on top covers the pixel discs, so no orientation leaves the frame.

  Raster (M12): `update` projects the ≤ 60 atoms and paints into a z-buffered `Pixels` raster the size of the art area; each pixel keeps its surface depth (Å) and its owner (atom `i + 1` or the stick marker). `render` only blits it.
  - Balls: pixel radius `2·r_rows`, with the centre pixel always lit; surface depth `depth + r_Å·√(1 − d²)`; Lambert `I = 0.3 + 0.7·max(0, n·L)` with `L = normalise(−0.45, 0.55, 0.70)` (upper left, front); a Blinn–Phong highlight (`n·H > 0.95`) at `s²` of full brightness.
  - Depth cue: `s²·(100 + 155·ν)/255`, with `ν = (depth + r_eff)/(2·r_eff) ∈ [0, 1]`.
  - Sticks: a cylinder of radius 0.10 Å (at least 0.55 px) between the projected centres; depth is interpolated along the axis plus the cylinder's bulge. Each half is in its atom's colour at 0.8 of the ball level, with the depth cue interpolated along the bond. Double bonds are two strands of radius 0.06 Å, 0.125 Å either side of the axis on screen; aromatic bonds a full stick plus a dashed strand (3 dashes over the middle 60 %) 0.25 Å toward the view centre, inside benzene's ring in any orientation. Strands split only when a thin strand is ≥ 0.6 px in radius; below that every order is one stick. Sticks are drawn only from `s ≥ 0.5` (below it the atoms crowd the centroid).
  - Electron-cloud haze (M12, Unicode only): on every pixel no ball or stick covers, each atom adds `0.13·(1 − (d/R)²)³` out to its van der Waals radius `R` on screen, the sum capped at 0.22 and scaled by `s²`, in `Ramp(Plasma)`; drawn only at or above `MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL`. It never sets the z-buffer or owner, so it is background to everything else, and ASCII leaves it out.
- **Stylisation (stated)**: balls are 0.20 × the Bondi radius (a ball-and-stick convention, not space-filling). Bond-order strands are offset in the screen plane (a 2-D convention), not in the π-bond plane, so a double bond reads as two strands at any orientation. Sticks are 0.10 Å. N and O use named ramps (false colour close to CPK blue and red); H and C are `Text` greys (C at 0.45 of H, M12; 0.6 before: CPK charcoal, so methane's four white hydrogens read apart from their carbon), which every theme keeps neutral: CPK dark-grey carbon and white hydrogen are kept on purpose (a tinted carbon would read as another element, and the cool ramps already stand for N). The electron-cloud haze (Raster) stands for the molecule's electron cloud as the sum of each atom's own cloud out to its van der Waals radius, so it reads denser where bonded atoms overlap. It does not model how bonding moves electron density; the title's 'atoms share electrons' is carried by the bond sticks. Its violet is false colour, chosen apart from every CPK colour. It gives methane its colour while CPK stays honest: carbon charcoal, hydrogen white. Caffeine and C60 are each drawn as one Kekulé structure (C60: double 6:6, single 5:6 bonds, matching their measured bond-length alternation); the true bonding is delocalised, and only benzene uses the aromatic stick.
- **Motion**: everything is a closed-form function of scene time `t`.
  - The gallery is a `Timeline` of `grow` 0.6 s → `hold` 8.8 s → `shrink` 0.6 s (10 s per molecule). The molecule is `(start + timeline.cycle(t)) mod 6`, where `start` is seeded.
  - Scene time begins at the start of the first `hold`, so the first frame shows a full molecule, and successive 15 s `sim` visits show different molecules.
  - The gallery scale `s` is `ease::smoothstep(progress)` in `grow`, 1 in `hold` and `1 − smoothstep(progress)` in `shrink`. Atoms converge on the centroid and their levels dim with `s²`. The molecule and label change only at the slot boundary, where `s = 0`.
  - Yaw is `ω·τ − 0.78 rad` with `ω = 2π/10 s`, where τ is the time since the slot began, so each molecule reaches full size in a three-quarter view (≈ −0.4 rad). Pitch is `0.30 + 0.30·sin(2πt/17 s)` rad.
  - C60's equator sweeps up to ≈ 9 columns per second at 80×24 (≈ 19 at 200×50).
- **Resize**: view only; there is no simulation state (ADR-0012). The centre, scale and label tier are recomputed. Scene time, slot and orientation are kept, and the seeded `start` is drawn only on the first resize.
- **Inks** (M12): H `Text` at `255·v`, C `Text` at `255·0.45·v`, N `ramp(Ice, v)`, O `ramp(HAlpha, v)`, where v is the shaded, depth-cued brightness; bond halves take their atom's ink at 0.8 of its level; the haze is `Ramp(Plasma)` at most `255·0.22`.
- **ASCII**: each cell takes the brighter of its two pixels (the haze is not drawn). Levels < 24 are blank; above that, `: + * # @` by level quintile, in that pixel's ink. Every atom that owns its centre pixel shows its element symbol in that cell, upper-case when `ν ≥ 0.5` and lower-case otherwise.
- **Label** (GEN-R13): `draw::label_right` on row `h − 1` (when `h ≥ 6`), in `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`: `caffeine  C8H10N4O2  194.19 g/mol`, all in ASCII, the longest tier that fits `title::readout_room` with the live caption: drop the mass, then the name, then show nothing. The Hill formula (C first, then H, then the rest alphabetically; H2O for water) and the molar mass (2 decimals) are checked against the table. While the title shows the molecule's name as its caption (`Title::fit` gives `Form::Full`, decided per molecule in `resize`), the name is left out: `C8H10N4O2  194.19 g/mol`, then `C8H10N4O2`, then nothing. The label is never clipped and switches only at the slot boundary. Row 0 is art.
- **Perf** (200×50, the same run and load as molecular_dynamics): the worst is C60, with 60 balls of ≈ 40 px and 90 sticks of ≈ 60 px checked; mean 0.06 ms, heaviest 1-s window median 0.20 ms. Worst single frame (`examples/worst_frame`, 180 s, fastest of 3 replays per frame, load ≈ 27–33 on 14 cores, M12 round 2): 0.32 ms at 106 s. Live bytes at 500×150 are 2.15 MiB; no heap use in `update` or `render`.
- **Acceptance**: `MOL-A1` every table is chemically consistent: with the covalent radii of Cordero et al. (2008) (H 0.31, C 0.76, N 0.71, O 0.66 Å), bonded pairs satisfy `0.80 ≤ d/(rᵢ + rⱼ) ≤ 1.12` and every non-bonded pair has `d/(rᵢ + rⱼ) ≥ 1.35`, so the bond list equals the distance-derived connectivity; each atom's summed bond order equals its valence (H 1, C 4, N 3, O 2; aromatic = 1.5); the Hill formula derived from the table equals the label's (H2O, CH4, C2H6O, C6H6, C8H10N4O2, C60), the derived molar mass matches 18.015, 16.043, 46.069, 78.114, 194.194 and 720.66 g/mol within 0.001, and the label's 2-decimal mass is within 0.005 + 1e-9 of it; and the centre of mass is at the origin within 1e-6 Å; `MOL-A2` reference geometry: water r(OH) = 0.9572 ± 0.0005 Å and ∠HOH = 104.52 ± 0.05°; methane r(CH) = 1.087 ± 0.001 Å with all six ∠HCH = 109.47 ± 0.05°; ethanol has 9 atoms and 8 bonds with r(CC) = 1.512, r(CO) = 1.431 and r(OH) = 0.971 (each ± 0.001 Å) and an H–O–C–C dihedral of 180 ± 1°; benzene's 12 atoms are coplanar within 0.001 Å in the xy plane with r(CC) = 1.397 ± 0.001 and r(CH) = 1.084 ± 0.001 Å; caffeine's 14 heavy atoms are coplanar within 0.05 Å; C60 has 60 atoms and 90 bonds, 3 bonds and exactly one 6:6 bond per atom, 30 bonds of 1.401 ± 0.001 Å and 60 of 1.458 ± 0.001 Å, every atom at 3.562 ± 0.002 Å from the centre, and its 5:6 bonds form exactly 12 disjoint 5-cycles; `MOL-A3` the motion is rigid and fits the frame: at 500 sampled `hold` times over a 60 s cycle, the rotated coordinates (recovered from an orthographic `Camera` at scale 1 as `(x/ASPECT, −y, depth)`) keep every pairwise distance of the table within 1e-9 Å; at 80×24, 200×50, 40×12, 120×36, 60×20, 100×30, 30×10 and 160×40 every atom projects (`Some`) and every ball's pixel disc (centre ± `2·r_rows`) lies in `[0, w] × [0, 2·art]`; at 80×24 and 200×50 every molecule's balls reach ≥ 0.85 of the framing radius at some point of its hold turn; the depth-cue factor `100 + 155·ν` and the projected ball radius (for a fixed element) are strictly increasing in depth; `MOL-A4` z-buffer: for a far O and a nearer N whose balls overlap, every pixel inside both discs carries N's ink (`Ramp(Ice)`), with more than 20 such pixels; over all six molecules × 12 yaws at 200×50, each atom's centre pixel shows the atom itself in its element role, or a nearer ball, or a stick running toward the viewer, and ≥ 80 % show the atom itself; `MOL-A5` gallery timeline: from the seeded start, one 60 s cycle shows the six molecules in gallery order, 10 s each; `s` is exactly 1 throughout `hold`, never outside `[0, 1]`, and continuous (`|Δs| ≤ 0.05` per 1/60 s step); the molecule index and label change only at slot boundaries, where `s = 0`; seeds 1..=32 yield all six starting molecules (six uniform draws would cover all six only 6!/6⁶ ≈ 1.5 % of the time); no pixel exceeds `round(255·s²)`, and the bonded molecule lights more pixels than its bare balls exactly when `s ≥ 0.5`; `MOL-A6` label and fallback: the chosen label tier is the longest one that fits (empty when none fits), so the label is never clipped; at 80×24 the readout is on row 23, ends ` g/mol ` and never repeats the caption, and row 0 carries no label; in ASCII mode over 60 s the frame is pure ASCII, every atom that owns its centre pixel shows its symbol there, and `H C N O h c` all appear; `MOL-A7` bond orders and palette: on a face-on C–C pair 1.4 Å apart at 120×40 the pixel column through the bond's middle has 1 run of drawn model pixels (owner ≠ 0, so the haze does not count) for a single bond, 2 for a double bond and 1–2 for an aromatic bond; `element_ink(H, 1) = Text 255`, C is `Text` below 200, N is `Ramp(Ice)` and O is `Ramp(HAlpha)`; `MOL-A8` electron-cloud haze: on face-on methane at 120×40 every lit background pixel is `Ramp(Plasma)` at a visible level, within some atom's on-screen vdW radius (+1 px); ball and stick pixels never carry it; over 400 pixels carry it, none above `255·0.22`; at `s = 0.3` its brightest pixel is under a quarter of the full-size peak; and carbon's full-brightness level is ≤ 128.

### physarum  (Living Systems, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: a slime mould solving a transport problem in time-lapse. A glowing inoculum spills outward in a lace of veins, finds the scattered oat flakes, and thickens into a few pulsing tubes that link them like a rail map (Nakagaki et al. 2000; Tero et al. 2010). The network then starves and dissolves tube by tube into darkness. Fresh flakes drop onto the empty plate, and a new colony starts somewhere else.
- **Model**: the agent-based approximation of Jones (2010). The plate is the screen above the label row: `h_p = h − 1` rows when the label is drawn and `h_p = h` otherwise, so the plate's absorbing edge keeps every tube off that row and the label sits under the organism, not inside it. The chemoattractant `T` is a `Grid<f32>` on a capped square lattice `L = grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h_p), 20_000, 1.25)` (CORE-R48). That gives 197×98 at 200×50 and 80×46 at 80×24; below the cap the lattice equals the pixel display, so the view is exact. There are `n = max(1, round(p·lw·lh))` agents with `p = 5 %` (965 and 184), within the population range Jones explored. Each agent has a continuous position and a heading in `Z₁₆` (22.5° units) with a 16-entry `(cos, sin)` table, so no trig runs per step; turns are addition in Z₁₆ (`rotate(h, t)`, + = clockwise on screen). Its site is its rounded position. Each Jones step is two passes over a fresh Fisher–Yates shuffle of the live agents (rand's `SliceRandom::shuffle` on a `SplitMix64` drawn from `ctx.rng`). **Motor pass**: step `SS = 1` px along the heading. If the target site is on the lattice and unoccupied (particle exclusion, one agent per site), or is the agent's own site (a sub-site move, possible after a diagonal step from a non-integer position), the agent moves and deposits `depT = 5` at its new site. Otherwise it stays, deposits nothing and takes a uniformly random new heading. **Sensory pass**: sample `T` at three sensors at distance `SO = clamp(round(0.09·min(lw, lh)), 3, 9)` px (4 at 80×24, 9 at 200×50) and angles `h − SA, h, h + SA` with `SA = 22.5°`. Off-lattice sensors read −1, so walls repel. The agent then rotates by `RA = 45°` according to Jones's rule: F strictly greatest ⇒ keep the heading; F strictly least ⇒ ±RA by coin; otherwise turn toward the strictly stronger side (FL < FR ⇒ toward R, FR < FL ⇒ toward L); any remaining tie ⇒ keep. **Chemoattractant**: after both passes, `T ← (1 − δ)·mean₃ₓ₃(T)` as a separable 3-tap box filter with `δ = 0.1`. The plate edge absorbs: sites off the lattice count as 0 in the mean, so attractant leaks out there and the network sits on the plate instead of tracing the frame. **Drawn field**: a second grid `V` receives the same deposits (at inoculation and in the motor pass) and the same diffusion and decay, but no food pinning. It is what is drawn (through the display EMA `S`, see Inks); the agents sense `T` only. **Food**: `n_f = clamp(round(lw·lh/800), 4, 9)` flakes (5 at 80×24, 9 at 200×50) at seeded positions in the central 80 %. They are at least `0.25·min(lw, lh)` apart and at least `max(2·SO, 0.15·min(lw, lh))` beyond the inoculum's rim, so the colony has to spread before it touches one. Placement uses at most 200 rejection tries and accepts fewer flakes on a crowded plate, including 0. Food discs of radius 1.5 px are pinned to `T = max(T, 20)` (4·depT) during explore and consolidate, which anchors trunk lines between sources as in Tero et al. (2010). **Inoculum**: a disc of radius `√(2n/π)` px (50 % occupancy) centred at a seeded point in the central 40 %, with random headings. Each agent deposits once at seeding, and `V` is then blurred twice by the same filter with δ = 0, so the colony shows at t = 0 as a soft drop rather than a checkerboard of single deposits. **Rate**: one Jones step per `SIM_DT` (60 per simulated second), counted by the core `FixedStep(SIM_DT, 15)` (CORE-R9..R11). This is a time-lapse: the network in Tero et al. took about a day to grow. **Timeline** (48 s, loops): explore 10 s → consolidate 30 s (the physics is unchanged; the network coarsens under its own dynamics while the food holds the trunk lines) → fade 6 s (food unpinned; each agent dies at a seeded uniform time in the phase, and a dead agent stops moving and depositing and frees its site, so tubes thin and snap one by one) → rest 2 s (the trail decays to black, `0.9^120 ≈ 3·10⁻⁶`). At rest start, the next cycle's inoculum centre and food are drawn from `ctx.rng`, and the new flakes fade in. The inoculum is seeded at the cycle boundary. A step spans `[t₀, t₁]`: starvation and the Jones step are decided at `t₀`, and these two events at `t₁`, so the state already holds them when the scene time reads `t₁` and the drawing never runs ahead of the colony (PHY-A5). Every cycle differs, and each seed is deterministic.
- **Motion**: agents move 60 px/s and the trail changes every step. Within 1 s the inoculum grows filaments that reach visibly past its rim; because the rim streams outward at 60 px/s, at 80×24 the nearest flakes are touched within about a second and tied in by `+` tubes by ≈ 3 s. By 10 s the sources are joined by a web with loops. During consolidation, brightness pulses along the stable tubes as agents stream through them, and a slow stylised streaming wave runs outward along them (see Streaming), while their edges hold still (PHY-A7). The fade reads as a slow disintegration, followed by a dark plate where new flakes appear, and then a new bloom.
- **Resize** (ADR-0012): the lattice comes from `lattice_for`. If it is unchanged (same aspect bucket, with both sizes at or above the cap in both axes), only the display map and `Pixels` are rebuilt, no `ctx.rng` is consumed, and the simulation stays bit-identical. If it changes, `T` and `V` are resampled with `grid::resample_bilinear`. Agent positions are scaled by `(lw'/lw, lh'/lh)` and re-inserted into the occupancy grid in index order, and any collisions are dropped. Outside fade and rest, the population is then brought to `n' = round(p·lw'·lh')`: trimmed evenly in index order (agent i of n is kept when `⌊(i + 1)·n'/n⌋ > ⌊i·n'/n⌋`, with no randomness), or topped up with agents placed on free sites where `T` is above the mean, using at most 16 `ctx.rng` tries per agent before skipping it. Food is scaled, SO is recomputed, and the timeline is untouched. Agent, order and occupancy buffers have capacity 1000 (= p·20 000) from `build`.
- **Inks**: the drawn field `S` is `V` through a display-only EMA, `S += (V − S)·(1 − e^(−h/0.15 s))` after every step, reset to `V` at inoculation and on a lattice change. The discrete agents' per-step deposits make tube edges flip grade on almost every frame (≈ 30 % of lit ASCII cells at 80×24); the filter keeps the pulsing and the growth but stills the edges. The agents sense `T`, so no model result depends on it. `S` is tone-mapped by `u = min(1, √(S / (2.5·T_ref)))`. `T_ref` is reset at every inoculation (the first and each cycle boundary) to the bright-half mean of the new inoculum's `V`, so every bloom opens on the same soft drop, and is otherwise an EMA (τ = 1 s) of the mean `V` over sites above `V`'s global mean (the global total comes out of the diffusion pass). During the first 1.5 s of explore it follows `max(bright-half mean, 0.45·max V)` with τ = 0.25 s instead: a young inoculum is a flat stack of deposits about 2.5× its bright-half mean, which that reference alone paints as one saturated `#` disc (≈ 44 % of lit cells), and exposing for its peak leaves `#` to the hottest ≈ 10 % of the drop. It is frozen during fade and rest, so the dying network dims instead of being re-exposed. (Inheriting the old network's frozen reference instead opened every later bloom as an underexposed `.`/`:` haze with holes, ≈ 25 % fewer lit cells than the first and no `+`/`#`.) The organism is `Ramp(Sulfur)` (M12 round 3, replacing `Ember`; M10 used `Seq`): near-black olive → chrome yellow → pale lemon, the colour of a real *Physarum* plasmodium. The ramp is still stylised (brightness stands for trail concentration, not tube thickness or pigment), and it keeps the organism apart from the Ember/amber scenes around it in the gallery and from the `Seq` fields of gray_scott, belousov_zhabotinsky and liesegang_rings. *Tubes* (`u ≥ 0.55`, raised from the M10 floor 0.45 so only a vein's channel is drawn, not the diffuse attractant around it): `ramp(Sulfur, 0.55 + 0.45·t^1.2)` with `t = min(1, (u − 0.55)/0.45)`, a yellow body around a pale channel, so junctions and busy trunks glow while thin trunks stay luminous; the tube floor is level `CORE_LO_LEVEL` = 140. *Rim* (`0.50 ≤ u < 0.55`): `ramp(Sulfur, 0.06 + 0.24·(u − 0.50)/0.05)`, the attractant just outside each tube in the ramp's near-black olive end, about one pixel wide. It antialiases the half-block edges without fattening the veins. `u < 0.50` stays `Ink::BLANK`, so the plate is the terminal's own dark. During the fade the frozen `T_ref` lets the dying network sink through the rim band, so it thins and goes out. *Flakes*: each oat flake is a 2 × 2-pixel mote (square on screen; M10 drew a 5-pixel plus), oat beige: `Blackbody` at level 105 (≈ 4 000 K) times its fade. That is an art-directed tint, not a temperature (ADR-0015 allows `Blackbody` as art-directed colour), chosen so the food belongs to the organism's warm world instead of reading as grey UI debris. Level 105 is below the tubes' pale-lemon peak, so in mono the organism outshines its food; fading the level takes a flake through the blackbody's dim reds into the plate. Because the pinned `T` is not drawn, a flake has no glow until the organism reaches it. The colony's flakes fade out over the last 1 s of the fade (`105·(1 − smoothstep)`), so rest opens on an empty plate, and the next colony's fade in from 0 to 105 over rest (`105·smoothstep(progress)`); a flake at level 0 is not drawn. Label `Role::Text` 110. Agents are not drawn: the trail is the organism.
- **Streaming** (M12, stylised): while the network consolidates, tube tones pulse as `u' = max(0.55, u·(1 + 0.2·e·sin(k·r − 2πt/3 s)))`. `r` is the pixel's distance from the inoculum centre and `k = 2π/(0.3·min(w, 2h))`, so the pulses run outward along the tubes as bright swellings. The envelope `e` eases in and out over 2 s at the ends of `consolidate` and is 0 elsewhere, so the bloom and the starving network hold still. Rim pixels are not pulsed, and no pulse is computed while `e = 0`. Tones already near `u = 1` clip on the up-swing, so the busiest junctions pulse mostly downward. *Stylisation*: real shuttle streaming reverses every 1–2 minutes and is driven by peristaltic contraction. The scene is a ≈ 1000–2000× time-lapse (a network that takes 10–26 h forms in about 48 s), at which a ≈ 100 s reversal cycle would last 50–100 ms, 1.5–3 frames at 30 FPS: close to the frame rate, too fast to show honestly. The 3 s one-way outward wave is deliberately slowed and never reverses.
- **ASCII**: half-block `Pixels` (CORE-R42) over the plate, `w × 2h_p`; each display pixel samples `S` through `grid::SampleMap` (CORE-R52) and is tone-mapped in `update`; the blank test runs on `S/(2.5·T_ref) < 0.50²`, so dark pixels skip the square root. The `blit` closure works per cell: a tube pixel's tone is read back from its ink (the inverse of the tube ramp, within 0.012), so `render` only blits and the ASCII grades follow the streaming pulse; rim and food pixels count as blank; both blank ⇒ untouched; otherwise `m = max(u_top, u_bottom)` maps to `.` (< 0.67), `:` (< 0.78), `+` (< 0.90) or `#` (steps of `(0.9 − 0.55)/3`, so `#` keeps the top 10 % of the tone range), using the brighter pixel's ink. In ASCII, `render` then puts a single `o` (M10: `@`) in the flake's ink in each flake's cell; in Unicode a flake is its 2 × 2 mote. At 80×24 the snapshots show a soft `.`/`:`/`+` drop and five `o` at t = 0, filaments already reaching the two nearest flakes at t = 1 s, and a `.`/`:` lace with `+`/`#` trunks through the `o`s at t = 5 s.
- **Captions** (M12): explore `veins seek food`; consolidate `None`, so the title's own `trails reinforce` shows; fade `network starves`; rest `fresh oat flakes`.
- **Label**: `SA 22.5 RA 45 SO 9` (ASCII, rebuilt in `resize` when SO changes) right-aligned in the row below the plate (GEN-R13), drawn only when `w ≥ 40` and `h ≥ 12` and it fits `title::stable_readout_room` (CORE-R83), so it never comes and goes with the beat, with a blank cell either side, so it never looks glued to a tube.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 (lattice 197×98, 965 agents, 1 Jones step per update): the shuffle, the motor and sensory passes, two separable diffuse-decay passes (`T` and `V`; the `V` pass also returns its total), one more pass over `V` for the bright-half mean and peak, food pinning (< 1 µs) and the display EMA of `S` (one pass), then the `SampleMap` tone map of 200×98 pixels, all in `update`; `render` blits 9.8 k cells. Criterion on a loaded machine (load average ≈ 15–26): 0.28–0.39 ms at 200×50 (0.64 ms at load ≈ 80–99 with the 197×98 plate) and 0.71–1.05 ms at 400×100, against the < 1 ms aim (GEN-R8 is measured at 200×50). At 400×100 the 80 k-pixel tone map is ≈ 0.45 ms of that; testing the floor on the square saves ≈ 5–10 % of it. The simulation cost is independent of terminal size above the cap; the tone map and the blit scale with the display. M12 (fastest of 5 runs per frame over 3600 frames): mean 0.29 ms, worst frame 0.35 ms at 200×50 (M10: 0.32 / 0.42 ms); streaming costs one `sin` and one `hypot` per tube pixel, during consolidate only. Steady state is allocation-free.
- **Acceptance**: `PHY-A1` `steer(f, fl, fr, coin)` returns exactly 0 / ±2 (by coin) / +2 / −2 / 0 in Z₁₆ units (+ = toward FR) for the five cases of Jones's rule, listed literally and then exhaustively over all orderings of three values with ties; every entry of the 16-direction table has unit length to 1e-6; `rotate` is addition in Z₁₆ (sixteen +1 rotations return to every start, `rotate(0, −RA) = 14`, `rotate(15, RA) = 1`) and + is clockwise on screen; and an agent heading east with attractant only at its FR sensor turns by +RA in the sensory pass; `PHY-A2` `diffuse_decay`: a constant field `c` maps to `(1 − δ)c` at interior sites, `(1 − δ)c·6/9` at edge sites and `(1 − δ)c·4/9` at corners (absorbing edge; relative error 1e-6), and the returned total matches the field's sum; with δ = 0 an interior impulse spreads to exactly its 3×3 block at 1/9 each and conserves the total to 1e-6; for any seeded non-negative field, `max(out) ≤ (1 − δ)·max(in)`, no output is negative, and `min(out) ≥ (1 − δ)·min(in)` at interior sites; `PHY-A3` over a seeded 600-step run on an 80×48 lattice followed by a full fade, after every step the occupancy count equals the live agent count, no two agents share a site and every agent is on the lattice; the live count is constant outside fade, non-increasing within it and 0 at its end; `PHY-A4` network forms (thresholds from a Jones-model prototype): from a seeded inoculum on a 96×48 lattice with 5 food sources, every flake lies at least `√(2n/π) + max(2·SO, 0.15·48)` from the inoculum centre, at step 0 no site within Chebyshev distance 2 of a flake centre has trail (no flake's 3×3 disc is 8-adjacent to the inoculum); after 600 steps (10 s) the trail's coefficient of variation is ≥ 1.2, the fraction of sites above the mean is in [0.2, 0.45], and the largest 8-connected component of {T > mean} holds ≥ 85 % of those sites; the largest component of the unpinned drawn field {V > mean} holds ≥ 4 of the 5 food sources (this holds for about 90 % of seeds; seed 42 is used); as a control, the same seeded colony stepped without food pinning, judged on the same unpinned field, links ≤ 3 of the 5 sources (seed 42: 4 fed against 0), so the pinning is what ties them in; on `T` the pinning itself would hold every fed flake centre above the mean (~0.5 s in debug); `PHY-A5` on a 40×24 display (lattice 40×46) the beats are explore [0, 10) / consolidate [10, 40) / fade [40, 46) / rest [46, 48) s and repeat at 48 s, the total trail at 47.9 s is < 1e-3 of its mean over consolidate, the second cycle's food layout and inoculum centre differ from the first cycle's, and both are identical across two runs with the same seed (< 1.5 s in debug); and across the loop seams, over every step of two cycles plus 1 s (seed 42): drawn flakes are `Blackbody`; during explore and consolidate the drawn flakes are exactly the colony's own layout at `level(105)`, below `organism_ink(1)`'s level; the flake level never changes by more than 12 between consecutive steps, and the drawn layout changes (≥ 4 times) only between frames whose levels are both ≤ 12, so no seam flashes or cuts a layout at full brightness (< 2 s in debug); `PHY-A6` resizing 220×60 → 240×64 (both lattice 197×101) leaves the lattice and `T` bit-identical, and after 60 more steps `T` is bit-identical to a run that never resized; resizing 200×50 → 80×24 (197×98 → 80×46) during consolidate gives a new max ≤ the old max, a new mean within 20 % of the old, every agent on the lattice with exclusion intact, an unchanged food count and an unchanged beat, and a population trimmed to at most `round(p·80·46) = 184` and at least 90 % of it; resizing 80×24 → 200×50 during consolidate tops the population up to ≥ 90 % of `round(p·197·98)`; `PHY-A7` drawing: on the seeded 80×24 display at t = 0, no organism pixel lies within 3 pixels of a flake's centre (the pinned `T` is not drawn), the ASCII frame holds exactly one `o` per flake, at the flake's own cell, and in both glyph modes the cells at x = 0 and just after the label are blank; at 1 s samples over the first 100 s (two cycles) the bottom row holds exactly the label and nothing else in both glyph modes; in ASCII frames at 30 FPS on the seeded 80×24 display, the first frame of the second bloom (t = 48 s) has a lit-cell count within ±20 % of the t = 0 frame and a nonzero `+`/`#` share (inheriting the old reference gives ≈ 25 % fewer and no `+`/`#`), and `#` is ≤ 20 % of the lit cells in every frame of the first 1.5 s of the first and the second bloom (≈ 44 % without the bloom exposure), and over 2 s of consolidation (from t = 77 s) ≤ 15 % of lit cells change glyph from one frame to the next (≈ 30 % without the display EMA; ≈ 8 % with it) (~1 s in debug); `PHY-A8` sulfur tubes with a thin rim, at 80×24 (t = 5 s) and 200×50 (t = 20 s), seed 42: every lit non-flake pixel is `Ramp(Sulfur)`, at a level either ≤ the rim top or ≥ the tube floor; both bands occur; and ≥ 90 % of rim pixels have a tube pixel among their 8 neighbours (the rim is one pixel wide); `PHY-A9` captions: over one 48 s cycle `caption()` follows the four beats above, and every caption is in `META.captions`; `PHY-A10` shuttle streaming: `stream` is the identity at depth 0, stays within ±depth and never goes below the tube floor; `tube_tone(organism_ink(u))` returns `u` within 0.012 on [0.55, 1], and `None` for the rim; `CORE_LO_LEVEL` equals `ink::ramp(Sulfur, CORE_LO).level`; the envelope is off at t = 1, 9 and 43 s and on at 13 and 25 s; and with the field frozen, half a period later more than half the tube pixels change level and no rim pixel does.

### solar_surface  (Sun & Planets, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: the Sun up close as a slowly turning ball. The granulated photosphere darkens and reddens toward the limb. A few sunspot groups with dark umbrae and striated penumbrae ride differential rotation from east (left) to west (right). Cool loops, with knots of coronal rain sliding along them, stand bright above the limb as their active regions rotate into view, and cross the disc as dark filaments. The disc fills the height, and the eye goes to the loops at the limb and to the spots. The black sky around it stays empty. At 200×50 the disc is 92 cols × 46 rows with 54 empty cols on each side; at 80×24 it is 44 × 22 with 18 on each side.
- **Model**: the view is a composite: the photosphere in white light, loops and rim as in H-α. The disc is centred with radius `R = min(0.46·P, 0.36·W)` px on `Pixels` (P = 2h), with solar north up and east left. The viewer is fixed in inertial space, so rates are sidereal (from Earth the synodic rate is 0.986 °/day slower). B0, the heliographic latitude of the disc centre, is seeded in [−7.25°, 7.25°]. Each disc pixel gets an observer-frame unit normal n = (dx/R, −dy/R, √(1 − ·)) with μ = n_z, tabulated per resize. *Limb darkening*: `I(μ) = 1 − u(1 − μ)` with u = 0.6 (linear law, visible continuum). *Differential rotation*: `ω(φ) = A + B sin²φ + C sin⁴φ` with A = 14.713, B = −2.396, C = −1.787 °/day sidereal (Snodgrass & Ulrich 1990). Time runs at 0.3 day per simulated second, so the equator turns in 81.6 s and latitude 60° in 100.7 s. Every surface feature advances its longitude by exactly `ω(φ)·0.3·dt` (closed form). *Granulation*: multiplicatively weighted Worley cells from `N_g = clamp(round(4πR²/s_g²), 64, 2400)` feature points on the sphere. The spacing at disc centre is `s_g = clamp(0.085 R, 3.6, 5)` px (3.91 px at R = 46, ≈ 45× the real ≈ 1.3 Mm spacing, which would be sub-pixel), so a cell spans 2–3 terminal cells and lanes read as polygons rather than a blocky maze. Each point carries (φ, λ), a lifetime of 6–10 s and a weight w that fades in and out over 1.5 s by smoothstep; a dead point respawns uniformly on the sphere. Per frame, points with μ > 0 are projected and counting-sorted into a screen bucket grid of cell size s_g. Each disc pixel takes F1 and F2 from the sites within s_g (its 3×3 buckets), widening the query to 1.5 s_g for the few pixels whose F2 reaches s_g, with squared distances scaled by m² where `m = 1 + 1.5(1 − w)`. Newborn cells therefore grow from a point and dying ones shrink, with no popping. The pattern is `g = 0.55·smoothstep(0, 0.3 s_g, F2 − F1) + 0.45·w₁·(1 − min(F1/0.9 s_g, 1))²`, giving bright granule cores and dark intergranular lanes. It is applied as `I *= 1 + (G − 1)·gate` with the granulation factor `G = 1 + C(μ)(2g − 1)`, `C = 0.12·√μ·smoothstep(0.1, 0.2, μ)`, and `gate` the product of the spots' photosphere weights (0 inside a spot, 1 outside, see *Spots*): convection is suppressed in umbrae and penumbrae, so a spot is a clean dark ring on the mottle rather than a dot on tiles. The real rms contrast is ≈ 0.13 at 500 nm and falls toward the limb; 0.12 keeps granule cores below the ramp's clip, so the mottle is a fine texture under the spots and loops, and the smoothstep avoids a hard ring. Worley is skipped for μ < 0.1. Granules evolve on display time, because real 5–10 min lifetimes would flicker at 0.3 d/s, but they rotate with the surface, so the texture itself shows the sphere turning and shearing. *Active regions* (3): mean latitude seeded in 10–25° (Spörer), ±5° per region, either hemisphere, ~120° apart in longitude. The first region starts at −78° (just inside the east limb) with its tallest loop at h = 0.32 R, so loops show at t = 0. Each region is a bipolar pair tilted by Joy's law, γ = 0.5|φ|, with the leading spot equatorward and the spots 2δ = 13–15° apart, so the pair's blended penumbrae (out to 1.07 ρ_p each) never touch: no δ-spots, which are a few % of real groups. Penumbral radius ρ_p is 4–6° for the leading spot and 2.5–4° for the following one, which is absent 40 % of the time. The region rotates rigidly at ω(φ_AR). *Spots*: angular distance from a spot centre is β ≈ √(2(1 − n·s)) (chord, no trig), with q = β/ρ_p. The umbra is 0.22 for q ≤ 0.375 and blends by smoothstep to the penumbra over q ∈ [0.375, 0.525]. Its radius of 0.45 ρ_p gives U/(U+P) ≈ 0.20 and U/P ≈ 0.25, as observed. The penumbra is `0.70 + 0.08 cos(nψ + ψ₀)` (a little darker than the observed ≈ 0.75–0.85, so that with the granulation gated out the ring stands about one ramp step below the intergranular lanes, 0.88, at 80×24) with `n = clamp(round(2π ρ_px / 3), 6, 24)` radial filaments (one per 3 px of penumbral circumference, so they never alias below a pixel; real penumbrae hold hundreds) and blends to 1 over q ∈ [0.93, 1.07]. The umbra is displaced toward disc centre by 0.10 ρ_p sin θ in the sky plane: the Wilson depression, ≈ 2.5× the real d/ρ_p ≈ 0.04 (≈ 10× in absolute depth, since spots are drawn 2–3× oversize). Lifted onto the sphere it grows as 0.10 tan θ, so the centre-side penumbra narrows toward the limb and is covered below μ ≈ 0.2 (≈ 0.08 at the real depth). Faculae brighten outside the spots as `I *= 1 + 0.35(1 − μ)·exp(−(β/3ρ_p)²)`, which shows only near the limb, as observed. Each spot acts only within its own facular cap `cos β ≥ cos 6.5 ρ_p` (where the Gaussian is < 1 %), and faculae never brighten a spot pixel, whatever the spot order. The only per-pixel trig is ψ inside penumbrae. Small discs floor the drawn spot size: each spot is drawn at `ρ_eff = max(ρ_p, min(2.0 px/R, sep/2.2))`, with sep the pair's angular separation, so at 80×24 (R ≈ 22 px, where a 2.5° following spot is ≈ 1 px in penumbral radius and its umbra under half a pixel) every spot is ≥ 2 px with an umbra ≥ 0.9 px and reads as umbra and penumbra. A floored pair's penumbrae still never touch: at 80×24 2δ ≥ 13° > 1.07·(6° + 5.2°), and on smaller discs, where both spots may reach sep/2.2, 2·1.07/2.2 < 1. At 200×50 the floor (2.49°) is under every ρ_p, so no spot is enlarged. This is a display exaggeration like the granule spacing, and it applies to the umbra, filaments, Wilson shift and faculae alike. *Loops*: each region has 3 nested field lines of a horizontal point dipole buried at depth `d_b = 2δ` (in R) below its midpoint, so B_z peaks under the spots. A field line is `p(θ') = c_d + L sin²θ'(cos θ' â + sin θ' ẑ)` with `L = d_b + h`; the apex heights are `h_max·{1, 0.72, 0.48}` (floored at 0.04 R) with `h_max` = 0.20–0.32 R (first region 0.32 R): 139–223 Mm, ≈ 1–1.5× the tallest observed cool H-α and coronal-rain loops, a stated display exaggeration like the granules and the Wilson shift, so the arcade is the focal point at 80×24 (≈ 7 px) and still fits the empty columns beside the disc. The three lines lie in one vertical plane through the region midpoint, turned 45–65° (either sense) from the spot axis: a sheared arcade over the inversion line. That is a display compromise, less sheared than real filament channels (≳ 70°), so the plane stays inclined to the line of sight at either limb and the loops show as nested arches rather than edge-on spikes. Loops with an apex below 2.5 px are not drawn, and on discs with R < 30 px (80×24 is 22) the middle arch is dropped so the outer and inner arches keep a black gap. Footpoints (|p| = R) are found by bisection at spawn. The 56 samples between them are stored in region-local coordinates and rotated each frame through an orthographic `geom::Camera`. Each loop carries 2 knots of coronal rain (Gaussian width 0.05 in σ) on display time (like the granules). A knot condenses within 0.1 σ of the apex and falls down one leg, its distance `a = |σ − ½|` from the apex growing as `ȧ = (0.3 + 1.4a)/T` (T = 5–9 s; closed form `rain_fall`, exact over any step split): slow near the top, accelerating down the leg. It fades in after condensing and out before the footpoint, over 0.05 σ, then condenses again. A sample is *Emission* if its projected radius is ≥ R, and *Filament* if it lies inside the disc with z ≥ 0. Otherwise the sample is *Hidden*. A Filament sample standing higher than 0.35 of its loop's apex darkens its pixel idempotently to `min(level, ramp(Ember, photosphere_value(0.6·I_disc)))`, except inside a spot (photosphere weight < ½), so the thread bridges a group's spots without slashing across their penumbrae: the low legs would trace a ring around the spots, while the elevated arches overlap as one short dark thread along the loop plane, still well above the umbrae (0.22). Cool H-α loops are thus bright against the sky and absorb against the disc. A region that has reached +150° and is fully hidden (all spots μ < 0, no Emission or Filament sample) respawns 0–60° further on with new parameters, so nothing pops on the visible disc. *Chromosphere* *(changed, M12)*: an unbroken 1 px rim at R < r ≤ R + 0.8, at `V_RIM·(0.9 + 0.1·wave)` (`V_RIM` = 0.55), with a glow above it out to the spicule reach `s_h = min(0.035 R, 1.6)` px (real spicules stand ≈ 0.014 R☉; fixed in pixels they would break a small disc's limb into stray pixels and notches) at `0.5·(1 − up/s_h)^1.3·(0.8 + 0.2·wave)`, `up` the height above the rim; a pixel more than 1 px up lights only if the next pixel inward is lit (rooted), and none is drawn below the visible floor. The wave is `½sin(n₁ϑ + 1.3t + φ₁) + ½sin(n₂ϑ − 0.9t + φ₂)`, where n₁ = round(2πR/3) and n₂ = round(2πR/2) are whole numbers of lobes (so the pattern closes around the limb) and ϑ is the limb angle: a smooth glowing ring whose brightness travels round the limb, with no spicule spikes or breaks. *Prominence sheets* *(new, M12)*: under each region's outer (tallest) arch, while it is drawn, points from each loop sample's foot on the surface (the sample pulled in radially to R☉) up to the sample, `clamp(⌈2·h·R⌉, 2, 64)` steps (≈ 2 per pixel of apex height `h`), are classified like loop samples; Emission points glow `Ramp(HAlpha)` at `0.40 → 0.52` from foot to arch, ± 0.04 shimmer `sin(2.2t − 9u + 3f)` (`u` along the loop, `f` up the sheet) travelling along the loop, drawn first so the arches and their rain draw over. **Stylised**: a filled sheet of cool plasma under the arcade, as hedgerow prominences show.
- **Motion**: granules boil with 6–10 s lives while the surface turns at ≈ 3.5 px/s at disc centre (200×50). Features foreshorten toward the limb, so the sphere reads in 3-D. An equatorial spot crosses the disc in ≈ 41 s while high-latitude features visibly lag. Loops rise over the east limb, cross the disc as dark filaments and set over the west limb. Rain knots condense near the loop tops and fall, accelerating, down one leg, the prominence sheets shimmer along the loops, and the chromosphere's glow swells and ebbs in waves round the limb. The label's day counter ticks 0.3 per second. For GEN-R9, chromospheric-glow, sheet and knot pixels change half-block occupancy within 1 s; interior colour changes alone do not change `to_lines` in Unicode mode.
- **Resize**: there is no simulation lattice. Heliographic state survives any resize untouched: regions, knots, granule (φ, λ, age), clock and B0. Resize rebuilds only the view: disc normals, the rim annulus list (pixel, r, ϑ), the bucket grid, the chromosphere's lobe counts and `Pixels`. The granule pool changes only when N_g moves by > 10 % (hysteresis). It is then truncated, or topped up with points that fade in (capacity 2400 is reserved at build). For R < 4 px, granulation and loops are skipped, and nothing panics at 1×1.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure averages the intensities `I` behind the pair's lit `Ember` photosphere values (`photosphere_intensity`, the inverse of `photosphere_value`). On the disc (cell centre inside R) the glyph is `#` for I ≥ 1.026, `*` for ≥ 0.923, `+` for ≥ 0.769, `=` for ≥ 0.59, `:` for ≥ 0.436, `.` for ≥ 0.308, and otherwise a written space, in `photosphere_ink(I)`. Granules read as `#*` mottling, the limb as `:.`, a spot as a `=` ring around a blank umbra, and filaments one step darker. Off the disc the cell takes the brighter pixel: a knot core (≥ 0.60 + 0.9·0.28 ≈ 0.85) is `o`; a cell holding a pixel within 1 px of the limb is the one-cell rim `'`; a loop (≥ the rim/loop midpoint 0.575) is `.`; anything dimmer, including the chromosphere's glow and the prominence sheets, stays blank so loops stand clear of the rim. Empty cells stay untouched.
- **Label**: `day 12.4  Teff 5772 K` (`Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`, CORE-R76), right-aligned on the last row (GEN-R13). The elapsed days (0.3·t, one decimal) are written into a stack buffer, and 5772 K is the IAU nominal effective temperature. The Teff part is dropped first when it would reach the title (`title::readout_room`), then the whole label; there is none when w < 30 or h < 10.
- **Perf** at 200×50: update ≈ 0.37 ms. Ages and longitudes cost ≈ 0.02 ms (1739 granules, ω precomputed per point; respawns; 3 regions and 18 knots); the rest is the rasterisation, which runs in `update`: projecting and bucketing ~870 visible granules ≈ 0.05 ms; disc ≈ 6.6k px × ~12 weighted-distance candidates ≈ 0.25 ms; spots and faculae ≈ 0.03 ms (3 dot products per pixel with early reject); loops (504 samples) and rim ≈ 0.02 ms. Render (blit and label) ≈ 0.03 ms. Total ≈ 0.4 ms (measured 0.42 ms in criterion at load ≈ 14, 2026-09-27), bounded at any size by the 2400-point cap, with no allocation after resize.
- **Inks** *(changed)*:
  - White-light photosphere, spots and faculae: `Ramp(Ember)` at `v = 0.08 + 0.74·I^1.25`. Granules are
    gold (v ≈ 0.91), intergranular lanes ≈ 20 % darker, the limb orange-red, penumbrae scarlet and umbrae
    oxblood (v < 0.2). **False colour**: the real disc is near-white. The power spreads the ramp's
    crowded pale end so the granulation keeps its contrast.
  - Every H-α feature (rim, chromospheric glow, loops, prominence sheets, rain knots) in `Ramp(HAlpha)` with the same values.
  - Filament darkening stays photospheric (`Ember`). Only the arcade's tallest loop
    absorbs, so the filament is a 1 px thread; the nested arches under it thickened it into a bar.
- **Arcade twist** *(new)*: each field line is bowed out of the arcade's vertical plane by
  `0.1·half·sin(π·a/half)` (`a` along the arcade, `half` its half-span): one leg one way, the other the
  other. Seen from above, the arcade and its filament form a gentle S, as sigmoidal filament channels
  do, instead of a straight rod. **Stylised**: the twist is a fixed shape, not a flux-rope model.
- **Rim** *(changed, M12)*: the rim's level follows the wave, `0.55·(0.9 + 0.1·wave)`, and it is
  never broken (it was dropped where the wave fell below −0.35 or −0.6, which read as notches); the
  glow above it fades smoothly outward (*Chromosphere*), so the chromosphere reads as a soft glowing
  ring, not a drawn outline, at every size.
  - ASCII glyphs are chosen by the pair's mean intensity, with the old thresholds.
- **Spectrograph inset** *(new)*:
  - **Slit**: the pixel column through the disc centre. Marked only by jaws, a thin dark
    hairline (`│`, `Dim` 0, over the photosphere's colour; Unicode only) in the first two cells inside
    the limb at the top and at the bottom, like the notches of a slit-jaw image; a hairline across the
    whole disc read as a render seam. (The disc fills the height at 200×50, so the jaws cannot sit
    outside it.)
  - **Axes**: 400–700 nm across, blue left, red right; one cell row per slit position, from the disc's
    top to its bottom.
  - **Colour**: every cell's background is `Phase` at `0.78·(700 − λ)/300` turns, a continuous band from
    red at 700 nm to violet at 400 nm. **False colour**: `Phase` is the only hue loop, and it is
    isoluminant, so it carries no intensity.
  - **Lines**: G 430.8, H-γ 434.0, H-β 486.1, Mg b 518.4, Fe E 527.0, Na D 589.3 and H-α 656.3 nm, each a
    thin dark glyph (`Dim` 0) on its nearest column; G and H-γ share one at this resolution. **Widths
    exaggerated**: a column is ≈ 7.5 nm at 40 cells, some 20–70× the real line widths.
  - **Doppler**: where the granulation under the slit is > 2 % brighter than its limb-darkened mean
    (rising gas) the lines shift blueward (`▏`), where > 2 % darker (sinking) redward (`▕`), else `│`.
    A shifted row keeps its shift until the excess falls inside ±1 % (hysteresis), so a
    row near the threshold does not flicker between glyphs.
    **Stylised**: a real granular shift is ~10⁻³ nm, far below one column.
  - **Umbra**: a slit row whose intensity is < 0.45 is a dark stripe (`▓`, about a quarter of the
    continuum) with heavy lines (`┃`). Rows off the disc stay empty.
  - **Size and placement**: `clamp(w/5, 24, 40)` × `clamp(h/6, 4, 8)` cells at the top right, from cell
    row 1 with a one-cell right margin; kept only if every pixel of it is ≥ 1.35 R from the disc centre
    (clearing the loops, ≤ 1.32 R) and it ends ≥ 2 px above the last row. In practice it shows at 200×50
    and wider.
  - **Label**: ` slit spectrum 400-700nm, false colour ` on row 0 (` slit spectrum 400-700nm ` if the
    inset is too narrow), at `READOUT_LEVEL`.
  - **ASCII**: continuum `=`, lines `|`, umbra `-`; no jaws.
- **Acceptance**: `SUN-A1` `limb_intensity(μ)` is 1 at μ = 1, 0.4 at μ = 0 and 0.7 at μ = 0.5, and strictly increasing; in a 200×50 render at t = 0 with regions off (test hook), the mean `Ember` photosphere level (`photosphere_value`) of disc pixels with μ > 0.8 exceeds that of pixels with μ < 0.3 by ≥ 60 levels (≈ 84 expected); `SUN-A2` `omega_deg_per_day(φ)` is 14.713 at 0°, 14.002 ± 0.001 at ±30° and 11.911 ± 0.001 at ±60°, even in φ and strictly decreasing in |φ|; the equatorial period is 81.56 ± 0.01 s at 0.3 d/s; over 10 s of updates split three ways (40 × 0.25, 600 × 1/60, mixed), a region and a granule that do not respawn in the window each advance by ω(φ)·3 days within 1e-9°; `SUN-A3` `spot_intensity(q, ψ)` is 0.22 for q ≤ 0.375, has azimuthal mean 0.70 ± 0.005 on [0.525, 0.93] with |modulation| ≤ 0.08, and is 1 for q ≥ 1.07; its azimuthal mean is non-decreasing in q, crosses the umbra–penumbra midpoint 0.46 at q = 0.45 ± 0.005 (U/(U+P) = 0.2025) and the penumbra–photosphere midpoint 0.85 at q = 1.00 ± 0.005; `wilson_offset(ρ_p, θ)` points toward disc centre with magnitude 0.10 ρ_p sin θ (1e-9); the filament count is `clamp(round(2π ρ_px/3), 6, 24)` and the azimuthal mean stays 0.70 ± 0.005 for every count in [6, 24], and a rendered 200×50 spot's filaments stand ≥ 2 px apart at mid-penumbra unless held at 6; faculae never brighten a spot pixel (and the result is independent of spot order), and a spot changes no pixel outside its facular cap `cos(6.5 ρ_p)`; a pixel within q ≤ 0.9 of a spot shades identically under any granulation factor; over seeds 0–199 at 40×12, 80×24 and 200×50, every shown spot is drawn at its own `ρ_eff` (above), which at 200×50 is its own ρ_p and at 80×24 gives an umbra of ≥ 0.85 px radius, and every pair with both spots present is strictly more than 1.07(ρ_eff,lead + ρ_eff,follow) apart; `SUN-A4` every loop sample satisfies `|p − c_d| = L sin²θ'` (relative 1e-9) in its vertical plane; both footpoints lie on the photosphere (||p| − R| < 1e-6 R) and every interior sample lies above it; a field line's apex height is L − d_b (1e-9 R); the two footpoints lie on opposite sides of the region midpoint along â; each region's 3 loops lie in one vertical plane through the region midpoint (1e-9), turned 45–65° from the spot axis, with strictly decreasing apex heights and footpoint spans; at 80×24 the first region's tallest apex is ≥ 7 px, every drawn loop clears 2.5 px, the middle arch is dropped and the outer and inner arches stay ≥ 2 px apart, and loop pixels clear of the limb lie in [0.40 − 0.04 − 0.01, 0.60 + 0.28 + 0.01] (*changed, M12*: down to the prominence sheet's values; was from rim + 0.03) with the loop body under the penumbra; in ASCII at 80×24 off-disc cells are only `'` (on the one-cell rim), `.`, `o` or blank, with ≥ 8 loop cells clear of the rim; rain: `rain_fall` composes over split steps (1e-12) and moves a knot > 2.5× faster at a = 0.4 than at the apex, a condensing knot starts within 0.1 σ of the apex at weight 0, and a falling knot moves monotonically toward its footpoint, where its weight is 0; `SUN-A5` `classify(p)` returns Emission iff the projected radius is ≥ R, Filament iff the point is inside the disc with z ≥ 0, and Hidden otherwise; in a 200×50 render with loops on versus off (test hook), off-disc pixels differ only within 1 px of a projected Emission sample of a loop or *(M12)* of the outer arch's prominence sheet, and no on-disc pixel is brighter with loops on; over 80 s at 200×50 (seeds 11 and 42), every on-disc pixel darkened by loops lies within 1 px of a Filament sample standing higher than 0.35 of its loop's apex and outside every spot (photosphere weight ≥ ½), and some filament crosses the disc; `SUN-A6` over 2000 seeded spawns the two spots of a region are 13–15° apart and more than 1.07(ρ_lead + ρ_follow) apart; at 80×24 over 1200 × `update(0.25)` with no renders (< 2 s in debug), exactly 3 regions exist at every step, all with |latitude| in [5°, 30°]; at t = 0 the first region is at −78° with ≥ 1 Emission sample east of the disc; every respawn happens at relative longitude ≥ 150° with all spots μ < 0 and no Emission or Filament sample; `SUN-A7` after warm-up at 200×50 (R = 46 px) `granule_spacing` is 3.6 px at R = 10, 3.91 at R = 46 and 5 at R = 200, and the pool holds exactly N_g = 1739 points, never grows, and every lifetime is in [6, 10] s; with regions off, the rms of I/limb_intensity(μ) about its mean (from `Ember` photosphere values mapped back to I by `photosphere_intensity`) is in [0.06, 0.18] for μ > 0.8 and < 0.6× that value for 0.15 < μ < 0.3; `granule_pattern` is < 0.26 whenever F2 − F1 ≤ 0.1 s_g and F1 ≥ 0.45 s_g (w₁ ≤ 1), and ≥ 0.55 whenever F2 − F1 ≥ 0.3 s_g; the bucketed Worley query returns exact F1/F2 within its 1.5 s_g reach (a site two buckets over is found) and F2 = the reach for a lone site. *M12*: `SUN-A1..A7`: unchanged (the SUN-A4 loop-body bound now reads "below the quiet disc's lanes, intensity 0.8", since the photosphere mapping changed), except:; `SUN-A4` *(changed)*: the arcade test reads "every sample lies within 1.05·0.1·half of the vertical plane through the region centre, its offset the negative of its mirror sample's (an S), and each loop bows by more than half the twist"; `SUN-A8` *(changed)*: no inset at 80×24, one at 200×50; every inset pixel ≥ 1.35 R from the centre and the inset above row h − 2; each line's nearest column is a line column (6 or 7 in all); on the middle row every cell has a `Phase` background and exactly the line columns carry a thin dark line; red end < 0.1 turn, violet end > 0.7 turn; an umbra row (I = 0.2) renders `▓`/`┃` while a rising row below it renders `▏` lines; an off-disc row is empty; the slit's column carries exactly four jaw cells, `│` in `Dim` 0 over `Ember`, two consecutive at each end, the first and last just inside the limb. `doppler_shift` sets a shift beyond ±2 %, holds it down to ±1 %, flips it past the opposite threshold, and changes the glyph once, not every frame, under a ±0.4 % wobble about +2 %; `SUN-A9` *(new)*: `photosphere_value(1.1) − photosphere_value(0.9)` ≥ 15 % of the former; the limb (I = 0.45) lies in [0.25, 0.45) and an umbra (I = 0.22) below 0.2; `photosphere_intensity` inverts it; `SUN-A10` *(new; replaces "the rim breaks into fuzz")*: at 200×50 and 80×24 with loops off, every rim pixel is lit `Ramp(HAlpha)`, and above the rim no lit pixel is brighter than the lit pixel inward of it (the glow never brightens outward); `SUN-A11` *(new)*: at 200×50 the loop pixels clear of the limb (with the sheet) number ≥ 12; sheet values stay under `V_LOOP` (0.52 + 0.04 < 0.60) and above 3× the visible floor; `fill_steps` is at least 2 and at most 64; those pixels change within 0.25 s.

### strange_attractors  (Forces & Waves, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: a slowly turning gallery of six classic chaotic flows: Lorenz's butterfly, Aizawa's shell, Thomas's labyrinth, Chen's two wings (Chen & Ueta 1999), Halvorsen's propeller and Rössler's band. Each attractor threads in its own false-colour palette, so the gallery changes colour as it changes shape. Each is drawn by a swarm of particles leaving short glowing threads on the braille lattice, so the flow itself builds the shape instead of a plotted curve. Every 12 s the swarm flows into the next attractor, with no cut and no fade to black. Each attractor is framed to its own projected extent, so it fills about 0.88 of the height (or of the width, if that is tighter). Each is also seen from its own best elevation: Halvorsen from above its 3-fold axis, so the three blades read, with the frame following the blades so the figure stays centred as they turn. Threads are continuous at every size and the particle count grows only linearly with the lattice, so 200×50 gains resolution but not clutter. The eye goes first to the bright dense cores (the Lorenz wing eyes, the Aizawa tube), then follows the threads outward. A single corner label is the only text.
- **Model**: model time in *tu*. Gallery: Lorenz (1963) `ẋ = σ(y−x), ẏ = x(ρ−z) − y, ż = xy − βz`, σ = 10, ρ = 28, β = 8/3. Aizawa (1982, Langford-type torus flow) `ẋ = (z−b)x − dy, ẏ = dx + (z−b)y, ż = c + az − z³/3 − (x²+y²)(1+ez) + fzx³`, (a,b,c,d,e,f) = (0.95, 0.7, 0.6, 3.5, 0.25, 0.1). Thomas (1999) `ẋ = sin y − bx` (cyclic), b = 0.18 (λ₁ ≈ 0.038). The popular b = 0.19 is a limit cycle, and at the often-quoted b = 0.1998 and 0.208186 orbits stay on one of several coexisting off-centre attractors. Halvorsen (Sprott 2003) `ẋ = −ax − 4y − 4z − y²` (cyclic), a = 1.27 (λ₁ ≈ 0.82); the popular a = 1.89 is periodic (λ₁ ≈ 0). Rössler (1976) `ẋ = −y − z, ẏ = x + ay, ż = b + z(x − c)`, a = b = 0.2, c = 5.7 (λ₁ ≈ 0.071). Starts are fixed in-basin constants, never random: Lorenz (1, 1, 1), Aizawa (0.1, 0, 0), Thomas (0.1, 0, 0), Halvorsen (−1, 0, 0), Rössler (1, 1, 0). Aizawa has a coexisting non-chaotic attractor: from (0.2, 0.1, 0.5), λ₁ ≈ 0 and the z extent is only 82 % of the chaotic one. Each particle is advanced in f64 with `ode::integrate` (RK4) at `h_max` = 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.1 / 0.005 / 0.02 tu, at a model rate `r_k` = 0.33 / 0.69 / 6.5 / 0.31 / 1.6 tu per simulated second. These rates are measured so the mean normalised speed is 1.2 box half-widths/s for every attractor (the peak is ≈ 9/s in Rössler's fold). That is ≤ 2 substeps per particle per 1/60 s. Normalisation: `x̃ = Q_k(x − c_k)/s_k`, where `c_k` is the reference box centre and `s_k` its largest half-extent: Lorenz (0, 0, 24.5)/26.0, Aizawa (0, 0, 0.74)/1.49, Thomas 0/4.2, Halvorsen −3.54·(1,1,1)/9.8, Rössler (1.17, −1.47, 11.36)/11.35. World position `p = x̃/ρ_k` with `ρ_k = max|x̃|` = 1.24 / 1.02 / 1.13 / 1.32 / 1.38, so `|p| ≤ 1` and the perspective stays bounded. `Q_k` maps native z (Lorenz, Aizawa, Rössler) or the (1,1,1) axis (Thomas, Halvorsen) onto the camera's up axis +y, so yaw spins the last two about their 3-fold axis. Ensemble: N = 1024 particles per attractor, sampled from one scout trajectory. The scout starts at the fixed start plus a seeded 10⁻³ Gaussian jitter, burns in for 40 s·r_k, and takes a sample every 0.15 s·r_k. Samples are stored at bit-reversed indices, so every power-of-two prefix is spread along the scout. Two slots hold the current and next ensemble. The next slot is filled during the first 2 s of each hold (≤ 400 scout steps per update, with any remainder finished at the morph boundary); a scout that escapes restarts from the fixed start offset by 10⁻³·i on every axis at its i-th restart, burn-in included, so no transient sample is kept; after 8 restarts it fills the rest of its slot by repeating the samples it has (the start if none), so filling always terminates (unreachable with today's in-basin starts, but `run` is called with an unbounded budget). Morph: at morph start each prefix is put in pairing order: 8 equal-count azimuth sectors about the vertical axis, each cut into 4 equal-count height bands, each band ordered by cylindrical radius (in place with `sort_unstable_by`, ties broken by index). Equal counts keep sector j of one attractor paired with sector j of the other however unevenly their mass is spread (Lorenz's wings). The next slot's prefix is then permuted onto the current slot's indices, so the points on screen are never re-indexed, and point i pairs a_i ↔ b_i. Both slots integrate during the morph, so the pairs decorrelate within about a second, and a plain lerp of two uncorrelated clouds is narrower (its spread falls to `√((1−w)² + w²)` ≈ 0.71 at w = ½): the swarm knotted to 0.65–0.83 of its height mid-morph. So the displayed point is `p_i,j = m_j + g_j·(lerp(a_i,j, b_i,j, w) − m_j)` per world axis j, with `w = smoothstep(s)`, `m_j = lerp(mean a_j, mean b_j, w)`, and `g_j` clamped to [1, √2] so that the blend's spread `(⟨|p_j − m_j|^16⟩)^{1/16}` equals the lerp of the two ensembles' spreads. The factor is measured after every morph step (O(N_eff), allocation-free), and the endpoints w = 0 and 1 are exact. A variance match (power 2) overshot: the extremes decorrelate last (pairs at the top of both stay there while the bulk mixes) and a blend has thinner tails than the shapes it averages, so Aizawa → Thomas spilled past the frame (80×24: 5.6 edge-row cells per morph frame, against 0.07 in holds, and 0.7 at power 16). One gain for all axes turns uncorrelated extremes into corners the shapes never have (4.8), and an order statistic jumped 0.3 world units in one update when a fast particle overtook. At s = 1 the next slot becomes current. Blow-up guard: a particle that is non-finite or has `|x̃|∞ > 3` is replaced by a clone of particle `(i + N_eff/2) mod N_eff` of the simulated prefix, or, if that has escaped too, of the next finite particle (the fixed start only if all have escaped, since it lies off the attractor, e.g. at Lorenz's origin saddle), plus a seeded 10⁻⁶·s_k jitter, so the clone separates by chaos instead of shadowing its source forever.
