- **Motion**: a 0.5 s dark gap, the flash, then the echo rings expand for 21 s, fading as they spread, while the older rings keep expanding beyond them; the loop repeats every 21.5 s over the same dust.
- **Resize**: sheet heights and densities rebuilt per size from the seed (same dust, rescaled).
- **Inks**: echo brightness through an asinh tone that rolls highlights off below white (level ≤ 236, no clipped flat bands), coloured by the outburst temperature at `t_e`: light that left the star at ≥ 5.5 kK (the hot first peak, each ring's leading edge) blue-white `Ramp(Ice)`, the cooler later light `Ramp(Ember)` (rust → ochre → pale gold); echo light below level 52 is shown as lit dust grey (`Ramp(Starlight)` at 60 % of its level, a soft fringe feathering each sheet into the dark instead of a hard edge or dark-red specks), and the echo weight fades beyond 0.95 half-heights over 0.5, so the far rings end cleanly instead of in dark-red streaks; dim unlit dust `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 20; the star `Blackbody`. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Light echo · flash lights dust`.
- **Stylisation**: dust as 4 sheets, not a 3-D cloud; time compressed (4 yr in ~21 s); the outburst stretched ~3× relative to the echo; apparent superluminal expansion is genuine geometry, not moving matter (said in the spec, not the title); the loop repeats the outburst, and the echoes of the two previous loops are kept at 55 % and 25 % so rings are always on screen; V838 Mon erupted once.
- **Acceptance**: `ECHO-A1` the echo paraboloid function satisfies `√(ρ² + z²) − z = c t` for sampled points; `ECHO-A2` for a flat sheet at depth `z0` the lit ring radius is `√(ct(ct + 2 z0))` (increasing, apparent ring speed exceeding `c` early); `ECHO-A3` dust pixels never move: the unlit dust density map is identical at two times; `ECHO-A4` a node lit by one (sheet, flash) pair carries the light curve's temperature at its `t_e` (`T(t_e)` non-increasing in `t_e`, so a ring's leading edge is the hottest); `ECHO-A5` a flash lights no dust before it happens (only older echoes show before loop time 0); `ECHO-A6` no echo pixel exceeds level 236, and both the hot blue-white and the cool amber light show over a loop, the amber dominating; `ECHO-A7` the lit dust is smooth, not blocky or ragged: at 120×40 over a loop the mean level step between 4-neighbouring echo pixels is ≤ 19 and ≤ 2.5 % of echo pixels are specks (fewer than two echo 4-neighbours).

### planetary_nebula  (Deep Cosmos, M13)
- **Intent**: a dying Sun-like star's last breaths: faint, evenly spaced red shells puffed off over millennia around a cool giant, then a fast wind from the exposed hot core blowing a bright bipolar bubble inside them, glowing teal and red like the Cat's Eye.
- **Model**: projected emissivity of thin spherical/ellipsoidal shells (analytic limb brightening: projected path through a shell of finite thickness). Act 1: AGB rings, shell `k` launched at `t_k = (k − 5)·τ` (τ = 2.2 s, five already out when the loop starts) expanding at `v_s`, so rings are evenly spaced (9 rings), fading softly with radius well before the outermost, over a faint haze of the giant's dusty wind; rings and haze are modulated by a clumpy domain-warped texture fixed to the gas, which also nudges each ring's radius by up to ±4 % so the shells do not read as a target, and the faint outskirts thin out through a fixed dither between levels 8.5 and 12 instead of stopping in one hard edge. Act 2: interacting stellar winds: the fast wind inflates an inner bubble `R_b(t)` (quickly, then creeping on through act 3) in the slow-wind density `∝ r^{-2}` with an equatorial enhancement, giving a prolate/bipolar shape `R_b(θ) = R_b·(1 + 0.6 cos² θ)` with a pinched waist, and the Cat's Eye's second, smaller bubble tilted 43° to it; ionisation: the central star heats to 100 kK and an ionisation front sweeps out; [O III] inside (textured, and a faint halo beyond the bubble), [N II] at the rim and in low-ionisation knots (FLIERs) at the tips, which drift outward and flicker.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: shells (6 s) → core exposed, bubble inflates (8 s) → ionised nebula (26 s) → fade (5 s); the axis turns slowly and precesses (the Cat's Eye's point symmetry).
- **Resize**: all geometry in units of the half-height; buffers rebuilt.
- **Inks**: [O III] `Ramp(Aurora)`, [N II] `Ramp(HAlpha)` (658 nm sits beside Hα), AGB rings and haze `Ramp(Ember)` ≤ 80 (faint red), central star `Blackbody` (3.2 kK giant heating to 30 kK, the scale's top). ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Planetary nebula · winds collide`; captions `shells`, `fast wind`, `ionised` replace the mechanism per act.
- **Stylisation**: narrowband false colour; shells are analytic, not hydrodynamics; ~10⁴ yr in 45 s.
- **Acceptance**: `PNE-A1` ring radii are evenly spaced (spacing variance < 1 %) and each ring expands at `v_s`; `PNE-A2` the bubble is prolate: polar radius / equatorial radius ≥ 1.4 at full size; `PNE-A3` [O III] brighter than the faint halo lies inside the bubble, and [N II] at its rim or tips (≥ 80 % within 3 px of the rim); `PNE-A4` limb brightening: a shell's peak brightness sits at its limb, within one shell thickness plus 1 px of its projected edge; `PNE-A5` the caption follows the Timeline act.

### jwst_unfolding  (Voyages, M13)
- **Intent**: the James Webb Space Telescope unfolding after launch: the fairing opens on the folded observatory, the sunshield pallets drop, the mid-booms stretch the shield and its five layers tension one by one, the secondary mirror swings out, the gold mirror wings fold into place, and the observatory cruises into its halo orbit around L2, the Earth and Moon small behind.
- **Model**: a diorama driven by a `Timeline` of deployment beats (the real sequence, compressed). Parts are convex polygons in a 3-D body frame (sunshield layers as two halves of an elongated hexagon each, the primary as an explicit 18-hex honeycomb: three panels (the centre's 12 segments round the open central hole, and the two 3-segment wing columns) rasterised per `Pixels` pixel by mapping the pixel back into the mirror plane (flat-top axial hex lookup), each segment a flat pale-gold fill with a one-pixel dark backplane gap measured in pixel space, the hole dark grey, an edge-on wing a flat gold strip; bus, solar array and fairing halves) projected with `geom::Camera` (then rolled 0.08 rad about the screen centre), painted far to near with per-polygon gradients, a specular band sweeping across the gold (each segment lit as a whole, keeping its flat fill) and a sheen across the Kapton; the secondary a gold dot in a dark baffle on a three-strut tripod of dark struts; edges and struts in braille dots, depth-tested against the painted surfaces. Each beat is an eased angle or extension: pallets fold down, the tower lifts the telescope, the mid-booms extend, then the layers tension from the Sun-facing layer 1 up to layer 5. Final act: a halo orbit drawn as a Lissajous-like loop around L2 in the rotating frame, with the telescope moving along it and the Earth–Moon in the distance.
- **Motion**: beats: fairing (3.5 s), pallets (5 s), tower (3 s), mid-booms and layers tension (10 s), secondary (4 s), wings (7 s), orbit (16 s); the camera sways.
- **Resize**: camera fit rebuilt; state in the Timeline.
- **Layout**: the deployed shield spans ~60–65 % of the width, seen as a thin tilted diamond (camera pitch 0.4 rad, roll 0.08 rad, yaw 0.14 ± 0.12 rad so the mirror faces the viewer) with the five layers' near edges visible under the top sheet (layers drawn lowest-first so the top sheet is never notched); stowed, the camera sees the stack in profile (yaw 1.1 rad, swinging round as the pallets drop): the telescope with its wings folded, the pallets two grey-silver slabs folded up beside it, the bus a box right under their hinge line and the solar array a thin panel hinged on the bus, one connected body.
- **Inks**: mirror the top of `Ramp(Ember)`: pale gold (level ~214 ≈ #E8C66A, ±9 per segment) to a near-white specular glint, a narrow band sweeping the frame every 14 s; sunshield top layer `Starlight` 172–204 (silver Kapton, with the sheen), the four layers beneath pale-grey `Starlight` ≤ 136, violet (`Ramp(Plasma)` ≤ 158) only on the lower layers' shadowed edges (line work; no pink or violet fills); the honeycomb's gaps `Starlight` 24, its central hole 34; stowed pallets `Starlight` with silver edges; structure and fairing `Starlight`, solar array `Ramp(Cobalt)`, Earth `Ramp(Cobalt)`, Moon `Starlight`. ASCII: blocks by level, lines by braille count.
- **Title**: `JWST · unfolding in space`; captions `fairing opens`, `sunshield opens`, `layers tension`, `mirror unfolds`, `halo orbit at L2`.
- **Stylisation**: two weeks in ~48 s (the fairing opened on launch day, the rest over the following two weeks); sizes, layer spacing and orbit not to scale; the halo orbit is an illustrative loop, not a computed CR3BP solution.
- **Acceptance**: `JWST-A1` beats happen in the documented order (fairing, pallets, tower, mid-booms, layers, secondary, wings); `JWST-A2` the five layers tension one at a time, Sun-facing layer 1 first; `JWST-A3` the mirror is fully deployed (all 18 hexes visible) at the end of the wings beat, each wing's three hexes showing once it has swung out (folded, a wing hides behind the centre panel); `JWST-A4` captions follow the Timeline; `JWST-A5` after the loop the diorama resets to stowed; `JWST-A6` fully deployed, ≥ 90 % of the mirror's gold pixels are pale gold (level ≥ 190) and the shield spans 55–72 % of the width at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50; `JWST-A7` the primary reads as JWST's honeycomb: ≥ 30 neighbouring segment pairs are separated by a dark gap between gold, the centre is a dark hole and the secondary shows as gold in front of it; stowed (5 s), the camera looks at the stack in profile and the bus joins the stack (no gap between bus and mirror); `JWST-A8` deployed, the shield is silver (> 40 top-sheet pixels ≥ 160) with no pink or violet fills at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50.

### orbital_rendezvous  (Voyages, M13)
- **Intent**: "slow down to catch up": a chaser spacecraft behind a space station fires its engine *backward*, drops to a lower, faster orbit, overtakes, and climbs back to meet it, shown both over the Earth's limb and in the station's own frame, where the path is a looping cycloid.
- **Model**: Clohessy–Wiltshire equations in the target's LVLH frame, closed form (no integration); impulsive burns at scheduled times: a retrograde phasing burn, cycloid hops (each orbit the chaser returns to the station's altitude further along), a prograde burn that stops the drift at the hold point, then a scripted approach along V-bar to the docking port. Main panel, scaled per axis to the trajectory zoomed onto the V-bar (the station at 76 % of the width, the chaser starting at 8 %, and ~68 % of the height above the limb, the station's altitude at 20 % and the hops' lowest dip at 75 %): the path still to fly a dim solid braille line, the flown path a bright solid braille line fading with age over a faint glow along its last 4 s, a star where each burn fired, the two CW burns labelled with their Δv (`Δv −0.48 m/s`, `Δv +0.48 m/s`: the model's Δv for a 20 km starting gap and a 92.6-min orbit) two rows above, clear of the station, and while a burn fires a plume flash (a small bright glow at the engine end) with a thruster puff (a spreading cone of exhaust dots opposite the Δv) and a Δv arrow; an 8-cell station sprite along V-bar (docking port facing the chaser, pressurised modules, two truss joints each with solar-array wings above and below) and a 6-cell chaser (solar array, hull, nose), held off the station so it docks at the port. Inertial inset (top right, from 100×30): a lit Earth globe (Lambert-shaded toward the Sun, soft terminator, a little cloud) filling 72 % of the station's orbit radius, the orbit and both craft; stars are kept out of the inset and from behind the labels. The Earth's limb along the bottom (a band ~15 % of the height), each pixel shaded by its signed distance to the limb (antialiased below the half-block): a faint airglow, a bright atmospheric rim, a deep ocean under soft cloud streaks (stretched, warped noise, foreshortened toward the limb) streaming past.
- **Motion**: `Timeline` of acts (40 s loop): phasing burn (2 s), overtaking (19 s = two 10 s orbits of cycloid hops), raise orbit (3 s, the prograde burn), docking (16 s: V-bar approach, arrival, braking at the port).
- **Resize**: panel layout and scales rebuilt; state is closed-form in time.
- **Inks**: chaser and path `Ramp(Aurora)`, station `Ramp(Ferroin)` 140–200 with a `Starlight` module, chaser hull `Starlight` and solar array `Ramp(Cobalt)`, Earth limb, ocean and clouds `Ramp(Ice)` (clouds at its white top), burn markers and arrows `Accent`, exhaust and plume flash `Ramp(Sulfur)`. ASCII: paths by braille count, craft `-=[##>`, station `=[#+H+#]` with `#` wings.
- **Title**: `Rendezvous · brake to catch up`; captions `phasing burn`, `overtaking`, `raise orbit`, `docking`.
- **Stylisation**: burns impulsive; the final approach is scripted along V-bar (continuous thrust implied), not free CW motion; the main panel's axes are scaled independently (the radial axis exaggerated to fit the height) and distances in the inset are exaggerated; time compressed; the Δv labels assume a 20 km gap and the real orbital period, so they are true to the CW model at that scale.
- **Acceptance**: `RDV-A1` the CW closed form satisfies the CW ODEs (finite-difference residual < 1e-6); `RDV-A2` after the retrograde phasing burn the chaser's along-track position gains on the target (moves toward it) while its altitude drops; `RDV-A3` with no burns a chaser at the same altitude stays fixed in LVLH; `RDV-A4` the approach ends within 1 % of the docking point; `RDV-A5` captions follow the burns; `RDV-A6` at 80×24 to 500×150 the path spans > 60 % of the width and 60–80 % of the height above the limb, its lowest dip stays above the limb, and the chaser never enters the station's sprite; `RDV-A7` the inset is drawn only from 100×30; `RDV-A8` the view is zoomed onto V-bar: the station sits at 74–78 % of the width, both sprites are 6–8 cells wide, and the path still to fly is a solid line (every planned sample lit); `RDV-A9` the phasing and raise burns carry their Δv label, whose number is `dv_ms(Δv)` (0.48 m/s) with the burn's sign, fully on screen and left of the station's sprite at 80×24 to 500×150.

### catalytic_surface  (Reactions, M13)
- **Intent**: a platinum surface seen atom by atom as carbon monoxide burns on it: CO and oxygen land, meet, and leave as CO₂ in tiny flashes; as the CO fraction in the gas is swept, the surface swings from oxygen-choked to reactive to CO-choked, with islands nucleating and flipping.
- **Model**: Ziff–Gulari–Barshad lattice model on a capped square lattice (≤ 200×100): pick a site; with probability `y` try CO adsorption (one empty site), else O₂ dissociative adsorption (two neighbouring empty sites); adjacent CO + O react to CO₂ which leaves (flash). Rejection-free batches: ≤ 12k attempts per update. `y` sweeps along a `Timeline` through the two kinetic phase transitions (`y₁ ≈ 0.39`, `y₂ ≈ 0.525`), with slow CO desorption so the CO-poisoned phase can recover (a 0.4 % desorption channel per attempt: CO always leaves, O a tenth of the time, so the O-poisoned phase also recovers). The gas is fed as a jet onto the centre: the local `y` falls by 0.14·r from the centre (r the distance over the half-diagonal), so each transition sits on a ring that moves with the sweep. The jet's `y` follows a looping 36.5 s Timeline: 5 s at 0.545 (CO islands nucleate at the centre), 7 s up to 0.65 (the CO disc grows to the corners), 2.5 s held, 9 s down to 0.53 (O eats the disc back along a ring front), 5 s held (reactive), 4.5 s down to 0.43 (the O layer closes in from the corners), 0.5 s held and 3 s back: the O-poisoned caption is a ≈ 5 s beat, and somewhere on the surface the local `y` is always inside the window.
- **Motion**: the loop opens part-way through the fall from 0.65 (at ≈ 19 s, jet `y` ≈ 0.59): rust CO and teal O domains already meet along a sparking front at t = 0, and the CO disc then recedes as it does every loop; fronts move as `y` sweeps; sparks throughout; the loop takes 36.5 s.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012, nearest site) and the gradient re-laid; the first lattice starts split the way the opening `y` splits it (CO where the local `y` exceeds `y₂` = 0.525, ≈ 40 % of the sites, O around it, 10 % vacancies by a fixed hash), is pre-rolled 12 sweeps at that `y`, then runs a tenth of a second of frames so the front already sparks.
- **Inks** (M14, ADR-0017: the lattice is the art): one half-block pixel per site, nearest (1:1 up to the cap), on black. Empty platinum is the terminal's black, O a grey just off it (`Ramp(Starlight)` 20; the Ice ramp has no near-black foot), CO a muted rust (`Ramp(Sienna)` 80) and its islands' edge sites (CO with a non-CO neighbour, periodic) brighter (`Sienna` 185), so the edges, where the reaction runs, carry the light and islands are not flat plateaus. A deterministic 4 % sample of the CO₂ events blinks its site near white (`Starlight` 170–250 by brightness), fading with τ = 0.1 s (gone in ≈ 0.3 s), exposed per update and drawn once per frame (GEN-R20). No blur, glow or time smoothing. ASCII: a density ramp by brightness (`raster::ascii_ramp_cell`). Readout `CO fraction y=0.54  CO2 <count>` (or `y=0.54`).
- **Title**: `Catalysis · CO burns on platinum`; captions `oxygen-poisoned`, `reactive`, `CO-poisoned`.
- **Stylisation**: a lattice-gas model (ZGB), not Pt(110) with its surface reconstruction; the desorption channel is a stylisation so both poisoned phases recover within the loop (plain ZGB poisons irreversibly); only a sample of the reactions is drawn as sparks; the PEEM images are greyscale, the colours are false.
- **Acceptance**: `CAT-A1` at `y = 0.3` the surface fills with O (coverage ≥ 0.9 after the relaxation time) and at `y = 0.6` with CO; `CAT-A2` at `y = 0.45` the CO₂ production rate stays > 0 and coverages stay mixed; `CAT-A3` adsorption never overfills a site and O₂ needs two empty neighbours; `CAT-A4` attempts per update never exceed the cap; `CAT-A5` captions follow the surface: `oxygen-poisoned` when O coverage ≥ 0.8, `CO-poisoned` when CO coverage ≥ 0.8, else `reactive`; the sweep reaches all three; `CAT-A6` over a loop the surface is poisoned (either caption) ≤ 14 s and CO₂ production never stalls (> 0.01 per site per sweep in every half second), and the gas gradient falls by `GRADE` from the centre to the corners; `CAT-A7` (M14) empty sites are black, O and CO their fixed inks, a CO edge site brighter than an inner one, a blink near white (≥ 230 at full) and dimmer as it fades, below its threshold the site's own ink; at 80×24 (1:1 sites and pixels) the front blinks from the first frame and, with no new events, every blink is gone within 18 updates (0.3 s). `CAT-A8` the loop opens mid-window (jet `y` in [0.52, 0.60]) on balanced domains (CO holds 35–55 % of the occupied sites), with sparks already lit and the `reactive` caption.

### kelvin_helmholtz  (Forces & Waves, M13)
- **Intent**: two layers of fluid sliding past each other: the interface ripples, curls into cat's-eye billows like wave clouds, neighbouring billows pair and merge, and the layer thickens into turbulence before it is reset.
- **Model**: D2Q9 TRT lattice Boltzmann (the vortex_street kernel, lifted into core as `lbm`, CORE-R98/R99) on a capped lattice (≤ 16 384 nodes, the display's square-pixel aspect clamped to [0.75, 4] and bucketed by 1.25), periodic in both axes, so the flow is the classic *double* shear layer `u = U[tanh((y − H/4)/δ) − tanh((y − 3H/4)/δ) − 1]` (no walls to shed their own vorticity; the two rows of billows spin in opposite senses); `U = 0.1`, `τ = 0.508` (Λ = ¼), `δ = max(H/28, 2.5)` (Re = Uδ/ν ≈ 95–200 with the aspect bucket; 121 at 200×50); the node budget is also capped at the display's half-block pixel count (never below 1 200 nodes), so small displays simulate a smaller block with fewer, larger billows instead of a minified one. Each interface is seeded with a small transverse velocity (Gaussian envelope of width 2δ) at the wavenumber nearest `λ ≈ 12δ` with an even count `n` across the width (the fastest-growing mode once the layer has thickened at this resolution) and its subharmonic `n/2` (pairing), phases from `ctx.rng`. A passive dye marks the middle band, advected semi-Lagrangian (clamped Catmull–Rom) with a weak interface sharpening (stylisation). A view whose pixels span more than 1.3 nodes box-filters the picture before sampling. 4 LBM steps per update; 1 s pre-roll in the first resize of a lattice; the act re-seeds after 36 s (before the under-resolved late turbulence can go unstable), or when the watchdog (1 s cadence) finds ρ outside [0.8, 1.2] or |u| ≥ 0.3, or when the row-mean flow keeps less than a quarter of the laid-down shear (the merged vortices have reorganised the block: turbulence, then reset).
- **Motion**: ripples at once, billows by ~3 s, pairing by ~9 s into half as many large cat's-eye vortices, which then turn slowly until the reset.
- **Resize**: within a bucket nothing changes; a new bucket gets its own lattice and a fresh act (the seeded modes depend on the lattice, so resampling the old flow would seed the wrong ones). The first act's 60-update pre-roll runs 15 updates in `resize` and one extra per `update` after it until paid (GEN-R19: ≈ 9 ms instead of ≈ 34 ms at 500×150).
- **Inks** (M14, ADR-0017): lines only, on black. The interfaces, where the passive dye crosses ½, are thin pale lines (`Ramp(Starlight)`, neutral so their anti-aliased edge dims to grey, not to a saturated navy) at `230·k²(3 − 2k)·(0.45 + 0.55·s)`, where `s` is |ω| in units of the saturation value and `k = 1 − d/1.1` for the pixel's distance `d` to the `c = ½` contour, estimated per pixel as `|c − ½| / |∇c|` in pixels: a line about a pixel thin with a smooth falloff at any zoom, brightest where the sheet spins. Everything else is the terminal's black (the dye bands are not drawn). GEN-R17 exception: dye, spin and slope are sampled bilinearly at any magnification, because the line is anti-aliased by its distance, not by the sampling, and no field is drawn as a filled area, so Catmull-Rom would only double the per-pixel cost at 500×150. A minified display box-filters dye and spin first. ASCII by strength (`. : - = # @`). Readout `Re 121` right-aligned on the title row when it fits.
- **Title**: `Shear billows · layers roll up`.
- **Stylisation**: 2-D, low Reynolds number, periodic domain; perturbation seeded for a clean first roll-up.
- **Acceptance**: `KH-A1` the lifted `lbm` reproduces the vortex_street results (its lattice after 2 s at 200×50 is bit-identical to the pre-lift kernel's, and its snapshots are unchanged), and the scene steps exactly the core lattice; `KH-A2` the interface perturbation's amplitude grows exponentially early (log-linear fit R² > 0.9); `KH-A3` the number of vortices (vorticity extrema along the interface) decreases after pairing; `KH-A4` total mass is conserved to 1e-6 relative over 10 s (f32 rounding, CORE-R99); `KH-A5` worst frame < 2 ms at 200×50 on the stepping frame (measured by `rs-worst`; a test pins the node cap × steps per update that bounds it).

### lichtenberg_discharge  (States of Matter, M13)
- **Intent**: a block of acrylic charged by an electron beam, discharged by a tap: a brilliant branching tree burns inward from the edge, fractal and fern-like; across acts the branching thins from dense coral to sparse lightning, and a final act shows the same Laplacian growth as viscous fingering in a Hele-Shaw cell.
- **Model**: `laplacian_growth` (shared `pub(crate)`): dielectric breakdown model on a capped lattice (≤ 160×80): potential φ solved by warm-started SOR (a few sweeps per update, residual-capped), the tree at φ = 0, the boundary at φ = 1; a perimeter site joins with probability `∝ |∇φ|^η` (η = 0.75 dense coral, 1.5 fern, 3 lightning-like). Tree nodes are a fixed-capacity pool (parent links) with a reset beat when the tree reaches the target or capacity; growth age ink. Viscous-fingering act: same model with η = 1 and an injection point (low-viscosity fluid pushing high). Growth is paced: sites are added two per perimeter snapshot, at most 24 per update, while the tree's depth (lightning: its reach) trails an eased 8.5 s schedule. Dense and fern are tapped at the bottom edge, with the top edge charged and insulating sides; they complete when the depth reaches 0.85 of the block. Lightning comes from the top toward a grounded bottom edge and completes on touch. Fingering injects at the centre with all edges at pressure and completes at 0.9 of the half-height. The scene opens 2 s into the first act with its tree pre-grown in `resize`; a lattice change pre-grows the fresh tree to the act's schedule. SOR is red–black, at most 10 sweeps per update, stopping once the largest update is below 2·10⁻⁴.
- **Motion**: up to 24 new sites per update. Each act is 13 s: growth, a near-white completion flash that cools through violet, a glow, a 0.8 s fade, and the next act's tree starts at once.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded; tree reset.
- **Inks**: one half-block layer: each link is stroked as an antialiased channel whose radius (`0.35 + 1.25·b²` pixels, +0.6 in the fingering act) and level (`b(0.25 + 0.75·b)`) follow b = 0.2 + 0.8·(log current share, the current being the subtree size), wrapped in a halo falling to zero over 1.4 pixels; strokes combine by maximum, not sum, so a dense coral stays a tree of distinct channels: a near-white trunk thinning through lavender to hair-fine indigo tips (`Ramp(Plasma)`; fingering `Ramp(Cobalt)`). New tips flash bright for 0.5 s but keep their width. Acrylic is black. ASCII: the tree by braille dot count (`. : + #`).
- **Title**: `Lichtenberg figure · breakdown`; captions `dense`, `fern`, `lightning`, `fingering`.
- **Stylisation**: DBM on a square lattice (weak lattice anisotropy); the acrylic figure is really a 3-D tree seen in projection; time slowed by ~10⁹ (a nanosecond discharge shown over seconds).
- **Acceptance**: `LICH-A1` SOR converges: residual falls below 10⁻³ within 60 sweeps from a warm start after growth; `LICH-A2` the tree stays connected (every node's parent is in the tree); `LICH-A3` the fractal dimension estimate (box counting at the end of growth), summed over 3 seeds, is larger at η = 1 than at η = 3 by > 0.15; `LICH-A4` the pool never exceeds capacity and resets at the beat; `LICH-A5` captions follow η (dense 0.75, fern 1.5, lightning 3, fingering 1).

### quantum_double_slit  (Forces & Waves, M13)
- **Intent**: electrons from a steady source meet a wall with two slits: the wave spreads through both into a fan of bright lobes and dark nodal rays, and on the screen behind, single dots appear one at a time until the fringes emerge from the clicks. A last act aims the same beam at a thin barrier that it partly tunnels through.
- **Model**: 2-D time-dependent Schrödinger equation on a capped grid (≤ 11 000 nodes, view aspect 1–3) with ADI Crank–Nicolson (unitary), a complex tridiagonal (Thomas) solver with caller-owned scratch (CORE-R99; the line matrices of both potentials factored once per grid), absorbing complex potential at the edges, 2 ADI steps per update. A soft line source near the left absorber adds `S·g(y)·e^{−iφn}` after every step (`g` Gaussian, σ = 0.15 of the view height, lighting the two slits; `φ` the step's eigenphase for the carrier `k`), so a monochromatic beam drives a steady diffraction pattern; a new grid starts from the analytic incident beam and runs 2× fast for 2 s. Clicks arrive at a rate proportional to the probability current reaching the screen column (as a share of the beam's current) at positions sampled from that current (`j = 2 Im(ψ*ₓψₓ₊₁)`, clamped ≥ 0) with `ctx.rng` via `InverseCdf`; clicks accumulate (pool 4000) and a per-row click histogram glows behind them.
- **Motion**: the fan is up within ~1 s of any start or resize and stays; acts of 20 s: `interference` (full exposure, ~30 clicks/s), `one at a time` (wave held at 92 %, so the fan never reads as fading; ~10 clicks/s, the histogram glow builds the bands), `tunnelling` (the beam on a V₀ = 1.6, 2-node barrier: reflected standing wave left, weak transmitted wave right at the same exposure).
- **Resize**: grid regridded and the beam restarted from the analytic incident beam; click histogram kept in screen fractions.
- **Inks**: the wave as a soft single-hue glow, `Ramp(Aurora)` (a phosphor green): brightness `|ψ|^0.65` against the per-side peak (the source side at 55 %), a soft knee instead of a hard cut, so faint density fades smoothly to black; where the view resolves the carrier a 25 % shimmer of `Re ψ/|ψ|` rides on it, so wavefronts stream through the steady fan of lobes and dark nodal rays. Walls `Ramp(Starlight)` 111, barrier 71, screen 55; the detector strip (≈ 1/6 of the width) glows `Ramp(Starlight)` 18–170 behind the clicks by where they landed (faded in over the first 500 clicks), and the clicks are warm-white dots, 255 settling to 185. ASCII: the glow's level on the ASCII ramp, walls `|`, the strip's glow `.`/`:`.
- **Title**: `Double slit · waves of chance`; captions `interference`, `one at a time`, `tunnelling`.
- **Stylisation**: units chosen so fringes are visible; a steady monochromatic beam stands for many electrons, and single clicks are sampled from the arriving current (Born rule): the wave itself is not observable; the density is shown as a glow rather than `arg ψ` (Phase inks carry no brightness).
- **Acceptance**: `QDS-A1` the source-free ADI step preserves the norm to 1e-10 without absorbing boundaries; `QDS-A2` the fringe spacing at the screen (between the dark fringes either side of the centre) matches `λL/d` within 10 %, with the lattice's paraxial wavelength `λ = 2π/sin k`; `QDS-A3` with one slit closed the fringes vanish (visibility < 0.2 over one fringe period on the open slit's axis); `QDS-A4` the click histogram converges to the arriving current j⁺ at the screen (the sampled profile; χ² test on 4000 clicks); `QDS-A5` the share of the beam's current transmitted through the barrier falls with its width; `QDS-A6` the source settles to the analytic beam (within 10 %, steady over a carrier period), and the fan lights ≥ 60 % of the rows next to the screen within 1.5 s of a start at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50; `QDS-A7` every act shows the wave at ≥ 90 % of full exposure.

### radical_chain  (Reactions, M13)
- **Intent**: hydrogen and oxygen in a vessel: at low pressure a few radicals wander and die on the walls, and nothing happens; step over the explosion limit and every radical makes two, the population doubles again and again, and the vessel flashes pale blue, leaving water behind.
- **Model**: radicals (pool 4000) random-walk in a vessel (a rounded rectangle); per step a radical branches (H + O₂ → OH + O, O + H₂ → OH + H: one becomes two) with probability `k_b(T)·p·g` (Arrhenius; ∝ p, a collision with O₂; `g` the local unburnt gas), terminates at the walls (on contact) or by three-body recombination with probability `k_t·p²` (H + O₂ + M: ∝ [O₂][M]); heat released per event deposits into a coarse temperature grid (≤ 100×50) that relaxes toward the wall temperature, feeding back into `k_b` (thermal runaway). Water product accumulates as a density field. Pressure `p` sweeps along a `Timeline` across the first explosion limit (at p ≈ 1 in these units); the second limit, where three-body recombination (∝ p²) overtakes branching (∝ p), lies at p = K_B0/K_T3 = 12, far above the sweep. The diffusion coefficient falls as 1/p, calibrated so the wall loss of the slowest mode equals the branching rate at p = 1 (the first limit). A radical–radical recombination term K_RR·n/capacity quenches the flash, and fuel is consumed per branching, globally and in a coarse unburnt-gas grid (4.5 branchings spend a cell; burnt gas adds 6/s of recombination), so the explosion runs as a front (a Fisher–KPP pulse) from the spark and burns out behind it. Initiation runs only in the slow phase and once the vent is below the limit (fresh gas flows back in at 1.5/s). The loop is 16 s: slow 2.5 s at p = 0.6 (25 seed radicals), a rise over 1.5 s to p = 2.2, burn 6.5 s, vent 5.5 s (p falls below the limit within its first third); when the burn begins (p = 2.2) an ignition spark (a Gaussian hot spot, +1.6 in T, with 30 radicals, replacing the stray ones, near the centre) starts the flame there.
- **Motion**: slow chains with fading trails (radicals wander, fork and die at the walls) for the first seconds; a flame kernel at the spark within ≈ 1 s of the burn, a bright front sweeping the vessel by ≈ 6 s with dimming trails behind it; then a violet afterglow fading over ≈ 3–4 s (the vessel is never empty) and fresh slow chains.
- **Resize**: vessel geometry in vessel units; grids regridded; pools kept.
- **Inks** (M14, ADR-0017): a black vessel, its wall a faint dotted outline (every third wall dot, `Starlight` 45). While fewer than 300 radicals are alive, each is one braille dot in `Ramp(Plasma)` 235 (the subject) with a violet 1-px chain trail (a `Glow` stroke from its last position, kept ×0.72 per update, toned and drawn up to `Plasma` 200). The flash is the gas's heat: `2·(T − 1) + 0.9·afterglow` per heat node (the afterglow decays with τ = 1.8 s), softened by one [1 2 1] pass, Catmull-Rom sampled (CORE-R102) and toned; it ramps in from toned level 48 (single reactions' heat stays black) as `255·a^1.6` of the excess `a` above it into `Ramp(Ember)`, black → red → orange → a white-hot core (a dim edge, a steep bright core), and inside the flame it wins over the trails. The water mist and the ambient glow are gone. `explosion` latches once the pool passes an eighth of its capacity or T exceeds the spark by 1.5. ASCII by level. The trails are exposed per update and the picture rasterised once per drawn frame (GEN-R20).
- **Title**: `Explosion · radicals multiply`; captions `slow reaction`, `chain branching`, `explosion` (they follow the state: `slow reaction` while p is below the limit; above it `chain branching` until the explosion latches, once the pool passes an eighth of its capacity or T exceeds the spark by 1.5, then `explosion` until the vent takes p back below the limit).
- **Stylisation**: a two-step branching scheme stands for the full H₂–O₂ mechanism; the radical count saturates at the pool (the flash takes over); time scales stretched from milliseconds.
- **Acceptance**: `CHN-A1` with branching off, the radical count never increases; `CHN-A2` when branching exceeds termination the count grows exponentially (log-linear fit, positive rate) and when it is below it decays; `CHN-A3` a radical that reaches the wall is removed; `CHN-A4` the pool never exceeds capacity and branching stops (without panic) when it is full; `CHN-A5` the temperature grid stays finite and relaxes to the wall value after the flash; `CHN-A6` the flame runs as a front: when `explosion` latches, < 25 % of the vessel is hot and < 10 % burnt, later > 30 % is hot at once, and after the flame goes out the afterglow keeps the vessel lit for 1.5–6 s; `CHN-A7` (M14) the ground is black (no flash and no trail draw nothing, nor faint ones), the flash is `Ember` ≥ 220 at its hottest and grows with the heat from its threshold, a heat below the threshold draws nothing, a chain trail is `Plasma` ≤ 200 and the flash wins over it, and burnt gas quenches branching. `CHN-A8` three-body recombination scales as p² and branching as p, so their ratio doubles when the pressure doubles. `CHN-A9` over two loops the caption is `slow reaction` exactly when p < 1, `explosion` exactly when p ≥ 1 and the explosion has latched, and `chain branching` otherwise; the spark strikes at the start of the burn and the explosion first latches during the burn.

### spinodal_decomposition  (States of Matter, M13)
- **Intent**: a well-mixed grey liquid suddenly unmixes like oil and water: fine worm-like domains appear everywhere, then coarsen, the small ones swallowed by the large ones; a second act at an off-critical mix breaks into droplets that ripen.
- **Model**: `cahn_hilliard` (shared `pub(crate)`): Oono–Puri cell-dynamics system on a capped periodic lattice (≤ 16 000 nodes): `Γ = A tanh ψ − ψ + D(⟨⟨ψ⟩⟩ − ψ)`, `ψ ← ψ − M(⟨⟨Γ⟩⟩ − Γ)`, with `⟨⟨X⟩⟩ − X = 0.3·Δ₉X` (a Δ₉-based variant of the Oono–Puri average) from the isotropic 9-point Laplacian (`grid::laplacian9`, wrap), `A = 1.3`, `D = 0.5`, `M = 1`; tanh by the `soft_clip` Padé approximant. This conserves ψ. The initial ψ is the mean plus ±0.1 noise smoothed by one periodic 3 × 3 box pass (mean kept). Acts reset: critical quench (mean 0) → off-critical (mean 0.35) droplets.
- **Motion**: domains form within the first second (the step rate starts at twice the act's opening rate and eases back over 2 s, so the dim quench mush is brief); length scale grows as t^{1/3}; the step rate rises from 120 to 360 steps/s through each act (at most 6 per update) so late coarsening stays visible; each act 30 s.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012), conserving the mean.
- **Inks**: a two-phase alloy of named ramps, shaded per lattice node: one phase deep teal `Ramp(Aurora)` (its lower 62 %, apart from kelvin_helmholtz's Eosin/Cobalt), the other cream `Ramp(Starlight)` (lifted toward its pale end); on formed domains the magnitude follows the sub-node distance to the interface (`|ψ|/|∇ψ|`, smoothstep from 0.1 to 1.7 nodes), so boundaries are even antialiased dark seams at any angle rather than stair-stepped ψ thresholds (a young quench is dimmed by its small amplitude as before); a domain's interior sits deeper (0.46) and its rim glows lighter (up to +0.34, depth from the blurred |ψ|); a sheen from a fixed upper-left light on that depth makes domains read as rounded liquid. Each act emerges from the terminal's black: pixels stay empty until the domains form. ASCII: `#` `+` for one phase, `:` `.` for the other, blank on interfaces.
- **Title**: `Unmixing · domains coarsen`; captions `spinodal`, `droplets ripen`.
- **Stylisation**: cell-dynamics (Oono–Puri) rather than the full Cahn–Hilliard PDE; lattice units.
- **Acceptance**: `SPIN-A1` the mean of ψ is conserved to f32 rounding: 1e-8 per step and 1e-6 over 300 steps; `SPIN-A2` the characteristic length (first zero of the correlation function) grows, with a log-log slope in [0.2, 0.45] over the act; `SPIN-A3` in the off-critical act the minority phase forms ≥ 10 separate droplets whose count then falls; `SPIN-A4` ψ stays within [−1.2, 1.2]; `SPIN-A5` regridding keeps the mean; `SPIN-A6` on a straight interface at 30° the display magnitude is dark at the line and rises with the distance to it (0.25-node bins), the teal phase is neither Eosin nor Ferroin, and the domains are formed 1 s after a quench at 120×40.

### chemical_garden  (States of Matter, M13)
- **Intent**: a seed crystal of a metal salt dropped in water glass: a membrane forms, swells with water by osmosis, bursts, and a jet of salt solution rises and sheaths itself in new membrane: blue tubes grow upward in jerks into a garden; a final act shows a hydrothermal vent chimney, where such mineral membranes are *hypothesised* to have housed early life.
- **Model**: tubes as a fixed-capacity segment pool (≤ 600) with tips; each tip has an internal osmotic pressure that rises at a rate ∝ concentration difference (C: precipitation) and bursts when it exceeds a membrane strength (seeded ±30 %); a burst emits a buoyant jet (P: buoyancy) that extends the tip upward by a random-walk-biased step and precipitates a new membrane segment; occasional branching; precipitate particles (pool 800) flake and sink. Vent act: a warm plume rising from a chimney with mineral walls growing (same model, different parameters) and a caption naming the hypothesis.
- **Motion**: bursts every 0.3–2 s per tip; garden grows 30 s; vent act 20 s; reset.
- **Resize**: tank geometry in tank units; pools kept.
- **Inks** (M14, ADR-0017; replaces Round 3's tank): a black tank. Each salt keeps its muted colour: copper blue `Ramp(Ice)`, cobalt pink `Ramp(HAlpha)`, iron rust `Ramp(Sienna)`, nickel green `Ramp(Aurora)`. Tubes are capsule strokes of radius 0.5–1 px (the chimney wall 1.5 px), anti-aliased by pixel coverage, at their salt's low level when old (90; cobalt 80), rising toward its top (190, 150, 180, 170) while fresh (first 6 s); growing tips are small discs at the top level plus up to 30 as the pressure builds; seed crystals are 1-px discs at the top level. The floor is one `Starlight` 40 pixel row; jets and flakes single dim pixels; vent smoke a faint `Starlight` glow (≤ 74), warm `Ember` only at the orifice. Gas bubbles are simulated but not drawn. ASCII: tubes `|` (≥ 90) or `,`, floor `_`, smoke `.`.
- **Title**: `Chemical garden · osmosis bursts`; captions `osmotic bursts`, `life's vents?` (a question: the vent link is a hypothesis).
- **Stylisation**: phenomenological burst model (no fluid solver); the origin-of-life link is a hypothesis (stated in the caption and here).
- **Acceptance**: `GRD-A7` (M14) thin muted tubes on a black tank: at 80×24 and 200×50, seed 7, at t = 5, 20 and 40 s, every lit pixel is at level ≤ 225, lit pixels cover 0.2–20 % of the tank, and no cell shows an `o` glyph; `GRD-A1` a tip's pressure rises monotonically between bursts and drops after one; `GRD-A2` tube tips only move upward on average (mean dy < 0 upward over 30 s); `GRD-A3` the segment pool never exceeds capacity and the scene resets when full; `GRD-A4` the vent act's caption is `life's vents?` (a question, never a claim); `GRD-A5` precipitate particles fall; `GRD-A6` a renewed garden fades out over 5 s while the new one grows, and the tank never shows an empty frame (act changes included).
- **As built (batch 5)**: pools 600 segments, 24 tips, 800 particles (jets, flakes, vent fluid); bursts every 0.35–1.7 s per tip (pressure rate 0.6–2.8, strength 1 ± 30 %), branch chance 14 %, 5–6 seed crystals. The vent act starts with a standing chimney (a tapered stack of mineral wall) whose crown keeps growing and venting warm fluid. Precipitate flakes are dim cobalt.
- **Round 2**: tube bursts step 0.028 tank units, 1–2 tips per seed, branch chance 10 %. The vent act's chimney is taller and wider (24 layers × 5 columns, crown at 55 % of the tank); vent fluid (40 parcels per tip per second) rises faster, spreads and billows, and is drawn as a glow: `Ramp(Ember)` at the orifice, grey `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 137 smoke above.
- **Round 3**: category States of Matter (precipitation and osmosis; was Life from Matter). A tank: dark `Ramp(Cobalt)` water lit softly from the upper left, a rippled `Ramp(Starlight)` sandy bed. Each seed is one metal salt and its tubes keep its colour: copper blue-green `Ice`, cobalt violet `Plasma`, iron rust `Ember`, nickel green `Aurora`; tubes are lit from the left with mineral grain; growing tips glow with a halo that brightens as the pressure builds; bursts release rising gas bubbles. A garden is renewed when it holds 380 segments or its tips have stopped, and at every act change: the old garden fades out over 5 s while the new seeds sprout, so the tank is never empty (GRD-A6). The vent chimney is rough rust rock (iron sulfides), its crown ember-hot.

### saturn_rings  (Sun & Planets, M13)
- **Intent**: Saturn at 20° tilt, the rings filling the frame: the dark Cassini Division, the Encke and Keeler gaps, Pan in the Encke gap trailing a wake and edge waves, Mimas orbiting beyond the rings, ghostly spokes and self-gravity clumps drifting across the rings, and the planet's shadow falling on the rings.
- **Model**: optical-depth profile `τ(r)` (C, B, A rings, Cassini Division, Encke and Keeler gaps, Huygens ringlet) as a static LUT built in `build()` from published radii. Per pixel, the inverse projection gives ring-plane `(r, λ)`; brightness `1 − e^{−τ/μ}` (μ = sin tilt), averaged over the pixel's radial footprint and dimmed where the planet's shadow falls (shadow averaged over 4 sub-pixel points). Keplerian speed `Ω(r) ∝ r^{-3/2}`: clumps (value noise in `(r, λ − Ω(r)·age)`, coarse in radius) move at the local Keplerian rate and, like real self-gravity wakes, dissolve and re-form over 10 s (two staggered generations cross-faded by `sin²`), so shear never winds them into radial grain. Pan (r = 2.2135) raises edge waves `∝ e^{−lag}·sin(k·lag)` on the Encke gap and a wake of brightness ripples of azimuthal wavelength `3π|Δr|` beside it, ahead on the faster inner side and behind on the slower outer one, decaying downstream and away from the gap. Mimas (r = 3.078) orbits at its Keplerian rate outside the rings; its 2:1 inner Lindblad resonance, `r_M·2^{−2/3} ≈ 1.94`, holds the B ring's outer edge and so the Cassini Division's inner boundary. Spokes (pool 10) corotate with the planet's magnetic field (`Ω_Saturn`), so they drift against the Keplerian clumps. The planet: an oblate disc (flattening 0.1) with soft zonal bands (bright equatorial zone, equatorial belts near ±19°, temperate belts near ±39°, a duskier polar hood), limb darkening `0.64 + 0.36√μ`, a smooth terminator and a 16-sample coverage limb that drops to space within one pixel (coverage < 0.4 stays space; above it brightness scales only by `0.8 + 0.2·cover`), occluding the far ring side; the rings' shadow bands the southern globe.
- **Motion**: clumps drift and re-form at Keplerian speeds (the inner rings overtake the outer); Pan's wake and edge waves travel with it; Mimas crosses the view (period ≈ 41 s); spokes form, drift, fade (6–10 s). Frames at t = 1 and t = 30 differ clearly.
- **Resize**: projection and buffers rebuilt; the τ LUT is size-independent.
- **Inks**: rings `Ramp(Starlight)` (icy, neutral) with a soft shoulder `b/(1 + 0.12b)` so the opaque B ring keeps its clumps; planet `Ramp(Sienna)` upper range (pale cream-butterscotch zones, tan belts; M13 review replaced Ember, which read as fire) with limb darkening held at or above level 0.5 (a muted tan-brown, never Sienna's rust-orange low end); globe dimmer than that (the terminator, the rings' shadow) is drawn in low `Starlight` (0.6 × its value); spokes darker `Starlight` (up to 50 % dimmer, soft-edged wedges, one every ~1.5 s, two already on the ansa at the start); Pan and Mimas `Starlight`, lit from the Sun's side. Where ring and globe share a pixel (limb, crepe ring) the pixel takes the owner's ramp (the owner is chosen from the static brightness, so clumps never flip it) at the coverage-blended brightness, so the edge steps smoothly. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Saturn rings · moons carve gaps`.
- **Stylisation**: Pan's wake, edge waves and spokes exaggerated in contrast; Pan and Mimas enlarged (Mimas ×10); the Encke and Keeler gaps widened to ~0.02 R_S and the Cassini Division ~15 % outward so they show; each pixel averages the profile over its radial footprint (sub-pixel gaps darken rather than flicker); the F ring is omitted; time-lapse ×2000 for the ring motion; clump lifetimes shortened to 10 s; the profile is simplified to ~20 features; spokes shown at 20° tilt (they are seen mainly near equinox).
- **Acceptance**: `RING-A1` the τ LUT has near-zero depth at the Cassini Division, Encke and Keeler radii; `RING-A2` texture at the inner B ring advances faster than at the outer A ring in angle (Keplerian shear, ratio matches `r^{-3/2}` within 5 %); `RING-A3` spokes turn at the planet's rate, not the local Keplerian one; `RING-A4` ring pixels behind the planet are hidden and the planet's shadow darkens the rings on the far side; `RING-A5` Pan's edge-wave amplitude decays with angle downstream of the moon; `RING-A6` the globe is `Ramp(Sienna)`, its brightest middle is ≥ level 200, it is darker two pixels inside the limb, the outermost limb pixel is no brighter than that, and no Sienna globe pixel is below the level-0.5 floor (no rust rim); `RING-A7` Mimas turns at `Ω(r_M)`, slower than any ring, its 2:1 resonance lies within 1 % of the Cassini Division's inner edge, and at 120×40 it is on screen at t = 1 and t = 30, in different places; `RING-A8` Pan's wake ripples decay downstream and away from the gap, vanish beyond its reach and tighten towards the moon (wavelength `3π|Δr|`); `RING-A9` the clump texture changes by < 0.3 × its contrast over 0.02 R_S in radius, and at least a third of the bright ring pixels change by ≥ 12 levels between t = 1 and t = 30.

### comet_tails  (Sun & Planets, M13)
- **Intent**: a bright comet near the Sun: a curved, fanned pale dust tail and a straight, narrow blue ion tail of fine rippling streamers pointing straight away from the Sun, which occasionally snaps off and regrows (a disconnection event).
- **Model**: dust grains released from the nucleus with small velocity spreads; each moves on an analytic two-body orbit about the Sun under reduced gravity `μ(1 − β)`, β log-uniform in [0.04, 0.9] (< 1 keeps gravity attractive; β > r/2a ≈ 0.015–0.03 everywhere on the shown arc, so every grain is on a hyperbolic orbit), propagated in closed form with the universal-variable Kepler solution (Newton, ≤ 3 iterations warm-started from the previous frame; the scene's own `Orbit`, since core has no Kepler-equation solver). Grains of one β form a syndyne, grains of one release time a synchrone. Dust pool 8000, grains dropped after 20 s; activity ∝ r^{−3/2}. Ion tail: plasma parcels (pool 3000 — room for a whole attached tail, `280/s × 5 s`, plus a whole disconnected one, so a cut never starves the regrowth) launched antisunward at 0.25 AU/s and accelerated at 0.16 AU/s², each riding one of 5 streamers fanned 0.026 rad apart, with a kink (2.8–4 % of the run, 0.35 Hz) that travels down the tail. A disconnection every 20 s (first at 16 s scene time) detaches the tail at the coma — the cut tail keeps its rays, is swept off by the solar wind and fades over 4 s — release pauses 1.6 s and the new tail regrows. The comet (q = 0.35 AU, e = 0.97) sweeps ν = −90° → 95° in a 30 s loop; each new pass starts with the tails it grew on the way in (grains seeded with past release times from the orbit's earlier states — they need no history, being analytic).
- **Motion**: comet sweeps round perihelion (30 s loop, 0.4 s cross-fade at the wrap); the view follows the nucleus (kept off-centre towards the Sun, orientation fixed), so the dust tail curves behind the orbit and the ion tail swings to stay antisunward; kinks run down the ion streamers and knots of plasma stream out along them.
- **Resize**: orbit and grain state in AU; view scale rebuilt; glow layers and the blur's line buffer resized.
- **Inks**: dust `Ramp(Starlight)` (sunlight reflected): grains younger than 2 s splat sharp, 2–6 s softer, older ones into a separate layer box-blurred (radius 2, twice) so the far tail is a smooth haze. Ion tail `Ramp(Ice)` (CO⁺ blue): each streamer a thin antialiased ray over a faint soft body, light ∝ `1/(1 + s/0.45 AU)` down the tail, plus the parcels as moving knots. Coma `Starlight` bright. Sun a small `Blackbody` 5800 K photosphere inside a white-gold `Ramp(Starlight)` glow (Gaussian core plus a `1/(1 + 4q²)²` wing) that fades smoothly into the sky with no coloured rim; no Ember anywhere. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Comet tails · light pushes dust`.
- **Stylisation**: comet brightened; dust offsets from the nucleus drawn ×5 (the real tail grows over weeks); β range and release rates tuned for looks; ion tail kinematic, its streamers and kinks drawn wider than the ~1° real rays.
- **Acceptance**: `CMT-A1` a grain's orbit conserves energy of the reduced-μ problem (closed form) to 1e-9, and on the whole shown arc β_min = 0.04 > r/2a, so even the heaviest grain released at the nucleus's velocity has positive energy (hyperbolic); `CMT-A2` the dust tail lies on the side of the anti-solar direction away from the direction of motion (curves behind); `CMT-A3` attached ion-tail parcels older than 0.3 s are within 8° of anti-solar; `CMT-A4` after a disconnection the tail near the coma (0.08 AU) is empty for ≥ 1 s (from 0.3 s after the cut) and then refills; `CMT-A5` the grain pools never exceed capacity and β < 1 always; `CMT-A6` (regression, M13 review: a cut-off tail used to fill the pool and leave a detached stub) at 120×40 over 70 s, outside a cut's first PAUSE + 1 s and the loop's wrap, the attached tail holds ≥ 60 % of its steady parcel count and its light (tone level > 20) runs unbroken along the axis from 0.03 AU to 0.4 AU or the frame edge, whether or not the Sun is on frame; `CMT-A7` the ion light near the root outshines the light 0.9 AU down by > 25 levels, and a cut across the tail at 0.6 AU shows ≥ 2 separate peaks (streamers, not one band); `CMT-A8` no pixel uses `Ramp(Ember)` and the Sun's photosphere is `Blackbody`; `CMT-A9` the far-dust blur spreads a point to a 5×5 plateau and conserves its light.

### jupiter_storms  (Sun & Planets, M13)
- **Intent** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: Jupiter's whole disc on black space: alternating belts and zones sheared by jet streams, the Great Red Spot rolling like a ball bearing, white ovals, and filaments wound into curls, the view following the spot as the planet turns.
- **Model**: `vortex_advect` (shared `pub(crate)`): semi-Lagrangian advection of two dye fields on a capped lattice (≤ 160×80, `lattice_for`) by an analytic velocity: zonal jets `u(y) = U Σ a_j cos(k_j y)` (6–8 jets, alternating) plus Gaussian stream-function vortices `ψ = s·e^{−dx²/a²−dy²/b²}` (GRS anticyclone elongated 1.4:1, its present shape — it was ~2:1 in the Voyager era — and 5 white ovals with dark collars) that drift with the local jet, velocities as the cell differences of ψ (discretely divergence-free); a weak relaxation back to the banded dye keeps it from mixing out; ~30 short-lived small eddies of random sign add filaments; the red chromophore is fed on the GRS's east side so its own rotation winds it into a spiral. A third advected dye, fed by three small slowly drifting sources on the fastest eastward jet at a belt edge and decaying at 0.35/s, is drawn out by the jet into dark festoons. *(M14)* The flow lives on a longitude × latitude strip (lattice aspect 4:1, one lattice height spanning 65° of latitude and of longitude) mapped onto an orthographic, oblate (0.935) disc of radius 0.47 of the frame's smaller side; beyond ±65° the poles fade into a dim featureless hood. Io's shadow crosses the disc every 20 s, starting on it.
- **Motion**: dye streams along jets and curls round vortices; ovals drift; festoons trail along the jet; the GRS slowly turns (period ~6 s); the view follows the GRS, swaying up to 0.5 rad of longitude either side of it; Io's shadow drifts across the disc.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012); vortex positions in planet units. *(M14, GEN-R19)* The first resize spins the flow up (100 steps) on a coarse lattice of 1/10 of the cap and then regrids it to the full lattice.
- **Inks** *(M14, ADR-0017; the cloud map is shared with `comet_breakup` through `shared::jovian`)*: everything on the disc is one ramp, `Ramp(Sienna)`, and features blend in *level* space before a single lookup, so no edge is a switch between ramps. Belts and zones map the zone/belt dye continuously (dark rust belts through tan to cream zones, embossed by a relief term), then are dimmed to 0.82 of the ramp by the shading (so the zones stay mid-light) with limb darkening `0.35 + 0.65·√μ` and the outermost 1.5 pixels feathered to black; the polar hood is level 0.33. The GRS, the one warm accent, is a brick-to-rust oval (darker where the wound-up chromophore is dense, textured by the zone dye and relief) whose outline comes from its vortex (0.8 of its half-axes), feathered into a pale cream collar; it is dimmed a little less (0.8). The clearing dye only darkens the belt edge (up to 60 %): no blue hot spots. Io's shadow is black with a 1-pixel smoothstep penumbra. Space is black. No Ember, Ice, Infrared, Starlight or Cobalt. ASCII by level. The previous row's dye samples are cached while shading, so most pixels take one bilinear sample.
- **Title**: `Jupiter storms · belts shear`.
- **Stylisation**: kinematic flow (prescribed, not solved); enhanced false colour, muted (ADR-0017); speeds time-lapsed; the flow strip repeats every 260° of longitude (at most 180° is ever in view).
- **Acceptance**: `JUP-A1` the velocity field is divergence-free (discrete divergence < 1e-9), and the lattice flow is that field; `JUP-A2` jets alternate in sign between neighbouring bands; `JUP-A3` total dye is conserved by advection within 1 % per second (before relaxation); `JUP-A4` the GRS rotates anticyclonically (counter-clockwise in the southern hemisphere view); `JUP-A5` the lattice stays within its cap at 500×150; `JUP-A6` *(M14)* after 2 s every pixel is `Sienna` or black, the four corners are black space and the disc covers more than an eighth of the frame; the shared cloud map is monotone in the dye and the shared latitude profile (EZ pale, NEB/SEB dark, tropical zones pale) is continuous; `JUP-A7` the GRS's half-axis ratio is in [1.3, 1.5]; `JUP-A8` *(M14)* at t = 0 the GRS sits on the disc's centre meridian at its latitude, and its centre is more than 30 levels darker than its collar just outside the drawn rim; the shared spot weights feather its rim and collar (never a step); `JUP-A9` *(M14)* across the disc's edge the level falls through a lit pixel darker than the disc's centre into black (limb darkening and a feathered edge, not a stepped rim).

### solar_eruption  (Sun & Planets, M13)
- **Intent**: the Sun's limb in extreme ultraviolet: a quiet arcade of glowing loops, then a twisted flux rope rises, field lines snap and reconnect beneath it, two flare ribbons flash at the footpoints, a coronal mass ejection balloons out, and bright coronal rain drains back down the new loops.
- **Model**: 2.5-D potential field of 4–6 photospheric sources (line dipoles) plus a flux rope (line current) whose height `h(t)` follows a torus-instability curve (slow rise, then exponential takeoff). Field lines are traced with RK2 from footpoints (≤ 20 re-traced per frame, 42 cached: 32 arcade, 10 rope strands kept only while closed) into a `Glow` buffer. Reconnection: lines whose apex passes under the rope's X-point are cut and replaced by shorter post-flare loops (cooling ages); flare ribbons brighten at their footpoints and move apart. CME: a three-part structure (bright front, dark cavity, core) expanding self-similarly. Coronal rain: blobs (pool 200) sliding down loops under projected gravity.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: quiet (6 s) → rise (8 s) → flare (6 s) → CME (10 s) → rain (12 s) → quiet.
- **Resize**: sources and rope in solar radii; caches invalidated.
- **Drawing** (M13 review): the traced field is a 2.5-D slice, so each arcade line gets a fixed 3-D *pose* (lean ±0.3, footpoint shift ±0.6 L along a neutral line that curves away from the viewer, so loops far along it are rooted up to ~0.26 L behind the limb and their feet are occulted; height ×0.62–1.22): the arcade reads as a row of loops seen at the limb, not concentric arches. The quiet arcade is sparse: only ~45 % of the loops glow (the rest are skipped until reconnection heats them), so isolated loops stand apart with dark corona between them. Hot post-flare loops stand upright on the ribbons and relax into their pose as they cool. Brightness varies along every loop (knots ~0.3 L long drifting along it, siphon flows) and between loops (gain 0.35–1.35 with a cubic spread: a few bright over many faint). The rope's closed field lines are not drawn; instead four strands (two below 26 px per `L`, e.g. 80×24, where more would merge into a blob) wind 2.5 turns round a sheared arch (one shoulder high, the apex leaning over: the limb view of a sigmoid's S), rotating slowly, faint while it forms, brightening as it rises, and gone by 60 % of the CME act so no stretched legs are left in the frame. The CME is a self-similar bubble (radius 0.55 × centre height) that swells out of the rope in the flare and accelerates off the top of the frame (centre 1.05 L → 6 L): a bright front (a lightbulb shell: a crisp outer rim fading softly inward into the cavity, brightest on top and falling off faster down the flanks as the bubble grows, the whole front fading out by 55 % of the CME act so it leaves as a whole, and filamentary: narrow radial streaks modulate its brightness strongly along it), a dark cavity that empties the loops and corona inside it, and the rope as the bright core low in the cavity, its legs still rooted.
- **Inks**: loops, rope, rain, ribbons and CME all `Ramp(Aurora)` (the AIA 131 Å teal-green look: one teal → white ramp) as wide soft `Glow` strokes (no moiré at 80×24), each quiet loop flickering on its own heating cycle, a light rain drizzle in the quiet act; open lines are mirrored about the inversion line (the field is mirror-symmetric); flare ribbons are two smooth antialiased lines hugging the limb at the post-flare loops' feet, brightest at their inner edge, in the same ramp. No stars (EUV). ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Solar eruption · loops snap`; captions per act: `quiet arcade`, `loops rise`, `flare`, `mass ejection`, `coronal rain` (every act is captioned).
- **Stylisation**: potential + one rope, not MHD; hours compressed into ~45 s; false EUV colour; loop poses are drawn, not traced (the field is a 2.5-D slice); the CME's three-part structure is drawn as seen in white-light coronagraphs.
- **Acceptance**: `ERUP-A1` a traced line follows `B`: the angle between its tangent and `B` at sample points is < 2°; `ERUP-A2` the rope height is monotonic in the rise act and its log-slope increases (takeoff); `ERUP-A3` flare-ribbon separation increases during the flare; `ERUP-A4` rain blobs move toward the surface along their loop; `ERUP-A5` at most 20 lines are re-traced per frame; `ERUP-A6` arcade loops vary: at least a fifth lean each way by > 0.08, a quarter are rooted > 0.05 L behind the limb, shifts and height scales each span > 0.3, gains span > 3×, brightness along ≥ 3/4 of the loops varies > 2× and drifts in time, a pose at strength 0 is the identity, 30–60 % of the arcade loops glow in the quiet corona, and loops rooted far along the neutral line (|shift| > 0.35 L) sit on average > 0.08 L further behind the limb than those near its middle; `ERUP-A7` the rope axis is rooted on the surface either side of the inversion line, peaks at `hr` (±2 %), is sheared (shoulder heights at u = 0.3 and 0.7 differ by > 20 % of `hr`, apex off-centre by > 0.1 L), each of ≥ 3 strands crosses the axis ≥ 4 times, its drawn apex height and brightness never fall from the rise through the flare, its brightness is 0 from 65 % of the CME act on, and two strands are drawn at 80×24 scale; `ERUP-A8` the CME is three-part (front at d = 1 over 4× brighter than at d = 0.7 or 1.3, cavity dimming to < 0.3 at d = 0.5, none outside), self-similar, its core below the centre by < half a radius, its top rising monotonically past 4 L by the act's end, and it is drawn only in `Aurora`; `ERUP-A9` every act shows a caption from `meta.captions`, the quiet act's first.

### io_volcanoes  (Sun & Planets, M13)
- **Intent** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: Io's limb against black space: a pale cream-yellow ground (cream plains with soft sulfur-yellow patches), whiter SO₂ frost, tan highlands, black paterae and white-hot lava pixels, and two volcanoes throwing umbrella-shaped plumes of pale grains, a giant Pele (300 km) and a squat Prometheus (80 km), whose fallout paints a red-brown ring and a white ring on the surface.
- **Model**: `plume` (shared `pub(crate)`): ballistic particles (pool 6k) in Io's gravity (1.8 m/s², no air), launched from the vent with speed `v₀·U(0.96, 1)` (Pele 1 km/s, Prometheus 0.5 km/s) at zenith angles uniform in solid angle over 0.15–0.9 rad in 28 azimuthal rays; height `v₀²/(2g)`, fall radius from the ballistic range. Landings are splatted bilinearly into a 32² deposit map per vent (±1.5 × the 45° range), fading 2.5 % per second, read relative to the map's densest cell and averaged over a fan one ray wide, so the ring is smooth; the rings are repainted one band of rows in 6 per step. *(M14)* The plume is the live grains themselves: each is one braille dot on its ballistic arc, drawn only against space (the thin plume vanishes over the bright ground, as Pele does on the limb in the Voyager and Galileo frames). Tidal heating is shown as a pulsing vent pixel in phase with Io's orbital eccentricity (period compressed to 20 s). The view: Io fills the lower frame, radius `min(0.95·H, 0.65·W)` pixels, centred one radius below mid-height, lit from the upper right with a soft terminator and gentle limb darkening (the lower-left limb in shade); no Jupiter. Sparse pixel stars (1 in 500 space pixels) dot the sky. The ground is shaded once per resize, the paterae each over their own box (GEN-R19).
- **Motion**: the eruption breathes (launch speed scaled by the breathing factor's square root, so the plume's size by ≈ ±8 %, on 7.3 s and 2.9 s beats), the grains fly along their arcs, vent pixels pulse with the tide, lava lakes flicker, deposit rings build then hold.
- **Resize**: geometry in km mapped to the frame; surface and lava-lake positions rebuilt; deposit maps kept.
- **Inks** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: pale cream-yellow plains at L ≈ 0.6: no ramp has a low-chroma yellow, so the plains are cream `Starlight` 0.6 with soft patches (value noise) of `Ramp(Sulfur)` 0.64 at the same lightness (0.42–0.74 with the shading), paling towards frost; SO₂ frost whiter `Starlight` 0.74–0.82 with mottle; highlands and the redder poles tan `Sienna` 0.5–0.56; all scaled by the same shading, so unit edges are small steps in lightness; paterae `Starlight` ≈ 0.1 (black) with a dark `Sienna` rim; each lava lake two `Blackbody` pixels (the second at 0.8 of the temperature) flickering between 3800 and 6000 K, and each vent one pixel pulsing 4500–7000 K: the white-hot accent; Pele's short-chain-sulfur ring `Sienna` ≈ 0.4–0.5 (wider and softer on its inner edge), Prometheus's frost ring `Starlight` 0.7–0.8; plume grains `Ramp(Ice)` braille dots from level 110 at the vent to 215 at the canopy top; stars `Starlight` 0.3–0.7. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Io volcanoes · tides melt rock`; captions `Pele` / `Prometheus` optional.
- **Stylisation**: plumes drawn ×1.6 relative to Io, heights exact in ratio; time scale ×100; tidal heating shown symbolically (a pulsing vent); the plume is drawn as its ballistic grains only (Pele's real canopy is a shock where gas-borne grains pile up into a bright rim); lava lakes and vents drawn white-hot (3800–7000 K) rather than Io's ≈ 1500 K, so they read as the accent; the pale plains are cream with sulfur patches (the ramps have no low-chroma yellow); Jupiter is left out (ADR-0017: less decoration).
- **Acceptance**: `IO-A1` a particle's apex equals `v₀²/(2g)` within 1 %; `IO-A2` Pele's plume is ≥ 3× taller than Prometheus's; `IO-A3` deposits fall in a ring whose radius matches the ballistic range for 45° launches (± 15 %); `IO-A4` the pool never exceeds capacity; `IO-A5` the vent glow pulses with the configured period; `IO-A6` *(M14)* at 120×40 and 200×50 the plume is more than 40 braille cells of `Ramp(Ice)` grains at levels 110–215, every one over space (not over Io), brighter in its top row than at its foot, and it moves between t = 1 s and 1.5 s; `IO-A7` *(M14)* at 160×50 more than a quarter of the disc is `Sulfur` at a mean level of 130–190, ≥ 2 % is cream or frost (`Starlight` ≥ 55), there are ≥ 10 black and ≥ 8 `Blackbody` lava pixels, Pele's ring shows as more than 20 `Sienna` pixels near its vent, and no ground pixel but the lava and the vents exceeds level 215; `IO-A8` the plume's size breathes by 8–25 % peak to peak, at least a third of the lava lakes change brightness by > 0.1 within 0.6 s, and at 120×40 the plume's top stays in the frame through the breathing (t = 1–5.5 s); `IO-A9` Io is shaded as a sphere: the light at the lower-left limb is ≥ 0.2 below the upper-right disc and below the centre, it changes by < 2 % per 1° across the disc (no hard terminator), and across neighbouring ground pixels of different units (sulfur, frost, highland) the ramp level jumps by more than 0.2 on < 8 % of the edges.

### comet_breakup  (Sun & Planets, M13)
- **Intent**: Shoemaker–Levy 9: a comet grazes Jupiter inside the Roche limit and is torn into a string of pearls; two years later (compressed) the fragments strike one after another, raising fireballs on the limb and leaving dark scars.
- **Model**: rubble pile of 240 particles with softened self-gravity and soft damped contacts (N² pairs, 29k per step, ≤ 3 sub-steps of 0.01 τ per frame), in the orbital plane on a Kepler orbit about Jupiter, integrated in the comet's frame with the tidal term (Hill approximation) by velocity Verlet with sub-steps near perijove; inside the Roche distance the pile shears into a chain whose clumps are found by a cheap union-find on neighbours (≤ 21 fragments tracked). Act 2: each fragment's impact at 60 km/s spawns a `plume` (shared) fireball on the limb and a persistent dark scar that Jupiter's rotation carries round.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: approach (2 s) → breakup (12 s) → string of pearls (12 s, the view follows the chain) → impacts (16 s) → scars (6 s), with orbit time advancing 0.7, 0.9 and 1.1 τ per second in the first three acts from τ = −2.6 (the approach is short so the tide visibly stretches the pile by t ≈ 5 s). Roche distance 2.5 R_J in the model's units, perijove 1.33 R_J. On the run-in the chain's spacing is shrunk if needed so the last fragment strikes by 13 s into the impacts act.
- **Resize**: dynamics in Jupiter radii; view rebuilt.
- **Inks** (M13 review, round 2): the comet is pale, not cyan. Nuclei are tight Starlight splats, so only the dense core saturates to a small white head; every third grain carries a faint wide Starlight coma halo and a pale Starlight dust tail (0.8 R_J, fading as `(1 − s)^1.5`) pointing away from a Sun to the lower left, and a narrow, fainter `Ramp(Ice)` ion tail (1.4 R_J, straight anti-Sun, 12° from the dust tail, never above Ice 0.42); where both overlap the brighter level wins (equal lightness at equal level), so the hand-over is seamless. Each fragment trails its own short pale dust tail and a fainter ion streak. Jupiter uses the same Sienna cloud map as `jupiter_storms` (`shared::jovian`): the belt/zone latitude profile (EZ, NEB/SEB, tropical and temperate zones and belts, polar hoods), its edges made wavy and mottled by value noise fixed in planet longitude (so the texture turns with the planet and the scars), a small Great Red Spot (brick oval with a faint cream collar, feathered in its own units), limb darkening `μ^0.9` with no floor (the limb sinks into dark sienna) and a 1.5-pixel antialiased edge. The impact is a soft radial Gaussian glow on the limb (0.16 R_J, e-folding 0.7 s, gone by 2.5 s) plus the plume's grains (ramped in over their first 0.4 s so the vent never saturates): warm white fading to black (`Ramp(Starlight)` by intensity, so no dull-red rim), a near-white `Blackbody` 5200–6500 K only at its saturated heart (tone level ≥ 238); over the clouds a faint glow lightens the Sienna instead of replacing it. The falling plume reads as a crescent on the limb; each strike leaves a persistent dark scar (dark core in a crescent) that darkens the clouds under it and rotates with the planet. The view leans half-way to the comet on the approach, locks onto the pile early in the breakup and zooms out (to ≥ 40 % of the home scale) as the chain lengthens so the whole string of pearls stays in frame. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Comet breakup · tides pull apart`; captions `Roche limit`, `string of pearls`, `impacts`.
- **Stylisation**: the pile is ~10³× coarser than reality; the 2-year gap is compressed to seconds; scars exaggerated; the orbit is drawn as a parabola, an approximation of SL9's actual bound (highly eccentric, ~2-year) Jovian orbit, which near perijove it matches closely; the fragments' final run-in is a straight line to the impact site.
- **Acceptance**: `BRK-A1` outside 3 Roche radii the pile stays bound (its radius changes < 10 %); `BRK-A2` after perijove inside the Roche limit ≥ 8 fragments form and they are ordered along the orbit (a chain: the principal axis ratio ≥ 5); `BRK-A3` the pool and the fragment table stay within capacity; `BRK-A4` impacts happen in fragment order and every fragment strikes within the act; `BRK-A5` scars move with Jupiter's rotation; `BRK-A6` by t = 5 s the pile's principal axis ratio is ≥ 2; from t = 0.1 s the nucleus is a white Starlight head (level > 200), the pixel 0.45 dust-tail lengths from it along the tail is Starlight while the sunward one is empty or faint, and half an ion-tail length out along the anti-Sun line there is Ice no brighter than `ION_PEAK`; `BRK-A7` at least 80 % of Jupiter's central pixels use the shared `Sienna` cloud palette.

### hurricane  (Earth & Sky, M13)
- **Intent** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: a mature hurricane seen from above on a black sea: a small clear eye inside a solid grey central dense overcast, broad ragged rain bands winding counter-clockwise into it and faint cirrus outflow fanning clockwise at the edge, and an infrared act in a muted enhancement curve where only the coldest cloud tops rise into teal.
- **Model**: `vortex_advect` (shared with jupiter_storms): semi-Lagrangian cloud-water field on a capped lattice advected by a Holland-profile vortex (tangential wind peaking at the radius of maximum wind) plus radial inflow near the surface that tightens bands into logarithmic spirals; convective sources in the eyewall ring (0.75–1.5 radii of maximum wind), a solid central dense overcast out to 2.2 radii (fading over 0.8 more) and 3 broad spiral bands (noise-modulated cells) that switch on over one radius past 2.4 radii so they merge into the overcast; the visible picture adds a subtle fixed convective-tower texture (overshooting tops, turning slowly with the storm) with its own sunlit and shadowed sides where cloud is thick; the eye (r < 0.6 radii of maximum wind) is kept clear by a sink (subsidence). Display only (not advected), refreshed every 6 steps: faint cirrus outflow streaks spiralling out clockwise (anticyclonic aloft) at 0.7–1.5 storm radii. Brightness is blurred once (1-2-1) for soft 2–4 px cloud edges. On a coarse lattice (height ≤ 64, i.e. 80×24 and smaller) the secondary band and the tower texture drop out, the bands start at 3.2 radii, the eyewall cells are weaker and the picture is blurred twice, so a small frame shows a few broad arms rather than thin stripes. Northern hemisphere: cyclonic = counter-clockwise (Coriolis).
- **Motion**: clouds rotate and spiral inward; bands pulse; `Timeline` switches visible → IR act every 25 s; the whole storm drifts slowly.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012); vortex in storm radii. *(M14, GEN-R19)* The first resize spins the storm up (180 steps) on a coarse lattice of 1/24 of the cap and then regrids it to the full lattice; the convective-tower tile is size-independent and built once.
- **Inks** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: visible: the open sea and the eye are black (no ocean colour or sunglint). Cloud is neutral `Ramp(Starlight)`: lightness `0.16 + 0.84·√b` of the cloud-top brightness `b`, with sun-facing relief and the tower texture where the cloud has body, times an opacity `smoothstep((b − 0.012)/0.128)`, so thin cloud fades into the black instead of ending on a rim; the whole is scaled to at most 0.8 of the ramp and left black below level 14. Rows of the cubic lattice sample are cached while shading. IR act (25 s visible, 12 s infrared, 1.5 s dithered dissolve): `Ramp(Infrared)` only, muted: the enhancement curve is compressed onto its grey-to-teal part, so low cloud is grey and the coldest tops reach only the teal (0.45 of the ramp, ≈ level 115); cloud-top brightness (standing in for height, hence coldness) is scaled by a height factor, 1 inside the overcast (2.2 radii of maximum wind) falling to 0.62 at 0.9 storm radii, so the overcast and eyewall are the coldest; thin cloud fades into the black sea as in the visible act. ASCII by level, the sea left blank; in Unicode a cell whose two pixels match is a space on that background.
- **Title**: `Hurricane · Coriolis spirals`; captions `visible`, `infrared`.
- **Stylisation**: kinematic vortex, not a moist-convection model; time-lapse; brightness stands in for cloud-top temperature in the IR act, and the enhancement curve is muted to its grey-to-teal part; the sea is drawn black (ADR-0017); seen from above (Earth's weather seen from above is Earth & Sky: ADR-0013 addendum, M13).
- **Acceptance**: `HUR-A1` the tangential wind profile peaks at the radius of maximum wind and turns counter-clockwise; `HUR-A2` a tracer released at 0.8 R spirals inward (radius decreasing, angle increasing CCW); `HUR-A3` the eye (r < 0.5 radii of maximum wind) holds < 10 % of the eyewall's cloud; `HUR-A4` in the IR act every ink is `Ramp(Infrared)` or black, and the coldest tops reach level 100–120 (the curve's teal, no further); `HUR-A5` the lattice stays within its cap at 500×150; `HUR-A6` *(M14)* at 120×40 and 80×24 the visible act is `Ramp(Starlight)` cloud on black, and more than a quarter of the pixels are black sea; `HUR-A7` *(M14)* no cloud pixel is brighter than 0.8 of the ramp; `HUR-A8` at 80×24, mean levels in 36 angular bins round annuli at 2.5, 3.5 and 4.5 radii of maximum wind show at most 4 arms (peaks with 20-level hysteresis); `HUR-A9` at 120×40 (t = 2 s and 9 s) the central dense overcast is unbroken: all 36 angular bins of the 1.2–2 radii annulus average level ≥ 150 and ≥ 0.75 of the brightest; `HUR-A10` in the IR act at 120×40 the overcast annulus (1.2–2 radii) averages more than 25 levels above the brightest quarter of the bins at 4.5–6 radii (coldest tops in the overcast, not the bands).

### total_solar_eclipse  (Earth & Sky, M13)
- **Intent**: totality seen from the ground: on a black sky over a dim skyline, the last sunlight breaks into Baily's beads and a diamond ring, then the pearly corona blooms with long streamers and small pink prominences at the limb; then it runs in reverse.
- **Model**: geometry in solar radii about the Sun's centre. The Moon (radius 1.03 R☉) moves along a tilted chord through the Sun's centre; its offset is piecewise linear in the `Timeline` (fast in the partial phases, slow through the beads and totality). Uncovered fraction `F`: the exact circle–circle lens area of the Moon's mountain-top envelope; once the envelope covers the Sun, `F` is the light through the limb valleys. The limb is a seeded valley profile (360 samples, 14 valleys up to 0.02 R☉ deep): a valley shows a bead when its floor still dips inside the photosphere, by its penetration depth. Beads and the diamond (the brightest bead, flaring most when it is nearly alone) are glare splats (`Glow`, `halo_kernel` 2 and 5) centred 0.8 px outside the lunar limb and masked by the Moon's disc, so the diamond is a small bead beside an intact dark disc with a soft glow round it. Photosphere: `raster::disc_cover` of Sun and Moon, `stellar::limb_darkening` (its colour shift softened, √I, so the limb is warm rather than an orange ring) and `brightness_temperature`; partly covered rim pixels at either disc's edge fade as pale `Starlight` (anti-aliased by brightness, not by a redder colour, and never in the sky's blue: no cyan rim on the crescent).
