Corona, precomputed per pixel in `resize`: a power law `r^{−3.3}` (toned with `asinh`, knee 0.35, with no taper or cut-off: it thins into the sky's light as far as it reaches) modulated by 6–10 helmet streamers (angular Gaussians crowding the equator, longer there: the solar-minimum shape, tapering and bending toward the equator with height), polar plumes and fine radial striations; shown only as the photosphere's glare falls. Prominences: 3–6 small arches on the limb. Sky brightness grows with `F^0.8` (dimming toward deep twilight through the partial phases) plus glare round the uncovered Sun; the Moon a black silhouette closing in, its rim anti-aliased against the air, and in the partial phases the Moon's shadow darkens the side of the sky it comes from first (and leaves last); a sunset glow along the bottom, strongest in totality; low hills (`Skyline`) in silhouette; Venus, Jupiter and two bright stars come out one by one below `F` = 0.12, Venus first.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: partial (8 s) → beads + diamond ring (4 s) → totality (14 s) → diamond ring + beads (4 s) → partial (8 s), symmetric.
- **Resize**: geometry in solar radii centred; corona (with and without its fine rays) and prominence buffers rebuilt; the limb profile reseeded.
- **Inks** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: the sky is black in every act. The glare round the uncovered Sun (`95·F·e^{−(r−1)/1.1}`), the corona and the beads' halo are summed as light and tone-mapped (linear to level 140, rolling off exponentially toward 245, never clipping flat), then drawn in pearl `Ramp(Starlight)` and cut to black below level 10: the corona's power law thins into black with no threshold visible and no blue. The twilight air and the sunset glow are gone (their levels are 0); a `Ramp(Ice)` pixel only appears where air would outshine the pearl, which never happens now. Prominences `Ramp(HAlpha)` ≤ 200 (small; the accent); photosphere and bead cores `Blackbody` (≤ 5800 K); the hills are black under a 1-px `Starlight` 34 skyline (the first land pixel of each column); Venus, Jupiter and two bright stars `Starlight` on black. ASCII: the Sun, beads and corona by level (`raster::ascii_ramp_cell`). The corona and prominence tables are filled 2 pixel rows per frame while the corona is hidden (`F ≥ 0.01`), or all at once when it is needed sooner, so a resize costs only the land and glare tables (GEN-R19); the picture is the same either way.
- **Title**: `Total eclipse · Moon hides Sun`; captions `partial`, `diamond ring`, `totality`.
- **Stylisation**: totality lengthened relative to the partial phases (real partial phases last ~25× longer than totality; here 8 s against 14 s); the Moon's apparent motion is uneven, ~180× faster in the partial phases than through totality (really uniform); limb valleys ~10× real lunar relief; streamers procedural; the corona's power law steepened to `r^{−3.3}` so it reads on a terminal; a black daytime sky (ADR-0017).
- **Acceptance**: `ECL-A1` the uncovered fraction is 1 at the start, 0 at mid-totality and symmetric in time (the smooth crescent exactly; the bead light, which differs between second and third contact, to within 0.005); `ECL-A2` beads appear only when the uncovered fraction is in (0, 0.005) and lie on the Moon's limb (within the valley depth); `ECL-A3` the corona is drawn only while the uncovered fraction < 0.01 (its visibility is 0 above that, and no pearl pixel off the disc lies outside the glare's reach); `ECL-A4` corona brightness falls with radius (median over angle monotone); `ECL-A5` captions follow the Timeline; `ECL-A6` the tone curve is continuous and monotone below its ceiling, and in totality neighbouring pixels along any ray from the limb differ by ≤ 48 levels (no edge); `ECL-A7` the sky's corner never brightens through the partial phase and ends ≤ 16, and the Moon's disc stays dark (≤ 16) through the beads and diamond ring; `ECL-A8` the timing stylisation holds (8 s partial, 14 s totality, a partial-to-totality speed ratio of 150–210); `ECL-A9` in the partial phase no partly covered crescent-rim pixel is the sky's `Ice`; the toned corona is still lit at 4 R☉ on every ray (no cut-off); in totality the pearl-to-blue change along each of 48 rays happens at most three times (once, or in and out of a bending streamer), never frayed. `ECL-A10` (GEN-R18) black overhead: in every act the top quarter's corners are black (but for the planets and stars), the land is black under at most a 1–2 px skyline, and in totality the corona fades into black in pearl above the Sun, with no `Ice` pixel within 3 R☉.

### rainbow  (Earth & Sky, M13)
- **Intent** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: a rain shower lit by a low Sun behind the viewer, under a dark storm sky: a primary bow (red outside, the full spectrum to violet inside), a fainter secondary with reversed colours, black sky between them (Alexander's band), faint supernumerary ripples inside the primary, and drifting rain curtains that brighten and dim the bow along its arc, while the Sun behind the viewer passes in and out of cloud.
- **Model**: per pixel, `cos θ` from the antisolar point (per-column `cos az` and per-row `sin/cos el` tables, so a pixel costs two products and a look-up). A size-independent LUT over `cos θ` (8192 bins, built once): Descartes' minimum-deviation angles for k = 1 and k = 2 internal reflections (`cos² i = (n² − 1)/(k(k + 2))`, Cauchy `n(λ)` of water fitted at 404 and 700 nm) at 700 and 400 nm, their spread about 550 nm widened ×2.5 (primary) and ×1.15 (secondary), give each band's red and violet edges; between them an analytic angle → wavelength map (700 → 400 nm) gives smooth bands, with Gaussian shoulders beyond the red rim (0.14 band widths) and the violet edge (0.32), and a body weighted by the eye's spectral response. Supernumeraries: `sin²` ripples inside the violet spaced as Airy's first two maxima of `Ai(−z)²` (drop radius 0.5 mm, `z ∝ (a/λ)^{2/3} Δθ`), fading over 0.55 band widths, drawn as lightness only (a fringe a pixel or two wide would alias into coloured specks). The secondary at 0.45 of the primary. The sunlit rain's smooth light: bright inside the primary (brightest near it), dim outside the secondary, darkest between (Alexander's band). Rain curtains: two octaves of drifting value noise along `x − 0.15 y` (slanted with the rain), refreshed per drawn frame into a per-column table; they modulate the sunlit rain and so the bow along its arc (×0.55–1). *(M14: the anticrepuscular shafts, the cloud-base mottling and the rain streaks are removed, ADR-0017.)* Sun breaks: the sunlight on the rain is value noise in time (6 s cells) mapped to 0.4–1, dimming and brightening the whole bow and the sunlit rain. The bow's strength is its band light × 1.35 × (0.35 + 0.65 · min(1, sunlit rain / 0.6)), the sunlit rain being rain strength × Sun × curtain factor.
- **Motion**: the Sun sinks from 18° to 8° over a 50 s loop (the scene opens 12 % in, with the shower under way), so the bow rises; the rain swells and ebbs and thins at the loop's seam, so the Sun's jump back is never seen; curtains drift across, brightening and dimming the bow along its arc; the Sun breaks through and hides over seconds, brightening and dimming the whole bow.
- **Resize**: the LUT is size-independent; view tables, curtain table and hills rebuilt.
- **Inks** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: the bow is drawn where its band's light exceeds 0.3 of its own peak (a property of the angle), in the nearest spectral ramp: red `HAlpha`, orange `Ember`, yellow `Sulfur`, green `Aurora`, blue `Cobalt`, violet `Plasma` (cyan has no ramp here), at level `200·(1 − e^{−1.6x})/(1 − e^{−1.6})` of its strength `x` (a soft shoulder to mid lightness, no flat plateau), and only where that is at least level 10 and above the sky's. Sky: `Ramp(Starlight)` `16·low²` (low = the pixel's depth toward the horizon, so black overhead and a faint grey low down) plus the sunlit rain's lift `8·sky light·sunlit rain` inside the primary, black in Alexander's band; the supernumeraries lift it by up to 18, lightness only; below level 10 black. Hills black. ASCII: bow cells `+` (one pixel) or `#` (both); the sky is blank.
- **Title**: `Rainbow · raindrops split light`.
- **Stylisation**: dispersion widened ×2.5 (primary) and ×1.15 (secondary); an analytic angle → wavelength band shape instead of summed Airy fringes; the bow's hues stepped through six named ramps; supernumeraries lightness-only and ~2.5× wider apart than for real 0.5 mm drops; the storm sky drawn black (ADR-0017); the framing shows 40° of sky above a horizon at 90 % of the height.
- **Acceptance**: `BOW-A1` at 589 nm the primary's minimum-deviation angle is 42.0 ± 0.5° and the secondary's 50.5–51.5°; `BOW-A2` in the primary red is outside violet, and in the secondary the order is reversed (in the Descartes angles and in the drawn wavelengths; the primary's rim is `HAlpha`, its inner edge `Plasma`); `BOW-A3` the mean sky brightness between the bows is lower than just inside the primary and just outside the secondary; `BOW-A4` supernumerary maxima lie inside the primary (Airy's and the drawn ripples); `BOW-A5` *(M14, replaces the rain pool)* at 120×40 the sky is black or `Starlight` no brighter than 16 + 8 + 18 levels, black in the top 8 pixel rows, and more than 300 bow pixels are drawn, none above level 200; `BOW-A6` the bow's light changes by < 0.12 per 0.05° from 30° to 60° (no threshold), and the primary shows the ramp sequence `HAlpha`, `Ember`, `Sulfur`, `Aurora`, `Cobalt`, `Plasma` in order; `BOW-A7` the bow's mean level in the left, middle and right thirds changes over 8 s (the curtains move along the arc); `BOW-A8` *(M14)* inside the primary (36°) the sky is black or a faint `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 40, and Alexander's band (46.5°) is black; `BOW-A9` the Sun factor stays in [0.4, 1] and swings by > 0.4 within a minute and by > 0.3 within 10 s somewhere, and at its dimmest the bow's summed level is < 90 % of that at its brightest.

### meteor_shower  (Earth & Sky, M13)
- **Intent**: a dark summer sky at the Perseids' peak: meteors flash in every part of the sky, yet every streak points back to one radiant; bright ones leave green glowing trains that linger and twist in the upper-atmosphere wind.
- **Model**: meteoroids share one velocity vector (the stream); each meteor is a straight path segment through the ablation layer (Perseids: begins 105–125 km, brighter ones higher; ends 80–95 km, brighter ones deeper), seen through a gnomonic (rectilinear) lens facing north, which keeps paths straight and sends them all back to one vanishing point, the radiant; paths seen near the radiant are foreshortened. The radiant is placed in frame (upper third, off to one side) and marked by a faint ring of 16 braille dots (`Ramp(Starlight)` 80) with a dim `radiant` label; 30 % of meteors start within 22° of it, short foreshortened streaks fanning out of it. Pool of 48 meteors; arrivals are a Poisson process (seeded `ctx.rng`, 2.4/s, slowly swelling and ebbing); magnitudes from a power law (population index 2.2, faintest drawn 3.5, clamped at −4); light curve `√(27/4 u²(1 − u))` along the path. Each streak is drawn in braille: a white-hot head (a 2 × 2 dot blaze over a small `Glow` glare) and a tail behind it over 40–75 % of the path (longer for bright meteors), two dots wide just behind the head, then one, thinning toward its end (hashed dots dropped, ever more of them), white-hot at the head (its last 12 %), green (`Ramp(Aurora)` 175–235, magnesium and oxygen) back to 45 % of the tail, then yellow (`Ramp(Sulfur)` 120–190, sodium and iron) to its end; a faint green glow a pixel or two wide is stroked along the bright half of the tail into the trains' layer (a thin anti-aliased thread: bilinear splats every half pixel). Finished tracks join a faint braille composite that fades in 3.5 s (as in a stacked photograph of a shower; ≤ 32 kept), so at any moment several recent tracks show, all pointing back to the radiant. Nothing is drawn over the land: streaks, heads, glare, glow and trains stop at the treeline (only the sky's anti-aliased edge shows below the land's top). Trains (pool 12) from meteors of magnitude ≤ 0.6: 24 points along the brightest part of the track that drift with a sheared wind (two sinusoidal layers, 3.5 and 2.2 km/s time-lapsed, of vertical wavelength 7 and 12.5 km), stroked each step into a second `Glow` as a thin anti-aliased thread one to two pixels wide, dimming as it spreads (time constant 0.8 s) and fading over 1.8–3.2 s (the O I 557.7 nm afterglow, time-lapsed), drawn at half their toned level so the streaks stay the hero. Background: fixed stars (`Blackbody`), a mottled Milky Way (peak 50: a broad glow plus a brighter spine 0.38 widths wide, knotted with star clouds by two octaves of value noise along and across it) with a dark rift and dust lanes (two octaves of value noise stretched along the band, darkening it by up to 80 % near its spine), horizon airglow (44 at the horizon, e-folding 9°) against which an uneven spruce ridge with clearings (`Skyline`, trees 1.4–5.2°, gaps 0.7–2.9°) stands with anti-aliased edges.
- **Motion**: several meteors a second; trains drift, twist and fade. The sky itself is fixed: the loop spans minutes of a night, and a 15°/h turn would only jitter single dots.
- **Resize**: lens, radiant and sky rebuilt; the shower restarts with a train already in the sky and a meteor due at once.
- **Inks**: meteor heads `Ramp(Starlight)` 255 (braille) over a `Ramp(Starlight)` glare, tails `Ramp(Aurora)` then `Ramp(Sulfur)` (braille) over their green glow, trains `Ramp(Aurora)` ≤ 128 (green O I), stars `Blackbody`, composite tracks `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 70 (braille), Milky Way and airglow `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 100 ending in true black (a narrow dissolve between levels 10 and 14, CORE-R89), land black. ASCII: trains by level; each flying meteor's last stretch as `-/\|` with the head `*`.
- **Title**: `Meteor shower · comet dust burns`.
- **Stylisation**: rate far above a real ZHR (~100/h); paths drawn 5× longer than the ablation layer allows and flown ×3.5 faster so a streak reads on a terminal; streak colours simplified to white, green and yellow; trains brightened and time-lapsed; the radiant marked.
- **Acceptance**: `MET-A1` every meteor's track, extended backward, passes within 1° of the radiant; `MET-A2` meteors near the radiant are shorter on screen than those 60° away (mean ratio < 0.5); `MET-A3` magnitude counts follow the population index within ±20 % over 1000 samples; `MET-A4` trains fade monotonically and drift (and bend: the wind is sheared); `MET-A5` the pools never exceed capacity; `MET-A6` every meteor begins 105–125 km up and (its drawn path divided by the stretch) ends 80–95 km up; `MET-A7` the radiant projects inside the frame above the horizon, and rendered streak cells are only white-hot `Starlight`, green `Aurora` and yellow `Sulfur` (besides the `Blackbody` stars), both tail colours appearing; `MET-A8` trains are thin (a stroked thread lights ≤ 2 pixel rows), short-lived (life ≤ 4.5 s), faint (`Aurora` ≤ 128) and never drawn over the land.

### noctilucent_clouds  (Earth & Sky, M13)
- **Intent**: late in a northern summer night, around midnight: the landscape and low clouds are dark, but silver-blue clouds glow on a black sky, rippled with soft waves, still in sunlight at 83 km, over a thin warm glow on the northern horizon.
- **Model**: geometry: the Sun at depression `d` below the northern horizon; a point of the 83 km layer seen at elevation `e` and azimuth `a` (spherical Earth) is lit when its ray toward the Sun clears the Earth plus a 20 km screening layer (ozone and haze), softened over 4 km; tropospheric clouds (≤ 12 km) never clear it. Overhead the layer is in shadow, so the lit clouds form a band above the northern horizon. The cloud's density: 6 drifting gravity-wave packets (2 long smooth "bands", λ 35–55 km; 4 "billows", λ 9–16 km, soft crests `0.35 + 0.65 (1 − |w|)^1.5` on a sheet of ice rather than thin lines on dark gaps) whose envelopes wander to and fro about their centres (12 s of drift at 14 km/s, 70 s period, so the field fills the sky all loop and never drifts away), times a patchy drifting value-noise envelope, projected onto the sky; brightness × forward scattering (brightest toward the Sun) × slant path. Evaluated on a lattice capped at 8500 nodes (ADR-0012) from per-node `cos/sin(k·x)` tables (so a node costs two products per packet), then sampled bilinearly to pixels (`SampleMap`); a packet's waves fade out where the lattice resolves fewer than 4–8 nodes per wavelength (far off, near the horizon), so they never alias into stripes. As the Sun sinks the ice's contrast against the darker sky rises (×1.8 at the deepest, stylised), so the late band stays bright; over the lowest degrees (2.8–6.4°) the ice fades out before the warm glow (long, reddened air path). Twilight sky: one smooth lightness (an arch brightest low toward the Sun's azimuth, e-folding 5–9°, plus a horizon glow peaking at 72 and e-folding 3°) whose hue runs by arch elevation from warm `Ember` (< 4.4°, its top an uneven soft edge: a slow value-noise wave of ±0.9° along the horizon plus a ±0.25° per-pixel fray) to deep teal `Aurora` (its dark end, < 11°) to indigo `Plasma` overhead, the bands meeting at equal lightness. One to three long, soft stratus wisps (thickness wandering along them by value noise, hiding ≤ 55 % of the glow) and low boreal hills with sparse spruce (the shared `Skyline`) in silhouette.
- **Motion**: wave packets drift and interfere (phase speed time-lapsed); `d` deepens from 8.2° to 9.8° and recovers over 80 s (the midnight Sun skims below the northern horizon), so the lit band sinks toward the horizon and rises again, while the Sun's azimuth swings from north-west to north-east.
- **Resize**: lattice, view and cover rebuilt; wave packets in km (reseeded).
- **Inks** *(M14, ADR-0017)*: black sky. The ice is sampled Catmull-Rom from the lattice (CORE-R102, one row of taps per pixel row) and stretched so the field's brightest node reaches 0.6 (at most ×2.5), so the clouds keep an area in every act; above a floor (0.08) each picture pixel's four braille dots light by a fixed hash against its density `u/0.45` (full from `u` = 0.45), in `Ramp(Ice)` 160–205 by density: pale silver-blue, bright on the crests, thinning into black with no navy floor. The warm glow is a few pixels of `Blackbody` over the land's top (so it runs on unbroken behind the hills): the 1800 K level times `e^{−up/1.6 px}`, stronger toward the Sun, its top uneven along the horizon (±0.9°), black below 0.12 of its peak. A few faint stars `Ramp(Starlight)`; land and low clouds black. ASCII: the ice by dot count.
- **Title**: `Noctilucent clouds · sunlit ice`.
- **Stylisation**: wave field procedural; lit/unlit edge softened; time-lapse ×30.
- **Acceptance**: `NLC-A1` the lit test: a point at 83 km is lit at depression 10° for elevations below the computed shadow line (toward the Sun) and unlit above it; a point at 10 km is unlit for any depression > 1°; `NLC-A2` as `d` increases the lit area shrinks monotonically; `NLC-A3` NLC pixels appear only where lit; `NLC-A4` the wave field moves (non-zero change over 1 s); `NLC-A5` the picture holds only black and the `Blackbody` glow; the ice is `Ramp(Ice)` braille at levels 160–205, reaching the top of that span; `NLC-A6` ice pixels (dotted) cover > 4 % of the picture at every 5 s through the whole 80 s loop; `NLC-A7` the sky is black over a `Blackbody` horizon glow at most 8 px deep, in more than 20 columns at 120×40; the warm glow's top edge varies along the horizon within ±0.9°.

### star_trails  (Earth & Sky, M13)
- **Intent**: a long-exposure photograph building before your eyes: concentric coloured arcs grow around the celestial pole above a treeline, blue and orange stars drawing arcs of their own colour, the pole star a tiny bright circle.
- **Model**: stars (≤ 2500, uniform on the cap of sky the frame can show, magnitudes `N(< m) ∝ 10^{0.5 m}` to 6.0, `Blackbody` from a naked-eye spectral mix via `ink::class_kelvin`, saturated away from the 6500 K white point as in a processed photograph) at fixed positions; Polaris 0.7° from the pole. The camera is a rectilinear (gnomonic) lens centred on the pole, which stands at the seeded latitude (35–50°): a star `ρ` from the pole is `f·tan ρ` from it on screen, so every trail is an exact circle about the pole, and the horizon (a great circle) is a straight row `f·tan φ` below it. Earth turns 15.04°/h, time-lapsed ×800, so a 45 s exposure sweeps ~150° arcs. The exposure is a per-dot buffer (the hottest trail's `Blackbody` level per braille dot, allocated once per size): each step paints the dots each star crossed since the last (stars brighter than magnitude 1.5 two dots wide). Stars fainter than magnitude 4.4 draw no braille line: their light goes into a soft `Glow` exposure (bilinear splats along the arc, an even light per unit of arc so the slow stars round the pole do not pile up) toned onto the sky, a dim haze of rings under the few bright coloured trails: a brightness hierarchy by level. The scene opens 10 s into an exposure, so the first frame already shows arcs. After 45 s the trails fade out over 5 s (a hashed share of dots dropped each step) and a new exposure starts. Stars below the treeline (the shared `Skyline`, `src/shared/skyline.rs`: near spruces, anti-aliased) are hidden, and no trail dot is drawn over a land pixel (the paint-time test sees one height per dot column, which can slip between two spires).
- **Motion**: arcs lengthen continuously; circumpolar stars sweep round the pole; others rise and set behind the trees.
- **Resize**: projection rebuilt; the exposure is cleared and restarts.
- **Inks**: bright trails (magnitude ≤ 4.4) `Blackbody` at the star's temperature (braille dots, drawn straight from the exposure buffer; the brightest thicker); faint trails a grey haze in `Ramp(Starlight)` (≤ +92, black below level 12); sky black *(M14, ADR-0017)* with a barely-lit `Starlight` skyglow on the horizon (≤ 22, e-folding 2.5°); trees black; trail colours saturated ×1.15 (was ×1.7). ASCII: braille dot counts (`.:+#`); the skyglow is not drawn.
- **Title**: `Star trails · Earth turns`.
- **Stylisation**: time-lapse; a stacked exposure; star colours saturated; bright trails carry colour, faint ones only light; faint-trail exposure is even along the arc.
- **Acceptance**: `TRL-A1` every trail point stays at a constant distance from the pole (± 0.5 px), hence a constant angular distance; `TRL-A2` arc length in angle is the same for all stars (15.04°/h × time-lapse × exposure ≈ 150°) and on screen ∝ the projected pole distance `f·tan ρ`; `TRL-A3` stars with declination > 90° − latitude never cross the horizon; `TRL-A4` the exposure buffer stays allocated once (no growth) and resets at the loop; `TRL-A5` braille trail inks are `Blackbody`; `TRL-A6` the sky is black at the top (or a faint trail's grey haze), barely lit just above the land (≤ 24), and no braille trail cell lies over land.

### lenia  (Living Systems, M13)
- **Intent**: smooth, glowing creatures that look alive: Orbium gliders swim across a dark field, bump, and a damage beat tears one apart, after which it regrows its shape.
- **Model**: Lenia (Chan 2019) on a capped periodic lattice (≤ 96×64, `lattice_for`): `A ← clip(A + Δt·G(K * A))`, ring kernel `K` of radius R ≤ 10 (static offset/weight list, ≤ 320 taps), growth `G(u) = 2 e^{−(u−μ)²/(2σ²)} − 1` with Orbium parameters (μ = 0.15, σ = 0.015, Δt = 0.1); seeds from a static pattern table (Orbium). Stepping at 12 Hz from the fixed 60 Hz update (every 5th update) with the field linearly interpolated between steps for display; the stepping frame is benched as the worst frame. Damage beat every ~25 s: a disc of cells zeroed; watchdog reseeds if total mass leaves [0.3, 3]× the seed's.
- **Motion**: gliders drift ~1 cell per step; shimmer from interpolation.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012); on too-small lattices one glider is reseeded.
- **Inks**: `Ramp(Aurora)` (bioluminescent look, false colour) via `Pixels`. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Lenia · life from smooth rules`.
- **Stylisation**: artificial life (no organism is modelled; Living Systems by ADR-0013's artificial-life clause).
- **Acceptance**: `LNA-A1` the Orbium seed on an empty lattice survives 30 s with mass within ±15 % of its start; `LNA-A2` its centre of mass moves (≥ 5 cells in 30 s); `LNA-A3` after the damage beat mass recovers to ≥ 80 % within 10 s; `LNA-A4` the stepping frame at 200×50 is < 2 ms (bench); `LNA-A5` values stay in [0, 1]; `LNA-A6` the staged engulf ends with one creature holding 40–60 % of the pair's mass at 6 s and 10 s, at several lattice sizes and headings; `LNA-A7` the staged glance leaves two creatures with ≥ 90 % of the mass at 8 s; `LNA-A8` a lost glider is replaced after the birth delay, not before (Round 3); `LNA-A9` no frame is empty: over two full act loops at 80×24 and 120×40, sampled every 0.25 s, every frame shows lit creature pixels (Round 4).
- **As built (batch 5)**: `R = 13` (the published Orbium table, un-resampled; at `R = 10` the resampled Orbium is lumpy and dies from most bites), so the kernel has ≤ 560 taps (LNA-A4 bounds taps × nodes, ≤ 6144 nodes); up to 3 gliders (one per 2000 nodes). The damage beat is a bite of radius 4 nodes centred 8 nodes from the heaviest node in a random direction (a disc through the core kills an Orbium); besides the reseed band, a glider that dies in a collision or bite is replaced by a new one opposite the heaviest creature.
- **Round 2**: a shoal of up to 3 gliders shares one heading (no mirroring), so they swim in parallel and never merge; a dead glider is replaced in the emptiest place; bites every 12 s; every 50 s the dish fades (1.5 s) and a new shoal on a new heading hatches. Inks: the thin membrane `Ramp(Ice)`, the dense core `Ramp(Aurora)` (two hues, one luminous body).
- **Round 3**: acts of 30 s cycle shoal → engulf → shoal → glance. *Engulf*: two gliders on courses a quarter turn apart, aimed to meet 2.5 s after hatching with an 8-node sideways offset: one glider absorbs the other and a single clean Orbium swims on (LNA-A6). *Glance*: opposite courses offset 12 nodes: they brush past and both survive (LNA-A7). The meetings are deterministic (Lenia has no noise), so these set-ups were chosen once by trial and the whole configuration turns with the heading; they are staged only on lattices ≥ 48 (engulf) / ≥ 56 (glance) nodes a side, else the act is a shoal. A glider lost to a meeting or a bite is replaced by a **birth** 3 s later in the emptiest place, fading in over 1.2 s (display only) (LNA-A8). The watchdog's upper bound drops to 1.6× the seeded mass, so a runaway merger is cleared early. Inks (warmer, distinct): the membrane and halo `Ramp(Eosin)` (rose), the dense core `Ramp(Ember)` (amber to pale gold). (acceptance IDs added to the Acceptance line).
- **Round 4**: the dish never goes dark. The fade-to-black between acts is gone; every reseed (act change or watchdog) cross-fades instead: the outgoing creatures are copied into a ghost world that keeps stepping while it dissolves, the new act condenses over the first second and the ghost fades over the second (display only; the ghost costs one extra world step per stepping frame for 2 s) (LNA-A9).

### firefly_sync  (Living Systems, M13)
- **Intent**: a mangrove riverbank at night: thousands of fireflies flash at random, then patches fall into step, and within a minute the whole tree pulses as one.
- **Model**: Mirollo–Strogatz pulse-coupled oscillators (600, positions on a seeded tree-silhouette mask); phase `φ` advances at `1/T` (T ≈ 0.6 s ± 5 %, the *Pteroptyx* rhythm of ~0.5–0.6 s); on crossing 1 a firefly flashes and neighbours within radius r (via `SpatialHash`) get their state bumped by ε through the concave charging curve `f(φ)`, absorbing when pushed past 1. Order parameter `R = |⟨e^{2πiφ}⟩|` shown as a small right-aligned inset sparkline. Every ~45 s a disturbance (a boat's wake of light) scrambles phases.
- **Motion**: flashes; sync spreads from clusters; order parameter rises to ~1.
- **Resize**: positions kept in mask units; hash rebuilt.
- **Inks** (M14, ADR-0017; replaces Round 2's moonlit scene): a black night. The tree is only its silhouette's 1-px outline (the leafy crown's edge, trunks and prop roots down to the waterline) in `Ramp(Starlight)` 38; no sky, moon, stars, water, reflection or R-sparkline. Flashes are the accent: one firefly in every `⌈8 · canopy px / N⌉` is drawn, each a 1-px point with a faint 1-px cross (weights 1 and 0.12) exposed into a `Glow`, toned into `Ramp(Sulfur)` (the yellow-green of *Pteroptyx*) from level 34, peaking above 190. Sync is never perfect: 5 laggard fireflies glow as dim `Sulfur` 90 points between the pulses, so the synchronised tree is dark but never blank (FLY-A6). The boat is a warm `Ramp(Ember)` 230 lantern point on the waterline with a short fading 1-px wake. The readout is `R 0.93` alone, right-aligned. ASCII: flash `*` or `.`.
- **Title**: `Fireflies · flashes fall in step`.
- **Stylisation**: behavioural rule (the flash reset), not photochemistry; ranges and coupling tuned so sync takes ~30 s.
- **Acceptance**: `FLY-A1` without coupling the order parameter stays below 0.3; `FLY-A2` with coupling it exceeds 0.9 within 60 s for 3 seeds; `FLY-A3` a flash resets a firefly's phase to 0; `FLY-A4` the `R` readout stays right of the title span with a gap (GEN-R13); `FLY-A5` the scramble lowers `R` below 0.4; `FLY-A6` a flash is bright (level > 190) and has left the screen by half a period, so a synchronised crown is dark between pulses. `FLY-A7` (M14) over 8 s at 120×40, seed 42, every lit pixel is the outline (`Starlight` 38), a flash or a laggard (`Sulfur`) or the lantern (`Ember`), and a flash peaks above 190.
- **As built (batch 5)**: coupling radius 0.06 mask units (≈ 18 neighbours; the mask is 2 × 1), `ε = 0.01`, which syncs in 12–31 s over seeds; flashes last ≈ 0.12 s. The boat's lantern crosses the water in 5 s every 45 s (first at 45 s); a firefly it passes gets a random phase and ignores its neighbours for 7 s (startled fireflies flash on their own), which is what drops `R` below 0.4: without it the synchronised majority re-absorbs them at once. The inset is a 16-sample sparkline of `R` (one per second) left of the readout `R 0.93`, both right-aligned on row `h − 1`. No captions.
- **Round 2**: a moonlit scene: moon disc and halo, foliage as dim `Ramp(Aurora)` clumps with moonlit crown edges (not black), water with ripples and a glitter path under the moon; each flash leaves a 0.6 s afterglow (0.18 of the flash), so a wave of flashes leaves a visible wake as sync spreads.
- **Round 3**: `T` = 0.6 s (was 1 s; the model and FLY-A2 hold unchanged, syncing faster in wall time). Each firefly is a one-pixel lamp with a radius-1 halo, light `e^{−s/0.08} + 0.12·e^{−s/0.3}` (FLY-A6): between flashes the crown is dark, so the synchronised flash is a whole-tree pulse. Where the canopy has fewer than 3 pixels per firefly (80×24) only every k-th firefly is drawn. The crown is a leafy silhouette (ellipse edge frayed by value noise, clump and fine leaf texture, moonward rim light); the three trunks are solid and tapered, and the prop roots fan outward like umbrella ribs (no closed loops). The moon is a soft-edged disc with faint maria in an exponential glow. The water carries a rippled, squashed reflection of the flashes (its top row mirrors the crown's underside at y = 0.46, 1.4 rows per water row; artistic, the true mirror image would fall off screen).

### heart_fibrillation  (Physics of Life, M13)
- **Intent**: a heart's electrical activity as a phase map: at first regular waves spread from the pacemaker; a premature beat breaks a wave into a spinning spiral (re-entry), which splits into many chaotic spirals (fibrillation); an ECG strip at the bottom turns from neat beats to chaos; a defibrillator shock resets everything.
- **Model**: `excitable` (shared `pub(crate)`): Aliev–Panfilov two-variable model on a heart-shaped mask (capped ≤ 160×80), explicit Euler with sub-steps inside 1/60 s, no-flux boundaries at the mask edge; spiral breakup imposed by patchy refractoriness (`ε` scaled ×0.2 to ×2 in smooth random patches during the VF act), not by restitution alone. Sinus rhythm: periodic stimulus at the sinoatrial corner; re-entry: an S1–S2 cross-field stimulus; VF: breakup; defibrillation: set all cells excited then recover. Phase `θ = atan2(v − v*, u − u*)` for display; phase singularities (points where the phase winds by 2π) marked. ECG: a pseudo-ECG (sum of `∇u·∇(1/r)` at an electrode point) scrolling in the bottom rows above the title.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: sinus (12 s) → re-entry (10 s) → VF (14 s) → shock and recovery (6 s).
- **Resize**: mask and lattice rebuilt (ADR-0012); the state resets to sinus.
- **Inks**: excitation phase `Role::Phase` (cyclic, a standard phase map), resting tissue dim, singularities `Starlight` 255, ECG `Ramp(Starlight)`. ASCII by level; singularities `@`.
- **Title**: `Fibrillation · spirals break up`; captions `sinus rhythm`, `re-entry`, `fibrillation`, `defibrillation`.
- **Stylisation**: a 2-D sheet shaped like a heart, not a 3-D ventricle; the model is phenomenological; breakup into fibrillation comes from imposed patchy refractoriness (`ε` ×0.2–×2), not from restitution alone; the pseudo-ECG is qualitative.
- **Acceptance**: `HRT-A1` a single stimulus on resting tissue produces one wave that leaves the tissue at rest again; `HRT-A2` the S1–S2 protocol produces a phase singularity that persists ≥ 3 s; `HRT-A3` in the VF act the singularity count is ≥ 4 on average; `HRT-A4` after the shock the count is 0 within 1 s; `HRT-A5` u and v stay finite and in range for all sub-steps; `HRT-A6` the outline has partly covered pixels, a thin rim band, and nothing outside the curve; `HRT-A7` the ECG cell at the write head carries the head ink (Round 3).
- **As built (batch 5)**: breakup comes from patchy refractoriness, not restitution alone: in the fibrillation act `ε` is scaled per node by smooth random patches (×0.2 to ×2), which breaks the re-entrant spiral within ≈ 4 s into 7–16 tips at 200×50 (Aliev–Panfilov `k = 8, a = 0.1, ε₀ = 0.002, μ₁ = 0.2, μ₂ = 0.3, D = 0.5`, `dt = 0.05` tu, 40 tu/s). The lattice is the square view over the heart (≤ 12 800 nodes); the node spacing keeps the heart 120 space units tall (≤ 1.6 per node, so small terminals hold fewer spirals). Sinus beats every 1.5 s from the upper-left lobe; re-entry is the S1–S2 cross-field protocol (S1 at the left edge, S2 on the recovered lower half once S1's tail crosses mid-heart); the shock sets every node to `u = 1, v = 0`. ECG: 4 (or 2) rows above the title, a sweeping braille trace. Readout `spiral tips N` right-aligned on row `h − 1`.
- **Round 2 (inks changed)**: the phase map is drawn in two saturated ramps instead of `Role::Phase`: excited tissue `Ramp(Ember)` (white-gold at the fresh front, orange on the plateau), the refractory tail `Ramp(Cobalt)` fading to near-black resting tissue (`Starlight` 20) with a one-pixel rim; the outline is the analytic heart curve at pixel resolution. The ECG is AC-coupled (0.15 s baseline) and drawn 3 braille dots thick in `Ramp(Aurora)` (monitor green) over a dim glow.
- **Round 3**: the heart outline is antialiased: per view pixel the curve's coverage and a ~1.3 px rim band (the heart minus a copy shrunk about its centre) are 4×4 supersampled once per resize; every tissue ink is dimmed by coverage and the resting rim is `Starlight` 80 × band coverage (HRT-A6). The ECG trace peaks at level 205 and is led by a white write head (`Starlight` 255, a braille disc of radius 2.1 dots) with a green halo in the pixels behind it (HRT-A7). (acceptance IDs added to the Acceptance line).

### motility_clustering  (Physics of Life, M13)
- **Intent**: a drop of swimming bacteria: each swims straight and then turns; where swimmers collide they block each other, and crowds jam into clusters that grow and merge while a gas of free swimmers streams around them.
- **Model**: active Brownian particles (≤ 3000 at 200×50, density-scaled) with self-propulsion `v₀` along heading `θ`, rotational diffusion `D_r` (persistence), and soft repulsion via `SpatialHash`; periodic box. Motility-induced phase separation (Péclet ≥ 80, area fraction ~0.4). Framed as bacteria (elongated glyphs by heading). Acts: dilute (no clusters) → crowded (MIPS) → dispersal: a repellent/stress cue makes them slow (`v₀` ×0.5) and tumble more (`D_r` ×6), `Pe` falls ~12×, and the clusters dissolve.
- **Motion**: swimmers move each frame; clusters coarsen over ~20 s.
- **Resize**: box rescaled; particles kept in box units, count adjusted to keep the area fraction.
- **Inks**: free swimmers by heading `Role::Phase`, clustered swimmers (≥ 5 neighbours) `Ramp(Starlight)` 200 so clusters read as solid, background black. ASCII: `.` / `o`.
- **Title**: `Bacteria crowds · swimmers jam`; captions `dilute`, `clusters`, `dispersal`.
- **Stylisation**: bacteria abstracted as self-propelled discs (no flagella, no hydrodynamics); B by framing (Physics of Life), as the research notes require.
- **Acceptance**: `MIPS-A1` with `v₀ = 0` the system has no clusters (largest cluster < 2 % of particles); `MIPS-A2` at the crowded setting the largest cluster exceeds 20 % within 30 s; `MIPS-A3` a lone swimmer's mean-square displacement is ballistic at short times and diffusive at long; `MIPS-A4` the particle count never exceeds its cap; `MIPS-A5` captions follow the acts; `MIPS-A6` a swimmer's rod level rises with its contacts (every jammed rod outshines every free one) and its heading shifts the hue within the band.
- **As built (batch 5)**: ≤ 2400 swimmers (not 3000: the stepping budget at 200×50), crowded area fraction 0.6 (0.5 separates only weakly with soft discs), `v₀ = 6 σ/s`, `D_r = 0.18/s` (`Pe = 100`), repulsion `k = 80/s`, 360 sub-steps/s, heading as a unit vector turned by uniform kicks of the right variance. Acts 6 s dilute (area fraction 0.12) → 34 s clusters (swimmers arrive over 3 s) → 12 s dispersal. **Dispersal changed**: faster running would strengthen MIPS, so the swimmers instead slow (×0.5) and tumble more (`D_r` ×6), `Pe ≈ 8`, and the clusters evaporate. Clusters (MIPS-A1, A2) are contact graphs of swimmers pressed closer than 0.97 σ. Clustered swimmers are Starlight, brighter with more contacts. Readout `N 2264  clustered 46%`.
- **Round 2**: swimmers are ≥ 2.6 px (fewer, larger: 1085 at 120×40, cap 2400), 240 sub-steps/s; free swimmers are one cold hue (`Ramp(Ice)` rods, bright head, dim tail), clustered swimmers expose a soft glow (radius-2 halo, asinh tone) so clusters read as textured luminous bodies; `Role::Phase` is no longer used. Dilute act 6 s.
- **Round 3**: every swimmer is a braille rod (`Dots`, 1.25 σ long) in one ramp, `Ramp(Aurora)` (deep teal → lime-white): its heading picks the hue within a band and its contacts lift the band from dim teal (free, levels 74–130) to bright lime (≥ 8 contacts, 186–255) (MIPS-A6). Crowds (≥ 3 contacts) splat a radius-3 halo into a `Glow`, blurred `[1 2 1]` and toned to a dark Aurora backdrop (≤ 100) under the rods (`Dots::blit_over`), so a cluster is the same rods packed and lit, not a fill. Dilute area fraction 0.08 (was 0.12), so the clusters pop when the crowd arrives. ASCII: the rods' dot-count fallback; the glow is Unicode only.

### dicty_aggregation  (Molecules of Life, M13)
- **Intent**: a lawn of starving slime-mould amoebae: waves of the signal cAMP spiral outward, each amoeba relays the signal and steps toward it, and the lawn breaks into branching streams that pour into mounds, as in the famous dark-field films.
- **Model**: `excitable` (shared `pub(crate)`): a Barkley-type two-variable excitable relay (stands in for the Martiel–Goldbeter reduction) on a capped lattice (≤ 160×80) with diffusion; cells (pool ≤ 3000) sit on the lattice, secrete cAMP when excited, and when the local cAMP rises they move up its gradient for a short burst (chemotaxis is behaviour; cell density raises local excitability, giving streaming). Spiral centres seeded by a few pacemaker cells.
- **Motion**: waves every ~7 s; cells step with each wave; streams form over ~30 s, mounds by ~50 s; reset beat.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded; cells kept in lattice units.
- **Inks**: cells `Ramp(Starlight)` (the dark-field look: moving cells bright), cAMP `Ramp(Cobalt)` ≤ 120 (false colour). ASCII: cells by density.
- **Title**: `Slime amoebae · cAMP waves call`.
- **Stylisation**: time-lapse (hours in a minute: real waves come every ~6 min, here every 7 s); cAMP is invisible in reality (shown as its rising edge only); relay kinetics reduced to two variables.
- **Acceptance**: `DICT-A1` a single excitation on resting medium produces a wave followed by refractoriness (no immediate re-excitation); `DICT-A2` cells move only up-gradient during a wave; `DICT-A3` the cell density's spatial variance rises by ≥ 3× over 50 s (streaming/aggregation); `DICT-A4` cell count is conserved and within the pool; `DICT-A5` cAMP stays non-negative and finite; `DICT-A6` a wave draws only its rising edge: nothing where the cells are refractory (`v ≥ 0.3`).
- **As built (batch 5)**: the relay is the shared Barkley kernel (`a = 0.75, ε = 0.03`, `h = 0.5`, `τ = 0.025`, 5 tu/s) with excitability `b` from the blurred local cell density (`b = 0.01` dense → `0.42` empty, where fronts die); up to 3 pacemakers fire every 7 s; a cell steps up `∇u` at 18 nodes/s only while `u` rises at a non-refractory cell; nodes with ≥ 8 cells take no more (mound packing). The lawn is re-scattered every 70 s (2 s fade). The cAMP wash is brightest at the rising front. No captions.
- **Round 2**: the lawn starts with 1–2 spiral seeds (broken fronts, painted with the shared `excitable::paint_front`, moved there from `belousov_zhabotinsky`); cells also drift up the density gradient (adhesion, 10 nodes/s per unit excitability gradient) when not chemotaxing, which makes streams coherent; chemotaxis 10 nodes/s; up to 14 cells per node. Streams by ≈ 20 s, mounds by ≈ 40 s; the cycle is 55 s. Cells render as a soft glow; the cAMP front wash reaches `Cobalt` ≈ 170.
- **Round 3**: the classic dark-field look. Cells are a smoothed density (the cell glow blurred `[1 2 1]`) in dim amber, `Ramp(Ember)` ≤ 200 through a gamma of 1.4, so the fresh lawn (0.24 cells per node, was 0.3) is quiet and the streams and mounds glow; bare ground stays black. The cAMP wave draws only its rising edge (`u·(1 − v/0.3)` above 0.2, DICT-A6) as a thin cyan-white `Ramp(Ice)` ring (150–255), with a faint wake (`v` < 0.55, levels ≤ 80) on bare ground. ASCII: cells `.`/`o`/`@` by level, waves `:`.

### seashell_pigmentation  (Molecules of Life, M13)
- **Intent**: a shell grows edge by edge and paints itself: along the growing lip a chemical reaction lays down pigment, and as rows accumulate, triangles, tents, zigzags and stripes appear, like an olive or cone shell.
- **Model**: Meinhardt activator–inhibitor on the 1-D growing edge (≤ 400 cells, periodic), explicit sub-steps; each "growth step" the edge's pigment (activator above threshold) is written as a new row of a scrolling `Pixels` image, so time becomes the shell's length. Parameter sets along a `Timeline` produce: oblique lines (travelling waves), triangles (branching and annihilation), stripes (oscillation); shell outline (a logarithmic spiral mask) frames the pattern.
- **Motion**: a new row every ~0.1 s; the pattern scrolls; each regime ~20 s, the shell redrawn.
- **Resize**: edge regridded to the width; the scroll buffer rebuilt.
- **Inks**: pigment `Ramp(Ferroin)` (the purple-brown of *Oliva porphyria*), shell ground `Ramp(Starlight)` 200 (cream). ASCII: pigment `#`. *Superseded by Round 3 (chestnut `Role::Blackbody` pigment).*
- **Title**: `Shell patterns · pigment waves`; captions `waves`, `tents`, `stripes`.
- **Stylisation**: the 1-D model runs on a flat strip mapped into a spiral outline; parameters chosen for looks from Meinhardt's *The Algorithmic Beauty of Sea Shells*.
- **Acceptance**: `SHL-A1` concentrations stay non-negative and finite; `SHL-A2` in the waves regime a pigment peak moves by a constant offset per row (oblique line); `SHL-A3` in the tents regime waves annihilate on collision (peak count drops when two meet); `SHL-A4` exactly one row is appended per growth step; `SHL-A5` captions follow the regime; `SHL-A6` at 80×24 the waves regime carries ≥ 2 lines after 12 s; `SHL-A7` at 80×24 the shell spans 75–95 % of the art rows and its flank pixels are antialiased (Round 3).
- **As built (batch 5)**: Meinhardt's activator–inhibitor with a non-diffusing, long-lived inhibitor (`r_a = 0.1, r_b = 0.014, b_b = 0.0015, D_a = 0.015`, 36 unit steps per row): **waves** are one-way pulses (one per 50 lip cells); **tents** are the same excitable lip re-igniting at random places (0.18 ignitions per row per 100 cells); **stripes** use `b_a = 0.01, b_b = 0.0003, r_b = 0.025`, a relaxation oscillation that fires the whole lip at once. Pigment is the activator or the inhibitor's fading wake (`max(a, 1.4(b − 0.3))`), so lines have a soft trailing edge. The outline is a cone shell (shoulder, spire of whorls, blunt lip), not a spiral mask; the shell grows at its lip row by row until full length (≈ 12 s), then the pattern scrolls away from the lip.
- **Round 2**: the lip has 3 cells per pixel of body width (crisper lines); pigment memory per regime (decay 0.7 per row for waves and tents, 0.2 for stripes) so lines carry a shaded trailing band and tents filled flanks; tents re-ignite at 0.2 per row per 100 cells; a new shell arrives half-painted (≤ 40 rows computed at once); the shell's length grows 72 % → 100 % over its regime (the lip advancing), and the silhouette edge is shaded by pixel coverage. Pigment is darkest (wine) in the fresh line, violet in the fading band.
- **Round 3 (inks changed)**: natural pigment on a cone shell (*Conus textile* colouring): a cream ground (`Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 222) lit from the upper left with a soft specular band; pigment `Role::Blackbody` 1.6–2.5 kK (false colour: a dim glow of that temperature is the dark orange we see as chestnut and umber; darkest in the fresh line, umber in the fading band), drawn only where the body covers ≥ 60 % of a pixel so the rim stays shell-coloured. The silhouette (flanks, shoulder, lip and a low concave spire with whorl sutures) is antialiased by per-pixel coverage from the signed distance to the outline. The growing lip glows: its newest rows are `Starlight` 255, or `Ramp(Ember)` 210 where fresh pigment is being laid. Spire and full body fill 88 % of the art rows (the body grows 80 % → 100 % over a regime, so ~80 % on average). The lip has at least 100 cells and one-way pulses are 50 cells apart (closer, a pulse runs into its leader's refractory wake and dies), so a small shell still shows several lines. (acceptance IDs added to the Acceptance line).

### molecular_motors  (Life from Matter, M13)
- **Intent**: two molecular machines fuelled by ATP: a kinesin motor walks hand over hand along a microtubule track, one 8 nm step per ATP, pausing between steps and jiggling in thermal noise; then ATP synthase, a rotary motor, turns in 120° steps as protons flow.
- **Model**: kinesin: two heads on a 1-D lattice of binding sites (8 nm period); dwell times exponential with a Michaelis–Menten rate `k = k_cat[ATP]/(K_M + [ATP])` ([ATP] swept on a `Timeline`); during a step the free head diffuses (Langevin, overdamped, in a tethered potential) and binds forward (biased by the neck-linker docking); cargo (a bead) on a spring behind. ATP synthase act: F1 rotor angle advancing in 120° steps each split into 80° + 40° substeps with exponential dwells, driven by a proton-flux rate; c-ring drawn as a wheel.
- **Motion**: kinesin steps at a rate set by [ATP]; thermal jiggle continuously; act 2 rotor steps.
- **Resize**: view scaled to the track; state in nm.
- **Inks**: motor `Ramp(Aurora)` (a GFP-labelled look), microtubule `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 150, ATP molecules `Ramp(Sulfur)` dots, rotor `Ramp(Plasma)` (Round 3; was `Ferroin`, whose dark end turned antialiased edges wine-red), vesicle membrane `Ramp(Ice)`. ASCII: sprites.
- **Title**: `Motor proteins · fuelled by ATP`; captions `kinesin walks`, `synthase turns`.
- **Stylisation**: kinesin dwells slowed 40× (the readout gives the real rate); the heads and rotor are cartoons; the stepping statistics are real in form. The synthase view and its 80° + 40° substeps are from F1 running as an ATP-driven motor (Noji et al. 1997, Yasuda et al. 2001); in the cell the Fo proton flow drives it the other way to make ATP, as shown here, so γ turns clockwise seen from the membrane (Fo) side (the synthesis sense).
- **Acceptance**: `KIN-A1` the mean dwell time matches `1/k` at two [ATP] values within 10 % over 2000 samples; `KIN-A2` steps are exactly 8 nm and heads alternate (hand over hand); `KIN-A3` the rotor's angle only increases, in 120° steps with 80°/40° substeps; `KIN-A4` the Langevin jiggle has zero mean drift when bound; `KIN-A5` captions follow the acts; `KIN-A6` γ turns clockwise on screen, viewed from the Fo side; `KIN-A7` every kinesin step leaves one footprint at the moved head's new site (Round 3).
- **As built (batch 5)**: dwells slowed 40× (`k_cat = 100/s`, `K_M = 60 µM`; `[ATP]` 1000 → 60 → 15 µM over the 24 s kinesin act); the head's swing is overdamped Langevin in a harmonic pull to the next site (display time). ATP synthase is seen from above as in Noji et al. (1997): the α₃β₃ ring, γ and an actin filament; mean dwells 0.45 s (binding, before 80°) and 0.25 s (catalytic, before 40°); each 120° turn releases an ATP. Readouts: `ATP 1000 uM   94 steps/s`, then `ATP made N`.
- **Round 2**: larger view (motor heads 4 nm, vesicle 10 nm radius at `min(w/60, h/32)` px per nm); a vesicle with a bright membrane rim and a mottled lumen (`rng::value_noise`); the microtubule as three staggered protofilaments with per-dimer shade variation; a lazy camera that pans only once the motor strays from the centre, so steps show; ATP synthase's filament drawn thicker with a motion-blur ghost during each substep.
- **Round 3**: a softer microtubule: a dim tube body lit from above fills the gaps, and the tubulin beads are antialiased and low in contrast (α 100, β 132); each site a head binds to leaves a faint `Ramp(Aurora)` footprint on the track that fades over 5 s (the last 8 kept, KIN-A7); the cargo is a 9 nm vesicle with one bright cool bilayer ring (`Ramp(Ice)`), a grainy translucent lumen (two octaves of value noise) and a soft highlight; the tether, heads, α₃β₃ ring and synthase stalk are antialiased (coverage-blended discs and distance-to-segment strokes). γ's screen direction is one function, `gamma_dir`, with the angle growing clockwise seen from the membrane side (KIN-A6). (acceptance IDs added to the Acceptance line).

### hp_protein_folding  (Life from Matter, M13)
- **Intent**: why a protein folds: a chain of oily (hydrophobic, H) and water-loving (polar, P) residues thrashes as a loose coil while hot, then, as it cools, collapses and tucks its oily residues into a core away from the water, leaving the polar ones on the surface.
- **Model**: the 2-D square-lattice HP model (Lau & Dill 1989): a self-avoiding chain of 48 residues, energy `E = −(number of H–H contacts)` between lattice neighbours that are not chain neighbours. The sequence is the 48-residue benchmark `PPHPPHHPPHHPPPPPHHHHHHHHHHPPPPPPHHPPHHPPHPPHHHHH` (best known 2-D fold: 23 contacts). Metropolis Monte Carlo (P: statistical mechanics) with end, corner, crankshaft and pull moves (Lesh et al. 2003); each attempt moves the chain, is checked for self-avoidance on an occupancy grid, and is accepted with probability `min(1, e^(−ΔE/T))`. Simulated annealing on a looping `Timeline` (C: the hydrophobic effect as a contact energy; B: a protein).
- **Motion**: 40 000 attempted moves per simulated second at `T ≤ 1.6`, fewer when hotter (`× (1.6/T)²`, ≥ 6 %), so the hot coil's beads stay distinct on screen; beads ease towards their lattice sites (50 ms), so the chain writhes smoothly; the camera follows the chain's bounding box closely and zooms in slowly (out quickly). Acts: **unfolded** (10 s, `T = 4`), **collapse** (16 s, `T` from 1.4, just above the coil–globule transition, cooled geometrically to 0.15; it reaches 20–21 contacts for seeds 1–6, 23 is the best known), **folded core** (14 s, `T = 0.15`); then the chain is heated again and the loop repeats.
- **Resize**: the chain lives in lattice units and is untouched; the camera (centre and pixels per lattice step) eases to frame the chain's bounding box, capped so a compact fold fills most of the height; water-shimmer tables are rebuilt.
- **Inks**: H residues `Ramp(Sulfur)` gold cores (brighter the more H neighbours they touch: buried H glows) with a warm `Role::Blackbody` halo; P residues `Ramp(Cobalt)` cores with a faint `Ramp(Cobalt)` hydration halo; the backbone a smooth braille line `Ramp(Starlight)` 140 that stops at the beads; the water a faint `Ramp(Cobalt)` ≤ 22 shimmer (two travelling interference patterns from per-frame row/column sine tables). ASCII: H `@`, P `o`, backbone by dot count, water blank.
- **Title**: `Protein folding · oil hides away`; captions `unfolded`, `collapse`, `folded core`. Readout right-aligned on row `h − 1`: `HH contacts N` (or `N`), the current H–H contact count.
- **Stylisation**: a 2-D lattice model, not a real protein: one bead per residue, only an H–H contact energy, no side chains, solvent or hydrogen bonds; Monte Carlo moves are not dynamics, and time and temperature are in model units; the annealing schedule is chosen for the picture and loops; the fold found is a low-energy one, not necessarily the best known.
- **Acceptance**: `HPF-A1` every move keeps the chain connected (consecutive residues one lattice step apart) and self-avoiding, and the cached energy equals the energy recomputed from scratch; `HPF-A2` at `T = 0` the energy never rises, while at high `T` uphill moves are accepted; `HPF-A3` annealing collapses the chain: at the end of the collapse act the radius of gyration of the H residues (the core) is < 60 % of the unfolded act's mean and the chain has ≥ 14 H–H contacts; `HPF-A4` in the folded act H residues face the water less than P residues (mean free lattice neighbours per H < per P); `HPF-A5` captions follow the acts.
